The same holds for the descent statement of Strategy B: a minimal bad anchor exists by DCC, so "every bad anchor has a smaller one" is equivalent to "there is none". These are proof *shapes*, not weaker theorems. Independent content can only come from stage-sensitive forms.
- `mem_R_iff_delay`: for r ∈ R(F), x ∈ R(F) ⟺ x ∈ Θ⁺ᵏ(↓r) for some k. So finite delay of κ⁺r is exactly κ⁺r ∈ R(F). Uses the actual `step` and `Rk`, and `step_mono`, `step_subset_R`.
- `F_eq_of_b_eq`: b y = r ⟹ F(r, y) = r, the equality-preimage identity of the brief's §3.3.
- `RelativeCut.cut_cross` now needs F monotone **only on cross pairs** D × Dᶜ. The DCL labelling only evaluates F there. This is exactly what the ground theory of §34.3g–h encodes: F-terms only for model-cross pairs. A cross-monotone F extends to a monotone total F (⊥ on D × D, ⊤ elsewhere) without creating witnesses.

### C. Stage-sensitive tests and the critical configurations
`insert_delay.py`, `orbit_gap.py`, `base_witness.py`, `gap_witness.py`. Hill-climbing over monotone F, built by join- or meet-closure of random tables.

| statement (delay from ↓u, resp. from ↓⊥) | max found | lattice |
|---|---|---|
| Base: u ⊔ a u ∈ Θ⁺ᵏ(↓u) | **2** | M₃ |
| Insert: u ⊔ a u ⊔ y ∈ Θ⁺ᵏ(↓u), b y ≤ u | **2** | M₃, N₅, 2×3, B₃ |
| κ⁺u ∈ Θ⁺ᵏ(↓u) | **3** | N₅ |
| orbit gap: least j with rₖ ∈ Rⱼ, minus k | **2** (meet-built F; 1 with join-built F) | N₅ |

- **Base is not one-step** (`base_witness.py`, M₃ = {0 < a, b, c < 1}).
  - Take u = b with a(b) = a. Then u ⊔ a u = 1, but Θ⁺(↓b) = {0, a, b}.
  - 1 enters only at the second step, through a(a) = 1. So Base needs a(a(u)), not just a(u).
- **The bottom anchor already needs a detour** (`gap_witness.py`, N₅ = {0 < p < q < 1, 0 < s < 1}).
  - B(⊥) = {0, p, s} and a(⊥) = 0, so κ⁺⊥ = r₁ = p ⊔ s = 1.
  - The stages are R₁ = {0, p, s}, R₂ = R₁ ∪ {q} via a(p) = q, and R₃ = everything via b(1) = q ∈ R₂.
  - The b⁻¹/b⁻¹ critical pair (p, s) at the bottom is resolved through an a-step and then a b⁻¹-step.
- **Strategy B's base case is the first orbit step.** κ⁺⊥ = ⋁R₁ = r₁. A minimal-bad-anchor induction must first prove r₁ ∈ R(F) for every F, which is the k = 1 case of `cutMaps_iff_rk_mem`. Unlike Base/Insert, **"r₁ ∈ R(F) for all F on chain-finite L" is not per-F equivalent to Cut**: it is a genuinely weaker target.
- **Exchange candidates for the critical pair** (`exchange.py`, pure lattice statements, so the finite tests are non-vacuous; y₁ ∥ y₂, b yᵢ ≤ r):

| candidate | failures / incomparable pairs |
|---|---|
| C1: b(y₁ ⊔ y₂) ≤ a y₁ ⊔ a y₂ | 10/7,272 (M₃), 216/4,812 (N₅), 450/8,390 (2×3), 342/36,052 (3×3) |
| C3: b(y₁ ⊔ y₂) ≤ r ⊔ a y₁ ⊔ a y₂ | 8, 96, 180, 114 |
| C4: b(y₁ ⊔ y₂) ≤ r ⊔ a yᵢ for some i | 8, 96, 180, 114 |
| C5: b(y₁ ⊔ y₂) ≤ r ⊔ a(r ⊔ yᵢ) for some i | 2, 60, 140, 98 |

  None fail on B₃ or M₃ × C₂. All four are refuted, so no one-step exchange through a resolves the pair.

### D. Term trees
- **Syntactic Kruskal holds.** Terms are binary trees with leaves ⊥, ⊤ and a finite label set (sort D/C per node), so they are WQO under homeomorphic embedding.
- **It gives no order information.** A monotone F constrains values only along comparable inputs.
- **Obstruction (M_ω-type).** Take atoms d₀, d₁, … of D, pairwise incomparable, and atoms c₀, c₁, … of C. Put F(⊥, ⊤) = d₀ and F(dᵢ, cⱼ) = d_{max(i,j)+1}, with values forced at ⊥ and ⊤ as required by monotonicity. Then F is monotone, since no constraints run between distinct atoms. The terms t_{n+1} = F(tₙ, c₀) embed each into the next while their values are pairwise incomparable.
- So an embedding relation that also demands value comparability is not WQO once values contain an infinite antichain. No pumping can be read off embeddings.
- What does hold is a **spine fact**: for a fixed sibling, if t₀ ≤ t₁ then t_{n+1} = F(tₙ, c) (or F(d, tₙ)) is a chain, hence finite. So an infinite same-side nesting either varies its siblings or never has t₀ ≤ t₁. The obstruction above is of the second kind (d₀ ∥ d₁). By rigidity (§34.3z³⁹), no symmetry can organize such sequences.

### E. Herbrand diagnostic
- The ground theory of `cut_ground.py` is the poset theory with F on cross pairs. It is faithful by the extension above, and `cut_cross` uses exactly this monotonicity.
- It is universal and first-order at fixed H. So a finite refutation exists iff there is no model of height ≤ H, finite or infinite (compactness; Herbrand models are term models).
- Since `cut_finite`, every model is infinite. The H = 6 runs (§34.3z⁴⁰) remain inconclusive.

### F. Smallest open statement (corrected in §34.3z⁴²)
As first written, this section proposed r₁ ∈ R(F), i.e. a(⊥) ⊔ ⋁{y | F(⊥, y) = ⊥} reachable, as a weaker target. That is true **only for a single F**. Quantified over all F on a lattice, it is equivalent to the conjecture (`Stable.cutMaps_of_rk_one`, §34.3z⁴²). The N₅ delay-3 instance stands as an observation.

## 34.3z⁴² The normal form: the R(F) program collapses to the cut

**Theorem (Lean, `Stable.lean`, standard axioms).** For F monotone and D = R(F), let G = `normalize F D` be
- ⊥ on D × D,
- F on the cross pairs D × Dᶜ,
- ⊤ on Dᶜ × L.

Then G is monotone, and:
- `R_normalize`: R(G) = R(F) = {y | b_G(y) ≤ ⊥}, i.e. **the reachable set is exactly the set of columns rescued by ⊥**.
- `a_normalize`: a_G(x) = ⊥ for x ∈ R(F). The a-rule is idle.
- `R_normalize_eq_Rk_one`: R(G) = R₁(G), so every element of R is reached in one step.
- `cutMaps_of_bottom` (DCC): if ⋁{y | b_G(y) ≤ ⊥} ∈ R(G) for **every** G, Cut holds for every F.
- `cutMaps_of_rk_one`: the same with r₁ = κ⁺(⊥) ∈ R(G).

Proof: D stays witness-free for G, because the cross values are unchanged. So D is closed for G:
- a_G(x) ∉ D would give a_G(x) = F(x, a_G x), hence a_G(x) ≤ a_F(x) ∈ D;
- b_G(y) ∈ D with y ∉ D would give b_F(y) ≤ b_G(y), hence y ∈ D.

Hence R(G) ⊆ D. Conversely y ∈ D gives G(⊥, y) = ⊥, hence b_G(y) = ⊥ and y ∈ R(G). Finally, if ⋁D = ⋁B_G(⊥) ∈ R(G) = D, then `sSup_R_mem_iff` applies. ∎

**Consequences for the R(F) program (§§34.3z³⁸–⁴¹).**
- *A single F.* Every R-based local statement (Base, Insert, (SJ), descent, rₖ ∈ R) is per-F equivalent to Cut for that F (`cutMaps_iff_base_insert`, `cutMaps_iff_kstep`, `cutMaps_iff_rk_mem`).
- *All F at once.* The universally quantified forms collapse further. Through the normal form, already the **bottom critical pair** (the join of the columns rescued by ⊥) is equivalent to the whole conjecture on each lattice.
- *Delays.* Stage delays and orbit gaps (§34.3z³⁸.4, §34.3z⁴¹.C) are **not invariants**: every counterexample has a normal form in which all delays inside R are ≤ 1 and a ≡ ⊥ on R. Delay-based well-founded measures cannot carry a proof.
- *What remains.* In normal form, R(F) is just a witness-free cut D with F on D × Dᶜ, and Insert is exactly join-closure of D. The reachability formulation adds nothing beyond the Cut Lemma itself.
- *Where progress has to come from.* Further progress must work on the cut directly, with the lattice structure of D and Dᶜ: compression to meet rows and join columns (`Skeleton`), finite capture (`cut_cross`, `captured`), and one-sided finiteness.
- *The smallest open case* recorded is FG∞ for a crossing triple: D = ↓{p₁, p₂, p₃} with distinct pairwise meets and Dᶜ arbitrary (DCC). By §34.3z³³ it is the chain labelling lemma (CLL) for an infinite DCC column poset. CLL is proved for finite C (`CLL.cll`); the infinite case is open.

## 34.3z⁴³ Infinite CLL: a DCC counterexample, max-only CLL, and the terminal switch

Response to the ChatGPT brief "terminal switching and infinite CLL". New module `lean/Mordor/InfiniteCLL.lean` (standard axioms, full build). `CLL.Labelling` no longer carries `[Fintype C]`; only `cll` needs it.

### 1. Theorem map
- **Finite tools:**
  - `CLL.cll`: needs `[Fintype C]`, `0 < k`.
  - `CLL.cut_of_meets`: produces labels z(A, σ) = F(m_A, cmax σ). These depend on max σ only.
  - `ConeSperner.exists_full`: finite.
- **Minimality and terminal state:**
  - `HistoryFix.min_terminal` (DCC): a column y ∉ D, minimal with F(r, y) ≤ y for r ≤ all pᵢ, and y ⊓ F(pⱼ, y) ∈ D for every j.
  - `MinSwitch.terminal_meet`, `terminal_transfer` (no chain condition).
- **Proved cases:** `FGTwo.cut_two` (DCC); `Laminar`, `Crossing.cut_of_tree` (finite families); `RelativeCut.cut_finite`.

### 2. DCC alone does not suffice for CLL (`not_cll_dcc`)
- **The poset.** `Gap` is ℕ with n < m ⟺ n + 2 ≤ m. It has DCC, infinite ascending chains, and is not a lattice. Let C = WithTop Gap.
- **The labelling.** On the nonempty chains σ of C, set
  - z(σ) = max σ + 1 if ⊤ ∉ σ;
  - z(σ) = max σ + 3 if ⊤ ∈ σ ≠ {⊤};
  - z({⊤}) = 0.
- **Why it works.** It is monotone: a chain's top level stays or jumps by ≥ 2 (`L_step`). It satisfies W2: max σ + 1 is incomparable with max σ, and nothing is above ⊤. It is a `CLL.Labelling 1 C`.
- **Consequence.** The brief's target `cll_of_wellFounded … [WellFoundedLT C] … : False` is **false already for k = 1**. The labels escape *upward* through an infinite ascending chain. They depend on the whole chain: they distinguish {n} from {n, ⊤}.
- **For path following** (brief Priority 2): a well-founded path argument on chain-dependent cells cannot use DCC alone. It needs ACC, or the max-only structure below.
- **For finite capture** (Priority 3): capture fails here by ascending escape, not by an antichain.

### 3. Max-only CLL: the right infinite target
- **FG∞ only needs labels depending on (A, max σ)**, because `cut_of_meets` builds them from F(m_A, cmax σ). `MaxLabelling k C` is the abstraction: w(A, c) with W1, W2, and monotonicity in (A ⊇ A', c ≤ c').
- `MaxLabelling.toLabelling` reads labels at `cmax σ`. `cll_max` gives finite max-only CLL from `cll`.
- **`cll_max_one` (DCC only): infinite max-only CLL for k = 1.**
  - For k = 1, W1 forbids label values, so w(univ, ·) is a monotone column map g with t ≰ g(t).
  - `column_descent`: a minimal t with g(t) ≤ t has g(g t) ≤ g t, so g t = t, contradicting W2.
  - This is the poset form of `cut_principal_down`.
- **`cll_max_two` (DCC only): infinite max-only CLL for k = 2.** This is the poset form of `FGTwo.cut_two`. Its proof uses no operation on columns, only:
  - orbits of the meet row h = w(univ, ·);
  - monotonicity;
  - the k = 2 label pinning (a label containing a, not containing b, and not the full set is {a}).

  The ingredients:
  - `Ends a s` (inductive): the h-orbit of s ends at a label containing a.
  - `Ends.squeeze`: every t with h(s) ≤ t ≤ s inherits it.
  - `Ends.not_lab`: row a cannot carry a label where the orbit ends at a.
  - A DCC-minimal column s with w(a, s) ≤ s and `Ends a s` has the smaller one w = w(a, s).
- **`cut_two_poset`: FG∞ for two generators on bounded posets with DCC.** Here D = ↓p ∪ ↓q, the glb p ⊓ q exists, and the complement need not be a lattice. This generalizes `FGTwo.cut_two`.
- **Status of max-only CLL:**
  - k = 1, 2: proved (DCC, posets).
  - k = 3 crossing: open. This is the smallest FG∞ case. The k = 2 proof breaks exactly where §34.3z¹⁴ says: a label below h(e_m) is pinned to {a} only when k = 2.
- In the counterexample of §2, the max-only restriction removes exactly the escape: a max-only g must satisfy g(⊤) ≥ g(n) for all n, which forces descent.

### 4. The terminal switch (Priority 1)
- **No descending transition at minimal states.** Let x be a `min_terminal` column for the triple-meet row r.
  - Then h(x) = F(r, x) ≤ x ⊓ gᵢ(x) for every i, so F(r, x ⊓ gᵢ x) ≤ F(r, x) ≤ x ⊓ gᵢ x.
  - Minimality therefore forces x ⊓ gᵢ(x) ∈ D. This is `min_terminal`'s last clause.
  - Combined with `terminal_meet` this gives **`MinSwitch.terminal_meet_le_p2`**: in case II, w = x ⊓ g₂ x ≤ p₂ (and w ≰ p₁, p₃).
  - So the brief's transition "Terminal(x) → column w ∉ D below x" **never fires from a minimal state**. The size-change graph built from the proved lemmas has no recurrent cycle at all, and no strict thread is needed.
  - The `terminal_transfer` comparisons concern contraction at w as a column. At a minimal state w is not a column.
- **The obstruction is static.** What remains is a single configuration:
  - x minimal for r;
  - g₁ x ≤ x with label {2};
  - h(x) with labels {1, 2};
  - every meet x ⊓ gᵢ(x) in D with fixed labels (`label_fixed_proper`).

  It must be refuted using columns other than x, because the near-model of §34.3z²³ satisfies it locally.
- **Distinguishing the rows.** Minimality is for r only, as the brief warned. No individual row is minimal at x, and none of the above transfers minimality to rows 1, 2, 3.

### 5. Next lemma and Lean target
- **Smallest open statement:** `MaxLabelling 3 C → False` for posets C with ⊤ and DCC, with the three rows {i} and the meet rows.
  - The k = 2 machinery (`Ends`, `squeeze`, minimal descent) is in place.
  - What k = 3 lacks is the pinning step. An orbit ending with labels Λ ⊊ A has to be transported to a state for the smaller label set. This is the strict switch of §34.3z¹⁴, now in a lattice-free setting where it can be attacked without meets of columns.
  - Proposed Lean statement: `theorem cll_max_three [WellFoundedLT β] (M : MaxLabelling 3 β) : False`.
- **For chain-dependent CLL** the correct infinite hypothesis is chain-finiteness (`[WellFoundedLT C] [WellFoundedGT C]`). Whether it then holds for k = 1 is open: the §2 counterexample needs an infinite ascending chain.

## 34.3z⁴⁴ Max-only CLL for three rows: a boundary ansatz search, and the exact squeeze obstruction

**Search** (`experiments/infcll/maxcll.py`). The aim was an infinite DCC counterexample to `InfiniteCLL.MaxLabelling 3 C`.
- *Columns:* boundary columns K (K₀ = ⊤) plus generic columns (i, t) with i ∈ ℕ and t in a finite set of types.
- *Order:* free on K. Between K and generic columns it depends on the type only. Generic columns are translation invariant: (i, t) < (j, u) only if j > i (class of j − i clamped to [1, W + 1]), or j = i with t < u. So every strict descent lowers (index, type) and **DCC holds**. Ascending chains are allowed.
- *Values:* a label (nonempty, W1 built in), a boundary column, or a generic column. Boundary inputs may output at an absolute index (e, v); generic inputs output at (i + e, v); 0 ≤ e ≤ E.
- *Solving:* counterexample-guided, solving on [0, N] and re-checking every axiom on [0, NV].

| k | types / boundary | W, E, N, NV | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1, 2 | 2 / 2 | 1, 1, 5, 10 | UNSAT (control: `cll_max_one`, `cll_max_two`) |
| 3, without W2 | 2 / 2 | 1, 1, 5, 10 | SAT, verified on [0, 10] (non-vacuity control) |
| 3 | 2 / 2, 3 / 3, 4 / 3 | 1, 1, 6, 14 | **UNSAT** (0.0 s, 3.6 s, 23.9 s) |
| 3 | 4 / 4 | 1, 2, 6, 12 | timeout (900 s) |
| 3 | 5 / 4 | 2, 2, 7, 14 | timeout (900 s) |

UNSAT on a window is sound for the ansatz, since a window only drops constraints.

**Why symmetric ansätze are weak here.** A row whose value at ⊤ is a column c_A has *all* its values in ↓c_A, because ⊤ dominates every column. A row with a label at ⊤ has labels everywhere. So unbounded (escaping) values can only live under boundary columns whose down-sets contain whole generic types. Shift-equivariant structures with ⊤ the only fixed column are impossible outright, by the fixed-point argument of §34.3z³⁹: the restriction to the fixed columns is a finite labelling.

**The squeeze obstruction, exactly.** The k = 2 proof (`cll_max_two`) tracks a family P of labels at which the orbit of the meet row h may end ("`Ends`").
- `Ends.squeeze` needs two closure properties of P:
  - *stop step:* if h(s) ∈ P and t ≤ s, then h(t) is a label ⊇ h(s), so P must be **up-closed**;
  - *label step:* a label h(t) ⊆ h(d) ∈ P must lie in P, so P must be **down-closed** among nonempty labels.
- With W1, the possible meet-row labels are the proper nonempty subsets of the generators.
  - For k = 2 these are {a} and {b}, two incomparable singletons, so P = {{a}} is both up- and down-closed. This is the pinning in `Ends.squeeze`.
  - For k = 3 the proper nonempty subsets of {1, 2, 3} form a connected poset ({1} ⊂ {1,2} ⊃ {2} ⊂ {2,3} …). So **no nontrivial P is both**, and the k = 2 descent cannot be run on the meet row alone.
- This is the strict switch of §34.3z¹⁴ in exact form. A k = 3 proof must change rows, i.e. combine the descents of the pair rows {i, j} with that of the triple row. It cannot track one row's orbit.

**Status.** Max-only CLL with k = 3 on DCC posets is open. No counterexample exists in the boundary ansatz at the sizes that finished. The next step is to formulate the row-switching invariant: a family of pairs (row, label set) closed under the stop and label steps of *every* row. Its existence is a finite check over the 7 rows and 6 proper labels, and it is the right first experiment.

## 34.3z⁴⁵ The descent game for max-only CLL: k = 2 is won by the abstraction, k = 3 is not

`experiments/infcll/descent_game.py` makes the "row-switching invariant" of §34.3z⁴⁴ precise as a finite two-player safety game. Every recorded constraint is a proved fact. If the prover can always move, DCC refutes any counterexample; the strategy, with the facts used, is then a descent proof in the style of `cll_max_two`.

**The game.**
- *State at a column c:*
  - for each of the 2ᵏ − 1 rows, its profile: a label (W1 built in), a column strictly below c ("lt"), or a column not below c ("nc");
  - E(c), the end label of the meet row's orbit.
- *Consistency of a state:*
  - row values decrease along row inclusion: labels below columns, label sets grow, contraction is inherited by larger rows;
  - the meet row is a label or contracting;
  - E equals the meet row's label when that row is a label.
- *Prover's move:* pick a contracting row A. The next column is w = w(A, c) < c.
- *Adversary's reply:* any consistent state at w such that
  - a label at (B, c) forces a larger label at (B, w), by monotonicity in the column;
  - rows B ⊇ A that hold columns are contracting at w, since w(B, w) ≤ w(B, c) ≤ w(A, c) = w;
  - E(w) is comparable to E(c), by `Ends.squeeze` without pinning, applicable because h(c) ≤ w(A, c) = w ≤ c.
- *Initial states:* columns at ⊤ are always "lt".

**Results.**

| k | consistent profiles | states | safe | initial states | prover loses at |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | 16 | 20 | 10 | 6 | **0**: the abstraction proves `cll_max_two` |
| 3 | 2,430 | 2,520 | 696 | 360 | **105** |

**A k = 3 adversary state at ⊤.**
- Every row containing generator 0 has label {1}: rows {0}, {0,1}, {0,2} and the meet row.
- Rows {1}, {2}, {1,2} contract.
- E = {1}.

The prover must move along a row avoiding 0. The adversary answers with states the recorded facts cannot exclude, so after finitely many moves no row contracts.

**Reading.**
- This is the exact abstract form of the strict switch: the meet-row end label plus inclusion-monotone profiles are too weak at k = 3.
- *Next strengthening:* track the end label E_B of every descending row B, with the squeeze applied to all B ⊇ A, and the cross-row bound for B ⊇ B′ (the B-orbit lies below the B′-orbit). This keeps every fact proved, but the state space grows to ~10⁶–10⁷. It needs a SAT- or BDD-based game solver rather than enumeration.
- *Face restriction suggests a route:* in the adversary state the rows avoiding generator 0 form a k = 2 subproblem on {1, 2}. It reduces to `cll_max_two` unless some row B ⊆ {1, 2} acquires the label {0}. So a k = 3 proof may go by induction on faces, with the escape label {0} as the only obstruction.

## 34.3z⁴⁶ Per-row end labels do not close the game; the face reduction (Lean)

**Strengthened game** (`experiments/infcll/descent_game2.py`, exact attractor in `descent_game3.py`). Every row that is a label or contracting now carries the end label E_B of its own orbit, and a move along A must satisfy:
- labels grow downward;
- rows B ⊇ A holding columns contract at w;
- **E_B(w) comparable to E_B(c) for every B ⊇ A** (`Ends.squeeze` for row B, valid since w(B, c) ≤ w(A, c) = w ≤ c);
- **E_A(w) = E_A(c)**, because the A-orbit passes through w.

Results:
- 186,282 states, 77,058 initial (no "nc" at ⊤). The attractor computed by bitset joins converges in 4 iterations (19 s) to 61,293 adversary-winning states.
- **The adversary wins from 20,250 initial states.** Example: every row contracts at ⊤, row {0}'s orbit ends at {1}, and every other row's orbit ends at {0}.
- What is missing is any relation between the orbits of *incomparable* rows ({0} versus {1}, {1,2}), together with the timing of orbits along B ⊇ B′ (the B-orbit lies below the B′-orbit step by step). The strict switch needs exactly this cross-row information. No per-row invariant suffices.

**Face reduction** (`InfiniteCLL.face_escape`, Lean, standard axioms).
- *Statement:* in any `MaxLabelling 3 β` with DCC, for every generator l some row B ∌ l carries the label {l} at some column.
- *Proof:* otherwise every label at a row avoiding l meets the face {l}ᶜ. Pulling back along `Fin.succAbove l`, the rows A ⊆ Fin 2 with labels α ∩ {l}ᶜ form a `MaxLabelling 2 β`, which `cll_max_two` refutes.
- *Consequence:* a k = 3 counterexample must use all three **escape labels** {0}, {1}, {2}, each on a row of the opposite face.
- The general form follows by the same argument: a k-row counterexample needs, for every proper face, the escape label of its complement. That would make induction on k work once every lower k is proved. It is stated in Lean only for k = 3.

**Next.** Add the escape constraints and the orbit timing along B ⊇ B′ (both proved) to the game, and recompute. If the adversary still wins, extract its winning region as a candidate infinite counterexample shape. Each adversary state is a column type, and the winning strategy is a recipe for building the poset.

## 34.3z⁴⁷ Closed-column normalization; the faithful target assumes ACC too

**Closed columns.** Call c *closed* if every row at c is a label or contracting (value ≤ c). Below a closed column, all values stay in ↓c: labels stay labels, and contracting values are ≤ w(A, c) ≤ c. So `MaxLabelling k` restricts to ↓c with top c.
- Taking a DCC-minimal closed column, a counterexample may be assumed to have **⊤ as its only closed column**: every c < ⊤ has a row whose value is not below c.
- *In the game* (`descent_game3.py onlytop`): successors must contain an "nc" row. The attractor is **unchanged**: 61,293 adversary-winning states, adversary wins from 20,250 initial states. Dead states cannot be all labels (finite CLL at one column), so the adversary's targets already have "nc" rows. The game's weakness is structural, not a missing global normalization.

**ACC.** FG∞ is stated for complete lattices with **ACC and DCC** (§34.3o). `cll_max_one` and `cll_max_two` use DCC only, and the k = 3 game above uses no ACC.
- Under DCC alone, unbounded values can only escape *sideways and upward* (§34.3z⁴⁴: rows with a column at ⊤ stay below that column). Such escape needs infinite ascending chains or transfinite "limit" columns above them, as in the chain-dependent counterexample `not_cll_dcc`.
- So the faithful k = 3 target is `MaxLabelling 3 C → False` for **chain-finite** C (`[WellFoundedLT C] [WellFoundedGT C]`). The DCC-only version may be strictly stronger and possibly false.
- *Next experiment:* a DCC-only counterexample search with transfinite boundary levels; the boundary ansatz of §34.3z⁴⁴ had one level of limits. In parallel, look for facts that use ACC: for example, a maximal column below w(A, ⊤) at which a given row escapes. Add them to the game.

## 34.3z⁴⁸ Levelled (limit) ansatz for max-only CLL, k = 3, DCC only

`experiments/infcll/maxcll_levels.py` extends the boundary ansatz of §34.3z⁴⁴ with **limit levels**, the escape route left open by §34.3z⁴⁷.
- *Levels:* generic types carry levels.
- *Order:* level ℓ elements lie below level ℓ′ > ℓ elements by type only, independent of indices. So a higher level sits above whole ascending chains, like a limit. The order is never downward across levels. Within a level it is translation invariant as before.
- *DCC:* holds by (level, index, type).
- *Values:* a generic input outputs relatively within its own level or at an absolute index of a lower level. Boundary inputs output anywhere at an absolute index.
- *Solving:* counterexample-guided as before (window [0, 5], check on [0, 10]).

| k | types (levels) / boundary | result |
|---|---|---|
| 1, 2 | 4 (0,0,1,1) / 2 | UNSAT (controls) |
| 3, without W2 | 4 (0,0,1,1) / 2 | SAT (non-vacuity control) |
| 3 | 4 (0,0,1,1) / 2 | **UNSAT** (1.9 s) |
| 3 | 4 (0,0,1,1) / 3 | **UNSAT** (105 s) |
| 3 | 6 (0,0,1,1,2,2) / 3, 6 (0,0,0,1,1,1) / 3, 6 (0,0,1,1,2,2) / 4 | timeout (600 s each) |

No DCC-only counterexample exists in the two-level ansatz at these sizes; the larger instances are undecided. Together with §34.3z⁴⁴–⁴⁷, no structured infinite DCC column poset has produced a k = 3 counterexample:
- the boundary ansatz with one limit level;
- the two-level ansatz;
- the shift-equivariant one, which is impossible.

The evidence points toward max-only CLL for k = 3 holding even under DCC alone, as for k = 1, 2. That would make ACC unnecessary. It is evidence, not proof.

## 34.3z⁴⁹ Response to the Le Chat brief "three proof programs" for max-only CLL, k = 3

**Priority 1, A1′ (label-value floor): ill-posed.**
- In `MaxLabelling` a label is a generator set only and carries no value z. W2 (`¬ c ≤ c'` for a column value c′) constrains column values only.
- A row may keep a constant label along any descent; label growth permits equality. So the "stuck case" of A2 (row A carries exactly {l} at every t ≤ c) **cannot be refuted from W2**. If it is refutable at all, it needs DCC-minimality or cross-row facts.
- In the FG∞ instance the label does stand for a D-value, but `cut_of_meets` forgets that value. Restoring it would mean a richer abstraction than `MaxLabelling`.

**Priority 2, A1 (face restriction): proved, after correcting the local form** (`InfiniteCLL.lean`, standard axioms).
- Only one pullback failure exists, Λ = {l}. The brief's second failure Λ ⊇ F cannot occur on a row A ⊆ F, because A ⊆ Λ would violate W1.
- **`face_escape_on`:** let S be a set of columns with a greatest element t, closed under the column values of the rows avoiding l. Then some row avoiding l carries {l} at a column of S. Rows containing l play no role. The restricted structure is a `MaxLabelling 2` on the subtype S with top t, refuted by `cll_max_two`.
- **`face_escape`** is now the case S = univ.
- **`face_escape_below`:** if every row avoiding l is a label or contracting at c ("F-closed"), then S = ↓c qualifies, so an {l}-escape exists at some column ≤ c.
- **The brief's local A1 on ↓c is false in general.** Values at x ≤ c leave ↓c unless the face rows are closed at c. Under the closed-column normalization (§34.3z⁴⁷) only ⊤ is closed for *all* rows, but F-closedness concerns only the three face rows. That is what makes the challenge move of Program B sound.

**Priority 3, Program C (two-row certificate R ⊆ L × L): dead, without enumeration.**
- Obligations 1 (stop step, up-closure) and 2 (label step, down-closure) apply to each coordinate along face moves, by the squeeze for every row ⊇ A.
- For fixed second coordinate, the first coordinate ranges over the pair row's legal end labels {0}, {1}, {2}, {0,1}, {0,2}. Under inclusion these form a connected poset ({1} ⊂ {0,1} ⊃ {0} ⊂ {0,2} ⊃ {2}). The second coordinate ranges over the six proper nonempty subsets of {0, 1, 2}, also connected.
- A relation both up- and down-closed in each coordinate is therefore ∅ or L × L, whatever the coupling (obligation 3).
- So no certificate of this form carries information. A cross-row invariant must constrain *which steps occur*, not just which labels.

**Priority 4, Program B (challenge–reveal game)** (`experiments/infcll/descent_game4.py`).
- The descent game of §34.3z⁴⁶ gets a new prover move "challenge l", available at F_l-closed states with no face row labelled {l}.
- The adversary must answer with a state strictly below in which some row avoiding l carries exactly {l}. Labels grow; no squeeze relation is assumed, because the reveal column need not lie on an orbit.
- Sound by `face_escape_below`.
- **Result: unchanged.** 61,293 adversary-winning states; the adversary wins from 20,250 of 77,058 initial states (38 s). The adversary always answers with an adversary-winning escape state, since the reveal relates the two columns only by label growth.
- So `face_escape` used as an oracle does not close the game. A useful challenge would need the escape column to lie on a descent from the current column, which `face_escape_below` does not give.

**Programs A2/A3.**
- The switch case (re-establishing the squeeze bookkeeping across an escape) remains the decisive unproved step, as the brief says.
- The stuck case is not refutable from W2 (Priority 1).
- No new proof obligations were discharged.

**Status.** New Lean: `face_escape_on`, `face_escape_below`, `MaxLabelling.lab_down_three`. Max-only CLL with k = 3 remains open.

## 34.3z⁵⁰ Response to Le Chat's second brief: the funnel (Lean), the funnel game, the audit and the enrichment

**F1, the funnel: proved** (`InfiniteCLL.face_escape_funnel`, standard axioms).
- `FaceReach M l c` is the inductive set of columns reachable from c by column-value steps of rows avoiding l.
- *Statement:* if c is face-closed (every row avoiding l is a label or contracting at c), some row avoiding l carries {l} at a column of `FaceReach M l c`.
- *Proof:* the reachable set lies in ↓c (a step from x ≤ c lands at most at the row's value at c, which is ≤ c; a label at c stays a label below). It has greatest element c and is closed by construction, so `face_escape_on` applies.
- The brief's worry that `face_escape_on` might demand more than column-value closure does not arise: its closure hypothesis quantifies over column values of nonempty face rows only.

**F3, the funnel-constrained game: no effect** (`experiments/infcll/descent_game5.py`).
- *The reveal* must follow face steps from c.
  - Contracting steps carry the usual move constraints (label growth, contraction of rows ⊇ A, squeeze on E_B for B ⊇ A, E_A preserved).
  - A non-contracting step lands below c, since its value is ≤ that row's value at c. It is constrained only by label growth from c.
- *Reachability:* both reachability sets are least fixpoints over the bitset join.
  - "A face row becomes non-contracting after contracting face moves" holds from **183,564 of 186,282** states.
  - Once that happens, the reveal is unconstrained.
- *Result:* the attractor is unchanged at 61,293 states, and the adversary wins from 20,250 of 77,058 initial states (151 s).
- *Why:* the abstraction constrains non-contracting rows at a successor only by label growth. So the funnel's location constraint is vacuous here. A sharper fact for non-contracting steps exists (the step value from x ≤ c lies below the row's value at c, i.e. below the state reached from c by that row's own move). It only moves the label-growth base from c to that successor, and the adversary chooses that successor too.

**Program A, the transition audit: premise false as stated.** "The first-labeling transition is free" fails. A label at (A, c) forces labels with growing sets at every smaller cell (A′ ⊇ A, c′ ≤ c), and a column at (A, c) forces columns at larger cells. Both are in every game. What *is* free in `MaxLabelling` is the contraction status of rows not ⊇ A at a successor, and the location of a first label *within* those constraints. The F3 result is the measured form of this freedom.

**Program V, the escape-value enrichment: premise mismatched.**
- The values `cut_of_meets` discards are the **D-values** F(m_A, max σ) ∈ D, not columns in C. So "z ≰ c in W2's form" is not the relevant constraint, and there is no label-value floor against column descent.
- The sound enrichment carries D-values with W1 in its lattice form (value ≰ m_A, i.e. not below every generator in A) and monotonicity in the cell. That is FG∞ itself in its row-compressed form (`Crossing.rows_to_meets`).
- So "retreat to T2" means working with the lattice D = ↓{p₁, p₂, p₃}, as §§34.3z¹⁴–²³ did. The meets m_A then become the extra structure that max-only CLL forgets.

**Net.** New Lean: `FaceReach`, `face_escape_funnel`. The k = 3 game abstraction is not tight at non-contracting steps. This is the measured residual of every face- or escape-based strengthening tried: challenge, funnel, per-row end labels, closed-column normalization. A proof must either supply facts about the values of non-contracting rows, or leave `MaxLabelling` for the lattice form of FG∞.

## 34.3z⁵¹ Response to a fresh ChatGPT brief: maximal label boundaries (Lean) and the pair anchor

Checkout at the start: `7afe6ea`, clean.

**Strategy 1, maximal label boundaries** (`InfiniteCLL.lean`, standard axioms).
- *A correction first.* The brief derives "⊥ ∈ L_A" from W2 at a bottom column. Column posets need not have a bottom: in FG∞ the columns are Dᶜ, typically with infinitely many minimal elements. With a bottom the problem is trivial: every row is a label at ⊥, and **`not_all_labels`** (finite CLL at one column, via `cll_max` on `Unit`) excludes that. This is **`cll_max_of_bot`**.
- *The correct statements:*
  - **`label_region_nonempty`** (DCC): every nonempty row is a label somewhere. A row that is a column everywhere is a monotone map g with c ≰ g(c), excluded by `column_descent`.
  - **`label_region_down`**: label regions are down-closed, with growing label sets.
  - **`maximal_label_boundary`** (DCC + ACC): every row A has a labelled column c_A above which it is a column everywhere, with c ≰ d by W2. Not unique in general.
- **The seven-row boundary table** (`experiments/infcll/boundary_table.py`, z3).
  - *Points:* ⊤, one boundary b_B per row, and m fresh value points.
  - *Row values:* known at ⊤ and the boundaries only.
  - *Constraints:* W1, W2, monotonicity among known points, the diagonal labels w(B, b_B), and columns at known points above b_B.
- *Results:*
  - **m = 2, open table: SAT.** In the model, the boundaries form a small poset with two value points below some of them. The non-diagonal rows take columns at value points or other boundaries.
  - **Closed tables (rows also evaluated at the value points; m = 0 is closed): z3 timeout** (600 s and 900 s). They are finite max-only labellings, so they are refuted by `cll_max` *as a theorem*. The timeout is the parity hardness of the finite contradiction (cf. §34.3z³⁶).
- *So:* the boundary table carries no obstruction of its own. Any contradiction needs the rows' values at the value points, i.e. closure under values, which regenerates the whole labelling. This is the finite-capture regress again (§34.3z³⁸). The extra "layer above each boundary" the brief asks about is exactly this closure. A finite certificate would need a *finite* closed set, and its existence is the open question.

**Strategy 2, value-aware pair-meet anchor: vacuous by normalization.**
- The anchor w = x ⊓ g₂(x) lies **in D** (`terminal_meet_le_p2`). So g₁₂(w) = F(p₁ ⊓ p₂, w), its fixed point z ≤ w, and "PairAnchorFace" (z ≤ p₁ ∨ z ≤ p₃) all concern F on **D × D**.
- Normalize F to ⊥ on D × D (§34.3z⁴², the same construction for the cut D = ↓{p₁, p₂, p₃}). This keeps F monotone and the cut witness-free.
- It also leaves every `MinSwitch.Terminal` hypothesis intact. Those mention F only at cross pairs (x ∉ D), and the derived bounds g₁(w) ≤ x, g₂(w) ≤ v follow from monotonicity for every F.
- In normal form g₁₂(w) = ⊥, so z = ⊥ and PairAnchorFace holds trivially.
- So the lemma is **true but carries no information**. No statement about F-values on D × D can contribute to a contradiction, since every counterexample has a normal form where those values are ⊥. A useful handoff must speak about F on cross pairs.

**Recommendation.** Neither route produces a contradiction.
- Strategy 1 reduces to finite capture (closure under values).
- Strategy 2 is about irrelevant values.

The remaining gap is unchanged: facts about the values of non-contracting rows on cross pairs (§34.3z⁵⁰).

## 34.3z⁵² Response to a ChatGPT brief on the product frontier (Ω × Ω × C₂)

Checkout at the start: `dc45bf0`, clean. The README (v3.8) and this log (file name v3.7, but all of round 26 including §34.3z⁵¹) agree. Every README frontier item is a section here.

The brief proposes three programs:
- **A:** finite-guide plane lifting via `KPass`.
- **B:** DC2 block certificates.
- **C:** two-sided stuck-box descent.

All three were already worked in this log; this section maps them onto it and adds the checks below.

**A. Finite-guide plane lifting = G1 (§34.3z⁗–⁵).**
- *The exact statement used.* `KPass.kpass` (any complete Q, K): the passed bifunction H is monotone and witness-free on cells. Each value is
  - (⊥, z₂), or
  - (⊤, z₂), or
  - kept, with e ≤ z ≤ r and e ≤ h ≤ r, where e.1 ≠ ⊥ and r.1 ≠ ⊤, around a base corner h = F((⊥, k), (⊤, k′)). Here (⊥, k) ∈ D and (⊤, k′) ∉ D are the layers of cell values.
- *What finiteness of K buys.* Finitely many base corners, and nothing more. The values are infinitely many, and the sandwich bounds coordinates only through ideals and filters of the corner's middle coordinates.
- *The brief's bridge lemma.* It says the guide configurations plus the relative plane theorem give a witness. With K = C₂ and Q = Ω², this is G1 for two layers, i.e. Cut on Ω × Ω × C₂.
  - The hand argument (§34.3z⁵.3) proves it when no base corner is anti-diagonal.
  - `Merge.cut_prod_two_of_bot/_top/_eq` prove it for non-thick cuts.
  - What remains is the thick cut, reduced by hand to the residues (A), (C), (A–B) (§34.3z⁵.6): layer escapes at anchor values. The solver-checked generalized collapse (`GenCollapse.gen_collapse`, Lean) leaves exactly 9 anchor cut types.
- *Falsification of the brief's simplified form.* A bridge lemma that uses only the KPass data (anchor cells and base corners, all cells over level-0 points of Q, with level-1 values) cannot hold. The **depth-1 instances are SAT even for the plane**, where Cut is a theorem (§34.3z⁶; reproduced now: plane, pools [1] and [2], depth 1: SAT). The witness needs cells over the atoms that the guides introduce (level 2), which is what `Plane`'s corner lemma uses through finite ideals and filters.

**B. DC2 certificates (§34.3z⁶–¹⁰).**
- *Reproduced from the current source* (`experiments/products/depth_support_gen.py`):
  - plane, depth 2: UNSAT for (1,1), (2,1), (2,2);
  - M_ω² × C₂, depth 2: UNSAT for (1,1), (2,1), (2,2), with up to 72 points and 70,506 variables;
  - depth 1: SAT.
- These are finite instances, evidence for Conjecture DC2, not a proof.
- The brief's critical warning is already a recorded result. Generation depth on Ω climbs chains without bound (§34.3z⁹), so a fixed-depth DC2 cannot settle Ω-products. §34.3z¹⁰ found that bounded-shape certificates do not exist there: the certificate is a *chain induction* (a threshold-indexed corner lemma along a chain).
- So the generic extraction theorem B asks for must be a chain induction, not a template family. That theorem is the open step.

**C. Stuck-box descent.**
- **Ranking fact, proved** (`Stable.boxRefine_wf`, standard axioms). Strict interval refinement s ≤ s′ ≤ t′ ≤ t, (s′, t′) ≠ (s, t) is well-founded under ACC and DCC. It is a strict increase in α × αᵒᵈ.
- **The splitting lemma cannot be falsified on any lattice where Cut is known.** It speaks about witness-free configurations, and there are none on Ω × Ω, finite lattices, horizontal sums, and so on. A test needs a relaxed ("local") witness-freeness, and the log's experience with such relaxations is that they are SAT trivially: local statements are falsified by fresh values (§34.3z¹⁹, §34.3z⁶ depth 1).
- On Ω × Ω the box/hub criterion fails at every corner (23 of 23, §34.3t), yet Cut holds by `Plane`'s anchors. So a splitting step has to produce children that `Localize.capture_contra` does not see. The child construction is the open content, as the brief says. No candidate child construction is supplied, and none was found.

**Ranking.**
- **B (DC2 as a chain induction)** has the best evidence: uniform UNSAT at depth 2 for two M_ω factors and every layer count tested.
- **A** reduces to the same residues, and the depth-1 SAT result says KPass-level data cannot close them.
- **C** is a proof architecture without a child construction.

None of this advances the full conjecture beyond the product case.

## 34.3z⁵³ Poset end-finite Cut; DC2 for M_ω² × C₃ at larger sizes

**`RelativeCut.cut_end_finite_poset`** (Lean, standard axioms). On every bounded poset whose proper principal ideals and filters are finite, every monotone F and every cut D ∋ ⊥, ∌ ⊤ have a witness.
- *Proof:* with c = F(⊥, ⊤) ≠ ⊥, every cross value lies below F(x, ⊤) ∈ ↑c ∖ {⊤}. That is a finite set of elements, each with a finite ideal, so all cross values lie in one finite set and `cut_cross` applies.
- This is the poset form of `cut_of_end_finite` (§34.3r), which used lattice operations. Like `cut_two_poset`, it moves a lattice theorem to posets through the double-cone lemma.
- Caveat: weakening the order of a lattice does not transfer Cut, because monotone maps need not stay monotone. So this does not by itself reach products. It is the poset building block that a poset version of `Layers` or `Plane` would start from.

**DC2 on M_ω² × C₃** (`experiments/products/depth_support_gen.py`, depth 2, three layers). This is the case §34.3z⁹ singles out: edge-height 6, so beyond `cl_height5`, and covered by no product theorem (M_ω² and M_ω × C₃ are not end-finite). Earlier runs went up to (2, 2).

| (|L₁|, |L₂|) | points | variables | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| (2, 2) | 108 | 171,450 | UNSAT |
| (3, 1) | 108 | 165,744 | UNSAT |
| (1, 3) | 108 | 173,880 | UNSAT |
| (3, 2) | 147 | 363,900 | UNSAT |
| (2, 3) | 147 | 369,606 | UNSAT |
| (3, 3) | 192 | 708,708 | UNSAT (1,308 s, §34.3z⁵⁸) |

These remain finite instances. DC2 needs UNSAT for *every* size to imply Cut on M_ω² × C₃, so this is evidence, not proof. On M_ω no chain induction is needed (all chains have length 1). The missing piece is uniformity in the pool sizes, not in chain length.

## 34.3z⁵⁴ The H = 6 ground refutation, longest runs so far: still SAT

Both runs use frontier selection (§34.3z⁴⁰) with WLOG and `forget=10⁶`. Each was capped at 7000 s (about 1 h 57 min) of wall clock on a 4-core, 15 GB container, the two in parallel.

| run | terms per round | final terms | variables | peak RSS | result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `run_height.py 6 20000 2 front,forget=1000000` | 2 | 797 (round 400, 5739 s) | 641,585 | 1.97 GB | SAT, timed out |
| `run_height.py 6 20000 4 front,allmid,forget=1000000,cd195` | 4 | 1,180 (round 300, 5959 s) | 1,401,840 | 8.05 GB | SAT, timed out |

- Earlier H = 6 attempts stopped at 453–701 terms (§34.3h, §34.3z³⁹, §34.3z⁴⁰). These are the largest term sets tried at H = 6.
- **Cost per term keeps growing.** In both runs the time per 25 rounds grew steadily: from 33 s (rounds 75–100) to 1,237 s (rounds 375–400) in run 1, and from 81 s (rounds 75–100) to 1,533 s (rounds 275–300) in run 2. Variables are about (terms)², the ratio falling to 1.01, because the order is encoded on all term pairs. So doubling the budget would add only a few hundred more terms.
- Term counts so far are 7, 19, 128 (frontier: 122) at H = 3, 4, 5. At H = 6 the runs are past 9× the H = 5 count with no sign of closing. This is not evidence for a counterexample: the search can only end in UNSAT or never (`cut_finite`, §34.3z³⁹), and a run that is merely slow does not show which.
- **Verdict: inconclusive.** Cut and CL at edge-height 6, and with them M_ω² × C₃, stay open. DC2 on M_ω² × C₃ (§34.3z⁵³) is the only evidence at that height. This search engine appears to have reached its practical limit at H = 6. Further progress needs either a structural reduction of the term space or a different proof method, not more wall clock.
- The checkpoints (`cut_H6_pr2frf1000000.ckpt`, `cut_H6_pr4framf1000000cd195.ckpt`) resume these trajectories with the same commands. They stay out of git, per `.gitignore`.

## 34.3z⁵⁵ External framework: "Frodo" (bialgebraic and dialgebraic axioms) read for proof strategy

The framework has three tiers.
- *Tier 1, algebra.* Monoid μ, η; comonoid ν, ε; two crossing laws behind a mode flag: the bialgebra law B1, ν(μ(x, y)) = μ(x₁, y₁) ⊗ μ(x₂, y₂), and the Leibniz law DA1, ν(μ(x, y)) = x₁ ⊗ μ(x₂, y) ⊞ μ(x, y₁) ⊗ y₂. Plus dendriform halves, Frobenius, antipode.
- *Ambient.* A semi-additive symmetric monoidal category: CMon-enriched, with biproducts, ⊗ distributing over ⊕ (A1–A4).
- *Tiers 2 and 3.* Numeric budgets (potential Φ, stretch, slack with a Lipschitz envelope, a halting guard).
- The one nontrivial theorem is **T3, mode exclusion**: under cancellative hom-monoids, tensor faithfulness, 2 cancellative and a connected grading, B1 and DA1 together force X = X₀.

Read against the program, five findings.

**1. The thin model is not an admissible ambient.** In a poset category a biproduct X ⊕ Y is both the meet and the join, so X = Y. A zero object needs ⊥ = ⊤. So A3 holds only for the one-point lattice. The semi-additive ambients that carry lattices are Sup (sup-lattices and join-preserving maps) and Rel. There ⊞ = ∨ is idempotent, which breaks H1 (cancellation) and H3 (2 cancellative). **T3 has no instance in this program's setting.**

**2. New (Lean, `Axioms/Leibniz.lean`, standard axioms): the unital Leibniz law collapses wherever sums cancel.**
- **`leibniz_collapse`:** an abelian group with a unital biadditive μ, an additive left counit for ν, and DA1 is zero.
- *Proof:* at x = y = η, DA1 reads ν(η) = ν(η) + ν(η), so ν(η) = 0. The counit gives η = 0, and then x = μ(η, x) = 0.
- *Generality:* the statement abstracts A ⊗ A to an arbitrary abelian group T. The unit laws enter only as "the two tensored unit maps are the identity", so it covers every module over every ring, and every semi-additive category with cancellative hom-monoids.
- *Consequence 1:* in every ambient where T3's hypotheses hold, di-mode alone has only the zero model. T3 is true there but vacuous.
- *Consequence 2:* the framework's claim that "each mode separately admits exact, non-degenerate models — di-mode: Aguiar's infinitesimal bialgebras" is false for its DA1 with unit and counit (DA6). This is Aguiar's observation that unital, counital ε-bialgebras vanish. The known repair is Loday–Ronco's unital infinitesimal law, Δ(xy) = (x ⊗ 1)Δ(y) + Δ(x)(1 ⊗ y) − x ⊗ y. The "2(p ⊗ q) di-mode signature" of the framework's worked trace (§9.2) is this double count. Already DA1 at (p, η) gives p ⊗ η = 0 there.
- *Converse, also in Lean:* in Rel, where ⊞ = ∪, the law has a nonzero exact model. Take words with concatenation and deconcatenation: `deconcat_leibniz` (by `List.append_eq_append_iff`), with `deconcat_counit` and `deconcat_seed`. B1 fails there (`deconcat_not_bialg`).
- So di-mode is consistent exactly in idempotent ambients, which is where this program lives, and there T3 does not apply.

**3. The T3 audit, rechecked** (`experiments/dialgebra/frodo_t3.py`, free nonassociative terms). The branch counts are right: 8 for B1 and 6 for DA1. The residual identity is not. After cancellation it reads z ⊗ a ⊞ μ(x, z) ⊗ y ⊞ μ(y, z) ⊗ x = a ⊗ z, not "= 0". The conclusion z ⊗ a = 0 survives, because a ⊗ z has bidegree (2n, n) and the bidegree-(n, 2n) component alone carries z ⊗ a. The two expansions of ν(a) alone already force y ⊗ x = x ⊗ y.

**4. The slack tier degenerates under idempotence.** In an idempotent monoid n·δ = δ, so every Lipschitz factor acts as 1, and Θ5b becomes δ_{n+1} ≤ δ_n ∨ ε_{n+1}. That is an ascending chain, stationary under ACC. The "running envelope" is the orbit r_{k+1} = κ⁺(r_k) of §32.6 / foundations §7.5. There is no explosion to guard against, so the guard carries no information. The defect corresponding to Frodo's slack is the lax square sup ∘ Θ⁺ ≤ κ⁺ ∘ sup. Exactness of that square is I6 (strict fusion), known to fail in general and to hold on principal down-sets and on top-closed lattices.

**5. One-sided versus two-sided distribution** is the framework's headline contrast (DA1 hits one factor, B1 both). It is already the shape of the program's objects:
- the Cut witness is a one-sided disjunction, F(x, y) ≤ x or y ≤ F(x, y);
- the closure step Θ(D) = ↓⊥ ∪ ↓a(D) ∪ b⁻¹(D) is a union of one-sided images;
- the two-sided expansion F(x ∨ x′, y ∨ y′) ≥ F(x, y) ∨ F(x, y′) ∨ F(x′, y) ∨ F(x′, y′) produces exactly the cross terms that cross-closure (`cut_cross`) tracks.

This is a restatement; no new statement follows from it.

**Verdict.** The framework supplies no proof strategy for the bridge (§34.4a), DC2 or `cll_max_three`. Its tier 1 is standard algebra in cancellative ambients, and this program's ambient is idempotent. There T3 has no instance, the slack tier reduces to the ACC orbit, and the mode contrast restates the shape of Θ. Two corrections to the framework itself came out of the reading, both checked: its di-mode is inconsistent in its own cancellative ambients (Lean), and T3's residual identity is misstated, though harmlessly (script).

## 34.3z⁵⁶ An outside strategy brief ("Proof Strategies v1", S1–S8): audit, two Lean results, one proposal refuted

The brief proposes eight strategy templates against a stated frontier. Every item was checked against the log and the Lean sources. New Lean is in `Transport.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**The stated frontier is partly out of date.**
- Item 4, *finite posets*, is settled: `RelativeCut.cut_finite` (§34.3z³⁷) proves the Cut Lemma on every finite bounded poset.
- D_ω and E_ω (named in S2) are settled by `cl_height3` and `cl_height4`.
- M_ω² × C₂ (the DC3 test bed in S8) has edge-height 5, so `cl_height5` settles it (§34.3z⁸).
- What is actually open: the bridge (join-closure of R(F) on chain-finite lattices); Ω³ and products with a factor that is not end-finite (DC2, §34.3z⁵³); FG∞ for crossing triples, via max-only CLL at k = 3 (§34.3z⁴³–⁵¹); edge-height ≥ 6 (§34.3z⁵⁴).

**S5, retraction case: refuted as a reduction (Lean).**
- **`commuting_retraction`:** every monotone F on a complete lattice has an idempotent monotone r with one-point image and r(F(x, y)) = F(r x, r y). Take r constant at a fixed point c = F(c, c) of the diagonal, which exists by Knaster–Tarski.
- So the proposal "a retraction onto a finite subposet commuting with F forces Cut" has a hypothesis that every F satisfies. It is equivalent to the Cut Lemma itself.
- The adjoint case fails the same way: a Galois insertion onto a one-point poset has g = ⊥, and the comonad g∘f is constant.
- **What survives** is **`cut_of_retraction`**: a commuting r with finite image gives a witness for every cut D with r(⊥) ∈ D ∌ r(⊤). Idempotence is not needed.
- It is a corollary of **`cut_of_straddle`**: in any partial order, a finite F-closed set K whose least element is in D and whose greatest element is not contains a witness (`cut_finite` on K). `cutCells_of_finite_Fclosure` is the case ⊥, ⊤ ∈ K.
- *Contrapositive:* in a counterexample, every finite F-subalgebra with a least and a greatest element lies entirely inside D or entirely outside it.
- Compared with `cutCells_of_equivariant` (§34.3z³⁹): an automorphism fixes ⊥ and ⊤, so its fixed set always straddles. A retraction need not fix them, and that is exactly the condition the proposal lost.

**S4, duality transport (Lean, partial completion).**
- **`cutCells_dual_iff`:** CutCells(Fᵈ) ⟺ CutCells(F) on any complete lattice, with `Duality.dualF`, the complemented cut and the two witness disjuncts swapped. No such lattice-level transport existed: `GroundCut.CutCx.dual` covers only the ground theory.
- *Audit of existing duals.* Transported by an inline `OrderDual` instantiation: `cut_of_filter_finite`, `cut_of_finite_hubs_dual`, `cut_of_region_dual`. Proved a second time by hand: `cut_principal_up` / `cut_principal_down`, `TwoSided.reset_core_dual` (about 75 lines, the dual of `Hubs.reset_core`), and `box_filter_finite`.
- Re-deriving those by transport is refactoring, not mathematics; it is not done here.

**S6, persistence dichotomy: vacuous on the conjecture's class.**
- Under ACC and DCC every κ⁺-orbit reaches ⊤ (`rk_eventually_top`, §34.3z³⁸), so every point is "absorbed". A persistent ascent is an infinite chain, excluded by chain-finiteness (that is Theorem A's direction).
- The dichotomy says nothing about the actual gap, ⋁R(F) ∈ R(F) (`sSup_R_mem_iff`). The proposed GPU sampling runs on finite lattices, where `cl_finite` already gives CL for every F.

**S7, witness-proximity calibration: cannot discriminate.** On the 17 exactly enumerated lattices the hard core is empty (`cl_finite`), so every sampler's estimate is exactly 0.

**S2, S3, S1, S8: consolidations of existing work.**
- S2's depth-2 enumeration is DC2 (§34.3z⁶–⁸, §34.3z⁵³).
- S3's first target, the crossing-meets frontier of FG∞, is the k = 3 max-only CLL. Its smallest instance (the crossing triple) is identified (§34.3z¹⁷), and the obstruction is measured: every escape game fails at non-contracting rows (§34.3z⁵⁰).
- S1's first target, "pin the hubs, then run the two-sided reset", is §34.3w (`cut_two_sided_capture`). Its falsification certificate (`crossClosure_infinite`) is already the standing normal-form constraint. The "split cap" target is not stated precisely enough to test.
- S8's DC3 asks for "finitely many third coordinates relative to a finite layer set". With a finite third factor that is `Layers.cut_prod_finite`; with Ω as third factor the values have infinitely many third coordinates by design. The proposed test bed is settled by height.

**Verdict.**
- No strategy reaches an open case.
- One proposal (S5) is refuted in Lean: its hypothesis holds for every F. Its sound residue, `cut_of_straddle` / `cut_of_retraction`, is a small generalization of finite F-closure.
- S4 gains the lattice-level transport lemma it asked for, plus an audit list of hand-proved duals.

## 34.3z⁵⁷ "Proof Strategies v2" (N1–N8): audit, five Lean results, one control experiment

The brief starts from the corrected frontier of §34.3z⁵⁶ and proposes eight items. Each was checked against the log and the Lean sources. New Lean is in `Transport.lean` (standard axioms, full build). The new script is `experiments/infcll/boundary_table_k.py`.

**N1, the join-witness series at the canonical cut Z = R(F) in normal form.**
- *N1a, N1b: true but immediate.* b(s) ∈ Z would give s ∈ Z by b⁻¹-closure. Every y ∉ Z has b(y) ∉ Z.
- *N1c: vacuous.* The claimed limit pair, v = F(v, s′) with v ≤ s′ and v ∈ U, is realized by s′ = ⊤ and v = b(⊤) on every lattice, with no iteration.
- *N1d, N1f: unproved.* The step "z < F(z, s)" needs z ≤ F(z, s). Witness-freeness gives only F(z, s) ≰ z, so the "escalation chain" inside Z does not exist. Nothing excludes F(z, s) ∈ Z. The "squeezed antichain" rests on the same step.
- *N1e: false as stated.* `cut_fingen` needs D **and** Dᶜ finitely generated (k maximal elements of D and l minimal elements of Dᶜ).
  - A witness-free cut with finitely many maximal elements but infinitely many minimal elements of the complement is exactly FG∞, open for crossing triples.
  - What is proved, under ACC and DCC: max(Z) or min(U) is infinite.
- *N1, vacuity check (decided, Lean):* **`finite_of_chainFinite_finite_width`**. A partial order with ACC, DCC and only finite antichains is finite. The proof applies Ramsey for sequences twice (`exists_increasing_or_nonincreasing_subseq`). So "chain-finite + bounded width ⟹ CL" is `cl_finite`.
- *N1g: not a new resource.* In normal form a ≡ ⊥ on Z. Under DCC the row iteration from ⊤ reaches ⊥. Its last value outside Z, y, has F(z, y) ∈ Z ∖ ↓z, which is just a(z) ∈ Z, i.e. map-form closure.
- *N1h(i), Zorn: exact but vacuous (Lean).*
  - **`exists_maximal_WFCut`:** unions of chains of witness-free cuts are witness-free, so every counterexample extends to a maximal one.
  - **`maximal_WFCut_row`:** at a maximal M, every minimal u ∉ M (with M ∪ {u} ≠ L) has y ∉ M, y ≠ u, with F(u, y) ≤ u or y ≤ F(u, y).
  - Both cells lie in Mᶜ × Mᶜ, where the normal form puts ⊤. There y ≤ F(u, y) holds for every y. So the "classified failure" is always this trivial same-side cell, never an infinite chain or a crossing triple.
  - The minimal side is R(F) itself: every witness-free cut contains it. Its growth obstruction lives in D × D, set to ⊥ by the normal form, as in §34.3z⁵¹ Strategy 2.
- *N1h(ii) and (iii)* inherit the unproved N1d–N1f.

**N2, the paired normal form: the target is impossible (Lean).**
- **`normalizeLow`:** ⊥ on L × D, F on D × Dᶜ, ⊤ on Dᶜ × Dᶜ. It is monotone (`normalizeLow_mono`) and keeps cross values (`normalizeLow_cross`), hence witness-freeness.
- `Stable.normalize` puts ⊤ on all of Dᶜ × L. The two differ exactly on the block Dᶜ × D, which contains (⊤, ⊥): **`normalizeLow_top_bot`** gives F(⊤, ⊥) = ⊥ there, against ⊤ in `normalize`.
- So no constraint on F(⊤, ⊥) can follow from the cut problem. F(⊥, ⊤) is a cross value, already WLOG ∉ D (`cut_of_wlog`). The "paired form" adds nothing beyond the choice of value on the free block.

**N3, merge the bridge with max-only CLL(k = 3): neither direction holds.**
- A bridge violator may have infinitely many maximal elements of Z *and* infinitely many minimal elements of U. FG∞ for every k reduces the open case to exactly those cuts (§34.3z¹¹), so proving k = 3 does not close the bridge.
- `MaxLabelling` is a lossy abstraction of FG∞ (§34.3z⁵⁰: not tight at non-contracting rows). A labelling counterexample at k = 3 need not come from a lattice, so refuting k = 3 does not refute the conjecture.
- The test object `not_cll_dcc` is a counterexample to *full* CLL, the reason max-only CLL was introduced (§34.3z⁴³); it is not a cut violator.

**N4, decide the descent game exactly: already done, with the outcome the brief calls a non-outcome.**
- §34.3z⁴⁵ solved the arena exactly. The prover loses 105 of 360 initial states at k = 3. No ranking function of any shape (scalar, lexicographic, multiset) exists, because a ranking function would be a winning strategy for the prover.
- The recipe branch was run in §34.3z⁴⁶–⁵⁰: every adversary strategy uses values of non-contracting rows that the abstraction leaves free. That is the measured residual, "still lost, abstraction not tight".

**N5, amalgamation of open boundary tables: no discriminating power (control experiment).**
- `boundary_table_k.py` generalizes the §34.3z⁵¹ table to k generators. **Open tables at k = 2 are SAT** (m = 1, 2, 4), exactly as at k = 3, although `cll_max_two` is a theorem. Closed k = 2 tables are UNSAT (m = 0–3), as `cll_max` requires.
- So open fragments see neither DCC nor closure under values. Any limit of them at k = 2 is not a DCC max-labelling, and the method cannot tell the proved case from the open one. This is the brief's own design rule 2.
- A Fraïssé argument would need closed fragments, and every finite closed fragment is refuted by `cll_max`.

**N6, normal-form ground encoding at H = 6: every proposed addition is already present or vacuous.**
- The ground theory has only cross values (`cut_cross`: F need only be defined on D × Dᶜ), so the normal form is built in. WLOG F(⊥, ⊤) ∉ D is already a unit clause.
- F(⊤, ⊥) lies in the free block (N2), so cubing on it is vacuous.
- The straddle clauses cannot fire: a partial term model is never F-closed, and frontier selection already exploits `cut_finite` (§34.3z⁴⁰).
- Of N1b–N1f: one is implied, one is vacuous, two are unproved and one is false as stated.
- The proposed first target M_ω² has edge-height **4**, not 6, and is settled by `cl_height4`. Edge-height 6 is M_ω² × C₃ (§34.3z⁵³).

**N7, PDR over a truncation ladder: not well-posed.**
- DC2 is indexed by pool sizes (|L₁|, |L₂|, n) on M_ω² × C_n, not by truncations of Ω.
- There is no N → N + 1 transition relation. A depth-2 model at size k + 1 does not restrict to one at size k, since values may use the dropped atom. So PDR has nothing to run on.
- The inductive invariant it asks for is exactly the uniformity in pool sizes recorded as missing in §34.3z⁵³.

**N8, witness-location mining of the census: all three hypotheses are already theorems.**
- (a) x ∈ R(F): R(F) = L on every finite lattice (`cut_finite` with `cutMaps_iff_R`).
- (b) Both coordinates in the F-closure of {⊥, ⊤}: `cut_cross` labels only cells of S ∩ D × S ∖ D, so the witness lies in any finite F-closed S ∋ ⊥, ⊤.
- (c) A witness on the skeleton grid: **`grid_witness`** (new, Lean). For every finitely generated cut, some cell (⋀p_A, ⋁u_B) with A, B nonempty has F ≤ ⋀p_A or ⋁u_B ≤ F. This is what `FinGen.captured` proves; `cut_fingen` forgot the location.
- So mining the census would rediscover theorems; there is no signal to mine.

**Verdict.**
- No item reaches an open case. Of the four promised merges or reductions (N3, N6, N7, and N1 to the bridge), none holds as stated.
- New and checked:
  - `finite_of_chainFinite_finite_width` (the requested vacuity decision);
  - `exists_maximal_WFCut` and `maximal_WFCut_row` (the Zorn route made exact and shown to be same-side only);
  - `normalizeLow` (the free block, which kills N2);
  - `grid_witness` (N8(c) as a theorem);
  - the k = 2 control for N5.
- The gap is unchanged: information about cross values on cuts whose two sides are both infinitely generated, or, for FG∞, about non-contracting rows.

## 34.3z⁵⁸ Stage-2 brief (ChatGPT): the trace argument does not extend to infinite cones as it stands; the grid dichotomy is a restatement; a DC2 pool bound

**Audit.** HEAD 8968179, branch `main`, clean.
- The README status paragraph still listed "finite posets" as open, though the same paragraph reports `cut_finite`. That is now corrected.
- The README had no entry after §34.3z⁴³. A summary of §34.3z⁴³–⁵⁸ is added.
- The brief's frontier (its §1) matches the log.

**Strategy A: an infinite relative index.**
- *Target.* Countable chain-finite Cut for posets: D a proper down-set of a countable bounded poset with ACC and DCC, and F monotone on D × Dᶜ.
- *Reduction facts.* By `crossClosure_infinite`, this target implies the conjecture. It is *at least as hard as* the crossing-triple front: D = ↓{p₁, p₂, p₃} with its meets is an instance. (Poset FG∞ follows from max-only CLL, not conversely, §34.3z⁵⁷ N3.)
- *Both chain conditions are needed.* The ladder ℕ ∪ {ω}, with D = ℕ and F(n, ω) = n + 1, is countable, bounded, DCC and witness-free. Its order dual is the ACC case (Sharpness, Theorem A).
- **The decisive test** (`experiments/infindex/ladder_trace.py`).
  - The ladder's labelling z(ρ, σ) = F(min ρ, max σ) = min ρ + 1 satisfies every axiom of `RelativeCut.Labelling` except finiteness of D. So the double-cone matrix G of `RelativeCut.G` is defined.
  - **It is exactly the shift τ ↦ τ + 1.** After ⊤, the only word over τ ∪ {⊤} with distinct labels lists τ in increasing order. There is no word over τ ∪ {⊥}, since no C-letter is available.
  - So G is a locally finite (row- and column-finite), degree-preserving chain map: G∂ = ∂G, checked on all faces of size ≤ 3 in {0..8}. On the contractible cone it induces the identity on homology, so the homological Lefschetz number is 1.
  - Yet every diagonal entry vanishes, so the chain-level trace, a finite sum, is 0. The column of {⊥} still sums to 1 (the word ⊤⊥, label 1), which is the quantity `ConeSperner.trace_chain` equates with the trace on a finite cone.
- **Consequence.** The brief's "infinite relative index lemma", in the form "the index is 1 by the cone structure and 0 by witness-freeness", is *false* on countable DCC posets. The two indices are exactly the homological and the chain-level trace, and the ladder separates them by a fixed-point-free shift of an infinite simplex.
- None of the brief's candidate replacements for the trace closes this gap:
  - *Locally finite chain maps:* the ladder's G is one.
  - *Direct-limit indices over an exhaustion:* every finite stage S has an escaping cross value (`cut_cross`), on the ladder as in any counterexample, so stage-wise parities carry no contradiction.
  - *Finite-rank perturbations:* tr(G + K) = tr K for finite-rank K, so this gives no invariant of G.
- The missing step is precisely a **Hopf trace formula for the double-cone complexes of chain-finite posets**, valid for the chain maps that labellings induce. ACC must enter there, because the ladder satisfies everything else.
- Such a formula would imply the target: a diagonal entry 1 is a word labelled by its own face, which violates W1 or W2, as in `dcl`. Infinite antichains admit simplicial "rays" through fences, so topology alone does not exclude a shift; the label structure would have to.

**Strategy B: minimal bad join pair and the grid dichotomy.**
- *The bad pair exists only under ACC.* On the ladder, D = ℕ is join-closed, witness-free and not principal. Under ACC a join-closed D is principal, and principal cuts have witnesses under DCC (`cut_principal_down`), so chain-finite lattice counterexamples have bad pairs.
- *The extraction lemma with ACC and DCC* concludes the negation of its own hypotheses. It is the statement that no such configuration exists, i.e. Cut. Without chain hypotheses, "T(p, q) contains an infinite chain" applied to the cross-closure is equivalent to Strategy A's target.
- *The grid dichotomy is the conjecture, not an intermediate.*
  - The grid exists only when max(D) and min(Dᶜ) generate, i.e. under ACC and DCC; on the ladder D has no maximal elements and the grid is empty.
  - There, alternative 2 (an infinite strictly monotone sequence) is excluded by chain-finiteness.
  - Alternative 1 (a finite captured subgrid) is excluded in any counterexample by `FinGen.captured`.
  - So proving the dichotomy means proving that no counterexample exists, with no intermediate statement in between.
- The "skeleton-grid monotonicity lemma" the brief asks for is in `FinGenAll.lean` (cells grow along inf/sup monotonicity, used in `captured`). Its finite consequence is `grid_witness` (§34.3z⁵⁷).
- *Counterexample search* ("closure infinite, no infinite chain") is a search for a counterexample to the conjecture in poset form. At bounded height it is the ground search, refuted to H = 5 and inconclusive at H = 6 (§34.3z⁵⁴).

**Strategy C: a pool-size bound for DC2** (`experiments/dc2/level_width.py`).
- *Mechanism (rigorous).* In a real witness-free (F, D), F is monotone on the cross cells. Two cross cells whose values carry different atoms in the same M_ω-coordinate are incomparable, because an atom is comparable only to ⊥, ⊤ and itself.
- Hence |L₁| ≤ dim · w₀, where w₀ is the maximum over cuts of the level-0 points of the width of the cross-cell poset (computed by Dilworth via bipartite matching). The same argument bounds |L₂| by the width of the cross cells over level-≤1 points.
- *Values.* w₀ = 2, 10, 15 for n = 1, 2, 3, giving **|L₁| ≤ 4, 20, 30**. The previous bound for n = 3 was 72.
- So DC2 on M_ω² × C₃ is **equivalent to finitely many instances**: k ≤ 30, and m ≤ M(k), where M(k) is that second width bound.
- This is the "finite N" the brief asks for, but the instances it needs are far beyond the solved range (k ≤ 3). The obstacle is not uniformity but size.
- A further reduction would need the witness conditions, not just monotonicity, to bound how many atoms can occur.
- **The (3, 3) control, completed:** pools (3, 3), three layers: 192 points, 708,708 variables, **UNSAT** in 1,308 s with CaDiCaL 1.5.3 (the default of `depth_support_gen.py`). A parallel CaDiCaL 1.9.5 run had not finished by then and was stopped. This extends the §34.3z⁵³ table; it is still a finite instance and not evidence of uniformity.

**Verdict.**
- Strategy A has a precise obstruction: the finite trace formula has no locally finite infinite counterpart, and ACC must supply one.
- Strategy B's dichotomy is the conjecture itself.
- Strategy C gets a rigorous finite reduction with bound 30 instead of 72, together with an honest size estimate.
- No main-theorem progress.

## 34.3z⁵⁹ ChatGPT brief "critical pairs and branch-WSTS": the critical rectangle (Lean), its truth table, and where the regress lives

**Audit.**
- HEAD 1091091 on `main`, clean. It is one commit past the 8968179 the brief cites, namely the §34.3z⁵⁸ commit.
- *Versions.* The README's "v3.8" is the foundations version (`docs/00-foundations.md` §10–§11). The log file is still named `26-v3.7.md` because round 26 began at v3.7, as §34.3z⁵² already noted. The two agree in content.

**Strategy A: boundary-matrix critical pairs** (`Rectangle.lean`, full build).
- *Setup.* Let m ∈ D (e.g. ⋀A for A ⊆ Max D), v ∉ D (e.g. ⋁B for B ⊆ Min Dᶜ), and boundary elements p, u. The four cells are z₀₀ = F(m, v), z₁₀ = F(m ⊓ p, v), z₀₁ = F(m, v ⊔ u), z₁₁ = F(m ⊓ p, v ⊔ u).
- **`rect_mono`:** z₁₀ ≤ z₀₀ ≤ z₀₁ and z₁₀ ≤ z₁₁ ≤ z₀₁. The cells z₀₀ and z₁₁ are not compared.
- **`rect_cross`, `rect_nowit`:** all four are cross cells. No-witness gives **both** z ≰ row and column ≰ z at each cell, whatever the side of z. The brief's side-conditional form is weaker than what holds.
- **`rect_side`:** z₀₁ ∈ D puts every cell in D; z₁₀ ∉ D puts every cell outside.
- **Capturers:**
  - `capturer_exists` (ACC) and `capturer_exists_dual` (DCC) give maximal and minimal capturers.
  - `fresh_capturer`: a capturer of an uncaptured value is new.
  - `max_incomparable`, `min_incomparable`: distinct boundary elements are incomparable.
  - These are elementary and stated for reuse.
- **Truth table** (`experiments/rectangle/rect.py`, z3, |A| = |B| = 2, values free in a preorder with glb/lub and boundary maximality imposed).
  - Of the 16 side patterns (z₁₀, z₀₀, z₁₁, z₀₁), exactly the antitone ones are consistent: **DDDD, DDDU, DDUU, DUDU, DUUU, UUUU**. That is, `rect_side` is the only constraint.
  - z₀₀ and z₁₁ can lie on opposite sides in either order.
- **Exchange candidates.**
  - *Valid, both trivial:*
    - X0: a capturer p* of z₀₀ ∈ D also captures z₁₀.
    - X1: a capturer u* of z₀₁ ∉ D also captures z₀₁, and z₀₀ when z₀₀ ∉ D.
  - *Fails, with countermodels:* once p* captures z₀₀ and u* captures z₀₁, **nothing forces anything about z₁₁ = F(m ⊓ p*, v ⊔ u*)**. Each of the following has a countermodel:
    - X2: z₁₁ is captured at all;
    - X3: z₁₁ is captured by p* or u*;
    - X4, X5: z₁₁ lies on a fixed side;
    - X6: z₁₁ is comparable to z₀₀;
    - X7: z₁₁ is captured by an old generator.
  - The printed countermodels are small: z₁₀ < z₁₁ < z₀₁, z₁₁ ∉ D, and us ≰ z₁₁.
- **The one surviving exchange property** (Lean):
  - **`capturer_meet_escapes`:** if p captures z₀₀ and q captures z₁₁, then **p ⊓ q ≰ m**. Both lie above z₁₀, which is not below its row m ⊓ p. In particular p ⊓ q ≠ ⊥.
  - Dually **`capturer_join_escapes`:** if u captures z₀₁ from below and w ≤ z₁₁, then v ≰ u ⊔ w.
  - So successive capturers are not independent: their meets stay above values that escape the base row. Capture on an M_ω-like boundary, where pairwise meets are ⊥, can never take two consecutive steps.
- **Where the regress lives.**
  - The *nested* extension terminates under chain-finiteness: alternately capture z = F(mₖ, vₖ), set mₖ₊₁ = mₖ ⊓ p, vₖ₊₁ = vₖ ⊔ u. DCC stops mₖ and ACC stops vₖ, and a stabilized cell is captured by the next step.
  - Each extension creates a new corner z₁₁ with both indices enlarged, and the table shows it is unconstrained by everything local.
  - So nontermination must come from the *non-nested* subfamilies, the other cells of the subset grid. That is exactly where `FinGen.captured` uses its global parity argument (Sperner) rather than any local rule.
  - A proof must turn `capturer_meet_escapes` into a global bound on the subset grid. No local completion lemma can do it, because every local pattern is realizable.

**Strategy B: branch-WSTS and pumping.**
- *The order tested.* An embedding of finite cross-closure diagrams that respects order, sides, the recorded F-dependencies and the active pair, and replays the next transition.
- *On the ladder* (§34.3z⁵⁸) branch comparability holds trivially. Stage Sₙ = {0..n, ω} with active pair (n, ω) embeds into Sₙ₊₁ by x ↦ x + 1, which replays F(i, ω) = i + 1. The pumped orbit is the ascending chain, i.e. conclusion 1. So branch-WQO is not the obstruction.
- **The obstruction is the pumping step.** A replayable embedding e whose orbits x, ex, e²x, … form an *antichain* yields none of the three conclusions:
  - no chain (conclusion 1);
  - no finite closure, since the orbit is infinite (conclusion 2);
  - no automorphism without surjectivity and back-and-forth (conclusion 3).
- Chain-finite runs can only pump through antichains.
- *Every* such pattern in a structure of edge-height ≤ 5 is refuted by the ground certificates (`cut_height5` is a statement about all bounded posets). For example, the antichain replay F(dᵢ, uⱼ) = d_{i+j+1} on {⊥, ⊤} ∪ {dᵢ} ∪ {uⱼ} forces F(⊥, uⱼ) = ⊥, a witness, as soon as the bounds are included.
- So a falsifying model must have edge-height ≥ 6 or unbounded height. That is the open ground search (§34.3z⁵⁴), and an infinite fair run with no witness *is* a counterexample.
- Branch-WQO is plausible on every infinite run that exists. The missing step is the semantic one the brief names: an antichain orbit has no order-theoretic consequence.

**Strategy C: DC2 bounds.**
- *Size of the second-pool bound.* Take D = {layer ≤ 1} in Q₁ = ({⊥, ⊤} ∪ L₁)² × C₃. The cross cells ((aᵢ, aⱼ, 1), (a_k, a_l, 2)) with atoms in both coordinates are pairwise incomparable, so the width bound is ≥ k⁴.
- At k = 30 this gives **1.62·10⁶ ≤ M(30) ≤ 2·|D||U| ≤ 4.7·10⁶** for the bounding method of §34.3z⁵⁸.
- The finite reduction is therefore real but astronomically large. Permutation symmetry is already factored out (instances are indexed by sizes only).
- Canonical boundary profiles were not attempted: they need a rule for atoms that occur as values, which is the same restriction problem as before.
- Formalizing the width bound needs Dilworth's theorem for finite posets plus the incomparability lemma. The latter is one line from monotonicity; the former is not in Mathlib.

**Recommended next experiment.** Make the subset grid explicit: the closure process on (Max D)-meets × (Min Dᶜ)-joins, with `capturer_meet_escapes` as a constraint. Run it SAT-side on the ground theory at H = 6, adding the meet and join lattice operations that the poset ground theory lacks. Those operations are where the new dependency lives, and the H = 6 poset search cannot see it.

## 34.3z⁶⁰ Heavy runs, packaged (`experiments/heavy/`)

The two experiments whose outcome would change the frontier but exceed a session's budget are packaged for an
external machine. See `experiments/heavy/README.md` for commands, measured resources and what to send back.
- **Edge-height 6** (`h6_portfolio.sh`):
  - Independent frontier-selection trajectories, with crash-resume from the term checkpoints.
  - On UNSAT, `run_height.py` now also writes the variable maps (`cut_H<H>_<tag>.maps.pkl`).
  - `certify_ground.sh` runs an external DRAT solver or Glucose, then drat-trim, `cut_lean.py` and `lake build`.
  - The whole path was tested on H = 5 outputs (`s VERIFIED`, 935- and 1,040-clause cores).
- **DC2 on M_ω² × C₃** (`run_dc2.sh`, `dc2_batch.py`):
  - The unchanged encoder of `depth_support_gen.py`, dumped as DIMACS: about 1 minute and 0.2 GB per instance up to
    (4, 4), 2.1 M variables and 24 M clauses.
  - Instances are solved by kissat or cadical, and the proofs are checked by drat-trim. (2, 2): 0.56 GB proof,
    `s VERIFIED` in 166 s.
  - `pysat_drat.py` is a slow stand-in solver with the same interface.
- **GPU:** not applicable. The open fronts are SAT refutations and infinite statements, and the GPU census only sees
  finite lattices, where the conjectures are theorems.

## 34.3z⁶¹ A double-categorical framework (`docs/00-foundations-double.md`), and what it says about CL

The user asked to raise the axioms to double categories: horizontal monads, vertical comonads, axioms as squares,
dialgebras as cells between double functors with left and right universal squares, tabulator bounds, a
representability condition, and interchange, mates and companions, without collapsing to a quintet. An outside draft
(ChatGPT) was corrected and written up as `docs/00-foundations-double.md`, with status tags and a mechanization table.
New Lean is in `Axioms/Double.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**Corrections to the draft** (doc §0):
- *"No quintet" cannot be an axiom.* The thin model of a lattice is the quintet Q(L), and every research result
  lives there.
- *Direction mix-up.* Algebra morphisms of horizontal monads are vertical: the draft declared fold horizontal but drew
  it vertically, and dually for ana.
- *Tabulators belong to arrows, not cells.*
- *The foundations' order-theoretic bounds must stay*, or I1 is lost.
- *The proposed "double-categorical commutation theorem" assumes the Cut condition.* In the thin model it is
  `Source.guard` (`double_commutation_thin`).
- *The cited Fiore–Gambino–Kock representability theorem is kept, but not checked.*

**The model that carries the problem** is not a quintet: it is the equipment E(L). Horizontal arrows are monotone
maps, vertical arrows are down-set relations, and squares are inclusions.
- **`companion_conjoint`:** companion ⊣ conjoint on down-sets.
- **`step_eq`:** the Cut closure step is ↓⊥ ∪ a_* ∪ b^*, so R(F) = μΘ is the initial algebra of an endo-proarrow.
- **`yoneda_adj`:** sup ⊣ ↓.
- **`mate_companion`, `mate_conjoint`:** the mates of Θ under sup ⊣ ↓ are lax; their sum is `Orbit.step_lax`.
- **`cut_iff_representable` (DCC):** Cut(F) ⟺ μΘ is representable, i.e. R(F) = ↓r.

So the framework's own non-degeneracy demand, a representable presheaf, is *exactly* the bridge, applied to μΘ.

**Leverage on CL: none found.**
- The mate condition is weaker. Its invertibility at R(F) gives only ⋁R(F) = ⊤, which always holds along the orbit.
- Representability of μΘ is the open statement itself.
- Every criterion for representability of an initial algebra in an equipment needs what is to be proved (ACC plus
  join-closure).
- A gain would need a property of the tabulator of Θ that forces representability and is visible only in the
  double category. None is known.
- Any candidate must exclude some F: on every lattice with an infinite chain some F has non-representable μΘ
  (Theorem A).

## 34.3z⁶² ChatGPT brief "stable zero-fibres and representability": in normal form the derived maps are indicators

**Audit.** HEAD 9dcded7, `main`, clean. Checks of the brief's frontier list:
- `R_eq_iUnion_Rk`, `stable_support`, `sSup_finite_support` and `rk_eventually_top` exist as cited.
- `crossClosure_infinite` is misdescribed. It does not say the cross-closure "can be infinite even in a countable
  chain-finite poset". It says that *in a counterexample* the cross-closure is infinite. No example exists.
- **"⋁Z = ⊤" is not established.**
  - No theorem gives ⋁R(F) = ⊤ for a counterexample. `stable_support` gives finite support of ⋁R(F); `rk_eventually_top`
    concerns the join orbit rₖ, and `orbit_lax` only bounds ⋁Rₖ ≤ rₖ.
  - The obvious derivation fails. Applying `cut_principal_down` to ↓⋁Z in normal form yields a witness (x, y) with
    x ∉ Z, and G(x, y) = ⊤ there makes y ≤ G(x, y) automatic.
  - So the brief's core lemma is the conjecture *restricted to* counterexamples with ⋁Z = ⊤, not a reformulation of
    it.

**The brief's preliminary lemma is correct, in general** (`ZeroFibre.exists_min_b_closed`, DCC): if Z is
b⁻¹-closed and ⊤ ∉ Z, a minimal c ∉ Z with b c ≤ c is a fixed point of b.

**In normal form it is empty** (`ZeroFibre.lean`, standard axioms).
- **`normalize_b_out`, `normalize_a_out`, `normalize_b_in`, `a_normalize`:** for G = `normalize F (R F)`, both
