  derived maps are the **indicator** of the complement: a_G = b_G = ⊥ on R(F) and ⊤ off it. Proof: b_G(y) ∉ R(F) for
  y ∉ R(F) (closure), and G is ⊤ on Rᶜ × L, so the column equation b_G(y) = G(b_G y, y) gives ⊤. The same argument
  with the row equation gives a_G.
- **`normal_form_S`:** S = {x ∉ Z ∣ b x ≤ x} = {⊤}. The minimal point is c = ⊤.
- The first-exit configuration (x < c, F(x, c) = c, b x ≰ x, b x ≤ c, b x ∉ Z) holds for *every* x ∉ Z: F(x, ⊤) = ⊤
  and b x = ⊤. So it carries no information.
- **`normal_conj_iff`:** from p ∈ R(F) the conjoint step b q ≤ p reaches exactly R(F). The "conjoint fork" is
  join-closure of R(F) verbatim, and the brief's question whether the stable zero-fibre lemma *is* the conjoint fork
  has answer: yes, tautologically.

**Answer to the brief's key question.** "Can a proper, join-dense zero-fibre remain invariant under the conjoint
when every element has trivial companion value?"
- As a statement about (a, b, Z): yes, for *every* witness-free cut, since in normal form a = b = 1_{Zᶜ}.
- Whether a witness-free cut exists is the conjecture. The derived maps of the normal form forget everything except
  Z.
- The information is in the cross values F on Z × Zᶜ, which a and b no longer see. This is the lesson of §34.3z⁵¹
  Strategy 2 ("a useful handoff must speak about F on cross pairs"), now in derived-map form: any argument using only
  a, b and Z after normalization proves nothing.

**Filteredness** (`joinClosed_iff_directed`, `principal_of_directed`): for down-sets, join-closed iff directed, and
under ACC a nonempty directed down-set is principal (its category of elements has a terminal object). This is the
brief's §2, mechanized. With `cut_iff_representable` it is the exact chain: Cut ⟺ R(F) representable ⟺ R(F) filtered.

**Regression** (`experiments/zerofibre/m3_check.py`): the brief's M₃ example is confirmed. It is monotone, a ≡ 0,
b = 0 except b(1) = c, and Z = {0, p, q, c} with p ∨ q = 1 ∉ Z and b⁻¹(Z) ≠ Z. Its cut Z has the witness (c, 1).
Backward stability is indeed not decorative, but it is not a source of information either: in normal form it holds
by construction.

## 34.3z⁶³ ChatGPT brief "provenance and the non-nested obstruction": capture processes, support patterns

**Audit.** HEAD a8ce377 on `main`, clean. The brief cites 9dcded7, from the §34.3z⁶² text; the commit of that section
is a8ce377. Its summary of §34.3z⁵¹–⁶² is accurate.

**The boundary transition** (`Provenance.boundary_step`, `boundary_step_dual`, standard axioms).
- A boundary cell F(p, u) in D has a maximal capturer p′ with p′ ≰ p, so p′ ≠ p.
- Dually, a cell outside D has a minimal capturer u′ with u ≰ u′.
- Both are immediate from no-witness and `capturer_exists`. This is the brief's map T.
- As the brief says, T alone can wander through an antichain forever.

**The capture process, and the central question.** "Can an infinite family of cross-pair interactions continually
acquire new capturers while its aggregate meet and join have already stabilized?"
- **In every counterexample it must.** The process never stops: by the contrapositive of `FinGen.captured`, some
  grid cell of the current supports is always uncaptured, and its capturer is fresh (`fresh_capturer`).
- The aggregates mₙ = ⋀Pₙ and vₙ = ⋁Uₙ stabilize by DCC and ACC.
- After stabilization at (m*, v*), every fresh row capturer lies in ↑m* and every fresh column capturer in ↓v*.
  The pivots satisfy the brief's sandwich F(m*, v_B) ≤ z_{A,B} ≤ F(m_A, v*).
- So "stabilized aggregates with fresh capturers" is a *property of every counterexample*, not a source of
  contradiction.
- It resembles the stuck-box residual of the localization calculus (§34.3p: a box whose boundary spans it on one
  side, with infinitely many boundary elements). It is not identical, because cell values need not stay in
  [m*, v*] (`Localize.Prob` asks that they do).
- In the nested case, Aₙ = Pₙ and Bₙ = Uₙ, the pivot cell is eventually the fixed cell (m*, v*). That cell is
  captured after one more step, so the process cannot stay nested (§34.3z⁵⁹). From stabilization on, pivots are
  non-nested.

**Support patterns: the proposed dichotomy is false** (`Provenance.support_family`).
- The brief asks whether pivot supports have a nested or a Δ-system subsequence. For finite sets of *bounded* size
  the infinite sunflower lemma gives a Δ-system. Supports here are unbounded.
- The family Aₙ = {0, 2, …, 2n} ∪ {2n + 1} (indices standing for boundary elements) has no nested pair of distinct
  members (`not_nested`: 2n + 1 ∉ Aₘ) and no Δ-subsystem of size three (`not_delta`: 2m ∈ Aₘ ∩ A_k ∖ Aₙ).
- **The correct trichotomy** is the standard one, not mechanized: by sequential compactness of 2^ℕ, a subsequence
  converges pointwise to some K. After thinning:
  - K finite gives a Δ-system with root K;
  - K infinite gives a **growing root**: A_{n_k} = (K ∩ I_k) ⊔ E_k, with I_k increasing initial segments and petals
    E_k pairwise disjoint and disjoint from K. Nesting is the case E_k = ∅.
  - The family above is a growing root with singleton petals.
- So a Δ-system exchange lemma would cover only one of the two infinite cases. Its outcomes 1 and 2 (strictly
  monotone anchor sequences) never occur in a chain-finite lattice, so the lemma says outright that Δ-patterned
  histories are impossible. That is the open content restricted to one pattern, not a reduction.
- It cannot be tested on finite partial tables either. Open partial tables are SAT at the proved case k = 2 too
  (§34.3z⁵⁷ N5), and closed finite ones are refuted by `captured` itself.

**The secondary program is the ground search.** The provenance DAG, with stages S_{n+1} = Sₙ ∪ F(Sₙ ∩ D, Sₙ ∖ D),
generating pairs kept, z ≰ x for D-values and y ≰ z for complement values, is exactly the term structure of
`cut_ground.py`.
- "An infinite provenance DAG contains a forbidden order configuration" is, at bounded height, the ground refutation:
  proved for H ≤ 5, open at H = 6 (§34.3z⁵⁴, `experiments/heavy/`).
- At unbounded height it is the countable poset form of the conjecture (`crossClosure_infinite`).

**Verdict.**
- New and checked: the boundary transition, and the counterexample to the support dichotomy.
- The capture process is a faithful picture of the regress, but its stabilization is forced in every counterexample.
- The non-nested obstruction remains: a growing-root or Δ-patterned family of pivots with fresh capturers, constrained
  locally only by `capturer_meet_escapes` and its dual.

## 34.3z⁶⁴ ChatGPT brief "growing roots, petal grids, hereditary capture": the petal grid survives every local constraint

**Audit.** HEAD 5904572 on `main` (the brief's SHA), clean. Its frontier summary is accurate.

**Lemma 1 already exists.** The finite support of infima under DCC is `Stable.sInf_finite_support`; its ACC dual,
`sSup_finite_support`, was the source of `stable_support`.

**Effective root** (`Provenance.effective_root`, `effective_root_dual`, standard axioms).
- For an increasing family of row supports Kₙ with union K, eventually ⋀(Kₙ ∪ Eₙ) = ⋀K ⊓ ⋀Eₙ.
- Dually, ⋁(Hₙ ∪ Gₙ) = ⋁H ⊔ ⋁Gₙ under ACC.
- This holds as stated. It needs nothing beyond stabilization of the descending sequence ⋀Kₙ; the proof goes
  through the finite basis.
- So in the growing-root branch the pivots are eventually F(r ⊓ pₙ, s ⊔ uₙ) over a fixed base (r, s). The petal grid
  z_{ij} = F(r ⊓ pᵢ, s ⊔ uⱼ) is a subgrid of the boundary subset grid.

**The petal grid as a finite order-constraint model** (`experiments/petal/petal_grid.py`, z3).
- *Elements:* r, s; petal meets pᵢ ∈ D and joins uⱼ ∉ D; rows r ⊓ pᵢ and columns s ⊔ uⱼ as glb/lub; all 16 (n = 3)
  or 25 (n = 4) values F(row, column).
- *Constraints:*
  - monotonicity on every pair of named cells;
  - no-witness at every cell;
  - a fresh maximal capturer cᵢ for each D-valued diagonal pivot, or a fresh minimal one dᵢ outside D;
  - the accumulation process's triangular non-capture: z_jj ≰ cᵢ and dᵢ ≰ z_jj for i < j, with capturers pairwise
    distinct and incomparable.
- `rect_*` and `capturer_*_escapes` are consequences of the encoding; `captured` (parity) is not encoded.
- **Result: of the 32 homogeneous side patterns, exactly 10 survive, at n = 3 and at n = 4.** These are:
  - all cells in D;
  - all cells outside D;
  - **all 8** interior patterns of the crossing case (row corners z(r ⊓ pᵢ, s) in D, column corners z(r, s ⊔ uⱼ) not
    in D), whatever the sides of the diagonal, the upper and the lower off-diagonal cells.
- The 22 excluded patterns are exactly those `rect_side` excludes. Fresh capturers and triangular non-capture rule out
  nothing further.
- **Inherited capturers** (option `inherit`: every earlier capturer lies in every later row support, i.e.
  r ⊓ pⱼ ≤ cᵢ for i < j, with the extra cells F(cᵢ, s ⊔ uⱼ) under monotonicity and no-witness) leave the same 10
  survivors.
  - The brief's dichotomy "inherited or not" is a valid two-colour Ramsey thinning on pairs.
  - Neither colour is excluded locally.

**Answer to the central question.** Yes: locally, the interactions of disjoint row and column petals over a fixed
base can stay compatible while every diagonal pivot takes a fresh capturer.
- Every homogeneous pattern that `rect_side` allows survives, including Pattern C (persistent crossing) with every
  interior mix, and both inheritance colours.
- The brief's homogeneous petal-grid obstruction lemma therefore needs a constraint that is not local to the petal grid.
- The only known candidate is the global one, `FinGen.captured`, which speaks about *all* subsets of the
  generators, i.e. the cells F(⋀_{i∈I}(r ⊓ pᵢ), ⋁_{j∈J}(s ⊔ uⱼ)) for |I|, |J| > 1. Those lie outside the petal grid.
- **The missing ingredient** is a statement that ties the multi-petal cells, the subset grid over the petals, to the
  single-petal grid. That is again the non-nested subset structure of §34.3z⁵⁹, now over a fixed base.
- Not done here: a model with multi-petal cells and capturers drawn only from maximal elements, which is the next
  finite abstraction. By §34.3z⁵⁷ N5 its open versions should be checked against the proved case k = 2 before any
  conclusion is drawn.

## 34.3z⁶⁵ Finite prefixes of the capture process cannot refute even the principal case

This follows up on §34.3z⁶⁴: the capture process on the **full subset grid** over the boundary generators
(`experiments/process/prefix.py`, CaDiCaL).
- *Model:* row generators p₁..p_k (maximal in D, an antichain, and generating D when `gen` is set); column generators
  u₁..u_b introduced one per stage (minimal outside D, an antichain); all meets m_A and joins v_B as glb/lub; all
  values F(m_A, v_B).
- *Constraints:* monotonicity on all named cells and no-witness at each.
- *Process:* stage t picks a cell over u₁..u_t that no current generator captures, and u_{t+1} is a minimal capturer
  of it.
- A real counterexample whose cut has k maximal elements gives a model for every b, by restriction. So UNSAT at some b
  would prove Cut for such cuts.

**Results.**

| test | k = 1 (principal, proved) | k = 2 (`cut_two`, proved) | k = 3 (crossing, open) |
|---|---|---|---|
| prefix of b stages, no height bound | SAT, b ≤ 4 | SAT, b ≤ 4 | SAT, b ≤ 4 |
| … with edge-height ≤ h | UNSAT at b = h for h = 2, 3, 4 (b stages force height ≥ b + 1) | UNSAT: h = 2 at b = 2, h = 3 at b = 4; h = 4 SAT at b = 5 | UNSAT: h = 2 at b = 2, h = 3 at b = 5; h = 4 SAT at b = 5 |
| … with some initial-segment join flat (v₀..ₜ₊₁ ≤ v₀..ₜ, t ≥ 1) | SAT, b = 3–5 | SAT, b = 3–5 | SAT, b = 3–5 |
| … flat and height ≤ b | UNSAT at b = 5 (h = 5); h = 6 SAT | — | — |

**Reading.**
- *No discrimination.* The proved principal case k = 1 is SAT at every prefix length, exactly like the open case.
  - Its proof (`column_descent`, `cut_principal_down`) is the strictly descending orbit ⊤ > g(⊤) > g²(⊤) > … of
    g(y) = F(p, κ(y)): g(y) ∉ D and y ≰ g(y) make every step strict, and DCC stops it.
  - That argument feeds *values back in as columns* and uses DCC on an unbounded orbit. The process model has
    neither, and no finite prefix can show the second.
- *Forced height is not an infinite chain.* Prefixes force growing height (linearly for k = 1). But no particular
  chain is forced to grow: the initial-segment joins can stay flat, and still height ≥ b + 1 is forced at k = 1. In a
  chain-finite lattice of unbounded height (Ω, for example) growing height is no contradiction.
- **Consequence for the provenance / capture-process line (§34.3z⁶³–⁶⁵).**
  - Every finite form of it either has a height bound, and is then the ground search (proved to H = 5, open at H = 6),
    or has none, and then cannot refute even k = 1.
  - At unbounded height a proof must use well-foundedness through an infinite argument, as `column_descent`,
    `cut_principal_down` and the `Ends` induction of `cll_max_two` do.
  - That points back to the descent proofs for max-only CLL at k = 3 (§34.3z⁴⁵–⁵¹), where the missing information is
    the values of non-contracting rows. It does not point to further finite capture models.

## 34.3z⁶⁶ ChatGPT brief "feedback and row-switching": the feedback closure, an infinite feedback ray (Lean)

**Audit.** HEAD d43aef1, `main`, clean. The brief's citations of `HistoryFix`, `Crossing`, `MinSwitch` and `FGTwo`
match the source.

**Deliverables 1–2 already exist.**
- *First-hit label theorem.* The orbit of g_A from any outside column enters D under DCC (`FGTwo.orbit_end`, with
  gⁿ(y) ≤ gⁿ(⊤)). The entry value z = F(m_A, q) satisfies z ≰ m_A, i.e. A ⊄ Λ(z) (`Crossing.endpoint_not_le`).
- *Row-switch theorem.* At an outside column q, `Crossing.no_closed_rows` with S = A gives i ∈ A with
  F(pᵢ, q) ≰ pⱼ for all j ∈ A. If that value is in D, its label is nonempty and disjoint from A: for |A| = 2 in the
  triple, the complementary singleton.
- Both are one-line instances, so no new file is needed for them.

**New: the feedback process as a theorem** (`Feedback.lean`, standard axioms; no chain condition except where
stated).
- **`feedback_finite`:** for D = ⋃ᵢ ↓pᵢ (finitely many generators), suppose the set Q of outside columns reachable
  from ⊤ by feeding back the outside outputs F(⋀{pᵢ ≥ x}, q) of meet rows is finite. Then the cut has a witness.
  - *Proof:* `cut_cross` applied to the row-compressed F(meetRow x, y) (`Crossing.rows_to_meets`), on
    S = {⊥} ∪ Q ∪ {meet rows} ∪ F(meet rows, Q).
  - So in a counterexample the feedback closure is infinite. This is the finitely generated form of
    `crossClosure_infinite`, with the D-side compressed to the at most 2ᵏ − 1 meet rows.
- **`konig`:** for a finitely branching relation, infinitely many points reachable from r give an infinite path
  from r.
- **`feedback_ray`:** without a witness there is an infinite **feedback ray** ⊤ = q₀ → q₁ → …, with each
  q_{n+1} = F(m_n, q_n) an outside output of a meet row m_n.
- **`ray_switches` (DCC):** the ray's row is not eventually constant. A constant row m from stage N gives
  q_{N+k} ≤ (F m)ᵏ ⊤, and that orbit enters D.

**What this gives the brief's central question.**
- The state (A, y) with transitions y ↦ F(m_A, y) is exactly the feedback relation `FStep`.
- A counterexample for a crossing triple carries an infinite ray of actual columns that switches meet rows infinitely
  often. Each switch at a first hit is one of the brief's two cases: a complementary-label value in D, which ends the
  branch, or a new outside column, which continues it.
- So "can the switches cycle forever?" is *equivalent* to the existence of such a ray: a witness-free 3-generated cut
  has one (`feedback_ray`), and no ray means a witness. This is a concrete form of the open case, carrying actual values.

**What it does not give.**
- The ray may revisit columns: König gives a path, not an injective one.
- Nothing yet orders consecutive ray columns, beyond `escape_below` (steps by rows that contract at q stay below q)
  and q_n ≰ q_{n+1} (no-witness).
- For k = 2, `cut_two` shows rays cannot exist under DCC. Its proof (the `sandwich` with the join anchor and the
  `Ends` induction) is therefore the template a k = 3 ray exclusion must generalize.
- **Test C (symbolic cycles)** cannot discriminate unless the cycle is closed under *all* rows. A partial cycle is
  SAT for k = 2 as well. A closed one is finite and refuted by `feedback_finite` itself.

## 34.3z⁶⁷ Injective feedback rays, support windows, three-generator blockers (Lean)

Response to the brief "Injective feedback rays and three-generator switching". **Audit:** HEAD 20e48f1 (the brief's
snapshot), clean. New Lean is in `Feedback.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**Structural strengthenings.**
- **`konig_injective`, `feedback_ray_injective`:** König with an injective path. The proof restricts the relation to
  steps that raise the BFS distance from the root by exactly one (`layer`, `layer_finite`, `reach_iff_layer`), so
  distance equals position along the path. Without a witness there is an injective feedback ray.
- **`antichain_subseq`:** in a chain-finite order an injective sequence has an antichain subsequence (Ramsey twice,
  as in `finite_of_chainFinite_finite_width`).
- **`tail_join_stable`** (DCC): the tail joins ⨆_{k ≥ n} c_k stabilize.
- For the brief's §3: thin the antichain subsequence c_k of the ray to a fixed outgoing row m_A, and let s be the
  stable tail join. Then s ∉ D, F(m_A, s) ∉ D (it lies above the outside successors F(m_A, c_k)), and s ≰ F(m_A, s)
  (no-witness).
- **F(m_A, s) ≤ s stays unresolved**, as the brief says. If it held, `escape_below` would put every later step by
  that row strictly below s.

**The dynamical constraint** (`feedback_window`, DCC).
- Along a ray q(t+1) = F(meetRow p (x t), q t) with supports A_t = {i ∣ x_t ≤ pᵢ}, there is N such that every window
  of N consecutive supports has empty intersection.
- *Proof:* if generator i were in all of them, the ray would sit below the F pᵢ-orbit of ⊤, which enters D
  (`FGTwo.orbit_end`). Take the maximum of the finitely many Nᵢ.
- This strictly generalizes `ray_switches`.
- *What it excludes:* "star" switching, where one generator is common to long blocks of supports, e.g.
  {1,2}, {1,3}, {1,2}, … (the bouquet shape).

**Blockers** (`blockers_core` by `decide` over 512 cases, `blockers_three`). A family of nonempty subsets of {1, 2, 3}
with empty intersection contains a disjoint pair, or the triangle {1,2}, {2,3}, {1,3}.
- So every N-window of the ray's supports contains one of the two blocker types. By pigeonhole, infinitely many
  disjoint windows carry the same type.

**Program A, disjoint pair.** For a strictly crossing triple the join c = m_A ∨ m_B of a disjoint pair is **outside D**
(proved by hand, not mechanized).
- Disjoint pairs on three points are two singletons, or a singleton and the complementary pair.
- c = pᵢ ∨ pⱼ ∉ D because the generators form an antichain.
- c = pᵢ ∨ m_{jk} ≤ p_l forces l = i and m_{jk} ≤ pᵢ, i.e. p_j ⊓ p_k = p₁ ⊓ p₂ ⊓ p₃, which strict crossing excludes
  (§34.3z¹⁷). In the weak-crossing case with that equality, c = pᵢ ∈ D.
- So c is a legitimate outside column with m_A, m_B ≤ c. It is bounded by F(m_A, c) ≥ F(m_A, y) for y ≤ c, but no
  comparison with the ray columns is forced. `FGTwo.sandwich` needs a split D = ↓a ∪ InB saturated over a meet anchor,
  which does not exist for a strictly crossing triple (§34.3z²⁰).

**Program B, triangle.** The pairwise joins of the pair rows lie below single generators (m₁₂ ∨ m₁₃ ≤ p₁ and so on).
- `feedback_window` is satisfied by the periodic triangle word {1,2}, {2,3}, {1,3}, … (each generator is missing
  every third step), and likewise by periodic disjoint-pair words.
- So the window lemma excludes stars but **no periodic switching through a blocker**.
- The brief's encoders A and B are not run. A support word satisfying the window condition is just a word with
  each generator missing at least once per window. The endpoint encoder, without a height bound, is a finite partial
  model, and §34.3z⁶⁵ shows those are SAT even for proved cases.

**Surviving blocker patterns.**
- *Triangle cycles:* the ray rotates through the three pair rows.
- *Disjoint-pair alternations:* singleton/singleton, or singleton/complementary pair.
- In both, each generator drops out within every window.

**The precise unresolved comparison.** Between ray columns at consecutive blocker steps. For a triangle step
q′ = F(m_{ij}, q), q″ = F(m_{jk}, q′), the two rows share only p_j. Monotonicity gives q″ ≤ F(p_j, q′) and
q′ ≤ F(p_j, q); since q′ ≰ q, nothing composes these into a comparison with the F pⱼ-orbit. The window lemma uses
exactly such a composition, and it breaks once the shared generator changes.

**Next smallest Lean target.** A two-step window: if generator j is common to two consecutive supports and the
earlier column is ≤ (F pⱼ)ᵏ ⊤, then so is the next one, (F pⱼ)ᵏ⁺¹ ⊤ (the induction step of `feedback_window`). Then
find what extra hypothesis lets the bound pass through a step whose support omits j.
- *Candidate:* the step's value lies below the column, i.e. the step contracts (`escape_below`).
- This must exclude some F, so test it first against the triangle word.

## 34.3z⁶⁸ The contraction-tolerant window: every generator is dropped by infinitely many sideways steps (Lean)

This continues §34.3z⁶⁷'s "next smallest target". **`Feedback.feedback_window_contract`** (DCC, standard axioms):
- *Setting:* a ray q(t+1) = F(meetRow p (x t), q t) of outside columns with x t ∈ D.
- *Claim:* for every generator i and every n there is a step t ≥ n that **omits i and does not contract**:
  ¬ x t ≤ pᵢ and ¬ q(t+1) ≤ q t.
- *Proof:*
  - Steps containing i advance the bound q ≤ (F pᵢ)ᵏ ⊤, and contracting steps keep it.
  - The count K of i-steps can never reach the orbit's entry time, or the ray would enter D. So K is eventually
    constant.
  - From then on every step contracts. Contractions are strict (q t ≰ q(t+1) by no-witness), so DCC is violated.
- This strengthens `feedback_window` (contracting steps no longer reset the window) and `ray_switches`.

**Non-contracting means sideways.** Since q t ≰ q(t+1) always, a non-contracting step has q(t+1) incomparable to
q t. So:

> In a counterexample for a finitely generated cut, for each generator i the feedback ray takes infinitely many
> sideways steps whose support omits i.

**Consequences for the blocker patterns of §34.3z⁶⁷.**
- In a triangle cycle (m₁₂, m₂₃, m₁₃, …), the steps omitting i are the m_{jk}-steps, and infinitely many of each must
  be sideways.
- In a disjoint-pair alternation, each of the two rows must go sideways infinitely often, as must every row whose
  support omits a given generator.
- Patterns in which, for some i, all i-omitting steps eventually contract are excluded.

**Where this meets the existing machinery.**
- A sideways step from q by the row m gives the meet q ⊓ F(m, q). That is exactly `HistoryFix.zK` (y ⊓ g_K(y)), whose
  label is fixed at a terminal configuration (`label_fixed`, `fixed_sideways`).
- **Next target:** along the infinitely many i-omitting sideways steps, track the labels Λ(q_t ⊓ q_{t+1}). These are
  the actual values that the terminal analysis of §34.3z²⁴–²⁶ controls. Then determine whether the label-fixed-point
  property of `HistoryFix` holds at those steps or can be forced there.

## 34.3z⁶⁹ Terminal columns feed the ray: the terminal step (Lean)

This continues §34.3z⁶⁸'s target: link the forced sideways ray steps to the label fixed points of `HistoryFix`.

**`HistoryFix.terminal_step`** (no chain condition). At a column y ∉ D with a label fixed point K
(Λ(y ⊓ g_K y) = K, from `label_fixed` at a terminal column), the value v = g_K(y) = F(⋀_K p, y) has labels a
**proper subset** of K.
- The meet y ⊓ v ≤ v gives Λ(v) ⊆ K.
- Equality would put v below ⋀_K p, a witness.
- When v ∉ D the label is empty. Together with `fixed_sideways` (v ≰ y), the K-step from a terminal column is
  sideways, and if it lands in D it does so with a strictly smaller label.

**`HistoryFix.singleton_step`.** If K = {i}, then g_i(y) ∉ D and g_i(y) ≰ y: a terminal column with a singleton fixed
label **continues the feedback ray sideways** by the generator row pᵢ. For |K| = 2 (the case `label_fixed_proper`
leaves open for three generators, |K| ≤ 2), the step either continues outside or enters D with a singleton label
from K. The second is the strict switch of §34.3z¹⁴.

**Where the two pictures meet.**
- From §34.3z⁶⁸: the ray takes infinitely many sideways steps omitting each generator.
- From terminal columns: the K-step is sideways and either continues the ray or switches to a smaller label.
- *The gap:* nothing makes the *next* column terminal again. The meets y′ ⊓ gⱼ(y′) at y′ = g_K(y) are values of F at a
  new column, unrelated by monotonicity to those at y.
- `min_terminal` produces terminal columns only as *minimal* columns contracted by a common row r (⊥ or ⋀p), and a
  ray column is not minimal in that sense.

**The precise next statement:**

> **Terminal recurrence.** A counterexample for a strictly crossing triple has a feedback ray that passes through
> terminal columns infinitely often.

- With it, `singleton_step` and `terminal_step` would turn the ray into a sequence of label fixed points, each
  continuing by its own row. The remaining work would be a finite analysis of the label transitions K → K′ between
  consecutive terminal columns, where actual values of F are carried along.
- Without it, terminality is a property of minimal columns only.
- A first test is whether the minimal contracted column y* of `min_terminal` lies on, or below, some infinite ray.
  That needs the feedback closure from y*, not from ⊤, to be infinite, and `feedback_finite` only speaks about ⊤.

## 34.3z⁷⁰ Terminal descent: weak terminal recurrence is free, the strong form is the gap

This continues §34.3z⁶⁹'s target, terminal recurrence.

**`HistoryFix.terminal_below`, `terminal_below'`** (DCC, standard axioms).
- *Claim:* below every column q ∉ D there is a terminal column y ≤ q, with y ∉ D and y ⊓ gⱼ(y) ∈ D for every j.
- *Proof:* if q is not terminal, some meet q ⊓ gⱼ(q) lies outside D, and it is strictly below q since q ≰ gⱼ(q)
  (no-witness). Descend and use DCC.
- *Carried invariant:* any common row r ≤ ⋀p that contracts at q also contracts at y.
- `min_terminal` is the special case where y is additionally minimal among columns contracted by r. Here no
  minimality is needed.

**Weak terminal recurrence holds.**
- Every column q of a feedback ray dominates a terminal column y. That column has a label fixed point K
  (`label_fixed`), a sideways K-row (`fixed_sideways`), and a K-step value g_K(y) with Λ ⊊ K (`terminal_step`).
- *Shadow comparison:* F(m_K, y) ≤ F(m_K, q).
  - If g_K(y) ∉ D, then the K-row also continues the feedback from q (F(m_K, q) ∉ D).
  - If g_K(y) ∈ D, its label is a proper subset of K.
  - For K = {i} this recovers `Crossing.column_lemma` (some generator row escapes D at q), so nothing new comes from
    the shadow alone.

**The strong form is the gap.** Two things would make the terminal analysis carry the ray, and neither is supplied
by monotonicity:
- *Terminal walk:* y₀ terminal, y_{n+1} terminal below g_{K_n}(y_n) while that value is outside D. The walk is
  well defined (terminal descent), but it can stop: a pair K_n can step into D with a singleton label, the strict
  switch. Nothing forces it to continue.
- *Ray columns terminal:* the meets q ⊓ gⱼ(q) at ray columns can lie outside D. Terminal descent then moves strictly
  below the ray, and successive descents need not be comparable.

**Status of the ray program (§34.3z⁶⁶–⁷⁰).**
- *Proved:* the feedback closure is infinite; there is an injective ray; every window of supports has empty
  intersection; for each generator, infinitely many sideways steps omit it; terminal columns lie below every ray
  column and continue sideways by their fixed-label rows.
- *Open:* a well-founded quantity that moves when the ray switches its shared generator (§34.3z⁶⁷), equivalently a
  reason for terminal columns to recur *on* the ray rather than below it.
- Every local and finite abstraction tried so far admits all surviving patterns. The missing statement is a global
  comparison between ray columns across a change of shared generator. That is the strict switch of §34.3z¹⁴ in its
  feedback form.

## 34.3z⁷¹ ChatGPT brief "terminal-shadow continuation and certificate bridges": the bridge is a label condition

**Audit.** HEAD 4df2b8e (the brief's snapshot), clean. New Lean is in `HistoryFix.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**The brief's §2 is correct, and implicit in existing results** (`shadow_escape`, `terminal_shadow_escape`).
- *General form:* if the meet row ⋀_K p takes at an outside column q a value w with Λ(w) ⊆ K, then w ∉ D, or some
  generator row pᵢ with i ∈ Λ(w) escapes D at q, with w ≤ F(pᵢ, q).
- *Proof:* every capturer of F(pᵢ, q), i ∈ Λ(w), lies in Λ(w), because w ≤ F(pᵢ, q). Otherwise
  `Crossing.no_closed_rows` on Λ(w) is violated.
- *Terminal form:* with y ≤ q terminal and fixed label K, the hypothesis Λ(w) ⊆ K holds because
  Λ(w) ⊆ Λ(F(m_K, y)) ⊊ K (`terminal_step`). The feedback from q continues by m_K, or by a singleton row pᵢ with i in
  the strictly smaller label, and the new column lies above the certificate a = y ⊓ F(m_K, y).

**Certificates** (`certificate_below`, `certificate_join`, `lab_sup`, `mem_D_iff`).
- Along the brief's certified ray, aₙ ≤ qₙ ⊓ q_{n+1}: each certificate lies below **both** consecutive columns.
- With cₙ = aₙ ∨ a_{n+1} ≤ q_{n+1}, we get Λ(cₙ) = Kₙ ∩ K_{n+1}, and cₙ ∈ D iff the labels meet. This is as the brief
  states.
- The certified ray itself is a recursion by dependent choice on (qₙ, yₙ, Kₙ, aₙ), using `terminal_below'`,
  `label_fixed` and `terminal_shadow_escape`. Each step is checked; the recursion is not packaged as a Lean structure.

**The bridge inequality is a label condition** (`le_meet_iff`: c ≤ ⋀_S p ⟺ S ⊆ Λ(c)).
- The sufficient condition cₙ ≤ r_{n+1} holds exactly when:
  - r_{n+1} = m_{K_{n+1}} and K_{n+1} ⊆ Kₙ (since Λ(cₙ) = Kₙ ∩ K_{n+1});
  - or r_{n+1} = pᵢ and i ∈ Kₙ ∩ K_{n+1}.
- So the proposed bridge F(cₙ, q_{n+1}) ≤ q_{n+2}, in its sufficient form, is a statement about label sequences. It
  cannot carry the value-level comparison the brief is after.
- *When the label condition fails:*
  - If cₙ ∈ D (labels meet), then cₙ and r_{n+1} are incomparable rows. F(cₙ, q_{n+1}) and q_{n+2} = F(r_{n+1}, q_{n+1})
    are related only through rows above both, such as cₙ ∨ r_{n+1}, whose value bounds both from above. Nothing
    compares them directly, and no smallest-example search is needed: any F with distinct values at the two
    incomparable rows refutes the stronger claim.
  - If cₙ ∉ D (disjoint labels), cₙ is not a row at all. It is a **new outside column below q_{n+1}**, the join of two
    certificates with disjoint labels: the brief's test 4. Feeding it back is the same analysis one level down
    (terminal descent below cₙ, a new certificate, a new shadow step), and no quantity is seen to decrease.

**Answers to the brief's report items.**
- *New theorems:* `shadow_escape`, `terminal_shadow_escape`, `certificate_below`, `certificate_join`, `lab_sup`,
  `le_meet_iff`, `mem_D_iff`.
- *Implicit?* Yes: shadow escape is `no_closed_rows` plus the terminal label inclusion.
- *Exact bridge conditions:* K_{n+1} ⊆ Kₙ (meet-row step), or i ∈ Kₙ ∩ K_{n+1} (singleton step).
- *Counterexample to the stronger form:* c ≤ r fails whenever the label condition fails, and the two row values at
  incomparable rows are unconstrained.
- *Remaining obstruction:* unchanged. It is a comparison between ray columns that survives a step whose row is not
  above the previous certificate, i.e. a change of shared generator.
- *Next smallest target:* the disjoint-label case. cₙ ∉ D with aₙ, a_{n+1} ≤ cₙ ≤ q_{n+1} gives an outside column
  bounded below by two D-elements with disjoint labels. Check whether no-witness at the cells (aₙ, cₙ) and
  (a_{n+1}, cₙ), together with the window lemma, bounds how often disjoint consecutive labels can occur.

## 34.3z⁷² The disjoint-label case adds nothing; a ground search over lattices

**The disjoint-label case** (§34.3z⁷¹'s target). With Kₙ ∩ K_{n+1} = ∅, cₙ = aₙ ∨ a_{n+1} is an outside column below
q_{n+1}.
- Its no-witness cells (aₙ, cₙ) and (a_{n+1}, cₙ) are dominated by the rows m_{Kₙ} and m_{K_{n+1}} at q_{n+1}
  (aₙ ≤ m_{Kₙ}, since Λ(aₙ) = Kₙ).
- Nothing ties K_{n+1}, a fixed point chosen freely at y_{n+1}, back to Kₙ. The recursion through cₙ repeats the
  certificate analysis one level down, with no decreasing quantity.
- Rounds §34.3z⁶⁶–⁷¹ all end at the same missing comparison. That comparison involves meets and joins of ray columns
  and certificates, which the **poset** ground search cannot express.

**A ground search over lattices** (`experiments/sat/lattice_ground.py`).
- *Method:* `cut_ground.Ground` extended by meet and join terms. z = s ⊓ t carries z ≤ s, z ≤ t and, for every other
  term w, w ≤ s ∧ w ≤ t ⇒ w ≤ z, dually for joins. These are ground instances of the lattice axioms, so UNSAT proves
  the Cut Lemma for every bounded *lattice* of edge-height ≤ H. That is enough for CL.
- *Each round:* frontier F-terms, plus J lattice terms (joins of incomparable D-classes, meets of incomparable outside
  classes of the current model).

| H | J = 0 (poset, frontier) | J = 1 | J = 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 7 terms | 7 | 7 |
| 4 | 17 | 20 (14 F + 4 lattice) | 20 |
| 5 | 122 (120 F), 3.5 s | **147 (85 F + 60 lattice), 5.0 s** | 221 (93 F + 126 lattice), 18.6 s |
| 6 | SAT at 553 terms / 31 min (§34.3z⁴⁰) | SAT at 474 terms (250 F + 220 lattice) / 15 min | SAT at 507 (343 F + 159 lattice) / 16 min (2 F-terms per round) |

**Reading.**
- Lattice terms *substitute* for F-terms: at H = 5 a third fewer F-terms suffice with one lattice term per round. So
  the lattice axioms carry refutation power that the poset theory lacks.
- At H = 6 the session budget shows no closure, and each term costs more than in the poset search.
- The expected obstacle is §34.3z⁴⁰: the partial models collapse onto chains, which are lattices, so lattice terms
  cannot by themselves remove the hole the frontier keeps open.
- The run is added to the heavy kit (`experiments/heavy/README.md` §3). UNSAT would settle edge-height 6 for lattices,
  including M_ω² × C₃. `cut_lean.py` does not yet translate the lattice clauses.

## 34.3z⁷³ ChatGPT brief "tail envelopes and minimal row coalitions": the coalition fixed point (Lean)

**Audit.** HEAD 5f0ebab (§34.3z⁷²), clean. New Lean is in `Feedback.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**Deliverables A–E are correct, and now checked.**
- **A** is `tail_join_stable` (§34.3z⁶⁷): S = ⨆_{n ≥ N} qₙ is eventually constant, and S ∉ D.
- **B, `essential_member`:** a minimal hitting set J of the recurrent support types has, for every i ∈ J, a recurrent
  type A with A ∩ J = {i}.
- **C, `coalition_postfixed`:** if from stage N every support meets J, then S ≤ H_J(S) = ⋁_{i ∈ J} F(pᵢ, S). Each
  successor q_{n+1} = F(r_n, q_n) ≤ F(pᵢ, S) for some i ∈ A_n ∩ J.
- **C′, `coalition_fixedpoint` (ACC):** iterating H_J from S stabilizes at the specific t = H_Jᵏ(S) ≥ S with
  t = H_J(t). It is the least fixed point above S reached by iteration, not an arbitrary Tarski point.
- **D, `coalition_components`:**
  - every component wᵢ = F(pᵢ, t), i ∈ J, satisfies wᵢ < t (no-witness at (pᵢ, t), as t ∉ D);
  - wᵢ ∉ D as soon as some outside column lies below it, which the ray successors at stages with i ∈ A_n provide
    (F(pᵢ, S) ≤ wᵢ).
- **E, also in `coalition_components`:** b = F(⋀p, t) ≤ ⋀_{i ∈ J} wᵢ, b < t, and the full-meet row contracts at b.

**A dividend: `coalition_not_singleton`.** A singleton coalition gives t = F(pᵢ, t), and no-witness forbids t ≤ F(pᵢ, t).
- So the minimal recurrent coalition has two or three members. This reproves the star exclusion of `feedback_window`
  by a different route, as a fixed-point statement.

**Is t new compared with ⊤?** Only in tightness.
- Every generator row already contracts at ⊤. That is all `cut_two` uses: its proof runs the meet-row orbit from ⊤
  and closes with `FGTwo.sandwich`.
- At t, only the rows of J are known to contract, which is weaker. What t adds is *equality*: t = ⋁_{i∈J} F(pᵢ, t),
  with each component an outside strict predecessor bounding infinitely many ray columns.
- The tools that apply at contracting columns (`anchor_sandwich`, `orbit_end`) give the same conclusions at t as at
  ⊤. For each i ∈ J the join-anchor orbit from t enters D at a value ≤ wᵢ whose label avoids i.
- The sandwich step for a three-generator D still needs z ≤ pᵢ ∧ z ∈ ⋃_{j ≠ i} ↓pⱼ ⇒ z ≤ r. For a strictly crossing
  triple that fails for every meet anchor r (§34.3z²⁰). This is the same obstruction, now at t.

**Case B (b ∈ D)** is consistent with everything here.
- Λ(b) = B with ∅ ≠ B ⊊ I (b ≰ ⋀p by no-witness). b is a common D-valued lower bound of the outside components
  wᵢ, i ∈ J, and labels impose nothing on such a bound.
- For |J| = 2, B can be {k} for the excluded k, or meet J. No constraint ties B to J, because b ≤ wᵢ with wᵢ ∉ D says
  nothing about b's generators.

**Coalition defection (F).**
- At wᵢ ∉ D, `no_closed_rows` with S = J gives j ∈ J with F(pⱼ, wᵢ) not captured by J, and F(pⱼ, wᵢ) ≤ wⱼ.
- This is a transition graph on J that carries actual values. Each edge i → j comes with an outside value, or one
  labelled by I ∖ J, below wⱼ.
- With |J| ≤ 3 the graph has a cycle. Nothing orders the values around it: wⱼ and wᵢ are incomparable in general.

**The precise remaining global inequality.** A comparison between two coalition components, wᵢ ≤ wⱼ or a relation
between F(pⱼ, wᵢ) and wᵢ, that survives the switch from row i to row j.
- With it, t = ⋁ wᵢ would collapse to a single component, and `coalition_not_singleton` would close the case.
- Without it, the coalition decomposition is a static restatement of the ray's switching at one fixed point.

## 34.3z⁷⁴ ChatGPT brief "recurrent exchange networks": the vector fixed point, cycle contraction (Lean)

**Audit.** HEAD f29982c (§34.3z⁷³), clean. No heavy runs are active in this container. The kit in
`experiments/heavy/` is for an external machine, and no results have come back to the repository.

**Abstract exchange networks** (`Feedback.lean`, section `Exchange`, standard axioms, full build). Vertices form a
finite type κ, edges a relation E, and gⱼ are monotone maps (in the application gⱼ = F(pⱼ, ·)).
- **`exch`, `exch_mono`:** Φ(x)ⱼ = ⋁_{i → j} gⱼ(xᵢ).
- **`exch_fixedpoint` (ACC):** if s ≤ Φ(s), Φ(w) ≤ w and s ≤ w, iterating Φ from s reaches u with s ≤ u ≤ w and
  Φ(u) = u. Coordinatewise ACC suffices (`asc_stable`), and the stabilization indices are maximized over the finite
  vertex set.
- **`exch_edge_le`:** E i j ⇒ gⱼ(uᵢ) ≤ uⱼ.
- **`walk_contract`:** along a walk i → j₁ → ⋯ → j_r of edges, the composite of the g's maps uᵢ below u_{j_r}. For a
  closed walk this is **G_C(u_{i₀}) ≤ u_{i₀}: every cycle word contracts at the vector fixed point**.
- **`contract_fixed` (DCC):** a monotone G with G(a) ≤ a has a fixed point below a, reached by iterating from a.

**The brief's construction in these terms.**
- *Inputs:* the recurrent transition graph on J, the edge envelopes s_i and s_{ij}, and the coalition components wᵢ.
- *The inequalities s ≤ Φ(s), Φ(w) ≤ w, s ≤ w* hold as the brief says: s_j = ⋁_{i→j} s_{ij}, s_{ij} ≤ F(pⱼ, sᵢ),
  F(pⱼ, wᵢ) ≤ F(pⱼ, t) = wⱼ, and sᵢ ≤ wᵢ.
- *Recurrent exchange values:* F(pⱼ, uᵢ) ≥ s_{ij} lies outside D.
- *Not mechanized:* the recurrence bookkeeping (stabilized edge tails, strong connectivity of the eventual edge set).
  They are standard: a sequence over a finite alphabet eventually uses only recurrent transitions, and an infinite walk
  using only those edges and visiting every recurrent vertex makes them strongly connected.

**The cycle dichotomy.** For a cycle C, the G_C-descent from u_{i₀} stabilizes at x* = G_C(x*) ≤ u_{i₀}
(`walk_contract`, `contract_fixed`). Along the cycle's intermediate values y₀ = x*, y_m = g_{i_m}(y_{m−1}), one of two
things happens:
- *First-D-entry:* some y_m ∈ D with y_{m−1} ∉ D. Its label excludes i_m (no-witness at (p_{i_m}, y_{m−1})).
- *Persistent outside cycle:* all intermediates stay outside D, giving a periodic outside orbit under a fixed cyclic
  word of rows.

**Neither branch is excluded.**
- *Self-loops* (i → i) are the one exception: G = gᵢ descends strictly (no-witness), so the descent enters D. This is
  `orbit_end` again.
- *The smallest surviving pattern* is a 2-cycle i ⇄ j with y₁ = F(pⱼ, y₀) and y₀ = F(pᵢ, y₁), both outside and
  incomparable. Monotonicity and no-witness admit it, and it is not closed under the other rows, so `feedback_finite`
  does not apply.
- *Even for k = 2, where Cut is proved, such a periodic pair is not contradicted by local constraints.* The proof of
  `cut_two` runs the meet-row orbit from ⊤ and closes with `FGTwo.sandwich`, a global argument.
- *The meet row of the cycle's generators.* m_C = ⋀_{k} p_{i_k} satisfies F(m_C, u_{i_k}) ≤ u_{i_{k+1}} along the
  cycle, so the m_C-orbit stays below the cycle's u-values. The sandwich step that would close it needs a meet anchor,
  which strict crossing excludes (§34.3z²⁰).

**Next precise statement.** A persistent outside cycle under a word w in the generator rows, together with the
m_C-orbit from ⊤, must produce a sandwich-type contradiction. Concretely, this generalizes `FGTwo.sandwich` from one
contracting row to a contracting *cycle word*:
- *hypotheses:* G_w(s) ≤ s at an outside s, and a join-anchor split of D adapted to the letters of w;
- *conclusion:* the meet-row orbit from s cannot enter D below the anchor.

For k = 2 this specializes to `FGTwo.sandwich`. For a strictly crossing triple it is open, and is the same
obstruction in cycle form.

## 34.3z⁷⁵ ChatGPT brief "generalized sandwich for a contracting cycle of rows": the precise phase obstruction

**Audit.** HEAD 183f6ba (§34.3z⁷⁴), clean. New Lean is in `Feedback.lean`, section `CycleWord` (standard axioms,
full build).

**Checked results.**
- **`cycle_block_le`:** a row m below every letter of a word w satisfies (F m)^{|w|}(x) ≤ G_w(x). This is the brief's
  h^r ≤ G_w.
- **`cycle_meet_contracts`:** for a periodic cycle x_{k+1} = F(p_{i_k}, x_k), x_r = x₀, and m below every letter, the
  meet z = ⋀_{k<r} x_k satisfies F(m, z) ≤ z. (Each F(m, z) ≤ F(p_{i_k}, x_k) = x_{k+1}, and the successors range over
  all states.)
- **`cycle_common_lower`** (DCC): there is e ∈ D below every phase state. Either z ∈ D, or e is the entry value of the
  descending F(m, ·)-orbit of z (`orbit_end`). In the second case e ≰ m by no-witness.
- **`phase_step`:** the step of `FGTwo.l4` that survives a change of letter. With D = ↓a ∪ B, ↓a ∩ B ⊆ ↓c and
  c ≤ a: if F(c, u) ≤ a at an outside u, then F(a, u) ∉ D.
  - In the strict-crossing instance a = pᵢ, B = ⋃_{j≠i} ↓pⱼ, and c = cᵢ is the join anchor. ↓pᵢ ∩ Bᵢ ⊆ ↓cᵢ holds
    because pᵢ ⊓ pⱼ ≤ cᵢ.

**The precise obstruction to a phase-synchronized sandwich.**
- `FGTwo.l4` iterates `phase_step` with one letter a. The hypothesis F(r, u) ≤ a passes to the next column u′ = F(a, u)
  by monotonicity, since u′ ≤ u. Then the g-descent never enters D, and DCC finishes the proof.
- In a cycle word the next phase needs F(c_{i_{k+1}}, u_{k+1}) ≤ p_{i_{k+1}}. Nothing derives this from phase k:
  - u_{k+1} ≤ u_k is not given; contraction holds only after a full cycle.
  - c_{i_{k+1}} and p_{i_k} are unrelated.
- Asking instead for the full meet row's value to lie below every phase generator at once puts it below m_w, which is
  already a witness.
- So the one-phase step is all that generalizes. The smallest checked generalized sandwich is `phase_step`.
- **Counterexample to the overstrong form** ("phase hypotheses propagate around the word"): the values
  F(c_{i_k}, u_k) and F(c_{i_{k+1}}, u_{k+1}) are values of F at different rows and at incomparable columns
  (u_{k+1} ≰ u_k is allowed). No constraint links them, so already a two-letter word admits F(c₁, u₀) ≤ p₁ with
  F(c₂, u₁) ≰ p₂.

**Label of the common lower bound e.** Let K = Λ(e) ≠ ∅.
- If |K| ≥ 2, then e ≤ pᵢ ⊓ pⱼ ≤ cᵢ for i, j ∈ K, so e lies below a join anchor.
- If K = {i} (e in the exclusive part of petal i), no anchor applies, and `singleton_step` needs a terminal column,
  which e's columns need not be.
- So the singleton petal is the exceptional case. It is not excluded, and with both phase constraints unlinked it
  cannot be reduced to `FGTwo.sandwich` or to `cut_bouquet` (which needs pairwise meets equal to one r).

**Next comparison required.** A reason for u_{k+1} ≤ u_k, or for F(c_{i_{k+1}}, u_{k+1}) ≤ p_{i_{k+1}}, at some phase
of every persistent cycle. That is a comparison between consecutive phase states, the same missing inequality as in
§34.3z⁶⁷ and §34.3z⁷³, now located inside the proof of `l4`.

## 34.3z⁷⁶ ChatGPT brief "anchor-word cycles and the singleton-petal obstruction": the bridge is not local

**Audit.** HEAD 120a354 (§34.3z⁷⁵), clean. New Lean is in `Feedback.lean` (standard axioms, full build). The new
experiment is `experiments/cycle/phase_bridge.py`.

**Checked.**
- **`anchor_word_le`:** if cᵢ ≤ pᵢ for every letter, then A_w(x) ≤ G_w(y) whenever x ≤ y. In particular A_w ≤ G_w, so
  G_w(s) ≤ s gives A_w(s) ≤ s.
- `contract_fixed` (DCC) gives an anchor fixed point x = A_w(x) ≤ s. Then x ≤ G_w(x), and the ascending iteration
  (ACC) reaches t = G_w(t) with x ≤ t ≤ s. The two nested periodic systems are x_k ≤ y_k, as the brief says.
- **First-entry labels (Branch A).** If x_k ∉ D and x_{k+1} = F(cᵢ, x_k) ∈ D, then x_{k+1} ≰ cᵢ, so its label K
  satisfies K = {i} or i ∉ K. A label containing i and some j ≠ i would put x_{k+1} below pᵢ ⊓ pⱼ ≤ cᵢ. For three
  generators: the current singleton, another singleton, or the other two generators.

**Branch B kills the phase hypothesis** (`anchor_outside_blocks`).
- If the anchor successor F(c, x) ∉ D with x ≤ y, then F(c, y) ∉ D, so F(c, y) ≤ a fails.
- So when the anchor cycle stays outside D, the `phase_step` hypothesis fails at *every* phase of the generator cycle
  above it. The anchor cycle bounds the generator cycle from below, which is the wrong direction for supplying
  F(c_{i_k}, y_k) ≤ p_{i_k}.

**The conjectured global phase bridge is not a local consequence** (z3, strictly crossing triple, rows
{pᵢ, cᵢ, m_ij, b} × columns {u_k, x_k}, monotone and witness-free on every cell).
- *The claim tested:* for a 3-letter word (1, 2, 3), "some phase has u_{k+1} ≤ u_k or F(c_{i_{k+1}}, u_{k+1}) ≤ p_{i_{k+1}}".
- *Result:* both alternatives can fail at every phase, in **Branch B** (all anchor states outside D) and in **Branch A**
  (the anchor cycle meets D). The cycle states are pairwise incomparable in the models.
- **Control:** the same test for two generators (word (1, 2), c₁ = c₂ = m₁₂), where Cut is proved (`cut_two`), is also
  SAT in both branches.
- So the bridge does not follow from the cycle's local structure even where the theorem holds. For k = 2 the
  contradiction is global: the meet-row orbit of ⊤, `sandwich` and DCC. These models are partial and diagnostic only.

**Singleton petals.** The common lower element e of `cycle_common_lower` with K = {i}, and the Branch A first entries
with a singleton label, both survive. No local constraint transports an exclusive-petal element through the other
phases.

**Conclusion for this line (§34.3z⁶⁶–⁷⁶).**
- Every cycle, coalition and certificate formulation has a local-model countermodel, including in the proved case k = 2.
- A proof of the crossing case must therefore be global in the way `cut_two` is: a well-founded induction over
  columns, here `FGTwo.sandwich`'s induction on s, carrying an invariant that is preserved under the meet-row orbit.
- The smallest open statement remains the induction step of a three-generator sandwich: an invariant replacing
  "F(r, u) ≤ a" that survives a change of letter.

## 34.3z⁷⁷ ChatGPT brief "global induction invariant via ko-threat accounting": transfer chains are finite, and stop at a sink

**Audit.** HEAD c7d186d (§34.3z⁷⁶), clean, matches the brief's snapshot. New Lean is in `Profile.lean` (standard
axioms, full build). The new experiment is `experiments/profile/ko_profile.py`.

- `Crossing.lean`'s `Closes`/`Tree`/`tree_closes`/`singleton_closes`/`cut_of_tree` are as the brief describes.
  `Closes F p univ` for the whole family is equivalent to Cut, since `orbit_end` makes the meet's orbit of ⊤ enter D.
  So the proposed `crossing_bag_closes` is `cut_three` restated, not a lemma on the way to it.
- The brief's "τᵢ(u) is defined because the orbit from u ≤ ⊤ is below the orbit of ⊤" is right. The checked form is
  `entry_exists_below`.

**Checked (profile).**
- **`entry_col_mono`, `entry_col_le`:** for u ≤ v outside D, τᵢ(u) ≤ τᵢ(v), and at equal times eᵢ(u) ≤ eᵢ(v), so
  Lᵢ(v) ⊆ Lᵢ(u). This is the brief's profile monotonicity lemma, generic in a monotone map.
- **`entry_label_single`:** the classification i ∈ Lᵢ(u) ⟹ Lᵢ(u) = {i}, with `meet_le_anchor`.
- **`entry_label_contracting`:** at a pᵢ-contracting column (F(pᵢ, s) ≤ s, e.g. ⊤), i ∉ Lᵢ(s). So at ⊤ every
  anchor obligation transfers. The case "current singleton" never occurs at ⊤.
- **Feedback transitions u′ = F(pⱼ, u).** If F(pⱼ, u) ≤ u, then u′ < u and the profile weakly improves; the brief's
  option 1 holds trivially. Otherwise nothing is forced: z3 test C finds u, u′ incomparable with τ(u′) = τ(u) and equal
  labels, for k = 3 **and in the k = 2 control**. So the profile Π is not a rank on feedback steps, even where Cut is
  proved.

**The new global lemma: the upward transfer digraph is acyclic** (`no_upward_transfer_cycle`).
- At a column u ∉ D write a → k (k ≠ a) if F(cₐ, v) ≤ pₖ for some v ≥ u.
- *Theorem:* no nonempty T ⊆ ι has every a ∈ T with an edge into T. So the digraph is acyclic.
- *Proof:* the meet rows ⋀_A p (A ⊆ T, |A| ≥ 2) lie below cₐ for every a ∈ A. So at u, F(⋀_A p, u) is below every
  pₖ that some a ∈ A transfers to. The label map A ↦ {k ∈ T : F(⋀_A p, u) ≤ pₖ} is antitone and never contains A
  (no witness at row ⋀_A p). A ⊆-minimal A with label ⊆ A has its label B ⊊ A. B has two members because every
  a ∈ A has a successor ≠ a. Then B is a smaller such set. This is `no_closed_rows` for the meet rows.
- This is the brief's §6 "cyclic separator transport", made exact. A transfer around the separator cycle
  m₁₂, m₂₃, m₃₁ never produces an anchor increase. It produces a closed meet-row family, which is a witness.
- Special cases:
  - `no_anchor_transfer_cycle` (v = u);
  - `simultaneous_entry`;
  - `contracting_simultaneous`: at a column contracting for every generator, some anchor row stays outside D.
- For k = 2 the digraph has the single anchor c₁ = c₂ = m₁₂. The entry of its orbit from ⊤ would give edges out
  of both generators, so the theorem is `cut_two` again.

**Ko accounting, answered** (`ko_chain_injective`, `ko_chain_finite`).
- *Formalization:* a ko chain is a sequence of states (uₙ, iₙ). The columns uₙ ∉ D weakly descend (the last point
  of the c_{iₙ}-orbit of uₙ ∈ S_{iₙ} is ≤ uₙ), iₙ₊₁ ≠ iₙ, and F(c_{iₙ}, uₙ₊₁) ≤ p_{iₙ₊₁}.
- *Theorem:* a chain never repeats a generator, so it has fewer than |ι| transfers. If i_m = i_{m′}, then
  {i_m, …, i_{m′−1}} is closed in the upward digraph at u_{m′}. **No chain condition is used.**
- So the central question's answer, in this formalization, is **yes**: the obligations are a finite reserve. Every
  transfer is permanent, because the edge set only grows downward and stays acyclic. The well-founded rank is the
  number of non-edges of the upward transfer digraph E(u), which is antitone in u.
- **But a chain can halt without a contradiction.** It halts when the last point w of the current orbit is not pⱼ-
  contracting for the generator j it transferred to. Then the sandwich does not apply to j's orbit from w.
- z3 test B: from ⊤ with τ = (2, 2, 2), every label profile with all three transfers unrenewable
  (F(pⱼ, wᵢ) ≰ wᵢ for j ∈ Lᵢ) is SAT. This includes the 2-cycle 1 → 2 → 1 at the incomparable columns w₁, w₂. In the
  k = 2 control no transfer profile exists (UNSAT).

**Where every accounting stops** (`sink_generator`, DCC).
- In a counterexample choose u ∉ D with a maximal digraph E(u). It is acyclic, so it has a generator j without
  out-edges. Below u no edge can appear.
- Hence for every v ≥ u, and every v ≤ u outside D, F(cⱼ, v) lies below no pₖ with k ≠ j. No column below u is
  pⱼ-contracting: from such a column the cⱼ-orbit would enter outside ↓pⱼ (`anchor_sandwich`) at a last point ≤ u.
- So the crossing case is equivalent to excluding a **sink generator**: a column u and a generator j whose anchor-j
  entries on the comparability cone of u all lie in the exclusive part of petal j.

**Profiles at ⊤** (z3 test A: rows {pᵢ, cᵢ, m_ij, b} × all named columns; `anchor_sandwich` and `l4` are imposed as
proved constraints).
- *Entry times (τ₁, τ₂, τ₃) ∈ {1, 2, 3}³:* exactly the 7 patterns with at least two τᵢ = 1 are UNSAT.
  - This is the upward digraph: the two immediate entries give edges at every column, and the third orbit's last
    point carries the third edge.
  - The other 20 patterns are SAT.
- *k = 2 control:* every pattern is UNSAT.
- **`lastpoints_no_common_lower` (Lean, DCC).** The anchor last points wᵢ of ⊤ (or of any column contracting for every
  generator) have no common lower bound outside D. In a lattice, ⋀ᵢ wᵢ ∈ D. The partial models of test A
  contain no meets of columns, so they cannot see this.
- z3 test D adds x = ⋀ wᵢ as a named glb:
  - x ∉ D is UNSAT in every pattern, matching the Lean theorem.
  - With x ∈ D, the patterns (2, 2, 2) and (1, 2, 3) stay SAT.
  - (1, 2, 2) becomes UNSAT, and the k = 2 control is UNSAT throughout.
- **The (1, 2, 2) refutation, explained.**
  - *Ablation:* with x ∈ D imposed (sound by the Lean theorem), the refutation needs no cells at x. It does need
    `anchor_sandwich`/`l4`.
  - *Mechanism:* here w₂ = F(c₂, ⊤) and w₃ = F(c₃, ⊤), so m₂₃ ≤ c₂ ⊓ c₃ gives F(m₂₃, v) ≤ w₂ ⊓ w₃ = x ∈ D at every
    column. A **meet of anchor values** is a new label source, although neither value is in D.
  - `experiments/profile/meet_closure_122.py` enumerates all 40 label assignments (σᵢ ≠ i, K = Λ(x) ⊉ {2, 3}). Each
    one has a closed meet family at ⊤, w₂ or w₃.
  - The closure step is the new Lean lemma **`no_closed_meet_family`**: at u ∉ D, no nonempty family of meet rows
    ⋀_A p has each value below another member. The case enumeration is a finite search, not a Lean proof.
- The bug found on the way: an unguarded `l4` constraint at the column x ∈ D was unsound. It was fixed before the
  runs reported here; tests A–C have no column in D and are unaffected.

**Report against the brief's §9.**
- **Profile monotonicity:** `entry_col_mono`, `entry_col_le`, `entry_exists_below`.
- **Surviving transition patterns:**
  - Every chain of distinct transfers survives locally (test B).
  - Feedback transitions leave Π unconstrained (test C, also for k = 2).
  - At ⊤ the entry-time patterns with at most one immediate entry survive (test A), except (1, 2, 2) once the meet
    of the last points is added (test D).
- **Well-founded rank:** the number of non-edges of the upward transfer digraph E(u), antitone in u and acyclic. It
  replaces the multiset of (τᵢ, Lᵢ), which is not monotone on incomparable columns.
- **Can the ko cycle persist?** No (`ko_chain_injective`): a repeated generator closes a meet-row family.
- **Countermodel to the failed invariant:** test C (Π non-descent, also for k = 2) and test B (stuck states).
- **First genuinely global lemma:** `no_upward_transfer_cycle`. **Corrected in §34.3z⁷⁸:** it is equivalent to its
  single-column case by monotonicity in the column, so it is local. The global inputs here are `anchor_sandwich`
  (in `lastpoints_no_common_lower`) and the DCC choice in `sink_generator`.
- **Meets of anchor values.** These are what the partial models of §34.3z⁶⁶–⁷⁶ never contained. They are the next
  source of labels to feed `no_closed_meet_family`.
- **Smallest Lean target:** exclude a sink generator. Given u ∉ D and j with F(cⱼ, v) ∉ ⋃_{k≠j} ↓pₖ on the
  comparability cone of u, derive a witness. An equivalent target: for some column contracting for every generator,
  find a column outside D below all its anchor last points; `lastpoints_no_common_lower` then finishes.

## 34.3z⁷⁸ Continuation: transfer sets, self-entry sets, and the refined sink

**Audit.** HEAD c11b2bb (§34.3z⁷⁷), clean. New Lean is in `TransferSets.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**Correction to §34.3z⁷⁷.** The "upward" transfer digraph adds nothing (`upward_le`). If v ≥ u, then
F(cₐ, u) ≤ F(cₐ, v), so an edge witnessed at v is already an edge at u. So `no_upward_transfer_cycle` is equivalent to
`no_anchor_transfer_cycle` at the single column u: it is the meet-row column lemma (§34.3z¹⁹) applied to anchor rows,
and it is local. In §34.3z⁷⁷ the global content is elsewhere:
- `anchor_sandwich`, used in `lastpoints_no_common_lower` and in the label exclusion at ⊤;
- the DCC choice of a maximal transfer pattern in `sink_generator`.
`ko_chain_injective` stands as proved; its use of lower columns is monotonicity. The claim in the §34.3z⁷⁷ report that
the digraph lemma is "genuinely global" was wrong.

**Transfer and self-entry sets.** For u ∉ D:
- Transferₐ(u) means F(cₐ, u) ≤ pₖ for some k ≠ a;
- Selfₐ(u) means F(cₐ, u) ≤ pₐ.

**Checked.**
- `transfer_down`, `self_down`: both are down-closed inside Dᶜ. So Dₐ = D ∪ Transferₐ is a down-set of L containing D.
- `transfer_self_disjoint`: the two sets are disjoint.
- **`self_entry_unique`:** at most one generator self-enters at any column. The meet row pₐ ⊓ p_b lies below both
  anchors, so two self-entries put F(pₐ ⊓ p_b, u) below its own row.
- `no_common_transfer`: ⋂ₐ Dₐ = D.
- `transfer_nonempty` (DCC): every Dₐ is strictly larger than D. It contains the last point of the cₐ-orbit of ⊤, via
  `transfer_below_contracting`.
- So **in a counterexample, D is the intersection of the strictly larger down-sets Dₐ**, and no column of Dᶜ lies in
  all of them.
- `contracting_not_self`: a pₐ-contracting column is not in Selfₐ (`l4`).
- `min_contracting_transfers`: a minimal pₐ-contracting column is in Transferₐ at the first step. There F(pₐ, s) ∈ D
  with F(pₐ, s) ≰ pₐ.

**Refined sink (`sink_below`, DCC).** Below any column s ∉ D there is a column u ≤ s outside D with:
- *uniformity:* on the down-cone of u in Dᶜ, every generator's transfer status is constant;
- *a sink generator j:* j transfers neither at s nor anywhere on the cone, and no cone column is pⱼ-contracting.
`sink_generator` is the case s = ⊤. Starting from a transfer column is what gives information.

**Three generators.** Start from s ∈ Transferₐ, e.g. the last point of the cₐ-orbit of ⊤. Then:
- the whole cone lies in Transferₐ (down-closure), and the sink is some j ≠ a;
- the cone lies in Dᶜ \ Transferⱼ, so it meets every Transferⱼ column only inside D (w ⊓ u ∈ D);
- the third generator b is uniform: either the cone is in Transferₐ ∩ Transfer_b, where acyclicity forbids
  a → b → a and so a → j or b → j, or b also never transfers on the cone;
- at each cone column, j's anchor value is outside D or in petal j only, and at most one generator self-enters.
None of this is contradictory locally. The k = 2 control shows why a local test would not discriminate here: there
Transfer₁ = Self₂ and Transfer₂ = Self₁ (one anchor), and a sink cone is also locally consistent, although Cut holds.
So no z3 run was made this round.

**Open statement, sharpened.** For a strictly crossing triple, the following is equivalent to Cut:
- a column outside D lying in all three Dₐ, or
- a transfer cycle at one column, or
- a pⱼ-contracting column (or a transferring one) below some sink cone.
The smallest Lean target remains excluding the sink: from u, j as in `sink_below` with s ∈ Transferₐ, derive a witness.
The only tool that has produced contradictions across different columns is `anchor_sandwich`, which needs a
pⱼ-contracting column. A sink cone has none, so a proof needs a new source of pⱼ-contraction below u, or a
sandwich whose hypothesis is weaker than F(pⱼ, s) ≤ s.

## 34.3z⁷⁹ ChatGPT brief "sink-row preservation and minimal-boundary descent": every step checks, none is a witness

**Audit.** HEAD c952a27 (§34.3z⁷⁸), clean. The brief cites the audit SHA c11b2bb from the §34.3z⁷⁸ entry. New Lean is
in `Boundary.lean` (standard axioms, full build). The new experiment is `experiments/profile/boundary.py`.

**Checked (brief milestones 1–7).**
1. **`sink_row_out`:** if j does not transfer at v ∉ D, then F(pⱼ, v) ∉ D. Below pⱼ is excluded by no witness;
   below pₖ (k ≠ j) gives F(cⱼ, v) ≤ pₖ. Only ¬Transfer j v at v itself is used.
2. **`sink_row_incomparable`:** on the sink cone F(pⱼ, v) ∥ v.
3. **`meet_proj_lt`, `meet_iterate_enters`:** v ⊓ F(x, v) < v holds for **every** row x ∈ D and every column v ∉ D,
   by no witness. So every meet-projected iteration enters D under DCC. As the brief itself warns, this carries no
   sink information.
4. **`minimal_sink_boundary`:** a minimal outside v below the sink cone, with q = F(pⱼ, v) ∉ D, q ∥ v,
   d = v ⊓ q ∈ D and d < v. The fact d ∈ D is minimality alone: v ⊓ q ∈ D for every q ≱ v.
5. **`lower_boundary_cases` (ACC):** v is a finite join of strict predecessors (Mathlib's
   `WellFoundedGT.isSupFiniteCompact`), or it has a greatest strict predecessor B.
6. **`cover_labels_empty`, `cover_labels_three`:**
   - a finite cover of v ∉ D by elements of D has empty total label intersection;
   - for three generators it contains two disjointly labelled elements, or the triangle {0,1}, {1,2}, {0,2}. This is
     `Feedback.blockers_three` (§34.3z⁷²), reused.
7. **`jump_fixedpoint`:** in the jump case, r = B ⊓ F(r, v) exists, with r ≤ F(r, v), F(r, v) ∥ v and F(r, v) ≰ B.
   The brief's derivation is correct.

**Sharper than the brief: no case split is needed** (`minimal_row_fixedpoint`, ACC).
- At any minimal outside v, the row map G(x) = v ⊓ F(x, v) sends {x < v} into itself, by no witness.
- A maximal post-fixed point above ⊥ is a fixed point r < v. Then r ≤ F(r, v), v ⊓ F(r, v) = r, and F(r, v) ∥ v.
- The jump fixed point is the variant over ↓B, and B ⊓ F(r, v) = v ⊓ F(r, v) there. In both lower-boundary cases the
  same configuration appears.
- **The missing inequality** is F(r, v) ≤ v, which would make r = F(r, v) a witness. Equivalently, F(r, v) ≤ r.
  This is again a comparison between an F-value at v and v itself.

**Milestone 8 (converting a boundary configuration into a witness): not achieved, and not local.** z3 test
`boundary.py`:
- *Model:* v minimal outside D among named elements; F on every row × {v, ⊤}, monotone and witness-free; `l4` at v and
  ⊤. Sink j at v (¬Transfer, F(pⱼ, v) ≰ v), a transfers, and for k = 3 the third generator transfers (case I) or
  not (case II).
- *Patterns:* A-disjoint, every disjoint label pair; A-triangle; B-jump with the fixed point r; and (argument R) the
  row fixed point r = v ⊓ F(r, v) together with a cover.
- *Result:* **every pattern is SAT in both cases**, and the **k = 2 control is SAT** for A-disjoint, B-jump and the row
  fixed point. Cut holds for k = 2, so the boundary data at a minimal column cannot by themselves yield a witness.
  These are diagnostic partial models.
- The meet-projected sandwich of the brief's §7 has no content at a minimal column: there x ⊓ F(cᵢ, x) ∈ D holds for
  every row at the first step. At a non-minimal column it is only a descent to a lower column.

**Answer to the central question.** No, as far as can be checked here. The meet-projected process always enters D,
and at a minimal sink column it produces d ∈ D and the row fixed point r. The three-generator transfer structure at
that column adds:
- a transferring generator a;
- uniform status for b;
- at most one self-entry;
- F(pⱼ, v) ∉ D.

None of these forces F(r, v) ≤ v or F(pⱼ, v) ≤ v, already in the k = 2 control.

**Next smallest Lean target.** As in §34.3z⁷⁸, a cross-column argument. Either:
- a sandwich-type induction whose hypothesis holds on the sink cone, for instance with the row fixed point r of a
  minimal column as anchor, so that the column-v fact v ⊓ F(r, v) = r replaces F(pⱼ, s) ≤ s; or
- a proof that some column of the sink cone is pⱼ-contracting.

The concrete statement to try: for a minimal outside v with row fixed point r, the F(r, ·)-orbit of ⊤ does not enter D
below r. It is a candidate only; no local model has excluded its failure.

## 34.3z⁸⁰ ChatGPT brief "the global orbit below a clipped row fixed point": the conjecture lives on D × D and is invisible

**Audit.** HEAD b23bf22 (§34.3z⁷⁹), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `OrbitGfp.lean`, plus a strengthening of
`Boundary.minimal_row_fixedpoint` (standard axioms, full build).

**Correction to §34.3z⁷⁹, accepted.** The "next target" stated there ("the F(r, ·)-orbit of ⊤ does not enter D below
r") is `Crossing.endpoint_not_le`, already proved for every row r ∈ D. The brief is right.

**Checked.**
- **`row_orbit_gfp` (DCC):** for r ∈ D, the F(r, ·)-orbit of ⊤ is eventually constant at z = F(r, z). Also z ∈ D,
  since z ∉ D would make z ≤ F(r, z) a witness. Every post-fixed point of F(r, ·) is below z, so z = ν(F(r, ·)).
  Milestone 1.
- **`minimal_row_fixedpoint`** now also exports maximality: no y with r < y < v has y ≤ F(y, v). This is the brief's
  Case A fact.

**The conjecture ν(F(r, ·)) ≰ r is Cut-strength and carries no information** (`normalLow_postfixed_bot`,
`normalLow_gfp_le`).
- After its first entry into D, the orbit evaluates F only on D × D cells. No-witness never constrains those cells.
- `Transport.normalizeLow` sets ⊥ on L × D, keeps F on D × Dᶜ, and puts ⊤ on Dᶜ × Dᶜ. The result G is monotone and
  equal to F on cross cells, so it is witness-free with the same minimal columns v and the same row fixed points r.
- For r ∈ D the only post-fixed point of G(r, ·) is ⊥, so ν(G(r, ·)) = ⊥ ≤ r.
- So any proof of ν(F(r, ·)) ≰ r from monotonicity and no witness would refute every counterexample at once. It is
  not an intermediate lemma.
- The same applies to the brief's crossed rectangles. F(r, y) and F(y, y) for y ∈ D are D × D cells, ⊥ in normal
  form, so "F(r, y) ≤ r" is automatic.
- The only meaningful entries of the square are the cross cells:
  - F(r, v) ≥ r and F(r, v) ∥ v;
  - for r < y < v: F(y, v) ≥ F(r, v) ≥ r, y ≰ F(y, v) (maximality), F(y, v) ≰ y and v ≰ F(y, v).
- Per the brief's §7 rule ("if the conjecture fails in the two-generator control, abandon it"): it fails in the normal
  form of every model, k = 2 included. **Abandoned.** No z3 run was needed, since the Lean statement covers all models.

**The weakened sandwich (brief §6).**
- **`clipped_squeeze`:** with the clipped step g′(s) = s ⊓ F(a, s), the squeeze h^{k+1}(s) ≤ h^k(g′ s) ≤ h^k(s) of
  `FGTwo.sandwich` holds under the hypothesis F(r, s) ≤ s, which is weaker than F(a, s) ≤ s. It is inherited:
  F(r, g′ s) ≤ g′ s. These are the replacement bounds the brief asked for.
- **What fails is the base case.** In `l4` the descent u ↦ F(a, u) stays outside D because F(a, u) ∈ D contradicts no
  witness at a or at r. The clipped value u ⊓ F(a, u) can lie in D with no contradiction, so the induction stops.
- The weakened anchor sandwich is therefore exactly its base case: **an anchor-contracting column does not self-enter**
  (F(cⱼ, u) ≤ u ⟹ F(cⱼ, u) ≰ pⱼ). Every point of a cⱼ-orbit from a cⱼ-contracting column is cⱼ-contracting, so no
  induction remains.
- **`sink_no_anchor_contracting`:** that base case for j would exclude every cⱼ-contracting column from a sink cone
  of j. The orbit from such a column descends inside the cone, enters D without transferring (hence below pⱼ), and
  does so from a cⱼ-contracting last point.
- For k = 2 the base case for both generators gives `cut_two` at once (the single orbit from ⊤ must self-enter for
  one of them). So it is not a weak statement.

**Report against the brief's §9.**
- **SHA:** b23bf22 (now advanced by this round).
- **First-entry statement:** covered by `endpoint_not_le`.
- **Greatest fixed point:** z = ν(F(r, ·)) ∈ D, which is ⊥ in normal form.
- **Smallest configuration with a later crossing below r:** the normal form of any counterexample.
- **Crossed-rectangle inequalities:** the cross-cell list above; the D × D entries are vacuous.
- **Two-generator control:** it admits the configuration (always).
- **Missing comparison:** a cross-cell statement. Either the anchor-contracting base case, or a pⱼ-contracting
  column in a sink cone. All orbit statements must stop at the first entry into D.
- **Next Lean theorem:** "no cⱼ-contracting column self-enters", or the weaker form needed in `sink_no_anchor_contracting`
  restricted to the sink cone, or a proof that the sink cone contains a cⱼ-contracting column whose self-entry
  contradicts something else. Each is a statement about cross cells only.

**General lesson.** Every orbit or fixed-point statement that passes the first entry into D reads only D × D cells. It
is either derivable without them or Cut-strength, as with `normal_form_S` in §34.3z⁶².

## 34.3z⁸¹ Continuation: the weak base case, reduced and calibrated

**Audit.** HEAD 0069033 (§34.3z⁸⁰), clean. New Lean is in `WeakBase.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**The target.** The cross-cell statement left by §34.3z⁸⁰ is the **weak base for j**: no column u ∉ D with
F(cⱼ, u) ≤ u has F(cⱼ, u) ≤ pⱼ. `l4` is the special case F(pⱼ, u) ≤ u.

**Checked.**
- **`weak_base_descent` (DCC).** A failure at u descends along the clipped step u ↦ u ⊓ F(pⱼ, u).
  - Both hypotheses are inherited: F(cⱼ, u′) ≤ u′ and F(cⱼ, u′) ≤ pⱼ.
  - The step is strict by no witness.
  - So a failure persists down to a column where d = u ⊓ F(pⱼ, u) lies in D.
- **`weak_base_reduced`.** At such a column the configuration is rigid:
  - e = F(cⱼ, u) ≤ d;
  - the label of d is exactly {j};
  - q = F(pⱼ, u) ∉ D, and q ∥ u.

  This is the smallest configuration a counterexample to the weak base must contain. All its entries are cross cells,
  so normalization cannot erase it.
- **`weak_base_two` (DCC): calibration.** For two generators, the weak base for both generators is contradictory
  outright. The single anchor orbit of ⊤ enters D from a contracting last point, in some petal k, which violates the
  weak base for k. So the weak base is not an easier lemma on the way to `cut_two`; it is `cut_two` restated.
- **`anchor_join_dichotomy` (DCC, any number of generators).** Let Ψ(u) = ⋁ᵢ F(cᵢ, u). Either the weak base fails for
  some generator, or the Ψ-orbit of ⊤ never enters D.
  - If it first entered D at step n + 1, every anchor value at uₙ would be in D and below uₙ. They would all transfer,
    which `no_common_transfer` forbids.
  - So in the second case ν(Ψ) ∉ D, with ν(Ψ) = ⋁ᵢ F(cᵢ, ν(Ψ)) and every term strictly below it (no witness).
  - This is the anchor analogue of the coalition fixed point of §34.3z⁷³. It is not contradictory by itself: the anchor
    orbits from ν(Ψ) reproduce the pattern at ⊤ (§34.3z⁷⁷, test A).

**Status of the proof-side line (§34.3z⁶⁶–⁸¹).**
- Every reformulation reached since §34.3z⁷⁷ is one of three kinds:
  - a local statement at one column, satisfiable in partial models, also for k = 2;
  - a statement on D × D cells, Cut-strength by normalization (§34.3z⁸⁰);
  - or a cross-cell global statement that `weak_base_two`-type calibration shows to be Cut restated for k = 2.
- The weak base for the sink generator, on its sink cone, is the sharpest remaining target. By `weak_base_descent` it
  suffices to exclude the reduced configuration. By `sink_no_anchor_contracting` it closes the case whenever the sink
  cone contains a cⱼ-contracting column.
- Neither half is reachable with `anchor_sandwich`, the only cross-column tool so far, because the sink cone has no
  pⱼ-contracting column.
- Recommendation: treat the brief-driven proof line as saturated, as in §34.3z⁷⁶. The external H = 6 poset and lattice
  runs and DC2 (`experiments/heavy/`) remain the discriminating experiments.

## 34.3z⁸² ChatGPT brief "weak base on the sink cone": packaged, reduced to one cross-column inequality, not closed

**Audit.** HEAD 66d96f2 (§34.3z⁸¹), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `SinkCone.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). The new experiment is `experiments/sinkcone/cone.py`.

**Checked (milestones 1, 2 and part of 4).**
- **`SinkCone F p u₀ j`** packages: u₀ ∉ D, no j-transfer below u₀, no pⱼ-contraction below u₀, and uniform transfer
  status below u₀. `sinkCone_exists` derives it from `sink_below`.
- **`cone_descent`:** a WB-cone failure descends to the reduced configuration at a column still below u₀.
- **`cone_no_anchor_contracting`:** WB-cone alone, not the global weak base, excludes every cⱼ-contracting cone column.
  The weak base is used only at the cⱼ-orbit's last point, which stays in the cone.
- **`cone_hull` (cross-column).** For a column v of the cone:
  - the pⱼ-contracting columns above v are closed under meets (`F(pⱼ, μ ⊓ y) ≤ μ ⊓ y`), so DCC gives a **least**
    one, μ ≥ v;
  - μ lies outside the cone (no pⱼ-contraction there);
  - its cⱼ-orbit transfers (`anchor_sandwich`) at a last point w ≤ μ whose down-set meets the cone only inside D. In
    particular w ⊓ v ∈ D.
- **`self_entry_not_above_orbit`:** a column where j self-enters is never above a pre-entry point of the cⱼ-orbit of
  a pⱼ-contracting column. The next orbit value would be below pⱼ, either inside D too early or as an entry below
  pⱼ against `anchor_sandwich`.

**The missing step, as one inequality.** For a reduced failure v with hull μ:
- If F(cⱼ, μ) ∈ D, then τ(μ) = 1 = τ(v), so by profile monotonicity the label of μ's entry is inside {j}. That is a
  self-entry at a pⱼ-contracting column, which `l4` forbids. So F(cⱼ, μ) ∉ D.
- **WB-cone at v would follow from (F(cⱼ, ·))^k(μ) ≤ v for some pre-entry k ≥ 1**, by `self_entry_not_above_orbit`.
- k = 0 is impossible, since μ is not in the cone. Nothing available compares the orbit of μ with v from above: monotonicity
  gives only (F(cⱼ, ·))^k(μ) ≥ (F(cⱼ, ·))^k(v).

**Priority A (fixed transfer graph): no forced contradiction.**
- At a reduced failure, j's self-entry acts as a loop j → j. A closed meet-row family then needs targets of size ≥ 2.
  Every family through m_aj or m_bj collapses to the singleton {j}, whose row pⱼ leaves D (`sink_row_out`), so the
  closure argument of `no_closed_meet_family` cannot finish.
- z3 (`cone.py`, a named partial model with six columns ⊤, u₀, v, q = F(pⱼ, v), w = v ⊔ q, o = F(cⱼ, ⊤)):
  - the cone package is imposed at every named column below u₀, with the full transfer-edge set constant;
  - also imposed: the reduced failure at v (d = v ⊓ q ∈ D with label {j}), `l4` everywhere, and `anchor_sandwich` at ⊤
    for cⱼ;
  - **all 16 cases are SAT**: the 8 acyclic edge patterns with an a-edge (b → a, b → j, both or neither; a → b, a → j
    or both), each with q ≤ u₀ and with q ≰ u₀;
  - the **k = 2 control is SAT** in both q cases.
- For k = 2 the cone package forces every cone column to be a j-self-entry, since a transfers uniformly and c_a = cⱼ.
  So the control is a genuine test of the cone data, not a vacuous one.

**Priority B (two columns).**
- Case q ≤ u₀: w = v ⊔ q is again a cone column. The climb x ↦ x ⊔ F(pⱼ, x) is ascending and would stop at a
  pⱼ-contracting column (ACC), so it must leave the cone. Case q ≰ u₀ therefore always occurs somewhere (already at u₀).
- Neither case yields a comparison of F-values across the incomparable columns v and q; z3 confirms both are locally
  consistent.

**Priority C.**
- The clipped iteration from pⱼ at v descends to t = v ⊓ F(t, v) ≤ d ≤ pⱼ, and e = G_v(cⱼ) ≤ d.
- No comparison of t with cⱼ or e follows (e ≤ cⱼ is excluded by no witness). It was not pursued further.

**Controls.**
- Every new lemma uses only cross cells (rows in D, columns outside), so it survives `normalizeLow`.
- None implies `cut_two` by itself: the k = 2 cone model is SAT.

**On countermodels.** A model satisfying the whole cone package with no witness anywhere would be a counterexample to
Cut. So WB-cone can only be refuted by such a counterexample; partial models give evidence only. They show that the
cone package, at any finite number of named columns, does not force WB-cone, in either the three-generator case or the
two-generator control.

**Next smallest Lean milestone (withdrawn in §34.3z⁸³: the inequality contradicts the cone package).** Prove (F(cⱼ, ·))^k(μ) ≤ v for some pre-entry k, for the hull μ of a reduced failure v.
Alternatively, prove that the reduced failure forces a pⱼ-contracting column whose cⱼ-orbit passes below v. Either
closes WB-cone via `self_entry_not_above_orbit`. Even with WB-cone, the branch without cⱼ-contracting cone columns
still needs a separate argument (`cone_no_anchor_contracting` is vacuous there).

## 34.3z⁸³ Correction: the §34.3z⁸² inequality is refuted by the cone itself

**Audit.** HEAD 27cc2f9 (§34.3z⁸²), clean. New Lean is `orbit_avoids_cone` in `SinkCone.lean` (standard axioms, full
build).

**`orbit_avoids_cone`.** On a sink cone of j, no pre-entry point of the cⱼ-orbit of a pⱼ-contracting column lies below
u₀.
- The orbit descends, so its last point would also be below u₀.
- That last point transfers (`anchor_sandwich`), against `SinkCone.noTransfer`.

**Consequence.**
- The "missing inequality" (F(cⱼ, ·))^k(μ) ≤ v of §34.3z⁸² is impossible for every cone column v, without any appeal
  to WB-cone.
- So `self_entry_not_above_orbit` is vacuous on the cone. Establishing that inequality would be a proof of Cut, not a
  step towards WB-cone. The §34.3z⁸² milestone is withdrawn.

**What the sink cone is.** Write:
- C = cone columns;
- Pⱼ = pⱼ-contracting columns;
- Orb(Pⱼ) = pre-entry points of the cⱼ-orbits from Pⱼ;
- Aⱼ = cⱼ-contracting columns.

Then:
- C ∩ Pⱼ = ∅ (`SinkCone.noContract`) and C ∩ Orb(Pⱼ) = ∅ (`orbit_avoids_cone`). Every hypothesis under which
  `anchor_sandwich`, `l4` or `transfer_below_contracting` say anything is absent from C, so the cone is exactly the
  region where the only cross-column tool has no foothold.
- WB-cone speaks about Aⱼ ∩ C. Those columns' cⱼ-orbits stay in C and enter D by self-entry. A counterexample has either
  Aⱼ ∩ C = ∅, or such a self-entering orbit. Both alternatives are consistent with every proved fact and with the
  multi-column partial models of §34.3z⁸².
- Closing either alternative needs a global tool for cⱼ-contracting columns that are not pⱼ-contracting, i.e. the weak
  base. By `weak_base_two`, for two generators that tool is `cut_two` itself.

**Conclusion.** With the tools of §34.3z⁶⁶–⁸³ the crossing case cannot be finished, and the sink-cone analysis shows why:
the sink cone is built to avoid exactly the hypotheses of `anchor_sandwich`. Progress needs a second cross-column
induction principle, independent of pⱼ-contraction, whose two-generator instance is a new proof of `cut_two`. The
external searches (`experiments/heavy/`) remain the discriminating experiments.

## 34.3z⁸⁴ ChatGPT brief "find a second cross-column induction": the frontier induction terminates, but not in a contradiction, already for k = 1

**Audit.** HEAD c2b1be4 (§34.3z⁸³), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `CrossCellInduction.lean` (standard
axioms, full build). The new experiment is `experiments/crosscell/frontier.py`.

**Priority A: mixed row/column frontier transitions.** For a cross pair x ∈ D, y ∉ D, write x⁺ = x ⊔ F(x, y) and
y⁻ = y ⊓ F(x, y).
- **Compiled:**
  - `cross_frontier_step`: x < x⁺, y⁻ < y and y⁻ ≤ F(x, y) ≤ x⁺, by no witness alone.
  - `cross_pattern`: x⁺ ∈ D implies y⁻ ∈ D, so there are three patterns.
