  - `seed_join_out`: for an antichain of generators and q ∉ D, pⱼ ⊔ q ∉ D. So the brief's seed gives the comparable
    cross pair d ≤ pⱼ ⊔ q.
- **The new well-founded induction (`frontier_pair`, ACC + DCC).** Step from a cross pair by:
  - moving the column down to y⁻ while y⁻ ∉ D (DCC on the column);
  - otherwise moving the row up to x⁺ while x⁺ ∈ D (ACC on the row).

  This is well founded in the product order. So from every cross pair (x₀, y₀) one reaches a **frontier pair**: x ≥ x₀,
  y ≤ y₀, y ⊓ F(x, y) ∈ D and x ⊔ F(x, y) ∉ D (pattern 3). This answers the brief's question about the same-side
  cases: both are transient, and pattern 3 is the only terminal state.
- **Pattern 3 does not continue well-foundedly.** The new comparable pair (y⁻, x⁺) has its row incomparable with x and
  its column incomparable with y, so no measure decreases.
- **Calibration, k = 1.** z3 (`frontier.py`): a frontier pair with monotonicity and no witness on rows {generators,
  meet, x} × columns {y, ⊤} is **SAT for k = 1**, also with x = p₁, and for k = 2.
  - For k = 1 (D = ↓p) Cut has a three-line proof: the *unclipped* orbit y ↦ F(p, y) stays outside D by no witness and
    descends strictly.
  - The clipped step y ↦ y ⊓ F(p, y) discards exactly the information that F(p, y) itself is outside D.
  - So the frontier induction does not contain even the k = 1 proof, and its k = 2 instance cannot give a new proof of
    `cut_two`. **Priority A fails the success criterion.**

**Priority B: least anchor-contracting hull.**
- **Compiled:**
  - `anchor_hull`: Aⱼ = {z | F(cⱼ, z) ≤ z} is closed under meets and contains ⊤, so DCC gives the least λ ≥ v in Aⱼ;
    F(cⱼ, λ) ∈ Aⱼ; for v ∉ D, v ≰ F(cⱼ, λ) (leastness plus no witness), so v ⊓ F(cⱼ, λ) < v. The brief's facts 1–4
    are all right.
  - `anchor_hull_entry`: the cⱼ-orbit of λ enters D from a cⱼ-contracting last point w ≤ λ. It either transfers (and
    then on a sink cone ↓w meets the cone only inside D, by `transfer_down` and `noTransfer`) or self-enters (a
    weak-base failure at w).
- **Both alternatives on the cone.**
  - A_j ∩ C ≠ ∅: λ of a cone column may be the column itself; its orbit stays in C and self-enters. This is the WB-cone
    failure again.
  - A_j ∩ C = ∅: λ lies above u₀, and the entry alternative gives no information about the cone.
  - Neither branch decreases anything: the strict descent v ⊓ F(cⱼ, λ) < v is a clipped step of the Priority A kind and
    can land in D.
- **Calibration, k = 2.** With c₁ = c₂ = m and v = ⊤, λ = ⊤. Its orbit self-enters for one generator and transfers for
  the other. Only `anchor_sandwich` (pⱼ-contraction at ⊤) excludes that, so the hull adds no new proof of `cut_two`.
  **Priority B fails the success criterion.**

**Controls.** All new lemmas use only cross cells (they survive `normalizeLow`). No order comparison between v and q, or
between distinct pairwise meets, was assumed.

**Smallest unresolved rule.** A continuation from a frontier pair that is strict in some well-founded order and
**retains the unclipped value F(x, y) ∉ D** in the pattern-3 case: the row stays and the column jumps to F(x, y). That is
the k = 1 mechanism. For k = 2 it fails at the first step where F(x, y) is not below the current column (the orbit is
not monotone), which is the point at which `FGTwo.sandwich` uses pⱼ-contraction.

The stated success criterion — a cross-cell induction independent of pⱼ-contraction whose k = 2 instance proves
`cut_two` — is not met by either candidate. The k = 1 calibration shows that clipping-based transitions cannot meet it.

## 34.3z⁸⁵ ChatGPT brief "from a frontier ladder to a finite net": the net is the profile at ⊤, and for k = 2 it excludes only the all-equal profile

**Audit.** HEAD 9520d2f (§34.3z⁸⁴), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `Net.lean` (standard axioms, full build).
The new experiment is `experiments/net/net_profile.py`.

**Net state.** For every nonempty A ⊆ ι: m_A = ⋀_A p and h_A = F(m_A, ·). The orbit of ⊤ gives τ(A), the last point
w_A ∉ D, the entry e_A ∈ D and the label Λ_A. All of these are cross cells, so the net survives `normalizeLow`.

**Compiled.**
- `net_entry`: the entry exists, Λ_A ≠ ∅ and A ⊄ Λ_A (`orbit_end`, `endpoint_not_le`). Milestones 1–2.
- `net_time_mono`, `net_equal_time`: A ⊆ B gives τ(B) ≤ τ(A), and at equal times Λ_A ⊆ Λ_B.
- **`net_sync`: synchronization at equal times.** For A ⊆ B, h_B^n(⊤) ≤ h_A^n(⊤) for every n. So at the column
  y = h_U^n(⊤), with U the union of a family, every row m_A of the family has F(m_A, y) ≤ h_A^{n+1}(⊤). This is the
  common outside column the brief asked for. It exists only at times n where the union's orbit is still outside D,
  i.e. n ≤ τ(U).
- **`net_no_closed`:** no family of meet rows is closed by its time-(n+1) values while h_U^n(⊤) ∉ D
  (`no_closed_meet_family` at the synchronized column).
- **`net_two_equal`:** for two generators, τ₁ = τ₂ = τ₁₂ is contradictory. The family {{1}, {2}} is closed at the
  last outside point of the meet orbit (Λ₁ = {2}, Λ₂ = {1}).

**Synchronization yields nothing more.** At a column h_A^n(⊤) (n ≤ τ(A)), rows m_B with B ⊆ A are bounded by
h_B^{n+1}(⊤). That value is in D only if τ(B) = n = τ(A). Rows with B ⊇ A are bounded the wrong way. So the
synchronized data are exactly the equal-time data: the net is the profile at ⊤ of all meet rows (§34.3z⁷⁷ test A did
the same for the anchor rows).

**Two-generator calibration (Milestone 3): a precise partial-model obstruction.** z3 with all three meet rows as glbs,
all three orbits of ⊤ named, and F on every row × orbit column (monotone, witness-free, no sandwich):
- **k = 1:** every profile is UNSAT. The net is the one-generator proof (Λ ⊆ {1} nonempty, against A ⊄ Λ).
- **k = 2:** of the 14 profiles allowed by time monotonicity with τ ≤ 3, exactly the three with τ₁ = τ₂ = τ₁₂ are
  UNSAT (`net_two_equal`). The other 11 are SAT, e.g. τ₁₂ = 1 < τ₁ = τ₂ = 2.
- **k = 2 with the sandwich added** (the meet orbit's entry below neither generator): all UNSAT.
- **k = 3, seven rows (sample):** all τ equal is UNSAT (the column lemma at a synchronized column), and τ(123) = 1
  below the others is SAT.
- **The omitted relation** is the sandwich's *shifted* comparison h^{k+1}(s) ≤ h^k(g s) ≤ h^k(s) with g = F(pᵢ, ·).
  It compares the h-orbit of ⊤ with the h-orbit of a *second starting column* g(⊤), and it needs g(s) ≤ s. The net
  has only one starting column, ⊤, so it cannot contain it. Adding orbits from the g-iterates of ⊤ is the sandwich
  induction itself. For k = 3 that is `anchor_sandwich`, which the sink cone avoids (§34.3z⁸³).

**Priority B (unclipped frontier value).** Λ(x) ∩ Λ(z) = ∅ whenever x ⊔ z ∉ D. This needs no antichain, and multiple
labels are fine: a common generator would lie above x ⊔ z. The case z ∉ D gives the cross pair (x, z), with no
comparison between z and y. No rank on augmented states was found that keeps z and decreases: the obstruction is the
same missing shifted comparison.

**Relation to the coalition fixed point (§34.3z⁷³).** The net is not equivalent to it. The coalition fixed point
H_J(t) = t uses one column and all generator rows. The net uses all meet rows at the synchronized orbit columns, which is
the meet-row column lemma applied along the bottom orbit. Neither contains a second starting column.

**Answer.** The net cannot keep transferring its obstruction forever only in the synchronous case. For two generators
every staggered profile is consistent with the whole net. So the net is not a new proof of `cut_two`; the success
criterion is not met.

**Next smallest Lean milestone (the example below is withdrawn in §34.3z⁸⁶: from ⊤ the clipped chain is the
sandwich chain).** A comparison between orbits from two starting columns that does not need g(s) ≤ s. For example, for s = ⊤ and s′ = F(pᵢ, ⊤) ⊓ w (any clipped column): bounds h^{k+1}(⊤) ≤ h^k(s′) without the
contraction. By `clipped_squeeze` this holds if F(m, ⊤) ≤ s′, i.e. if the meet row's first value is below the second
start. That is the precise inequality to look for.

## 34.3z⁸⁶ Correction, and a map of the crossing-case obstructions (§34.3z⁶⁶–⁸⁵)

**Correction.** The example milestone of §34.3z⁸⁵ is the sandwich again (`clipped_eq_of_contracting`). From a
contracting start s, the clipped step s ⊓ F(a, s) equals F(a, s), and contraction is inherited. So the clipped chain
from ⊤ is the g-chain of `FGTwo.sandwich`. A second start below it differs only once contraction is lost, and that is
exactly where the squeeze's base case fails (§34.3z⁸⁰). Withdrawn. New Lean is `clipped_eq_of_contracting` in
`Net.lean` (standard axioms, full build).

**Map.** Every statement examined in §34.3z⁶⁶–⁸⁵ falls into one of four classes. Each class has a test that decides
it.

| class | test | outcome | examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| L: one column, or one orbit from ⊤ | partial model with the k = 2 control | SAT also for k = 2, so it cannot force Cut | cycle bridges (⁷⁶); profiles and nets at ⊤ (⁷⁷, ⁸⁵); boundary and minimal columns (⁷⁹); sink-cone patterns (⁸²); frontier pairs (⁸⁴, SAT even for k = 1) |
| DD: uses F on D × D | `normalizeLow` | Cut-strength: false in the normal form of every counterexample | orbits past the first entry; ν(F(r, ·)) ≰ r (⁸⁰); D-columns in rectangles |
| G: global cross-cell statement | k = 2 specialization | Cut restated | weak base for all generators (`weak_base_two`, ⁸¹) |
| V: holds vacuously, or its hypothesis contradicts the sink cone | `orbit_avoids_cone`, `SinkCone` | no content on the cone | the hull-orbit inequality (⁸², withdrawn ⁸³); cone_no_anchor_contracting when Aⱼ ∩ C = ∅ |

**The proved global facts in the crossing case:**
- `anchor_sandwich` and `transfer_below_contracting`: from a pⱼ-contracting column, the cⱼ-orbit transfers;
- `lastpoints_no_common_lower`: the anchor last points of ⊤ have a meet in D;
- `sink_below` and `SinkCone`: some column u₀ and generator j have a cone with no pⱼ-contraction, no pⱼ-contracting
  orbit points, and a constant acyclic transfer pattern;
- the meet-row column lemmas (`no_closed_meet_family`, `no_upward_transfer_cycle`), which are local.

**The single missing object.** A comparison between the h-orbits of two *different starting columns* s and s′ that
does not need g(s′) ≤ s or g(s) ≤ s. The sandwich supplies h^{k+1}(s) ≤ h^k(g s) ≤ h^k(s) only under contraction,
and the sink cone is exactly where no contraction is available. Any successful argument must contain such a
comparison. Its k = 2 instance has to be a proof of `cut_two` that does not go through FGTwo's g-chain.

**Recommendation.** Further briefs in this line should be accepted only if they name such a comparison explicitly.
Otherwise they fall into one of the four classes above and can be decided by the corresponding test. The
discriminating experiments remain the external H = 6 and DC2 runs in `experiments/heavy/`.

## 34.3z⁸⁷ ChatGPT brief "cross-orbit comparison without contraction": the lag compares histories only in A1; A2 needs the sandwich's upper bound

**Audit.** HEAD 9166f7b (§34.3z⁸⁶), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `Lag.lean` (standard axioms, full build).
The new experiment is `experiments/lag/lag.py`. This brief does name a two-start comparison, as §34.3z⁸⁶ required.

**Compiled.**
- **`iterate_succ_le_of_map_le` (L):** h(s) ≤ s′ gives h^{n+1}(s) ≤ h^n(s′). There is no contraction anywhere.
- **`lagged_time`:** if the orbit of s enters at step n + 1 and that of s′ at step n′ + 1, then n ≤ n′ + 1. The second
  start cannot enter more than one step earlier.
- **`lagged_label` (A1, n′ = n − 1):** the second endpoint lies above the first, so its labels are among the first's.
  The brief's A1 claim is correct.
- **A2 (n′ ≥ n):** the column h^n(s′) is outside D and above the first endpoint. This is a comparable cross pair, the
  brief's A2.
- **`upper_bound_kills_A2`:** the sandwich's upper bound h^n(s′) ≤ h^n(s) forces the second orbit into D by step
  n + 1, below the first endpoint, so with its labels. This is the only use of contraction in `FGTwo.sandwich`.
- **`lastpoint_meet` (M) and `lastpoint_meet_same`:** opposite labels for the pair meet row put w_s ⊓ w_t in D. Equal
  labels give an outside meet with immediate entry into that petal. Both compile. Both are monotonicity at the single
  column w_s ⊓ w_t, i.e. `no_closed_meet_family` with one row; (M) needs no i ≠ j hypothesis.

**Two-generator calibration (z3, `lag.py`).**
- *Model:* columns s, s′ = F(p₁, s), both h-orbits up to entry, and the glb z = w_s ⊓ w_{s′}. F is given on all
  rows × columns, monotone and witness-free, with no sandwich.
- *Results* (24 cases: entry times n ≤ 2, n′ ∈ {n − 1, n, n + 1}, equal or opposite labels):

| start s | A1 (n′ = n − 1) | A2, n′ = n | A2, n′ = n + 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| contracting (F(p₁, s) ≤ s) | same label SAT, opposite UNSAT | same label SAT, opposite UNSAT | UNSAT |
| non-contracting (F(p₁, s) ∥ s, the sink situation) | same label SAT, opposite UNSAT | SAT both | SAT both |

- From a contracting start, the label of s propagates to s′ in every case. This is exactly the sandwich's propagation
  step, and the reason `cut_two` holds.
- From a non-contracting start the lag gives only A1's label inheritance, which `lagged_label` already proves. A2 is
  unconstrained, with either label.
- **Smallest two-generator counter-profile:** a non-contracting start s with entry at step 2 below p₁ only, and
  s′ = F(p₁, s) ∥ s still outside at step 1, entering at step 2 below p₂ only. By (M) the last points meet in D. It is
  SAT with every lemma above.
- So the lagged comparison does not give a new proof of `cut_two`. In a proof along the g-chain from ⊤ it coincides
  with the sandwich, because there every start is contracting.

**Equal-label branch.** Repeated meets z₁ = w_s ⊓ w_t, z₂ = z₁ ⊓ w_u, … descend weakly. A step is strict only when the
new last point is not above the current meet, and nothing forces that. DCC therefore stops the sequence at a column in
D or at a meet that further starts no longer lower. Neither is a contradiction: an outside meet with immediate entry
is a single-column fact (class L of §34.3z⁸⁶).

**Three generators.** The pairwise rows h_ij give the same picture: A1 inherits labels, and A2 is free off contracting
columns. By `orbit_avoids_cone` and `SinkCone.noContract`, every start on the sink cone is non-contracting, so on the
cone only A1 information exists. That is a one-step lag with label inheritance, and it does not decrease any rank in
A2. No strict transition was found.

**Controls.** All new lemmas use only cross cells (they survive `normalizeLow`). None compares s with F(pⱼ, s), and
none assumes orbits descend from arbitrary starts.

**The exact missing comparison.** A substitute for the upper bound: some column c with h^n(s′) ≤ c and h(c) below
the first endpoint's label set, available when s′ ∥ s. Equivalently, in A2, a reason why the second orbit's entry
inherits the first's label. `upper_bound_kills_A2` shows that c = h^n(s) works exactly when h^n(s′) ≤ h^n(s), that
is, under contraction.

## 34.3z⁸⁸ ChatGPT brief "row switching at an A2 entry": the switching system terminates, in the clipped-frontier pattern

**Audit.** HEAD fb63c10 (§34.3z⁸⁷), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `RowSwitch.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). The new experiment is `experiments/rowswitch/terminal.py`.

**The brief's §2 correction is accepted.** A column c ≥ h^n(s′) with h(c) labelled is equivalent to A2 label
inheritance (take c = h^n(s′)). The "missing comparison" sentence of §34.3z⁸⁷ was a restatement.

**Compiled.** A *pair state* (x, y, t) means x ≠ y, t ∉ D and F(pₓ ⊓ p_y, t) ≤ p_y.
- `switched_row_escape`: z = F(p_y, t) is below neither pₓ nor p_y, and t ≰ z. All three of the brief's claims are
  correct. For two generators, z ∉ D.
- `switched_meet_strict`, `switched_meet_entry`: t ⊓ z < t, and the pair entry is inherited by t ⊓ z.
- `third_petal_switch`: if z ≤ p_k, then k ≠ x, y and (y, k, t) is again a pair state, **at the same column t**. The
  brief expected the transition columns to differ; in the third-petal branch they do not.
- **`switch_bounded`:** at a fixed column, switching cannot go on. Let S be the set of generators y with some valid
  state (x, y, t). If every F(p_y, t) with y ∈ S were in D, each would lie below some p_k with k ∈ S (the switched state
  is valid), so S would be a closed row set, against `no_closed_rows`. So some valid state at t has F(p_y, t) ∉ D.
  This synchronizes the brief's pair cycle (i, j) → (j, k) → (k, i): all its states live at one column, so the cycle is
  excluded.
- **`switch_terminal` (DCC):** every pair state leads, by switches at a column (bounded by `switch_bounded`) and strict
  clipped descents t ↦ t ⊓ F(p_y, t), to a **terminal state** (x′, y′, t′) with:
  - t′ ≤ t;
  - the pair entry F(p_{x′} ⊓ p_{y′}, t′) ≤ p_{y′};
  - F(p_{y′}, t′) ∉ D;
  - t′ ⊓ F(p_{y′}, t′) ∈ D.

  The rank is the column under DCC. Within a column, switching is finite by the closed-row argument, not by a cyclic
  order on labels.

**Comparison with existing results (the brief's control).**
- The descent t ↦ t ⊓ F(p_y, t) is the clipped step of `weak_base_descent` (which also carries F(cⱼ, t) ≤ t) and of
  `frontier_pair` with the row fixed at p_y.
- The terminal state is the frontier terminal pattern of §34.3z⁸⁴: y⁻ ∈ D and x⁺ = p_{y′} ⊔ z ∉ D, by
  `seed_join_out`.
- `lastpoint_meet` and `switch_bounded` are both `no_closed_rows`/`no_closed_meet_family` at one column.
- So the row switch adds one genuinely new bounded step, the same-column switch, and then lands in class L of
  §34.3z⁸⁶.

**Two-generator calibration (z3).** The full A2 profile, with no sandwich:
- non-contracting s;
- s′ = F(p₁, s) ∥ s;
- both h-orbits, with s entering at step n + 1 below p₁ and s′ entering later below p₂ from its last point t;
- z = F(p₂, t) as a column;
- d = t ⊓ z (glb), with all cross-cell monotonicity and no witness.

It is **SAT for every (n, n′) tested and for both labels of d** (below p₁ or below p₂). For three generators, the
terminal pair state alone (all seven meet rows, strict crossing) is SAT with d below each of p₁, p₂, p₃.

**The additional fact a genuine counterexample must satisfy beyond this profile.** The profile contains no
contracting column. A full model must also contain ⊤ and its m-orbit, and `anchor_sandwich` at ⊤ then excludes every
k = 2 model. So the two-generator obstruction is again exactly the sandwich at a contracting column. The row-switching
system does not prove `cut_two`.

**Report.**
- **Strongest compiled theorem:** `switch_terminal`, with `switch_bounded` as its new ingredient.
- **Terminal alternatives:** d = t′ ⊓ F(p_{y′}, t′) ∈ D with any label (SAT for each).
- **Two-generator status:** the remaining obstruction is the profile above.
- **Three-generator rule:** (x, y) → (y, k) at the same column, bounded by closed rows.
- **Rank:** the column under DCC.
- **Controls:** all cross cells, so it survives `normalizeLow`.
- **Missing comparison:** a cross-cell fact about the terminal d that is not a consequence of one column. Every such fact
  found so far needs a contracting column.

## 34.3z⁸⁹ ChatGPT brief "globalize the row-switch terminal state": the envelope is monotonicity, and the extended diamond is SAT

**Audit.** HEAD 686c812 (§34.3z⁸⁸), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `Envelope.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). The new experiment is `experiments/rowswitch/diamond.py`.

**Compiled.**
- **`orbit_le_top_orbit`:** h^r(s) ≤ h^r(⊤) for every start s, since s ≤ ⊤.
- **`terminal_below_top_orbit` (DCC):** a pair state for the *original* pair (x, y) at a column of depth r in an
  h-orbit (h = F(pₓ ⊓ p_y, ·)), e.g. the A2 last point, runs through `switch_terminal` to a terminal state. The
  terminal pair (x′, y′) is returned separately, with t′ ≤ v = h^r(⊤) ∉ D. This is the brief's TopEnvelope, with
  provenance kept. Its content is monotonicity: it holds for every depth-r column of every h-orbit, so it carries no
  information from the A2 history beyond the depth r.
- **`terminal_entry_row_output`:** with e = F(m, t′) ≤ p_y, q = F(p_y, t′) ∉ D and t′ ≤ v ∉ D, the brief's
  items 1–6 hold: Q = F(p_y, v) ∉ D, R = F(e, v) ≤ Q, R ≰ e, v ≰ R and v ⊓ R < v. It uses the D-element e as a row at
  column v, a cross cell, so it survives `normalizeLow`.

**The two cases for v ⊓ R.**
- *B2 (v ⊓ R ∉ D):* the smaller column inherits only the envelope, which is trivial (it is below v). It inherits no pair
  state: F(m, v ⊓ R) ≤ F(m, v) = h^{r+1}(⊤), which is in D only at the top orbit's entry time. So B2 is a frontier step
  with row e (class L).
- *B1 (v ⊓ R ∈ D):* z3 below shows every label is consistent with every label of d.

**Extended diamond (z3).**
- *Model:* columns ⊤, v = h(⊤), t′ ≤ v, q = F(p_y, t′) and u = t′ ⊔ q (lub). Rows are all generators, all meet rows
  and the entry row e. Named meets are d = t′ ⊓ q ∈ D and w = v ⊓ R. F is given on every row × outside column,
  monotone and witness-free; nothing is imposed on D × D; there is no sandwich.
- *Result:* **all 6 two-generator and all 12 three-generator cases are SAT** (d below each generator; w below each
  generator or outside D). The complete diamond and the envelope add no comparison absent from the two-column terminal
  model.

**Priority D (top-orbit dichotomy at v), two generators.**
- *If Q = F(p_y, v) ≤ v:* v is p_y-contracting. At equal first-entry times, profile monotonicity and the terminal
  entry e ≤ p_y put the entry of v's h-orbit in the y-petal, against `l4`/`anchor_sandwich` at v.

  This is the existing squeeze applied at a contracting column. It is not independent of `FGTwo.sandwich`, and the
  brief's warning applies.
- *If Q ≰ v:* v ⊓ Q < v is another clipped descent, with no inherited invariant.

So the two-generator calibration closes only through the sandwich.

**Report.**
- `terminal_below_top_orbit` holds but is monotonicity.
- The strongest terminal-entry-row comparison is `terminal_entry_row_output`.
- B1 has every label SAT; B2 is class L.
- The diamond is SAT throughout.
- The two-generator calibration is not independent of the sandwich.
- **Smallest unresolved inequality:** at the envelope column v, the contraction F(p_y, v) ≤ v, or any substitute that
  makes the terminal entry's label propagate to the top orbit's entry. With contraction it is the squeeze; without it,
  nothing found links them.

This brief's success criterion (a theorem connecting the A2 entry, the terminal output and a second column) is met
only formally. The connecting theorem is monotonicity under ⊤ and carries no label information.

## 34.3z⁹⁰ ChatGPT brief "independent row-orbit baseline": the second row orbit is the equal-time net again

**Audit.** HEAD 25538a5 (§34.3z⁸⁹), clean, matches the brief. The brief arrived in two parts; the second completes
§6 onwards. New Lean is in `Baseline.lean` (standard axioms, full build). New experiments are
`experiments/rowswitch/independent_baseline.py` and `common_column.py`.

**Compiled (M1, M2).**
- **`independent_row_baseline` (DCC):** the F(pⱼ, ·)-orbit of ⊤ enters D from a last point v with H = F(pⱼ, v) ≤ pₖ
  and k ≠ j. For every x, F(pₓ ⊓ pⱼ, v) ≤ pₖ and it is not below pₓ ⊓ pⱼ.
  - It uses only `orbit_end`, no witness and row monotonicity; it is independent of `FGTwo.sandwich`.
  - The brief misses that **v is pⱼ-contracting** (H ≤ v, since the orbit descends).
- **`opposite_label_diamond`:** for oppositely labelled pair-row values e = F(m, t) ≤ p_y (not ≤ pₓ) and
  b = F(m, v) ≤ pₓ (not ≤ p_y):
  - t ∥ v, and t ⊓ v ∈ D (`lastpoint_meet`);
  - t ⊔ v ∉ D;
  - e ⊔ b ≤ F(m, t ⊔ v), and e ⊔ b is below neither pₓ nor p_y.

  Every claim of the brief's §4 is correct.

**Two-generator test (M3, M4).** z3 model of the whole baseline profile, with no sandwich:
- *Model:* the p_y-orbit of ⊤ with N steps; the terminal state at t (e, q, d); b and H; z = t ⊓ v, u = t ⊔ v and
  w = e ⊔ b. Rows px, py, m, z, e, b, H and d; columns are all orbit points, t, q, u and w; every cross cell, nothing on
  D × D.
- **SAT in all 8 label variants** (N ∈ {1, 2}, z and d below either generator).
- **The one UNSAT case explains the mechanism.** Add the m-orbit of ⊤:
  - with entry times equal (N = 1, both entering at step 2), an entry below p_y is UNSAT;
  - staggered (N = 2), both labels are SAT.

  The reason is the row comparison hⁿ(⊤) ≤ gⁿ(⊤) (m ≤ p_y, `Crossing.iterate_le`): two different row orbits from the
  *same* start compared at *equal* times. That is `Net.net_sync`, and the closure is `net_two_equal` (§34.3z⁸⁵). The
  independent baseline therefore re-derives the equal-time net. It closes the two-generator case exactly when the
  entry times coincide, and it is silent otherwise.
- No relation at u, w or row z was found that is not monotonicity. `cut_two` is **not** proved without the sandwich.
  The missing condition is the staggered-time comparison, the same gap as in §34.3z⁸⁵.

**Three generators (M5).** Start from (i, j, t) and the baseline (j, k, v).
- **`pair_triangle`:** pair states (i, j), (j, k) and (k, i) at one outside column are contradictory: the triple meet
  row would lie below itself. This is the brief's triangle, compiled.
- **`common_column_exit`:** so if z = t ⊓ v ∉ D, the switch from (j, k) at z must leave D: F(p_k, z) ∉ D. The z3 run
  (`common_column.py`) confirms this. The third pair state is not forced (its negation is SAT), and asserting it is
  UNSAT. Switching at z therefore stops after one step, and `switch_terminal` continues by clipped descent (class L).
- **`baseline_meet_cone`:** v transfers for j (F(cⱼ, v) ≤ H ≤ pₖ), so on a sink cone of j every column below v,
  in particular t ⊓ v, lies in D. On the open region the common-column branch never occurs, and z ∈ D. As with
  `cone_hull` (§34.3z⁸²), the baseline is a pⱼ-contracting object and stays outside the sink cone.

**Report.**
- **Compiled:** M1, M2, `pair_triangle`, `common_column_exit`, `baseline_meet_cone`.
- **Solver-tested:** the profiles above.
- **`cut_two`:** not proved without the sandwich; ACC was not used anywhere.
- **Smallest unresolved inequality:** a comparison of the m-orbit and the pⱼ-orbit of ⊤ at *different* times, i.e. the
  staggered net profile.
- **Three-generator rule:** (i, j, t) → (j, k, v) → at t ⊓ v, either z ∈ D, which always happens on a sink cone, or
  one switch followed by an exit from D.
- **Controls:** all cross cells, so it survives `normalizeLow`.

## 34.3z⁹¹ ChatGPT brief "face-wise provenance and cycle compression": faces close only when the J-meet row enters above them

**Audit.** HEAD f1e7fa2 (§34.3z⁹⁰), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `Faces.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). The new experiment is `experiments/faces/faces.py`. As the brief asked, the finite profiles were run before
the Lean was written.

**Setup.** For each generator i, take the last outside point wᵢ of the anchor orbit from ⊤, and eᵢ = F(cᵢ, wᵢ) ≤ p_{λᵢ}
with λᵢ ≠ i (`anchor_sandwich`). The faces are v_S = ⋀_{i∈S} wᵢ.

**Checked.**
- **`cycle_meet_in_D`:** the brief's cycle-meet observation. It is `no_upward_transfer_cycle` with T = C (and
  `lastpoints_no_common_lower` when C = ι). Correct, and already proved in substance.
- The face-transfer inequalities F(m_A, v_S) ≤ ⋀_{i∈A} p_{λᵢ}, and λ(A) ⊉ A, are correct.
  `inf_le_anchor_of_mem` gives m_A ≤ cᵢ.
- **`face_closure`:** the only closure the face system supports at one column. Let y be an outside column. Suppose the
  anchor rows of S ⊆ J give labels λ(S) at y, the J-meet row has labels K at y, and λ(S) ∪ K ⊇ J. Then
  F(⋀_J p, y) ≤ ⋀_J p, a witness.
- **`top_entry_face_closure`:** the labels K may be imported from any column u ≥ y, in particular from ⊤.
- **`length_one_sync`:** if the anchor orbits have length one (wᵢ = F(cᵢ, ⊤)), then F(⋀_J p, ⊤) ≤ v_J. So a dependent
  J makes the J-meet row enter at once at ⊤.

**Profiles (z3, three generators, strict crossing).** All meet rows, anchors and anchor orbits from ⊤ are included,
with every face of J as a named glb column, all cross cells, multiple labels allowed, and nothing on D × D.

| configuration | orbit length 1 | orbit length 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Hamiltonian 3-cycle 1→2→3→1, J = {1,2,3} | **UNSAT** | SAT |
| same cycle, J = {1,2} (label of 2 escapes J) | SAT | SAT |
| 2-cycle 1↔2 with tail 3→1, J = {1,2} | SAT | SAT |
| Hamiltonian, length 2, with F(m₁₂₃, ⊤) ∈ D imposed | – | **UNSAT** |
| Hamiltonian, length 2, with F(m₁₂₃, ⊤) ∉ D imposed | – | SAT |

**Four generators** (`faces.py k4`: the Hamiltonian 4-cycle and the dependent pair {1, 3}, with pairwise meets and
anchors only): **not completed**. The first case exceeded the 40-minute solver budget, so there is no k = 4 solver
evidence. The Lean lemmas below hold for any number of generators.

**What this decides.**
- The Hamiltonian closure is exactly `top_entry_face_closure`. The triple-meet row b gets a D-label at a column above
  the faces (⊤ when it enters at once). Each 2-face's two cycle labels then complete it, so b lies below itself at that
  face. In the length-one case `length_one_sync` supplies that entry, the face-system version of the §34.3z⁷⁷
  (1, 2, 2) refutation. **With longer orbits the J-meet row need not enter at ⊤, and the Hamiltonian profile is SAT.**
- The brief's alternative 3 ("the Hamiltonian configuration is impossible") is therefore refuted as a consequence of the
  face data. Alternatives 1 and 2 are refuted too: a proper subset of a label cycle can be the minimal dependent set
  with a label escaping it, and nothing forces a smaller configuration.
- **The weakest missing condition** is that the J-meet row enters D at a column above an outside face. For the J-meet
  orbit of ⊤, `entry_mono` gives τ(m_J) ≤ τ(cᵢ). If the times are equal for all i ∈ J, its last point lies below every
  wᵢ, hence below v_J ∈ D, a contradiction. So the J-meet row enters strictly earlier, at a column *above* the faces
  only if that column is comparable with them. That is the staggered comparison of §34.3z⁸⁵ and §34.3z⁹⁰ again. The
  face system adds `face_closure` as a clean statement of what one column can do, but not the asynchronous step.
- **Baseline (§5).** `baseline_meet_cone` keeps the pⱼ-contracting baseline column's meets with sink-cone columns in D.
  A face column below u₀ meets the baseline in D, so the baseline cannot be pulled into a face. This is the same
  exclusion as `orbit_avoids_cone`.
- **Secondary strategy (mixed h/g words).** Not attempted. Every comparison between words of equal length is
  `iterate_le` row monotonicity, and between lengths only the lag (L) of §34.3z⁸⁷ is available. The word grid has no
  new comparison to offer without one of them.

**Deliverable** (the brief's option 2): a minimized SAT counterprofile, the Hamiltonian 3-cycle with length-two orbits
and the J-meet row outside D at ⊤. It refutes the face-compression lemma. The weakest missing condition is an entry of
the J-meet row above an outside face.

**Controls.** Everything uses only cross cells, so it survives `normalizeLow`. Cycles whose edges live at different
columns were not treated as common-column cycles.

## 34.3z⁹² ChatGPT brief "set-valued provenance and dual-face lifting": exact labels proved, dual faces add nothing derived

**Audit.** HEAD a882257 (§34.3z⁹¹), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `FaceLabels.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). The new experiment is `experiments/faces/dualface.py`.

**Compiled.**
- **`label_closed_eq` (Target A, any number of generators).** At a minimally dependent J, every nonempty
  label-closed S ⊆ J (each eₐ, a ∈ S, below some p_k with k ∈ S) equals J. The proof is `no_upward_transfer_cycle`
  at v_S, using `anchor_sandwich` for k ≠ a. This is the entire content of the brief's dichotomy. Escape means "not
  every vertex has an internal label"; otherwise the internal-label graph has no proper closed subset. The general
  "exactly one internal label, one cycle" refinement is the brief's combinatorial argument on top of it. It is
  formalized for three generators:
- **`labels_core`, `hamiltonian_full_triple` (three generators, J = univ).** If all three pairwise faces are outside D,
  then eᵢ ≤ pⱼ ↔ j = σ(i), where σ is one of the two 3-cycles. Exact labels, no extra labels (`decide` over all label
  triples).
  - **For three generators with J = univ the escape branch is empty:** eᵢ ∈ D has a label, and every label is in J.
  - The escape branch therefore arises only for |J| = 2, with a label on the third generator. That is the §34.3z⁹¹
    "escaping label" profile, which is SAT.
  - The minimality hypothesis v_J ∈ D is not even used; it follows from `lastpoints_no_common_lower`.
- **`boundary_labels`:** on the codimension-one face yᵢ, F(m_J, yᵢ) ≤ p_{σ(t)} for every t ≠ i, and not below all of
  J (no witness). This is the brief's exact boundary-label theorem.
- **`hybrid_entry`:** if F(m_J, q) ≤ p_k at an outside q, then q ⊓ v_{J∖{σ⁻¹(k)}} ∈ D. It is `face_closure` with
  K = {k}, as noted in the audit. So it adds no closure beyond §34.3z⁹¹.
- **`dual_face_bounds`:** for u = yᵢ ⊔ yⱼ, F(m_J, u) ≥ F(m_J, yᵢ) ⊔ F(m_J, yⱼ), so it is below neither p_{σ(i)} nor
  p_{σ(j)}. The brief's §4 claims are correct.

**What the dual layer can and cannot add.** The brief's z = ⋀ u_ij lies in D only under the extra hypothesis that
every F(m_J, u_ij) is in D with its remaining label. (Corrected in §34.3z⁹³: this hypothesis always holds, since
u_ij ≤ w_k gives F(m_J, u_ij) ≤ e_k.) Also z ≥ v_J holds trivially
(u_ij ≥ yᵢ ≥ v_J). Whether z > v_J is forced was tested.

**Full-orbit experiment (z3, the brief's recommended first experiment).** The model has:
- the Hamiltonian 3-cycle with exact labels;
- anchor orbits of length 2;
- the full m_J-orbit q₀ = ⊤, q₁, … entering at step N;
- the hybrid columns q_r ⊓ yᵢ, the dual faces u_ij and their meet z;
- all named elements as columns, all cross cells, and no-witness conditional on the column being outside D.

| anchor lengths | m_J entry step N | dual values F(m_J, u_ij) required in D | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| (2,2,2) | 2 | no | **SAT**: all three hybrids in D; z ∈ D with z = v_J; m_J-entry labels {1, 2} |
| (2,2,2) | 2 | yes | **SAT**, same features (z = v_J) |
| (2,2,2) | 1 | no | UNSAT (control: F(m_J, ⊤) ∈ D, which is `top_entry_face_closure` / §34.3z⁹¹) |
| (2,2,2) | 3 | no | not completed (exceeded the 7000 s budget) |
| (1,2,2) | 2 | no | not run (budget) |

- Each finished case took over an hour. The UNSAT control is explained by the Lean theorem
  `top_entry_face_closure`, a sharper certificate than a solver core.
- **z > v_J is not forced.** In both SAT models z = v_J. The "join-then-meet" pass returns to the dependent meet itself,
  so it is not a new invariant.

**Answer.**
- **Deliverables 1 and 2 are met:** the set-valued dichotomy (general core, plus the exact three-generator form) and
  the exact codimension-one boundary labels.
- **Deliverable 4:** a SAT model for the full-orbit profile with N = 2. In it all hybrids are in D, z = v_J (so
  z > v_J is not forced), and the m_J-entry carries labels {1, 2}.
- The dual-face construction adds no constraint beyond `faces.py` (§34.3z⁹³ shows why: the dual layer collapses onto the
  faces), so per the brief it is abandoned. The
  hybrid entries in D are exactly what `hybrid_entry` predicts. They are D-valued columns below the m_J-orbit's last
  point and a face, and they start no descent: a D-column carries only D × D cells (§34.3z⁸⁰).
- **The missing condition is unchanged.** The m_J-orbit must enter at a column *above* a codimension-one face yᵢ,
  with label σ(i). `hybrid_entry` shows the m_J-orbit's last point q and the face y_{σ⁻¹(k)} meet in D, so q ≱ that
  face. The comparison would have to be with a face whose missing label is the entry label, which is the staggered
  synchronization again.

**Controls.** All new lemmas use only cross cells (they survive `normalizeLow`). Hybrid columns in D were given no
no-witness constraints. ACC was not used.

## 34.3z⁹³ ChatGPT brief "second-order face incidence": the second-order frame collapses onto the first (vᵢ = yᵢ)

**Audit.** HEAD c0fb9d8 (§34.3z⁹²), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `DualVertices.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). The new experiment is `experiments/faces/face_incidence.py`.

**Brief claims checked.**
- **Exact edge labels:** if F(m, u_ij) ∈ D, its label is exactly {σ(k)}. This holds because F(m, u_ij) ≥ F(m, yᵢ) ⊔ F(m, yⱼ),
  so its labels lie in (J∖{σ(i)}) ∩ (J∖{σ(j)}).
- **Lattice identities:** yᵢ ≤ vᵢ, vᵢ ∨ vⱼ = u_ij and ⋀ vᵢ = z all hold. **`dual_vertex_geometry`**, with no F.
- **Dual-vertex signature:** under "all edge outputs in D", F(m, vᵢ) has exactly the labels J∖{σ(i)}.
  **`dual_vertex_label`** gives the lower bound; no witness at vᵢ ∉ D excludes σ(i).
- **Branch A has the inclusion backwards.** h(r_ij) ≤ h(vᵢ), h(vⱼ) makes the labels of h(r_ij) a *superset* of the
  union (J∖{σ(i)}) ∪ (J∖{σ(j)}) = J, not a subset of the intersection. So with all edge outputs in D, r_ij ∉ D gives
  h(r_ij) ≤ m, a witness. **`lower_vertex_in_D`:** every lower vertex lies in D, so Branch A is empty.

**The decisive fact: `second_order_collapse` (lattice only).** With yᵢ = ⋀_{j≠i} wⱼ, write a, b, c for the last points
and y_a = b ⊓ c, etc.
- Each edge lies below the opposite last point: y_a ⊔ y_b ≤ c.
- Hence F(m, u_ij) ≤ F(c_k, w_k) = e_k ≤ p_{σ(k)}. **All edge outputs are in D, always.** The brief's case
  "some edge output outside D" is impossible. This corrects §34.3z⁹², which called "dual values in D" an underived
  hypothesis; it explains why both §34.3z⁹² variants behaved alike.
- The dual vertex equals the face: vᵢ = u_ij ⊓ u_ik ≤ w_k ⊓ wⱼ = yᵢ, so **vᵢ = yᵢ in every lattice.** Then
  r_ij = yᵢ ⊓ yⱼ = v_J and z = v_J.
- The "self-reproducing triangle" is literally the original triangle. The brief's tests 5–7 are decided: v = y always,
  no strict expansion, and z = v_J, as observed in §34.3z⁹².

**Structural conclusion (subsumed by the collapse).**
- By `dual_vertex_geometry`, the edges of the second triangle are the edges of the first: vᵢ ∨ vⱼ = u_ij.
- Under "all edges in D", the second triangle (v₁, v₂, v₃) has outside vertices, pairwise meets r_ij in D, and the same
  exact signatures J∖{σ(i)} for the full-meet row. This is the shape of the original triangle (y₁, y₂, y₃) with
  yᵢ ⊓ yⱼ = v_J ∈ D. It carries *less* information, since the anchor-row bounds F(cₜ, ·) ≤ eₜ do not transfer upward
  from yᵢ to vᵢ ≥ yᵢ.
- So the face operation y ↦ u ↦ v is idempotent on edges, and the "self-reproducing triangle" closes after one step
  with no new constraint. This answers the brief's question: the triangle reproduces itself, and the lower
  intersections do not break the cyclic symmetry; they are forced into D.

**z3 (`face_incidence.py`).** Hamiltonian last points with exact labels, faces, and every second-order column named
and used as a column. Run order:
- (1) all edge outputs in D: **SAT**, with every r_ij in D, vᵢ = yᵢ and z ≤ v_J;
- (2) some edge output outside D: **UNSAT**. Its explanation is the first clause of `second_order_collapse`: u_ij ≤ w_k
  and m ≤ c_k.

The remaining cases (3–7) were stopped after the collapse was proved, since they are decided by it: 3 and 5 are SAT
as (1), and 4, 6 and 7 are impossible. Each solver case took over ten minutes.

**Deliverable.** This is option 1: a complete Lean classification. The second-order frame collapses onto the
first-order faces (`second_order_collapse`), with the edges automatically labelled and every lower vertex equal to
v_J ∈ D. It is also option 4: the all-D lower branch is the only branch, and it is the original configuration. The
pairwise-intersection cells carry no new information, so no new contradiction is possible from them.

**Controls.** All new lemmas use only cross cells; the geometric identities use no F. No D × D cell is constrained (no
witness is conditional on outside columns). z > v_J was not assumed, and was tested.

## 34.3z⁹⁴ ChatGPT brief "response re-entry and nested frontiers": re-entry is valid, the progress lemma fails at the first step

**Audit.** HEAD f161686 (§34.3z⁹³), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `ResponseFrontier.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). New experiments are `experiments/response/reentry.py` and `experiments/certgrammar/classify.py`.

**Compiled (the brief's targets 1–5).**
- **`response_reentry`:** at a frontier pair (a = y ⊓ z ∈ D, b = x ⊔ z ∉ D, z = F(x, y)), case A (z ∈ D) gives the
  comparable cross pair (z, b), and case B (z ∉ D) gives (a, z).
- **`response_interval_nested`:** the new *pair* interval ([z, b] resp. [a, z]) lies in the response interval [a, b].
- **`response_interval_strict`:** strict containment fails exactly when z ≤ y (case A) or x ≤ z (case B).
- **`response_degenerate_join`, `response_degenerate_meet`:** in degenerate A, x and z are incomparable D-elements
  with x ⊔ z ∉ D. In degenerate B, y and z are incomparable outside elements with y ⊓ z ∈ D.
- **`principal_join_closed`:** for D = ↓p, case A cannot occur at all, because ↓p is join-closed. z3 confirms this
  (k = 1, case A: UNSAT).

**The gap in the progress lemma.** The brief's nesting is between the new *pair* interval and the old response
interval. Its conjecture is about *response* intervals [y′ ⊓ F(x′, y′), x′ ⊔ F(x′, y′)]. Nothing relates the next
response interval to [a, b]:
- F(x′, y′) is a fresh value;
- no witness gives only F(x′, y′) ≰ x′ and y′ ≰ F(x′, y′);
- terminalization moves the row up and the column down freely.

**z3 (`reentry.py`).** The model is a chain of frontier pairs linked by re-entry, with a full cross table on all named
elements, monotone and witness-free, and nothing on D × D.
- **(N0) The next response interval is not nested, even with no terminalization:** SAT for k = 1 (case B) and for
  k = 2 (cases A and B). With terminalization it is also SAT. The progress lemma fails at its first step, before any
  degeneracy.
- **(P) Persistent degenerate re-entry:**
  - SAT for every pattern tested: k = 1, B, BB, BBB; k = 2, A, B, AB, BA, AAA, BBB, ABA; k = 3, A, B, BBB, ABA;
  - persistence is not excluded by the chain conditions: in degenerate B the columns yₜ, zₜ are incomparable, so
    columns do not descend, and the rows aₜ = yₜ ⊓ zₜ are unrelated across steps;
  - **minimal persistent pattern:** k = 1, two degenerate case-B steps.
- **One-generator calibration failed.** For D = ↓p, Cut follows from the unclipped orbit of F(p, ·) from ⊤. Re-entry
  replaces the row by a = y ⊓ z, which may be smaller than p, and the k = 1 contradiction is lost. As in §34.3z⁸⁴, a
  process that changes the row away from a generator does not contain the one-generator proof.

**Secondary strategy: certificate grammar (`classify.py`).** The verified `tv4` (17 terms) and `tv5` (86 terms) are
classified as follows:

| height | terms | bottom descent F(⊥, cₖ) | row iterates on the descent | F(⊥, ·) on non-descent columns | mixed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 17 | 4 | 7 | 2 | 2 |
| 5 | 86 | 5 | 26 | 15 | 38 |

- **Every height-4 term is a height-5 term (17 of 17)**: the certificates are nested.
- Height 4 uses 3 distinct F-values as rows and depth 4; height 5 uses 16 rows and depth 7.
- The rows reused most are the descent values c₂, c₃, c₄ and the first mixed term.
- The brief's "fresh descent restarted at an escaped value" is the class F(⊥, c) with c not on the initial descent:
  2 terms at height 4, 15 at height 5.
- This is a first data point for a recursive grammar (nested term sets, with row reuse concentrated on the initial
  descent). No grammar or termination measure is proposed yet. The H = 6 DRAT route was not run in this round.

**Answer to the central question.** Using an F-value as a new row or column does create new inputs. But the
response-interval measure is not monotone under it, and degenerate re-entry persists in partial models even for one
generator. The value that must be retained is the generator-row orbit itself, which re-entry discards when it moves
the row to y ⊓ z.

**Controls.** All new lemmas use only cross cells; no D × D constraint was imposed; ACC was used only where
`frontier_pair` uses it.

## 34.3z⁹⁵ ChatGPT brief "branch-complete frontiers and certificate extraction": branching has no local transfer content; T₅ ⊆ Cl³(T₄)

**Audit.** HEAD ae68655 (§34.3z⁹⁴), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `BranchFrontier.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). New experiments are `experiments/response/branching.py` and `experiments/certgrammar/dag.py`.

### Part A — branch-complete frontier induction

**Compiled.**
- **`frontier_split_A`:** z ∈ D gives the two cross pairs (x, b) and (z, b), with x ∥ z and x ⊔ z = b ∉ D.
- **`frontier_split_B`:** z ∉ D gives (a, y) and (a, z), with y ∥ z and y ⊓ z = a ∈ D.
- **`branch_children_cross`:** each child has its row below its column.
- **`branch_provenance`:** one child keeps the parent row (A) or the parent column (B), and the other child uses the
  response z.

**Calibration of the branch-alignment conjecture.**
- For one generator, the anchor c₁ = ⨆ ∅ = ⊥ and no anchor transfer exists. The conjecture then says the tree has no
  infinite branch, which is Cut for ↓p restricted to the tree.
- For two generators (c₁ = c₂ = m), a transfer is just F(m, u) ∈ D.
- The conjecture is therefore at least as strong as Cut on the tree. An infinite branch cannot be encoded in a finite
  model.
- The decidable question is whether finite branching forces any transfer at all. The proof of a branch-alignment lemma
  would have to manufacture one.

**z3 (`branching.py`).**
- *Model:* a binary tree of frontier pairs to depth 2. Every child is split by the case rules and re-terminalized
  (row ≥, column ≤). There are generators, pairwise meets and anchors; anchor and generator rows at every named
  outside column; each pair's own cell; monotone, witness-free, nothing on D × D.
- *Constraint:* **no anchor transfer at any named outside column.**

| generators | depth 1 | depth 2 |
|---|---|---|
| k = 1 (case B only; case A is impossible, ↓p is join-closed) | SAT | SAT |
| k = 2 (patterns AAA, BBB, AAB, BAB) | SAT, all | SAT, all |
| k = 3 strict crossing (patterns AAA, BBB, AAB, BAB) | SAT, all | not completed (over 20 min on its first case) |

- So depth-2 branch-complete trees exist with no transfer anywhere. The source-indexed transfers that `ko_chain_finite`
  needs are not produced by the branching itself. Any alignment would have to come from global information, i.e. from
  infinitely deep branches, which is the open problem.
- In the brief's list of failure causes, this is the first and the second. Sibling columns are incomparable (b vs y in
  A, z vs y in B, `frontier_split_*`), and the generator-row relation is lost in the child that uses the response
  (`branch_provenance`).

### Part B — certificate extraction

**DAG reconstruction (`dag.py`, on the verified `tv4`/`tv5`).**

| height | F-terms | rows used | columns used | grid filled | used both as row and column |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 15 | 4 | 6 | 15 of 24 (62%) | 3 |
| 5 | 84 | 17 | 21 | 84 of 357 (24%) | 12 |

**A candidate expansion rule, with evidence.** Let Cl(S) = S ∪ {F(r, c) : r, c ∈ S}.
- **T₅ ⊆ Cl³(T₄)** exactly: the generation levels have sizes 17 → 60 → 85 → 86.
- 58 of the 69 new height-5 terms already lie in the one-step pool F(T₄, T₄), which has 289 terms.
- The candidate recursion is "T_{H+1} ⊆ Cl³(T_H)". It explains T₄ ⊆ T₅ and the new mixed and escape terms (they are
  level 1–3 products).
- It is an observation on one step (H = 4 → 5), not a theorem. No termination measure is proposed; the multiset
  measure of the brief's B4 needs state transitions that the certificates do not exhibit, since their terms are not
  generated by a monotone process.

**H = 6.** The candidate pool Cl(T₅) has 86² = 7396 terms, and Cl³ is far larger. The earlier H = 6 searches at 478–701
terms ran for hours inconclusively, so a Cl(T₅)-seeded run is a job for `experiments/heavy/` and was not attempted here.
No H = 6 DRAT certificate exists.

**Limitation (the brief's B6) acknowledged.** Uniform finite-height certificates would not give the chain-finite case,
which has no uniform height bound.

### Answer
- **Part A:** a minimized counterprofile to the local content of branch alignment. It is the depth-1 tree for k = 2
  (cases AAA or BBB) with no anchor transfer at any named column. The failure is incomparable sibling columns plus loss
  of the generator row in the response child.
- **Part B:** a data-backed candidate expansion rule, T₅ ⊆ Cl³(T₄), without a termination proof.
- Neither yields a new invariant.

## 34.3z⁹⁶ ChatGPT brief "global frontier envelopes and sparse certificate growth": the squeeze needs no types; certificates grow by promoting outputs

**Audit.** HEAD b8e7888 (§34.3z⁹⁵), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `GlobalEnvelope.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). New experiments are `experiments/response/envelope_escape.py` and `experiments/certgrammar/feedback.py`.

### Part A — global envelope

**Compiled (the first requested deliverable).**
- **`envelope`** (complete lattice, any nonempty index type): d = ⨅ (xᵥ ⊓ aᵥ) ∈ D, u = ⨆ (yᵥ ⊔ bᵥ) ∉ D, and
  d ≤ xᵥ, yᵥ, zᵥ ≤ u. Only xᵥ ∈ D and yᵥ ∉ D are needed.
- **`mixed_squeeze`:** with L = d ⊔ ⨆_{v∈A} F(d, yᵥ) and U = u ⊓ ⨅_{w∈B} F(x_w, u), we get d < L ≤ U < u. L ≤ U uses
  d ≤ a_w ≤ z_w ≤ F(x_w, u), a step the brief omitted.
- **`squeeze_cross_pair`:** one of (L, U), (d, L), (U, u) is a cross pair, strictly below (d, u) in the product order
  (row up, column down).

**The decisive observation: the types are irrelevant.** Lean flagged the brief's hypotheses as unused, and the theorem
is stated without them:
- the type conditions (z ∈ D on A, z ∉ D on B);
- even the frontier property.

The squeeze holds for any nonempty index sets A and B of any family of cross pairs. The types only make F(d, yᵥ) ∈ D
and F(x_w, u) ∉ D, which the strict inequalities do not use. So the "mixed-type squeeze" is the frontier step
`cross_frontier_step` performed at the envelope pair (d, u), and iterating it is `frontier_pair` again (§34.3z⁸⁴).

**Iteration (the brief's alternative A).** z3 (`envelope_escape.py`): one cross pair, its envelope, L and U, the
extracted pair (each of the three cases), and that pair's next response interval.
- "The next response interval escapes [d, u]" is **SAT in all three cases, for k = 1 and k = 2**.
- Cumulative envelopes therefore only move outward: dₙ descends and uₙ ascends, and DCC/ACC stop them. At the
  stabilized envelope the squeeze still produces a strictly inner pair, and frontier steps from it are again just
  frontier steps.
- As the brief warned, stabilization yields no contradiction.

**Monochromatic subtrees (alternative B).**
- For one generator every frontier pair is type B (type A needs D not join-closed, `principal_join_closed`). So the
  mixed squeeze could never be the one-generator engine.
- A type-B tree has no fixed row: the children (a, y), (a, z) change the row to a = y ⊓ z. The one-generator proof's
  fixed row F(p, ·) is not present.
- A type-A tree likewise changes the column to b = x ⊔ z. No existing orbit theorem (`anchor_hull`, `anchor_sandwich`,
  `cone_hull`) applies to either without a fixed generator row, and `orbit_avoids_cone` rules out recovering one on a
  sink cone.

**Answer to the central question.** Global completeness does produce a jointly constrained cross pair (the squeeze),
but the constraint is the generic frontier step at the envelope. It does not use the branching structure, and it
inherits the frontier induction's failure to contain the one-generator proof.

### Part B — sparse certificate growth (`feedback.py`)

**Feedback vertices (terms used as both row and column).**
- There are 3 at H = 4 and 12 at H = 5, and all 3 from H = 4 remain feedback vertices.
- Six of the new feedback vertices (terms 8, 9, 10, 14, 15, 21) are H = 4 *outputs*, never inputs at H = 4, that
  become inputs in both coordinates at H = 5.
- Three are new depth-5 terms (23, 38, 65).
- The term DAG is acyclic (subterm order), so its strongly connected components are singletons.

**Provenance of the 69 new H = 5 terms** (row, column), where "old" means the term was already used in that role at
H = 4, "T4" means an H = 4 term not previously in that role, and "new" means an H = 5 term:

| (row, column) | count |
|---|---|
| (T4 output promoted to row, old column) | 20 |
| (old row, T4 output promoted to column) | 20 |
| (new row, old column) | 10 |
| (old row, new column) | 8 |
| (new row, T4 output column) | 6 |
| (T4 output row, new column) | 2 |
| (old row, old column) | 2 |
| (T4 output row, T4 output column) | 1 |

**What this says.**
- Growth is not grid filling. Only 2 of the 69 new terms are cells of the H = 4 row × column grid.
- Growth **promotes earlier outputs to inputs in one coordinate while keeping the other coordinate in an established
  role**: 60 of 69 new terms have at least one coordinate in its H = 4 role.
- That is the data-derived role-aware rule: Cl_role(R, C) = {F(r, c) : r ∈ R, c ∈ C ∪ T} ∪ {F(r, c) : r ∈ R ∪ T, c ∈ C}.
- The rule explains why all H = 4 terms survive, why most new terms are F(T₄, T₄) products (58 of 69), and why only 84
  of 357 grid cells appear.

**H = 6.**
- The naive grid pool rows(T₅) × cols(T₅) ∪ T₅ has 359 terms, but the grid rule predicted only 2 of 69 terms at the
  previous step, so it is the wrong pool.
- The promotion rule gives a pool of about |R₅|·|T₅| + |T₅|·|C₅| ≈ 3300 terms. That is a job for `experiments/heavy/`
  and was not run.
- No H = 6 DRAT certificate exists, and no termination measure is proposed. Per the brief's §11, even a uniform
  finite-height result would not give the chain-finite case.

### Report
- **Proved:** the envelope and squeeze theorems, in a stronger form (no type hypotheses).
- **Iteration:** a counterprofile to nesting exists (escape SAT for k = 1, 2).
- **Monochromatic reduction:** none; there is no fixed row or column.
- **Part B:** a data-derived promotion rule (60 of 69 new terms) without verification at H = 6.

## 34.3z⁹⁷ ChatGPT brief "finite boundary support and provenance-preserving certificates": supports are finite and unconstrained; role promotion misses 12 essential terms

**Audit.** HEAD 24d6e66 (§34.3z⁹⁶), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `FiniteSupport.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). New experiments are `experiments/response/support.py` (with named envelope elements added to `branching.py`)
and `experiments/certgrammar/role_pool.py`.

### Part I — finite support

**Compiled (the primary deliverable).**
- **`iSup_finite_support` (ACC), `iInf_finite_support` (DCC):** every indexed supremum or infimum is attained on a
  finite index set. A maximal finite partial join exists by ACC, and adding any further index cannot raise it. The
  proofs are self-contained and independent of F and D.
- **`finite_envelope_support`:** d and u have separate finite supports.
- **`finite_squeeze_support`:** so do the squeeze values ⨆_A F(d, yᵥ) and ⨅_B F(x_w, u).
- **`support_between`:** any index set between a support and the whole family preserves the value.
- **`prefixClosure`, `subset_prefixClosure`, `prefixClosure_closed`:** for tree nodes as binary words, the prefix
  closure of a finite support is finite and prefix-closed, and preserves the value by `support_between`.

**Minimal-support analysis (z3, `support.py`).**
- *Model:* depth-1 frontier trees (root plus two re-terminalized children), with lᵥ = xᵥ ⊓ aᵥ and uᵥ = yᵥ ⊔ bᵥ named
  as glb and lub elements. Four support shapes were tested:
  - d and u both supported by the root alone;
  - both by one child;
  - d by one child and u by the other (divergence at the root);
  - no single node supports d (two needed).
- **Every shape is SAT**, for k = 1 (BBB) and k = 2 (BBB, AAA, ABB).
- So minimal supports carry no structural constraint: they can be a single leaf (one-sided), mixed, or divergent at the
  root. Every strengthening in the brief's §5 list is refuted at depth 1. The minimal counterprofile is the
  root-only support, k = 1.
- This is expected: d and u are lattice combinations of the nodes' own data, and essentiality of a leaf is a lattice
  fact that says nothing about F.
- *A first encoding error, caught before reporting.* A first encoding compared lᵥ ≤ l_w through the stronger
  coordinatewise condition xᵥ ≤ x_w ∧ aᵥ ≤ a_w and got UNSAT everywhere. That was an artifact; the glb/lub encoding is
  the faithful one.

### Part II — role-promotion grammar (`role_pool.py`)

The exact operator is P(T; R, C) = {F(r, c) : r ∈ R, c ∈ C ∪ T} ∪ {F(r, c) : r ∈ R ∪ T, c ∈ C}.

- **One round:** |P(T₄)| = 146, and it explains **42** of the 69 new H = 5 terms.
- **Correction to §34.3z⁹⁶.** The "60/69" figure there counted terms with *some* coordinate in an established role,
  where the other coordinate may itself be a new term. It is a role statistic, not the operator's coverage.
- **Iterated** promotion (H = 4 roles fixed, T growing by certificate terms) explains **57** of 69 in 2 rounds.
- **The 12 exceptions** are double promotions, in which neither coordinate is in its H = 4 role: terms 56, 65, 66, 70,
  73, 74, 76, 77, 82, 83, 84, 85. Their promoted coordinates are mostly the new feedback terms 23, 38, 43 and 65, so
  some exceptions depend on others (70 on 56; 73, 83, 84 and 85 on 65).

**Seeded ground tests** (`cut_ground.Ground` at H = 5 with a fixed term set and no expansion):

| term set | terms | result |
|---|---|---|
| T₅ (control) | 86 | UNSAT, 0.6 s |
| T₄ | 17 | SAT |
| T₄ ∪ P(T₄) (one promotion round) | 148 | **SAT** |
| T₄ ∪ one-round-explained certificate terms | 59 | SAT |
| T₅ minus the 12 exceptions | 74 | **SAT** |

- **The 12 double-promotion terms are essential**, and one round of role promotion does not refute height 5.
- The role-aware grammar is therefore refuted as a generator of the H = 5 certificate. The certificate needs
  double promotions, i.e. the full closure F(T, T) restricted in some other way, and the 58/69 figure in F(T₄, T₄) is
  the operative statistic.
- **H = 6:** the role pool P(T₅) ∪ T₅ has 2913 terms. It is written to `certgrammar/p5_pool.pkl` in `cut_ground`'s
  checkpoint format, with instructions in `experiments/heavy/README.md`. It was not run (memory). By the H = 5 analogy it
  is expected to need further expansion.

**Termination (the brief's §10).** No measure is proposed. The H = 5 data show that the certificate's term order is not
generated by a monotone role process, so an ordinal or multiset measure on role obligations has no observed
transitions to decrease along.

### Report
- **Proved:** finite support of suprema and infima, the envelope and squeeze supports, and prefix closure.
- **Minimal supports:** counterprofiles to every proposed strengthening (all support shapes SAT).
- **Role-promotion grammar:** refuted at H = 5 by the seeded ground test. The exact explained and exceptional terms are
  listed. The H = 6 pool is prepared but not run.

## 34.3z⁹⁸ ChatGPT brief "dual finite-support boundary and joint activation": the infimum lemma is the dual one-generator case, and the grid is SAT

**Audit.** HEAD 1e57d5b (§34.3z⁹⁷), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `BoundaryGrid.lean` (standard axioms, full
build). New experiments are `experiments/finite_support/boundary_grid.py` and `experiments/certgrammar/activation.py`.

### Part I — dual finite-support boundary

**Compiled.**
- **`no_least_outside` (ACC):** a witness-free proper cut D ∋ ⊥ has **no least outside element**.
  - If t were one, F(·, t) would map D into D: an outside value would be ≥ t, a witness y ≤ F(x, y).
  - Its ascending orbit from ⊥ would then stabilize at x = F(x, t) ∈ D, a witness F(x, y) ≤ x.
  - This is the order dual of the one-generator case: Dᶜ = ↑t is co-principal.
- **`R_nowit`:** R(F) is a witness-free cut, via `le_a`, `b_le` and the closure rules.
- **`inf_compl_R_mem` (ACC):** ⋀ (L ∖ R) ∈ R. This is the brief's target lemma. Its proof needs neither normalization
  nor the b-preimage rule, and no hypothesis R ≠ L (if R = L, ⋀ ∅ = ⊤ ∈ R).
- **`minimal_join_support`, `minimal_meet_support`:**
  - a finite P ⊆ R that is minimal with ⋁P ∉ R, whose nonempty proper subfaces are in R;
  - a finite Q ⊆ Rᶜ that is minimal with ⋀Q ∈ R, whose proper subfaces are outside R.

  Both families have at least two members, and the members of each are pairwise incomparable: p ≤ p′ in P would put
  ⋁P in R. These come from the existing `sSup_finite_support`, `sInf_finite_support` and `sSup_R_mem_iff`.

**The grid (z3, `boundary_grid.py`).**
- *Model:* atoms pᵢ, qⱼ; all face joins p_A (lub) and face meets q_B (glb); the face membership pattern; first-layer
  cells z_{A,B} = F(p_A, q_B) on proper faces; the normalized boundary F(p_P, q_B) = ⊤ and F(p_A, q_Q) = ⊥; all
  monotone; no witness on cross cells.
- **SAT** for (|P|, |Q|) = (2, 2), (2, 3), (3, 2), (3, 3).
- **Second layer** (every first-layer value fed back as a row and as a column): **SAT** for (2, 2), (2, 3), (3, 2).
- **Why it carries little.**
  - The rows p_A are pairwise incomparable at the singleton level (minimality), and so are the columns q_B. So
    monotonicity relates first-layer cells only through the face inclusions.
  - The normalized boundary values sit off the cross block (⊤ on Rᶜ × L, ⊥ on R × R). By §34.3z⁸⁰ they constrain
    nothing.
  - The second layer is the frontier and branching step of §§34.3z⁸⁴–⁹⁵.
- **The missing constraint:**
  - A dual support pair (P, Q) exists for every cut that is neither principal nor co-principal, including the
    two-generator cuts, where Cut holds by `cut_two`.
  - So any contradiction must use global information beyond the grid: an orbit like the sandwich's.
  - The grid has the same status as every class-L configuration of §34.3z⁸⁶.

### Part II — joint activation in the H = 5 certificate (`activation.py`)

**Individual necessity.** Each exception was removed, together with every T₅ term containing it (deletion must be
upward closed), and the seeded H = 5 ground test was rerun:
- **11 of the 12 are individually necessary**: removing any of t56, t65, t70, t73, t74, t76, t77, t82, t83, t84, t85
  makes the profile SAT.
- t66 alone can be removed.
- Deletion-minimal sufficient subset: {56, 65, 70, 73, 74, 76, 77, 82, 83, 84, 85}.
- This strengthens §34.3z⁹⁷'s collective necessity to individual necessity for 11 terms.
- **Activation hypergraph:** the 11 necessary double promotions hang off four promoted inputs. t23 generates 56 and 82,
  with 70 on 56; t38 generates 74 and 76; t43 generates 77; t65 is itself necessary and generates 73, 83, 84 and 85.
  So the minimal activation set is the four feedback terms {23, 38, 43, 65}, each used in a role it did not have at
  H = 4, plus their eleven necessary products.
- **The verified Lean replay** (`CutHeight5.lean`) references 85 of the 86 terms, including all 12 exceptions. The
  relevant certificate for necessity is therefore the seeded ground profile, not the replay's clause set.
- **H = 6:** no sparse pool was derived that would include the analogous double promotions without approaching the full
  F(T₅, T₅) square, and the 2913-term role pool of §34.3z⁹⁷ omits them by construction. Nothing new was run.

### Answer to the central question

The join boundary of R and the meet boundary of its complement do assemble into one finite configuration with actual
F-values. That configuration is consistent, also with a second layer of responses, so it is not an obstruction. The
binding structure would have to come from outside the grid. The one new theorem, `no_least_outside`, is the dual
one-generator case. It explains why Q exists, and gives no leverage beyond it.

## 34.3z⁹⁹ ChatGPT brief "reciprocal support orbits and the first-failure matrix": the dichotomy is generic and every escape pattern is SAT

**Audit.** HEAD 4ecf1ae (§34.3z⁹⁸), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `ReciprocalOrbits.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). The new experiment is `experiments/finite_support/reciprocal_orbits.py`.

**Compiled (milestones 1–10).** D is any witness-free down-set and F is monotone.
- **`column_orbit_mono`, `column_orbit_exits` (ACC):** c₀ = ⊥, cₙ₊₁ = F(cₙ, q) ascends and leaves D. Otherwise it would
  stabilize at c = F(c, q) ∈ D, a witness. This needs neither ⊥ ∈ D nor anything about q beyond q ∉ D.
- **`row_orbit_anti`, `row_orbit_enters` (DCC):** y₀ = ⊤, yₘ₊₁ = F(p, yₘ) descends and enters D. This is
  `FGTwo.orbit_end` in this notation; it does not need ⊤ ∉ D.
- **`interlacing`:** cₙ ≤ p and q ≤ yₘ give cₙ₊₁ ≤ yₘ₊₁, for independent n and m.
- **`first_failure_le`, `first_failure_dichotomy`:** at the first support failures, c_s ≤ y_t, so not both c_s ∉ D and
  y_t ∈ D. Equivalently s < N_B or t < M_A. This is the brief's principal target.
- **`threshold_mono_row`, `threshold_mono_col`:** a failure for a larger row (smaller column) is a failure for a smaller
  row (larger column). This gives the brief's s_{A,B} ≤ s_{A′,B} and t_{A,B} ≤ t_{A,B′}. N_B ≤ N_{B′} and M_A ≤ M_{A′}
  follow from `colOrbit`/`rowOrbit` monotonicity in q and p together with downward closure.
- **`petal_chain`:** in ↓p₁ ∪ ↓p₂, an ascending pair inside D cannot escape p₁ first and then p₂.
- **`staircase_row`, `staircase_col`, `dynamic_squeeze`:** the dynamic cells F(cₙ, yₘ) are monotone in n and antitone
  in m, and in the support region c_{n+1} ≤ F(cₙ, yₘ) ≤ y_{m+1}.

**The decisive observation.** None of these statements uses minimality of P or Q, or any support face. They hold for
**every** cross pair (p, q). The dichotomy is the single-cell interlacing of two monotone orbits, and the support
families only choose which pairs to look at.

**z3 (`reciprocal_orbits.py`, |P| = |Q| = 2).**
- *Model:* all orbit points are named, with a full cross table on every named (D-element, outside element) pair,
  monotone and witness-free.
- *Escape types per pair (i, j):* A-side means some column point in D is not below pᵢ (s < N); B-side means some row
  point outside D does not dominate qⱼ (t < M).
- *Encoding check:* forcing a pair with neither type is UNSAT, as `first_failure_dichotomy` says.

| D | orbit lengths (N₁, N₂; M₁, M₂) | free | all A | all B | alternating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| abstract down-set | (2, 2; 2, 2) | SAT | SAT | SAT | SAT |
| abstract down-set | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT | SAT | SAT | SAT |
| ↓p₁ ∪ ↓p₂ (P = generators) | (2, 2; 2, 2) | SAT | **UNSAT** | **UNSAT** | SAT |
| ↓p₁ ∪ ↓p₂ | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT | **UNSAT** | SAT | SAT |

- **All-A is impossible for two generators** at every length, by `petal_chain`: a column orbit escapes at most one
  generator. This is lattice structure of a two-petal cut, not dynamics.
- **All-B is impossible only when the two row orbits enter at the same time** (M = (2, 2)). All-B puts every column
  point below p₁ ⊓ p₂. The two generator rows then enter D at the same step with opposite labels (yⁱ₂ ≤ p₍₃₋ᵢ₎), and
  the meet row at a common column below both is bounded by p₁ ⊓ p₂, a witness. This is the equal-time synchronization
  of `net_two_equal` (§34.3z⁸⁵). Staggering the entry times (M = (3, 2)) makes all-B SAT again.
- **Alternating** A/B patterns are SAT in every case. Minimized SAT models are the 2 × 2 cases above.
- So the threshold surface has exactly the constraints of one-step interlacing, two-petal chain structure and
  equal-time synchronization. None of them crosses unequal entry times.

**Relation to the sandwich (the brief's §10).**
- For two generators the proper join faces of P are the generators themselves. So the row orbits yⁱ are the sandwich's
  g-orbits F(pᵢ, ·) from ⊤.
- The sandwich also needs the anchor orbit F(p₁ ⊓ p₂, ·) and the shifted comparison
  h^{k+1}(s) ≤ h^k(g s) ≤ h^k(s), which uses g(s) ≤ s.
- The reciprocal construction contains neither: p₁ ⊓ p₂ is a meet, not a join face, and the column orbits run from ⊥,
  not along g.
- So the generalized sandwich the brief asks for would need the anchor row, which takes it back to §§34.3z⁷⁷–⁸⁶.

**Answer.** Opposite-direction orbits do interlace (c_s ≤ y_t) and produce a monotone threshold surface. But the
interlacing is one monotonicity step per cell. Every escape pattern is consistent for an abstract cut and for a
two-generator cut, so they produce no global exchange structure the static grid cannot see.

**Part V (H = 5 activation roles).** The four sources split into the brief's three roles:
- a hub: t65, which is necessary itself and supports four products;
- a bridge: t23, which supports t56 and t82, with t56 supporting t70;
- earlier-promotion products: t38 and t43, each with one or two dependents.

There is nothing beyond §34.3z⁹⁸, and no H = 6 result.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁰ ChatGPT brief "global reciprocal aggregation": the common boundary is a tightened (⊥, ⊤) and adds no constraint

**Audit.** HEAD dbdb00f (§34.3z⁹⁹), clean, matches the brief. New Lean is in `GlobalReciprocal.lean` (standard axioms,
full build). `reciprocal_orbits.py` gains an `agg` mode.

**Compiled (milestones 1–10).** D is any witness-free down-set with ⊥ ∈ D and ⊤ ∉ D. Here Φ(y) = ⨆_{x∈D} F(x, y) and
Ψ(x) = ⨅_{y∉D} F(x, y).
- **`aggregate_row_mono`, `aggregate_column_mono`.**
- **`aggregate_row_preserves_complement` (ACC):** y ∉ D ⇒ Φ(y) ∉ D. It is `column_orbit_exits`: the exit value is a
  row response below Φ(y).
- **`aggregate_column_preserves_D` (DCC):** x ∈ D ⇒ Ψ(x) ∈ D, via `row_orbit_enters`.
- **`aggregate_row_fixedpoint`:** the Φ-iteration from ⊤ stabilizes (DCC) at s = Φ(s) ∉ D, so F(x, s) ≤ s for every
  x ∈ D.
- **`aggregate_column_fixedpoint`:** the Ψ-iteration from ⊥ stabilizes (ACC) at r = Ψ(r) ∈ D, so r ≤ F(r, y) for every
  y ∉ D.
- **`common_boundary_strict`:** r < F(r, s) < s.
- **`finite_row_response_support`, `finite_column_response_support`:** s = ⨆_{p∈P} F(p, s) and r = ⨅_{q∈Q} F(r, q)
  with P and Q finite.
- **`no_singleton_support`:** no single row gives F(p, s) = s, and no single column gives F(r, q) = r. So both supports
  have at least two members.
- **`rectangularization_dichotomy`:** either p ⊔ r ∈ D (and then F(p, s) ≤ F(p ⊔ r, s) ≤ s) or p ⊔ r ∉ D; dually,
  either q ⊓ s ∉ D (with r ≤ F(r, q ⊓ s) ≤ F(r, q)) or q ⊓ s ∈ D.

**Why it adds nothing.**
- **⊤ is already a common contracting column** for every row (F(x, ⊤) ≤ ⊤), and ⊥ a common expanding row (⊥ ≤ F(⊥, y)).
  So "s is common contracting for every anchor row" (the brief's §9) gives nothing ⊤ did not; the anchor orbits from s
  meet exactly the obstruction of the orbits from ⊤ (`lastpoints_no_common_lower`).
- The genuine content of (r, s) is **tightness**: s = ⋁_D F(·, s) and r = ⋀_{Dᶜ} F(r, ·).
- **The pair can be degenerate** (`degenerate_boundary`): if Φ(⊤) = ⊤ and Ψ(⊥) = ⊥, the iterations stop at once and
  (r, s) = (⊥, ⊤). Nothing excludes that.

**z3 (`reciprocal_orbits.py agg`).** The base is the two-generator profile of §34.3z⁹⁹, with every orbit point and cross
cell named.

| aggregate layer | (N; M) | free | all-B |
|---|---|---|---|
| degenerate: ⊤ = F(p₁, ⊤) ⊔ F(p₂, ⊤), ⊥ = F(⊥, q₁) ⊓ F(⊥, q₂) | (2, 2; 2, 2) | SAT | UNSAT (as without the layer) |
| degenerate | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT | SAT |
| named r ≠ ⊥, s ≠ ⊤ (see below) | (2, 2; 2, 2) | SAT | UNSAT (as without the layer) |
| named r ≠ ⊥, s ≠ ⊤ | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT | **SAT** |

The named layer imposes, on every named row and column:
- the support equations s = F(p₁, s) ⊔ F(p₂, s) ⊔ F(r, s) and r = F(r, q₁) ⊓ F(r, q₂) ⊓ F(r, s);
- the uniform inequalities F(x, s) ≤ s and r ≤ F(r, y).

**Answer.**
- The aggregate fixed-point pair exists and is strictly separated by its corner response.
- Its finite supports, with r ∈ P and s ∈ Q (by `rectangularization_dichotomy`, this needs no comparability), add **no
  constraint** to the staggered all-B profile: it stays SAT with a non-degenerate named pair.
- The equal-time UNSAT is the §34.3z⁸⁵ synchronization again, unchanged by the layer.
- The pair is the canonical tightening of the trivial box (⊥, ⊤), and the open step is the same staggered comparison.

## 34.3z¹⁰¹ ChatGPT brief "irredundant reciprocal supports and response exchange": row supports collapse onto the generators, Case A is the column lemma, and regular exchange is an orbit

**Audit (HEAD ad685db).**
- The brief's Part I summary of §34.3z¹⁰⁰ is accurate.
- So is its limitation: the named profile of `reciprocal_orbits.py agg` put r into P and s into Q, and imposed no
  irredundancy.
- The Part II and Part III claims are all correct, and all elementary. The input-join/output-meet consequences need
  only the support equation and no witness, not minimality and not the uniform inequalities.
- The exchange conjecture (Part III §7) is ill-posed as stated. P ↦ U does **not** preserve the support equation in
  general. Where it does (the *regular* case), it is an orbit step: the conjecture becomes `column_orbit_exits` /
  `row_orbit_enters` again.

**Lean (`SupportExchange.lean`; full build passes; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `minimal_finset_support` | an inclusion-minimal subset with a property below any given one (pure finset fact, kept separate) |
| `row_support_essential` / `column_support_essential` | in a minimal support, s ≰ ⋁_{P∖p} F(p′, s) and ⋀_{Q∖q} F(r, q′) ≰ r |
| `row_input_join_out` / `column_input_meet_in` | for **any** support: ⋁P ∉ D, ⋀Q ∈ D |
| `response_trichotomy`, `mixed_response_cross` | U ⊆ D, or V ⊆ Dᶜ, or ∃ u_p ∉ D, v_q ∈ D; then (v_q, u_p) is a cross pair (W1/W2 at F(v_q, u_p)) |
| `exchange_regular` | P ↦ U keeps s = ⋁ F(u, s) when p ≤ F(p, s) ∈ D. The iterates (F(·, s))ⁿ(p) form the column orbit along s, which leaves D |
| `exchange_regular_column` | dual: Q ↦ V keeps r = ⋀ F(r, v) when F(r, q) ≤ q and F(r, q) ∉ D |
| `regular_column_iterate_enters` | (DCC) under F(r, q) ≤ q the iterates (F r)ⁿ q reach D: iterated regular column exchange ends in Case C |
| **`generator_row_support`** | for D = ⋃_{g∈G} ↓g and s with the aggregate row equation: s = ⋁_{g∈G} F(g, s) |
| **`generator_response_other_petal`** | for antichain generators: F(g, s) ∈ D ⇒ F(g, s) ≤ g′ with g′ ≠ g, and g ≰ F(g, s) |
| **`generator_not_caseA`** | = `Crossing.column_lemma`: at s ∉ D some generator response F(pᵢ, s) is outside D |

**What the new rows say.**
- **Row supports collapse onto the generators.**
  - For a finitely generated cut, every row response F(x, s) with x ∈ D lies below a generator response F(gᵢ, s).
  - So {gᵢ} is a row support, and every minimal row support can be chosen among the generators.
  - Their responses uᵢ = F(pᵢ, s) are the first row-orbit steps gᵢ(s) from the common contracting column s.
  - The "second inside family" of Case A is therefore anchored data, not new data.
  - The column side has no such collapse: Dᶜ has no finite generators.
- **Case A is impossible on the generator support** (the old column lemma).
  - Minimized UNSAT core: rows p₁, p₂, p₁ ∧ p₂ and the single column s.
  - Proof: u₁ ≤ p₂ and u₂ ≤ p₁ (`generator_response_other_petal`), so F(p₁ ∧ p₂, s) ≤ u₁ ∧ u₂ ≤ p₁ ∧ p₂, a witness.
  - So for k = 2 the trichotomy on the canonical support is Case B or Case C.
- **Exchange termination.**
  - Row side: on the generator support the regular hypothesis p ≤ F(p, s) ∈ D never holds
    (`generator_response_other_petal`), and outside the regular case P ↦ U is not a support replacement at all.
  - Column side: regular exchange is the descending row orbit of F(r, ·) from each q; it reaches D after finitely
    many steps (`regular_column_iterate_enters`).
  - The strictly descending sequence of actual lattice elements that the brief asked for exists, and it is that
    orbit.
  - Repeated B-exchange therefore cannot go on forever. It ends in Case C, i.e. in one more cross pair (v, u),
    and nothing about that pair is new.

**z3 (`experiments/finite_support/support_exchange.py`).**
- **Setup.**
  - The base is the two-generator orbit profile of §34.3z⁹⁹, with every orbit point named.
  - r and s are named separately; they are not support members.
  - The uniform inequalities F(x, s) ≤ s and r ≤ F(r, y) hold at every named row and column, with r ≤ F(r, s) ≤ s.
  - The supports are true and essential: s = ⋁ uᵢ with uᵢ < s, and r = ⋀ v_j with r < v_j.
  - For size 3, irredundancy is encoded through named pairwise joins and meets.
  - The exchange layer names u_p and v_q as a column and a row, adds every cell F(v_q, u_p) with monotonicity
    against all cells, and applies no witness conditionally on membership.
  - Everything is k = 2, where Cut is proved (`cut_two`). Every SAT is therefore a partial model only, and the
    k = 2 setting is itself the control.

| profile | (N; M) | all-B |
|---|---|---|
| generator support P = {p₁, p₂}, size 2 | (2, 3; 3, 2) staggered | SAT (U ⊆ Dᶜ, V ⊆ D: 4 mixed pairs) |
| + exchange layer | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT |
| + exchange, forced Case A (U ⊆ D) | (2, 3; 3, 2) | UNSAT (= `generator_not_caseA`; core needs only rows p₁, p₂, p₁∧p₂, column s) |
| + exchange, forced Case B (V ⊆ Dᶜ) | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT |
| + exchange, forced Case C (mixed, with cells F(v_q, u_p)) | (2, 3; 3, 2) | **SAT** (4 mixed pairs) |
| free pattern + exchange | (2, 3; 3, 2) | SAT |
| size 2 / + exchange | (2, 2; 2, 2) equal-time | UNSAT / UNSAT (the §34.3z⁸⁵ synchronization, unchanged) |
| size 2 / + exchange | (3, 2; 2, 3) reverse | SAT / SAT |
| size 3 containing both generators, irredundant | (2, 3; 3, 2) | UNSAT (trivially, by `generator_row_support`: u₃ ≤ u₁ or u₂) |
| size 3 on named rows a₁, a₂, a₃ ∈ D, irredundant | (2, 3; 3, 2) | **SAT** (V: two inside, one outside; 6 mixed pairs) |

Two further notes on the runs.
- Size 2 on named rows a₁, a₂ (in place of the generators) is also SAT, with 4 mixed pairs.
- The combined run (named size-3 supports, exchange layer, forced Case C) was killed by the out-of-memory killer and
  is **not completed**.

Outputs: `out_exchange.txt` (generator supports, by case of (N; M)) and `out_exchange_named.txt` (named supports, and
forced Cases A/B/C).

**Answer.**
- **Strongest deliverables.**
  - (1)/(2): Lean proofs of minimal-support extraction, irredundancy, the input-join/output-meet memberships and the
    trichotomy.
  - (3): minimized z3 results for irredundant two- and three-element supports.
  - (6), in the only form it holds: a strict-decrease proof for *regular* exchange, whose measure is the orbit
    element itself.
- **No new obstruction (4).** The response-exchange layer, Case C included, leaves the staggered all-B profile SAT.
- **No counterprofile of type (5) is needed.** Regular exchange cannot reproduce itself indefinitely, but the reason
  is the old orbit entry/exit theorem.
- **Central question.**
  - Irredundant response families cannot exchange roles indefinitely under any exchange that keeps the support
    equations, because that exchange is a reciprocal orbit.
  - The histories do eventually produce a mixed cross pair (v_q, u_p).
  - That pair is just one more cross pair: its response F(v_q, u_p) obeys W1/W2 and nothing stronger. In the finite
    profile it is consistent with the staggered configuration.
  - The missing staggered-time comparison is untouched.

## 34.3z¹⁰² ChatGPT brief "cofactor exchange and staggered reciprocal supports": the second cofactor family is constant, so exchange stops after one step; stable cofactors are petal-injective

**Audit (HEAD f3125b1).**
- The brief's summary of §34.3z¹⁰¹ is accurate.
- Part II §§1–4 are correct as stated: the decompositions, both cofactor exchanges (no regularity needed) and the
  dichotomy. The antichain property of irredundant supports and the strict relations pⱼ < p⁻ᵢ for |P| ≥ 3 are correct.
