- **§5 (termination via ACC/DCC and minimum cardinality) is correct in outcome, but the proposed mechanism is not
  the reason.**
  - The cofactor operator C(p)ᵢ = ⋁_{j≠i} pⱼ satisfies C²(p)ᵢ = ⋁ p for every i once |ι| ≥ 3 (`rowCof_rowCof`).
    Every index l ≠ i is omitted by some third j ∉ {i, l}.
  - So after one successful exchange, the next cofactor family is the constant ⋁P, which is outside D
    (`row_input_join_out`).
  - The second exchange therefore crosses at **every** index. No ascending chain, ACC/DCC or minimum-cardinality
    choice is involved.
  - Dually, C²(q) = ⋀Q ∈ D (`colCof_colCof`).
  - With supports indexed as families ι → α, as the brief asked, nothing needs re-indexing. The "infinite ascending
    sequence" has length two: pⱼ < p⁻ᵢ ≤ ⋁P.
- §6 is right that |ι| = 2 is the swap: C is an involution, and nothing can be extracted from it.
- Part III's "cofactor-sensitive first-failure lemma" exists only as threshold monotonicity (`cofactor_threshold`), which
  is not stronger than per-pair `first_failure_dichotomy`. The z3 profile below confirms that no new constraint appears.

**Lean (`SupportCofactors.lean`; full build passes; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `rowCof`, `colCof`, `le_rowCof`, `colCof_le`, `sup_le_rowCof_sup`, `colCof_inf_le` | input cofactors; two distinct cofactors cover the family |
| `row_cofactor_decomposition` / `column_cofactor_decomposition` | s = uᵢ ⊔ U⁻ᵢ with uᵢ < s, U⁻ᵢ < s, incomparable; dually r = vᵢ ⊓ V⁻ᵢ |
| `support_antichain_row` / `support_antichain_col` | an irredundant support is an antichain |
| `rowCof_strict` / `colCof_strict` | (|ι| ≥ 3, antichain) pⱼ < p⁻ᵢ and q⁻ᵢ < qⱼ for j ≠ i |
| `row_cofactor_exchange` / `column_cofactor_exchange` | (|ι| ≥ 2) s = ⋁ F(p⁻ᵢ, s) if all p⁻ᵢ ∈ D; r = ⋀ F(r, q⁻ᵢ) if all q⁻ᵢ ∉ D |
| `cofactor_exchange_dichotomy` | each exchange succeeds, or a crossing cofactor gives r < F(r, p⁻ᵢ) or F(q⁻ᵢ, s) < s |
| **`rowCof_rowCof` / `colCof_colCof`** | (|ι| ≥ 3) C²(p) ≡ ⋁p, C²(q) ≡ ⋀q |
| **`second_cofactor_crosses`** | (|ι| ≥ 3) every second-generation row cofactor is ∉ D and every column cofactor is ∈ D |
| **`cofactor_petal_injective`** | in D = ⋃ ↓gₖ, if p⁻ᵢ ≤ g_{σ i} for all i, then σ is injective (else ⋁P ≤ g_{σ i} ∈ D) |
| **`cofactor_stable_card`** | all row cofactors in D ⇒ |ι| ≤ k |
| **`cofactor_stable_comeets`** | then pⱼ ≤ ⋀_{i≠j} g_{σ i}, and these co-meets are themselves a row support |
| `row_orbit_mono_anchor`, `cofactor_threshold` | the cofactor row keeps every column-orbit containment, row-orbit containment and outside row-orbit point of each non-omitted row |
| `family_join_out` / `family_meet_in` | ⋁p ∉ D, ⋀q ∈ D for indexed supports |

**Consequences.**
- **Exchange sequence.** Repeated cofactor exchange on a support of size ≥ 3 has the form
  P → C(P) (if every p⁻ᵢ ∈ D) → crossing at ⋁P.
  - The only boundary-crossing pairs it can produce are (r, p⁻ᵢ), (r, ⋁P), and dually (q⁻ᵢ, s), (⋀Q, s).
  - Alternating row and column cofactors ends at the cross pair (⋀Q, ⋁P), the old input meet and input join.
  - So it cannot force an impossible membership pattern: the brief's Part III Q3 has a negative answer.
- **Two generators.**
  - Every size-3 row support has a crossing cofactor (`cofactor_stable_card`; z3 UNSAT below).
  - Size-2 supports swap.
  - So for k = 2, cofactor exchange yields either nothing (the swap) or the cross pair (r, a_j ∨ a_l).
- **Three generators (the brief's focus).**
  - A size-3 support with every cofactor in D puts the three cofactors under the three distinct generators.
  - Each support row then lies under a pairwise generator meet m_{ab} = g_a ∧ g_b, and {m₁₂, m₁₃, m₂₃} is itself a row
    support of s (`cofactor_stable_comeets`).
  - So cofactor stability lands exactly in the strict-crossing pairwise-meet configuration of the three-generator
    problem (§34.3z⁹⁰, Priority D).
  - That configuration is already open, and s is already a common contracting column for every row in D, including
    the meets and the join anchors. The support adds only that the meet responses join to s.
  - Size ≥ 4 is impossible there (z3 UNSAT below).
  - On the canonical generator support of size ≥ 3, every cofactor gᵢ ∨ gⱼ is outside D immediately (z3 UNSAT for
    "all in").
- **Exceptional size 2.**
  - A size-2 row support exists in a 3-generator cut (z3 SAT), so size 2 does not mean D is two-generated.
  - `cut_two` and the bouquet results apply only to the cut, not to the support.
  - The swap carries no information, so this case falls back on whatever handles the cut.

**z3 (`experiments/finite_support/cofactor_exchange.py`).**
- **Setup.**
  - The cut is ⋃ ↓gₖ with pairwise incomparable generators.
  - r and s are named separately, with the uniform inequalities.
  - Supports are true and irredundant, via named partial joins and meets of the responses.
  - Cofactors are named with **only** the cells F(p⁻ᵢ, s) and F(r, q⁻ᵢ), plus generator cells F(gₖ, s).
  - The generator cells are needed. Without them, "all row cofactors in D" was SAT for k = 2, n = 3: the partial
    model lacked the upper bound at which the Lean proof evaluates F. With them it is UNSAT, matching
    `cofactor_stable_card`.

| k, n | row cofactors all in | one out | ≥ 2 out | exchanged equations negated (all in / all out) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2, 2 | SAT (swap; column all-out only) | UNSAT (trivial: p⁻ᵢ = pⱼ ∈ D) | UNSAT | UNSAT |
| 2, 3 | **UNSAT** (`cofactor_stable_card`) | SAT | SAT | UNSAT (vacuous) |
| 3, 3 | SAT | SAT | SAT | UNSAT (`row/column_cofactor_exchange`) |
| 3, 4 | **UNSAT** (`cofactor_stable_card`) | SAT | — | — |
| 3, 3, generator support | UNSAT (gᵢ ∨ gⱼ ∉ D) | — | — | — |
| 3, 2 (named) | SAT (size-2 support in a 3-generator cut) | — | — | — |

For n = 3, every column mode (all out / one in / ≥ 2 in) gives the same result as the row mode in its row.

**Test D: the two-generator orbit profile of §34.3z⁹⁹ with cofactor cells added** (rows p₁, p₂, p₁ ∧ p₂, orbit points,
r; columns q₁, q₂, ⊤, orbit points, s):

| (N; M) | support | cofactor membership | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| (2, 2; 2, 2) equal-time, all-B (**control**) | named n = 3 | rows one out, columns all out | UNSAT (the old synchronization) |
| (2, 3; 3, 2) all-B | generator support n = 2 (C = swap) | — | SAT |
| (2, 3; 3, 2) all-B | named n = 3 | rows one out / ≥ 2 out × columns all out / one in / ≥ 2 in (all six) | **SAT** |
| (2, 3; 3, 2) all-B + Case C | named n = 3 | rows one out, columns all out | **SAT** |

- The first version of the profile omitted the row p₁ ∧ p₂. Its equal-time control then came out SAT, which flagged
  the omission.
- The table is the rerun with that row included.
- Only the cofactor cells were named (no U × V exchange layer), and no run hit the memory limit.

Outputs: `out_cofactor_small.txt`, `out_cofactor_card.txt`, `out_cofactor_orbits.txt`.

**Answer.**
- Deliverables 1–3 are proved.
- Deliverable 4 is proved in a sharper form: termination after at most one successful exchange, by the algebraic
  identity C² = const. It needs no ACC/DCC and no minimum-cardinality hypothesis.
- Deliverable 5: the staggered profile acquires no new restriction (see the orbit table).
- Deliverable 6: the cofactor-sensitive first-failure lemma is threshold monotonicity, and nothing stronger holds at the
  tested depth.
- **Central question.** The "combination of all the other members" does create a new support once, but its history
  collapses at the next step onto the input join ⋁P and the input meet ⋀Q.
  - The only structural content is petal-injectivity of stable cofactors.
  - For k = 2 this excludes stable size-3 supports.
  - For k = 3 it reduces cofactor stability to the pairwise-meet crossing configuration that is already open.
  - The missing staggered-time comparison is untouched.

## 34.3z¹⁰³ ChatGPT brief "extremal support normalization and the three-petal incidence problem": the normal form holds without extremality; the incidence profile with orbits yields two "two outside" lemmas

**Audit (HEAD dc8ee25).**
- **Part I's normal form is correct as stated** (`maximal_support_dichotomy`), but the extremal selection is unnecessary.
  - Every stable support p (all row cofactors in D, cofactor pᵢ⁻ ≤ g_{σ i}) lies pointwise below its co-meet tuple
    mᵢ = ⋀_{j≠i} g_{σ j}.
  - That tuple is itself a **stable** support, with the same σ (`stable_support_dominated`, `comeet_stable`).
  - Hence stable supports exist iff an injective co-meet tuple is a support (`stable_support_iff_comeets`). For k = 3,
    that tuple is the three pairwise meets.
  - No chain condition is needed for this. The maximal-element existence holds (`maximal_row_support_exists`, via
    Mathlib's `Pi.wellFoundedLT`), but the dichotomy's content is the domination.
- **The first branch of the dichotomy is always inhabited.** S_s ∋ (g₁, g₂, g₃) (`generator_row_support`). The
  generator tuple is pointwise maximal, and for an antichain of ≥ 3 generators all its cofactors gᵢ ∨ gⱼ cross
  (`generator_tuple_maximal`). So "maximal" does not single out the stable case.
- **Part II's incidence is correct and elementary** (`pairwise_meet_anchor_incidence`). It has three levels:
  e_{ij} ≤ aᵢ, aⱼ and aᵢ ≤ uᵢ = F(gᵢ, s), with s = ⋁e = ⋁a = ⋁u.
- **Part III.** F(x, s) ≥ s and F(r, y) ≤ r for x = p₁ ∨ p₂, y = q₁ ∧ q₂ are exactly the content of
  `family_join_out` / `family_meet_in` (§34.3z¹⁰²).

**Lean (`ExtremalSupport.lean`; full build passes; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `finite_product_wellFoundedGT`, `maximal_tuple_exists`, `maximal_row_support_exists` | ACC on ι → α (pointwise); a maximal support above any support |
| `rowSupports`, `comeet`, `comeet_stable`, `comeet_mem` | definitions; a co-meet tuple is stable, entries in D |
| `stable_support_dominated` | stable p ≤ comeet g σ, and comeet g σ is a stable support |
| `maximal_stable_support_eq_comeets`, **`maximal_support_dichotomy`** | the brief's normal form |
| **`stable_support_iff_comeets`** | a stable support exists ⟺ an injective co-meet tuple is a support |
| `generator_tuple_maximal` | the generator tuple is maximal; for an antichain of ≥ 3 generators, all its cofactors are outside D |
| `pairwise_meet_anchor_incidence` | the six edge–vertex inequalities, aᵢ ≤ F(gᵢ, s), and s = ⋁a = ⋁u |
| `common_missing_petal` | some petal g_k contains no response F(x, s) with x ≥ ⋀g (no witness at ⋀g) |
| **`not_all_but_one_in`, `two_outside_generator_responses`** | (DCC) at a column contracting for every generator row, ≥ 2 generator responses are outside D |
| **`not_all_but_one_anchor_in`, `two_outside_anchor_responses`** | (DCC) the same for the join-anchor rows |

**The two new lemmas.** Both strengthen a one-outside result already in the repo: `column_lemma` for generators,
`contracting_simultaneous` for anchors. Both were found by z3 as UNSAT orbit profiles. They apply to any column that
contracts for every generator row: the aggregate s, ⊤, and every s of §34.3z¹⁰⁰.
- **Generators.** Suppose F(gⱼ, s) ∈ D for every j ≠ i₀.
  - Follow the g_{i₀}-row orbit of s to its last outside point v ≤ s.
  - At v every generator response is in D: for j ≠ i₀ because F(gⱼ, v) ≤ F(gⱼ, s), and for i₀ because the orbit
    enters D at v. This contradicts `column_lemma`.
- **Anchors.** Suppose F(cⱼ, s) ∈ D for every j ≠ i₀. Follow the c_{i₀}-orbit of s to its last outside point v. Every
  anchor takes v into D away from its own petal:
  - c_{i₀} by `anchor_sandwich`;
  - cⱼ because F(cⱼ, s) ≤ g_k with k ≠ j (`anchor_sandwich` at s, n = 0). So F(cⱼ, v) ≤ gⱼ would give
    F(cⱼ, v) ≤ gⱼ ∧ g_k ≤ cⱼ, a witness.

  This contradicts `simultaneous_entry`.
- **For k = 3.** At such a column, at most one generator response and at most one anchor response lie in D.
  Since aᵢ ≤ uᵢ, an inside generator response uⱼ forces the inside anchor response to be aⱼ.

**z3 (`experiments/finite_support/three_petal_incidence.py`).**
- **Setup.**
  - Rows g₁, g₂, g₃, m_{ij}, g₁₂₃ and c₁, c₂, c₃, named as exact meets and joins.
  - Column s ∉ D, contracting for every row, with the stable normal form s = e₁₂ ∨ e₁₃ ∨ e₂₃.
  - Optionally the meet support is irredundant, and optionally the anchor and generator row orbits from s are added,
    each with its first entry into D at a given length, over a full monotone grid with conditional no witness.

**Local labels** (`local`, no orbits). The label of a response is the set of generators above it ("out" when the
response is outside D).
- 289 label vectors for (e | a | u), **53 classes up to S₃**, with or without irredundancy. The incidence is far from
  rigid.
- One regularity holds in every class: some petal is missed by all nine responses (`common_missing_petal`).
- Classes with two of the uᵢ inside D appear here. They are artefacts of the missing orbits:
  `two_outside_generator_responses` excludes them, and the orbit runs reject them.
- Control: all uᵢ in D gives UNSAT (`generator_not_caseA`).

**With orbits** (`orbits0/1`, irredundant meet support; lengths are the first-entry times of the orbits from s).
Every UNSAT is predicted by a Lean theorem:

| anchor lengths (c₁, c₂, c₃) | generator lengths (g₁, g₂, g₃) | result | explained by |
|---|---|---|---|
| (1, 1, 1) | (2, 2, 2) | UNSAT | `contracting_simultaneous` |
| (2, 2, 2) | (2, 1, 1) | UNSAT | `two_outside_generator_responses` |
| (1, 1, 2) | (2, 2, 2) | UNSAT | `two_outside_anchor_responses` |
| (1, 2, 2) | (2, 2, 1) | UNSAT | `two_outside_anchor_responses` (u₃ ∈ D ⇒ a₃ ≤ u₃ ∈ D, with a₁ ∈ D) |
| (1, 2, 2) | (1, 2, 2) | **SAT** | — (sample entry labels {2} {3} {1}, {3} {1} {2}, …) |
| (1, 2, 2) | (2, 2, 2) | **SAT** | — (artefact: excluded by `no_equal_time_two`, §34.3z¹⁰⁴) |
| (2, 2, 2) | (2, 2, 2) | **SAT** | — (artefact: excluded by `no_equal_time_two`, §34.3z¹⁰⁴) |
| (1, 2, 3) | (1, 3, 2) | not completed (6500 s timeout) | |

**How the anchor lemma was found** (`minimize`).
- The (1, 2, 2) / (2, 2, 1) UNSAT already holds with only the c₁ orbit (a₁ ∈ D) and generator lengths (2, 2, 1),
  (3, 2, 1), (2, 1, 2), or with the c₂ orbit in place of c₁.
- It holds without the meet support equation and without rows c₂, c₃.
- It becomes SAT when any one of the rows g₁₂₃, m₁₂, m₁₃, m₂₃ is dropped (the element kept, its cells removed), or
  when the g₂ orbit is dropped.
- It is also SAT for the anchor/generator pair (a₁, u₁), which the lemma allows.
- This pointed to `simultaneous_entry` at the end of an anchor orbit, which gave the Lean proof above.

**Part III: the size-2 aggregate cross pair** (`cofactor_exchange.py agg0/agg1`).
- Setup: the two-generator staggered (2, 3; 3, 2) all-B profile, with the row p₁ ∧ p₂, a named size-2 row support
  {a₁, a₂} and a size-2 column support {b₁, b₂}.
- Named: x = a₁ ∨ a₂ and y = b₁ ∧ b₂, with the cells F(y, x), F(y, s), F(r, x), F(x, s), F(r, y).
- **All 8 classes of F(y, x) are SAT.** The classes cover in/outside D, y < F(y, x) or y ∥ F(y, x), and F(y, x) < x or
  F(y, x) ∥ x. The orders F(y, x) ≤ y and x ≤ F(y, x) are excluded by no witness.
- So the aggregate pair adds no restriction to the staggered profile.

**Answer.**
- **Priority 1** is proved, together with the stronger, extremality-free form `stable_support_iff_comeets`. The stable
  three-generator case is exactly "the pairwise meets are a support of s".
- **Priority 2** is proved.
- **Priority 3** yielded new lemmas rather than a three-petal contradiction: `two_outside_generator_responses` and
  `two_outside_anchor_responses`.
  - These generalize `column_lemma` and `contracting_simultaneous` to "at least two outside", for any k and any common
    contracting column.
  - The allowed orbit profiles remain SAT, so the incidence does not determine the crossing configuration.
- **Priority 4:** no restriction found.
- **Central question.** The two decompositions (meets/anchors versus generators) are linked at s by the three-level
  incidence and by the two "two outside" bounds. That is the extent of the rigidity found: for k = 3, at most one row
  from each family keeps s inside D.
  - What remains is the transfer of the outside responses, i.e. where the orbits of the ≥ 2 outside generator and
    anchor rows enter D. That is the open staggered entry question again.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁴ ChatGPT brief "missing-petal filtration and paired orbit endpoints": the palette is rigid on endpoint 3-cycles but does not exclude them; the endpoint meets give a new equal-time-two theorem

**Audit (HEAD ba4a202).**
- Parts I–III are correct and elementary.
  - The palette Π(y) = {i : F(m, y) ≤ gᵢ}, with m = ⋀g, is the label set of the meet row.
  - Its three properties are no witness at m, row monotonicity and column monotonicity.
  - Paired dominance (F cᵢ)ⁿ s ≤ (F gᵢ)ⁿ s is row monotonicity.
  - The two-common-outside corollary needs only `two_outside_anchor_responses` and down-closure (A ≤ U), not the
    generator version.
  - At a generator endpoint vᵢ, F(cᵢ, vᵢ) ∈ D but not under gᵢ: this is `anchor_sandwich` at n = 0 at vᵢ.
- §8's 2-cycle exclusion at an outside meet vᵢ ∧ vⱼ is `no_upward_transfer_cycle` with T = {i, j}.
- **The conjectured Hamiltonian cycle is correct.** If all three pairwise endpoint meets are outside D, the chosen
  labels form a 3-cycle (`three_endpoint_cycle`).
- **Sharper:** the meet of **all** generator endpoints is always in D (`endpoint_total_meet_in_D`). This is the
  generator-orbit analogue of `lastpoints_no_common_lower`.
- **Critical audit (§11).**
  - The cells F(m, vᵢ) and F(cᵢ, vᵢ) are already in the full grid of §34.3z¹⁰³ (rows g₁₂₃, cᵢ; column vᵢ, an orbit
    point). The palettes Π(vᵢ) and labels Λᵢ are therefore only read off, not added.
  - The genuinely new constraints are the columns v_{ij} = vᵢ ∧ vⱼ and v₁₂₃ (exact meets) and their cells.
  - These turned out to be decisive in one case: generator lengths (2, 2, 2), below.

**Lean.**
- `ResponsePalette.lean`:
  - `labels`, `palette`, `mem_labels`;
  - `palette_proper`, `palette_card_lt`, `palette_response_subset`, `palette_antitone`, `palette_filtration`;
  - `palette_changes_bound`: at most |κ| − 1 change times n < N, needing only y_N ∉ D. It goes through
    `count_strict_increases` for the monotone n ↦ |Π(yₙ)|.
  - A palette change is an event of the meet row only, independent of any row orbit's entry.
- `PairedEndpoints.lean`:
  - `paired_orbit_order`, `paired_entry_order` (the smaller row enters no later), `two_common_outside`;
  - `paired_endpoint_responses`: vᵢ ∉ D, F(gᵢ, vᵢ) ≤ vᵢ, both endpoint responses in D, neither under gᵢ, and a label
    j ≠ i;
  - `endpoint_closed_meet_in_D`: any label-closed set T has ⋀_T vᵢ ∈ D;
  - `endpoint_two_cycle`, **`endpoint_total_meet_in_D`**, `labels_sub_palette` (Λᵢ ⊆ Π(vᵢ));
  - `three_cycle_of_no_two_cycle`, `three_endpoint_cycle`;
  - **`palette_cycle_rigidity`**: with all pairwise endpoint meets outside D, every endpoint palette is the singleton
    {σ i}, so Λᵢ = Πᵢ = {σ i}. The reason is that Π(vᵢ ∧ v_k) ⊇ Π(vᵢ) ∪ Π(v_k) ∋ σ i, σ k is proper.
- `EqualTimeTwo.lean`: **`no_equal_time_two`** (three generators, no chain condition).
  - Statement: at a column s contracting for every generator row, it is impossible that every uᵢ = F(gᵢ, s) is outside
    D while every yᵢ = F(gᵢ, uᵢ) is inside D. In words, the three generator orbits of s cannot all enter D at step 2.
  - Two facts carry it, for v_T = ⋀_{j∈T} uⱼ and m_T = ⋀_{j∈T} gⱼ:
    - F(m_T, s) ≤ v_T;
    - F(m_T, c) ≤ yᵢ ≤ g_{σ i} for i ∈ T and c ≤ uᵢ.
  - With them, no witness at the rows m₁₂₃ and m_P kills every label assignment to the yᵢ, v₁₂₃ and the inside v_P.
  - The finite part is a kernel `decide` (`eq2_comb`, 5184 label assignments), and the build uses standard axioms
    only.
  - The analogous argument for k = 2 is immediate, consistent with `cut_two`.
- All files: full build passes, no `sorry`, standard axioms only.

**How `no_equal_time_two` was found** (`three_petal_incidence.py endpointsB`, `out_endpoints_B.txt`).
- With the endpoint-meet columns added, the base profile with generator lengths (2, 2, 2) is UNSAT.
- Without them it was SAT (§34.3z¹⁰³), and it stays SAT with only the pairwise meets, or only v₁₂₃.
- An assumption-tracked core over the no-witness cells, split by the labels of the yᵢ, used only the rows gᵢ, m_{ij},
  g₁₂₃ at the columns uᵢ, v_{ij}, v₁₂₃. Neither the anchors nor the support equation was needed.
- That reduced it to the label combinatorics, which a Python brute force (0 of 5184 assignments survive) and then
  Lean `decide` confirmed.
- **Correction to §34.3z¹⁰³.** Its SAT orbit profiles with generator lengths (2, 2, 2), namely (1, 2, 2)/(2, 2, 2) and
  (2, 2, 2)/(2, 2, 2), are partial-model artefacts: they lacked the meet columns, and `no_equal_time_two` excludes them.
- **The obstruction is specific to time 2.** With the meet columns, the base profiles (2, 2, 3) and (3, 3, 3) are SAT.
  At later equal times the needed inequality F(m_T, vᵢ) ≤ v_T would compare orbits of different generators at shifted
  times, which is the missing staggered comparison again.

**z3 endpoint battery** (`endpoints0/1`, generator lengths (2, 2, 3), irredundant meet support, endpoint meets as
columns). "Π along g-orbits" lists the meet-row palette at each outside orbit point, from s to vᵢ; ∅ means
F(m, ·) ∉ D.

| test | result | model: Λ / Π(vᵢ) / meets v₁₂, v₁₃, v₂₃, v₁₂₃ |
|---|---|---|
| C1 control: v₁₂₃ ∉ D | UNSAT (`endpoint_total_meet_in_D`) | |
| C2 control: 2-cycle 1 → 2 → 1 with v₁₂ ∉ D | UNSAT (`endpoint_two_cycle`) | |
| T1 singleton 3-cycle 1 → 2 → 3 → 1 | **SAT** | {2},{3},{1} / {2},{3},{1} / out, out, out, D |
| T2 some endpoint with ≥ 2 labels | SAT | {2},{1,3},{2} / {2,3},{1,3},{2,3}; Π along the orbits {3} → {2,3} (a palette change while outside) |
| T3 all endpoint palettes of size 1 | SAT | the T1 model |
| T4 all endpoint palettes of size 2 | SAT | {2,3},{3},{2} / {2,3} ×3 / out, out, D, D |
| T5 two equal palettes, one different | SAT | {3},{1},{2} / {1,3},{1,3},{2} / out, D, D, D |
| T6 all pairwise endpoint meets outside | SAT | the reverse cycle {3},{1},{2}, singleton palettes (`palette_cycle_rigidity`) |
| T6 + T1 (3-cycle, all pairwise meets outside) | **SAT** | singleton palettes, v₁₂₃ ∈ D |
| T7 exactly one pairwise meet in D | SAT | {2},{3},{1} / {2},{2,3},{1,2} / out, out, D, D |

- **Equal time 3** (`endpointsA`): with generator lengths (3, 3, 3), T6 + T1 is **SAT**. The model has the same
  cycle and singleton palettes, the palettes along the orbits are ∅ → ∅ → {σ i}, and v₁₂₃ ∈ D.
- T7 on (3, 3, 3) was stopped after about 20 min and is not completed.
- Outputs: `out_endpoints_0.txt`, `out_endpoints_1.txt`, `out_endpoints_A.txt`, `out_endpoints_B.txt`.

**Answers to Part IV.**
1. **Size.** An endpoint palette has size 1 or 2: it is nonempty because it contains Λᵢ, and proper. At s it can be
   empty (T1, T6).
2. **Palette change before entry.** Yes: in T2 the palette grows {3} → {2,3} while the column is still outside D.
   Palette changes and orbit entries are independent events.
3. **Comparing endpoint palettes.** They compare only through meets: Π(vᵢ ∧ vⱼ) ⊇ Π(vᵢ) ∪ Π(vⱼ), and this is proper
   when the meet is outside D.
4. **A 3-cycle together with the filtrations.** Yes. The cycle forces singleton palettes (`palette_cycle_rigidity`)
   and an inside triple meet, and the profile is SAT (T1, T6 + T1).
5. **Missing-petal change forcing a transfer.** No: T2 changes palette without any forced transfer.

**Answer.**
- Deliverables 1–3 are proved. Deliverable 4 is `three_endpoint_cycle`, `palette_cycle_rigidity` and
  `endpoint_total_meet_in_D`.
- Deliverable 5: the palette-cycle obstruction is **refuted at the tested depth**. A persistent directed 3-cycle with
  all pairwise endpoint meets outside D is consistent, with palettes rigidly equal to the labels.
- The brief hoped for a lemma linking different entry times through a common palette. What was found instead is the
  equal-time-two theorem: it links the three generator orbits at the first step from s, where s is a common column.
- **Central question.** A common palette constrains staggered orbits only through meets of their columns.
  - On its own it exposes no impossible cycle.
  - The new impossibility (`no_equal_time_two`) comes from the meet columns v_T, at the one time where every v_T sits
    below a common column.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁵ ChatGPT brief "asynchronous meet-grid and minimal boundary extraction": the Cut Lemma for every finitely generated cut under DCC

**Result.**
- `Mordor.AsyncGrid.cut_fingen_dcc`: in a bounded lattice with DCC, every cut D = ⋃_{i<k} ↓gᵢ with k ≥ 1 and ⊤ ∉ D
  has a witness, for every monotone F.
- Standard axioms only, no `sorry`.
- This settles the three-generator problem (`cut_three`) and every k. `cut_two` is recovered as the case k = 2
  (`cut_two_of_grid`), without the sandwich.
- The remaining frontier for chain-finite Cut is a down-set D that is **not** finitely generated (see the end).

**Audit (HEAD b51718b).**
- Part A of the brief is correct.
  - One correction: W(r − eᵢ) ∧ W(r − eⱼ) = W(r) because the orbits descend, so the meet takes the coordinatewise
    **maximum** of the index vectors (`boundary_pairwise_meet`).
  - `minimal_mixed_boundary` is correct: at least two active coordinates, outside predecessors, pairwise meet W(r), and
    F(m, W(r − eᵢ)) ≤ W(r) (`boundary_predecessor_response`).
- The decisive object is the brief's own `asynchronous_meet_step`: F(m_T, W(t)) ≤ ⋀_{i∈T} uᵢ(tᵢ + 1).

**Step 1: a label abstraction, and z3** (`experiments/asyncgrid/abstract_grid.py`).
- **The abstraction.**
  - Every lattice model induces a labelling L(t) = {k : W(t) ≤ g_k} on the grid ∏ᵢ [0, nᵢ + 1].
  - L is monotone. Axis points with tᵢ ≤ nᵢ are empty, and the far faces are nonempty.
  - The no-witness cells give: for every empty t and nonempty T, T ⊄ L(π_T(t) + 1_T), where π_T(t) + 1_T advances
    the T-coordinates by one and resets the others to 0.
  - All other cell constraints follow from monotonicity (`reduced_grid.py`).
- **UNSAT in every profile tested:**
  - k = 2 with nᵢ ≤ 4;
  - k = 3 with nᵢ ≤ 4 (35 profiles, including (1, 1, 2) and (2, 2, 2), the brief's 36- and 64-column grids);
  - k = 4 with nᵢ ≤ 2.
- **Ablation** (`out_reduced_ablate.txt`). Removing monotonicity, or the no-witness constraints for any one size |T| ∈
  {1, 2, 3}, makes it SAT, so every family is used.
- **Cores.** For (1, 1, 1) the core uses only steps from s; this is `no_equal_time_two`. For (2, 2, 2) it uses
  asynchronous steps at mixed points such as (1, 1, 0) and (0, 1, 1). These are exactly the intermediate meet columns
  that the endpoint-only profiles of §34.3z¹⁰³–¹⁰⁴ lacked, which is why those were SAT.
- **The k = 2 case by hand.** On the staircase between the empty and labelled regions, the label is forced to be one
  singleton, while its two ends must carry {1} and {0}: a discrete intermediate-value (Sperner-type) argument.

**Step 2: the grid labelling problem is the finite Cut Lemma.**
- Make the grid a finite lattice Λ = (∏ᵢ [0, nᵢ + 1])ᵒᵈ, where the point t stands for W(t). Let D_Λ = {t : W(t) ∈ D}.
- Define F_Λ(x, y)ᵢ = yᵢ + 1 if W(x) ≤ gᵢ, and 0 otherwise.
- F_Λ is monotone: a larger x has more labels; the step is monotone in y.
- **First witness type, F_Λ(x, y) ⊑ x.** With T = {i : W(x) ≤ gᵢ}:
  - F(m_T, W(y)) ≤ W(F_Λ(x, y)) by the asynchronous meet step, and W(F_Λ(x, y)) ≤ W(x) ≤ m_T.
  - So F(m_T, W(y)) ≤ m_T, a witness of F.
- **Second witness type, y ⊑ F_Λ(x, y).** This needs yᵢ + 1 ≤ yᵢ at a labelled coordinate, which is impossible.
  (An outside y has every yᵢ ≤ nᵢ, so no clipping occurs.)
- So F_Λ is witness-free on a finite lattice, against `FinGen.cut_finite` (Sperner parity, §34.3n).
- Take s = ⊤, which is contracting for every row. DCC (`FGTwo.orbit_end`) makes every generator orbit enter D, so the
  grid is finite, and `cut_fingen_dcc` follows.
- The theorem therefore combines two of the programme's tools: the orbit machinery reduces the lattice to a finite
  grid, and the Sperner/finite-Cut theorem finishes it.

**Lean (`AsyncMeetGrid.lean`; full build passes; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `W`, `W_le`, `grid_antitone`, `grid_inside_upward` | grid columns W(t) = ⋀ᵢ uᵢ(tᵢ) |
| `asynchronous_meet_step` | F(m_T, W(t)) ≤ ⋀_{i∈T} (F gᵢ)^{tᵢ+1} s |
| `boundary_predecessor_response` | rᵢ ≥ 1 ⇒ F(m, W(r − eᵢ)) ≤ W(r) |
| `boundary_pairwise_meet` | i ≠ j ⇒ W(r − eᵢ) ⊓ W(r − eⱼ) = W(r) |
| `sc`, `sc_mono`, `sc_val` | the clipped successor on Fin (m + 2) |
| **`cut_fingen_dcc`** | DCC, D = ⋃_{i<k} ↓gᵢ, ⊤ ∉ D ⇒ ∃ x ∈ D, y ∉ D, F x y ≤ x ∨ y ≤ F x y |
| `cut_three`, `cut_two_of_grid` | k = 3; k = 2 recovered |

**Consequences for the open items of the last forty rounds.**
- The staggered-time comparison (§34.3z⁸⁵ onward) is not needed. The finite Cut Lemma supplies the global argument
  that no local comparison could.
- The sandwich (`cut_two`), the sink cone, the reciprocal orbits, the supports, the palettes and the endpoint cycles
  are all superseded for finitely generated D. The endpoint 3-cycle of §34.3z¹⁰⁴ is consistent only because those
  profiles omitted the mixed-time columns.
- The brief's Part B (boundary palettes and join columns) is not needed for the result. Its lemmas are proved as
  stated.

**What remains.** `cut_fingen_dcc` needs finitely many generators: one grid coordinate per generator orbit, and the
labels L(x) must index coordinates.
- Chain-finite Cut for an arbitrary down-set D needs either a finite set S of maximal elements of D such that every D-valued grid column lies below S, or a reduction to that case.
- This is a capture problem, the analogue of `FinGen.captured`. Adding a generator adds an orbit coordinate, hence new
  columns, which may need further generators.
- Under ACC every element of D lies below a maximal element, so the question is whether this capture can be closed
  finitely.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁶ ChatGPT brief "infinite boundary capture and the Sperner extension": duality, one-sided grid capture, and why the refinement measure must use F

**Audit (HEAD f9b2136).**
- The brief's setup (Part II §3) is correct: P = Max D, U = Min Dᶜ, every cell z(A, B) is a cross evaluation, and
  every value is captured by the full families.
- The elementary facts p ∨ q ∉ D and u ∧ v ∈ D are correct.
- The finite-capture target (§4) is a correct **sufficient** condition: `FinGen.captured` applies verbatim.
- Two corrections.
  1. **The finite-capture refinement terminates iff Cut holds for that F.** Under no witness, every stage has an
     uncaptured cell (`FinGen.captured`); conversely, a terminating refinement contradicts no witness. So the
     requested termination measure cannot rest on ACC/DCC: it must use F.
     - A refinement that grows the representatives while every aggregate stays fixed (the brief's §6 case 2) already
       happens with no F at all.
     - Example: in the height-2 lattice with infinitely many atoms, D = {⊥} ∪ atoms. Then s_n = ⊤ from n = 1 on,
       while Pₙ can grow forever.
  2. **Capturing the response table is more than needed.** The asynchronous grid of §34.3z¹⁰⁵ needs capture on one
     side only, and only of its own finitely many columns (`cut_of_grid_capture`, below).

**Lean (`InfiniteCapture.lean`; full build passes; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `exists_maximal_above` (ACC), `exists_minimal_below` (DCC) | extremal representatives |
| `maximal_pairwise_join_outside`, `minimal_pairwise_meet_inside` | p ≠ q maximal ⇒ p ⊔ q ∉ D; u ≠ v minimal ⇒ u ⊓ v ∈ D |
| `cell_cross` | ⋀A ∈ D, ⋁B ∉ D for finite nonempty A ⊆ D, B ⊆ Dᶜ |
| `no_witness_of_captured` | a finite pair capturing its response table contradicts no witness (the brief's §4, via `FinGen.captured`) |
| **`cut_of_grid_capture`** (DCC) | for finitely many rows gᵢ ∈ D, *not necessarily generating D*: if every D-valued grid column W(t) = ⋀ᵢ (F gᵢ)^{tᵢ} ⊤ lies below some gᵢ, the cut has a witness |
| `uncaptured_column_exists` | under no witness, every finite row family leaves an uncaptured D-valued grid column: the refinement never stabilizes |
| **`cut_cofingen_acc`** (ACC) | the order dual of `cut_fingen_dcc`: if Dᶜ = ⋃ⱼ ↑uⱼ is finitely generated, the cut has a witness |
| **`cut_chainfinite_of_finite_side`** | chain-finite L: Cut holds whenever D **or** Dᶜ is finitely generated, e.g. Max D finite or Min Dᶜ finite |

**What `cut_of_grid_capture` says.** In the proof of `cut_fingen_dcc`, generation is used once: every D-valued
column of the finite grid must carry a label. Everything else works for an arbitrary down-set.
- **The open condition, exactly.** Find finitely many rows in D whose orbit grid from ⊤ has all its D-valued columns
  below those rows.
- Nothing is required of the complement, and nothing of the cells F(⋀A, ⋁B).
- **The duality.** The dual statement captures the Dᶜ-valued joins of the column orbits from ⊥. Chain-finite Cut is
  therefore reduced to one-sided capture on either side.

**Status of the frontier.**
- With `cut_chainfinite_of_finite_side`, Cut is open only when **both** Max D and Min Dᶜ are infinite.
- In that case, under a counterexample, `uncaptured_column_exists` gives an infinite sequence of maximal
  representatives p₁, p₂, … with each new D-valued column c_{n+1} below none of p₁, …, pₙ.
- Chain-finiteness alone does not stop this: the pₙ may form an infinite antichain.
- A proof must use the specific columns. Each c_{n+1} is a meet of orbit points of the rows p₁, …, pₙ, and the
  pivot cell of `cut_finite` relates it to them through F(m_T, c_{n+1}) ≤ m_T-type inequalities. The invariant would
  have to be built from those.

**z3 / Python: Part III good-word compatibility** (`experiments/infcapture/good_words.py`, `out_good_words.txt`).
- **Setup.** Selectors f1(A, B) ∈ A, f2(A, B) ∈ B are fixed globally on every cell of a universe P* ⊔ U, as they
  would be if derived from one F. For P₁ ⊂ P₂ ⊂ … ⊂ P*, we test three things:
  - (R) the restriction of a good word of the larger instance is good;
  - (E) every good word of the smaller instance extends;
  - (C) a compatible chain of good words through all sizes exists.
- **Results.**

| \|P*\|, \|U\| | random selectors | R fails | E fails | no compatible chain (C) | odd count (`odd_goods`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3, 1 | 300 | 100/600 | 100/600 | 100/300 | always |
| 3, 2 | 200 | 564/1062 | 207/582 | 110/200 | always |
| 4, 2 | 60 | 388/654 | 166/366 | 50/60 | always |
| 4, 3 | 15 | 314/447 | 96/191 | 12/15 | always |

- **Interpretation.** Parity gives existence at every finite stage but no compatibility. A compatible chain fails in
  33–83% of these random global selector systems.
- An infinite or transfinite Sperner argument would therefore need selectors with the monotone structure that F
  imposes. With z monotone, the admissible label sets {p ∈ A : z ≰ p} grow with B and shrink, on A, under
  restriction. Random selectors ignore this.
- The next diagnostic is to repeat the test with selectors drawn from monotone admissibility systems, choosing the
  matching at each stage.

**Answer to the central question.** For the cells that matter, a finite set of representatives suffices exactly when
one-sided grid capture holds (`cut_of_grid_capture`). Chain-finiteness cannot provide it by itself (the antichain
example). Cut is now proved for chain-finite lattices whenever one side of the cut is finitely generated. The
remaining case, both sides infinitely generated, needs an F-dependent invariant on the sequence of uncaptured grid
columns.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁷ ChatGPT brief "Stage 2: monotone compatibility and infinite term structure": compatibility fails for fixed exhaustions, the WQO lemma is the conjecture, Kruskal reverses evaluation

**Audit (HEAD 2385bd9; the brief pins f9b2136, and nothing relevant changed since).**
- Part 1 and the inventory are accurate.
- A1–A2 are correct, with one addition used below. Write S_P(c) = {p : z(c) ≤ p} and S_U(c) = {u : u ≤ z(c)} for a
  cell c. Then S_P(c) and S_U(c) are **never both nonempty**: u ≤ z ≤ p would put u in D.
- So an F-induced admissibility system is exactly a monotone assignment of these two sets to cells, with A ⊄ S_P and
  B ⊄ S_U (no witness). The brief's two persistence laws are the monotonicity.

### Work package A

**Lemma A, fixed exhaustion: refuted.** (`experiments/infcapture/monotone_good_words.py`; 2QBF by CEGAR, where the
outer solver picks a system and the inner solver picks global selectors and a compatible chain of good words.)

| universe, exhaustion | result |
|---|---|
| \|P*\| = 3, 4; \|U*\| = 1; P letters added one at a time | every system admits a compatible chain |
| \|P*\| = \|U*\| = 2; (1,1) → (2,1) → (2,2) | **counterexample** |
| \|P*\| = 3, \|U*\| = 2, alternate or P-first; \|P*\| = 4, \|U*\| = 2, alternate | counterexample |

**The minimal counterexample.** It has P* = {p₀, p₁} and U* = {u₀, u₁}; all other cells carry empty sets.
- Every cell with A = {p₀, p₁} has S_P = {p₀}, i.e. z ≤ p₀ for every B. So only p₁ is admissible there.
- The cell ({p₀}, {u₀, u₁}) has S_U = {u₁}. So only u₀ is admissible there.

By hand:
- **Stage 2.** The stage-2 word must restrict to u₀p₀. Its first cell ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀}) is labelled p₁, so the only
  good choice is u₀p₁p₀.
- **Stage 3.** The three insertions of u₁ all fail:
  - u₁u₀p₁p₀ and u₀u₁p₁p₀ label p₁ twice;
  - u₀p₁u₁p₀ labels u₀ twice, on ({p₀}, {u₀}) and ({p₀}, {u₀, u₁}).

**Checks.**
- `brute_check.py` confirms it without z3: 4 selector systems, none compatible, each stage with an odd number of good
  words.
- **Locally realizable** (`realize.py`): there is a finite partial order with the needed exact meets and joins, a
  down-set D, maximal p₀, p₁ ∈ D, minimal u₀, u₁ ∉ D, and monotone cell values with no witness on all nine cells and
  exactly these relations. The Dedekind–MacNeille completion makes it a bounded lattice.
- Three cell values lie in D below neither p₀ nor p₁, exactly the uncaptured situation of a real counterexample.

**Lemma A′, exhaustion chosen per system: holds at the tested sizes.**
- The same system is compatible for 4 of the 8 exhaustions of the 2 × 2 universe.
- Searching for a system that fails for **every** exhaustion gives no such system for (2, 2) or (3, 2) (36
  exhaustions; CEGAR, 2260 iterations). This is a positive solver result only.
- (2, 3) gives the same answer: none (36 exhaustions; 2746 iterations, `out_all_exhaustions_2x3.txt`).

**Why compatibility would not suffice (A4).** The tail argument needs the pivots z*ₙ of the chain to be monotone, so
that chain-finiteness stabilizes them. Under adaptive capture they cannot be monotone across both sides
(`GoodWordPivot.lean`, Lean):
- after adding a maximal p ≥ z*, the new pivot satisfies z′ ≰ z* (`pivot_not_le_of_P_capture`);
- after adding a minimal u ≤ z*, it satisfies z* ≰ z′ (`pivot_not_ge_of_U_capture`).

A monotone pivot sequence must stay on one side of the cut, increasing in D or decreasing outside. Showing that it
does is the one-sided grid-capture problem of §34.3z¹⁰⁶ again.

**Missing step (precise).** A pivot-monotone compatible extension lemma: when a pivot z* ∈ D is captured by p,
there is a compatible extension whose new pivot is ≥ z*.

### Work package B

- **B1 is the conjecture, not an intermediate lemma** (`TermOrder.lean`, Lean).
  - `finite_of_pwo_chainFinite`: in a poset with ACC, a partially well-ordered set is finite (a monotone subsequence
    of an injective sequence is a strictly increasing chain).
  - `crossClosure_not_pwo`: so in a counterexample the cross-closure is never a WQO (`crossClosure_infinite`).
  - Any WQO route must therefore *be* a proof that the cross-closure is finite.
- **Kruskal embedding reverses evaluation** (`kruskal_step_strict`, Lean). The term F(⊥, ⊤) homeomorphically embeds
  in F(⊥, F(⊥, ⊤)), but when F(⊥, ⊤) ∉ D no witness forces F(⊥, F(⊥, ⊤)) < F(⊥, ⊤).
- **The evaluation-sound syntactic relations are not WQOs.** These relations are:
  - replacing ⊥-leaves by arbitrary D-typed terms (raises the value);
  - replacing ⊤-leaves by arbitrary outside-typed terms (lowers it).

  They are not WQOs: the row-orbit terms (F(⊥, ·))ⁿ(⊤) are pairwise incomparable under them, although their values
  form a chain. Comparability of evaluations is semantic, and any useful term order must quotient by F-dependent
  identities.

### Summary

| claim | status |
|---|---|
| F-admissibility system = monotone (S_P, S_U) with S_P, S_U never both nonempty | proved (above) |
| Lemma A for a fixed exhaustion | **refuted**, minimal 2 × 2 locally realizable counterexample |
| Lemma A′ (some exhaustion works) at (2, 2), (3, 2), (2, 3) | solver-verified; open in general |
| A4 tail argument | needs pivot monotonicity; adaptive capture moves pivots in side-dependent directions (Lean) |
| B1 | equivalent to the conjecture (Lean) |
| Kruskal-type term orders | not evaluation-monotone (Lean); the sound ones are not WQO |

**Reuse.** `FinGen.captured` (pivot cell), `Sperner.odd_goods`, `Stable.crossClosure_infinite`,
`InfCapture.cut_of_grid_capture`.

**Next formalization target.** Extract the pivot of `FinGen.captured` as a standalone lemma. A good word for (A, B)
yields a cell value z* with z* ≰ p for all p ∈ A and u ≰ z* for all u ∈ B. Then state the pivot-monotone
extension lemma as the single open combinatorial target of work package A.

## 34.3z¹⁰⁸ Continuation of work package A: monotonicity buys compatibility, but not the captor step

Follow-up to §34.3z¹⁰⁷ (`experiments/infcapture/`).

**1. The deletion lemma and arbitrary selectors** (`deletion_lemma.py`, random global selectors).
- **Deletion lemma (D): false.** It says every good word has a letter whose deletion leaves a good word of the smaller
  instance. It fails for 3–14 % of good words. The smallest failure is at 2 × 2: the word u₁u₀p₁p₀.
- **Weaker (D′): also fails for arbitrary selectors.** It says some good word of the full instance admits a deletion
  chain down to (1, 1), which is Lemma A′ for those selectors. Failures: 5/300 systems at (3, 2), 2/300 at (2, 3),
  3/60 at (3, 3).
- **The case \|U\| = 1 is trivial for every selector.** The good word is the greedy peeling a₁ = f(A),
  a₂ = f(A − a₁), …, and deleting a₁ leaves the peeling of A − a₁.

**2. F-monotone versus arbitrary** (`monotone_vs_arbitrary.py`).
- **The arbitrary counterexamples break both laws.** At (3, 2) and (2, 3) the full instance has a single good word, with
  no deletion chain. Those selector systems break both persistence laws (9 and 5 violations), so no F induces them.
- **Monotone systems satisfy A′ at every size tested:**
  - **every** system at (2, 2), (3, 2), (2, 3) (§34.3z¹⁰⁷, adversarial CEGAR);
  - **12/12** random systems at (3, 3) and **11/11** at (4, 2);
  - the adversarial CEGAR at (3, 3) (216 exhaustions) was stopped by its 3300 s timeout and is **not completed**.
- **Answer to the brief's first question.** Yes: at these sizes the F-induced monotone structure gives a finite-stage
  compatibility property (some exhaustion admits a compatible chain) that arbitrary selector systems lack. This is
  computational evidence only.

**3. The captor step fails, even with helpers** (`helper_rescue.py`, `realize.py captor`).
- **What the infinite argument needs.** Not A′ for each finite universe separately (that gives no coherent infinite
  chain), but this: extend the **current** chain until it contains the representative that captures the current
  pivot, possibly inserting other ("helper") letters first.
- **Setup.** Fix the prefix ({p₀}, {u₀}) → ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀}). Require u₁ to capture every possible pivot of the
  stage-2 word (u₁ ≤ z at ({p₀}, {u₀}) and ({p₁}, {u₀})).
- **Result.** CEGAR finds a monotone system in which **no compatible continuation reaches u₁**. This holds in the
  universes {p₀, p₁} + {u₀, u₁}, with a helper p₂, with a helper u₂, and with both (up to 3 + 3 letters).

| universe | captor of the pivot reachable? |
|---|---|
| {p₀, p₁} + {u₀, u₁} | no (2 iterations) |
| + helper p₂ | no |
| + helper u₂ | no |
| + p₂ and u₂ | no (83 iterations) |

**The 2 × 2 system.**
- The constraints are:
  - u₁ ≤ z at ({pᵢ}, {u₀}) and ({pᵢ}, {u₀, u₁}) for i = 0, 1, and at ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀, u₁});
  - z ≤ p₀ at ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀}).
- **The stage-2 word is forced** to be u₀p₁p₀. Its pivot z(p₀, u₀) lies outside D, above u₁.
- **No continuation can label u₁.** Every cell with B ⊇ {u₀, u₁} has only u₀ admissible, so u₁ is a U-label only on a
  cell with B = {u₁}. That needs u₁ read before u₀, which the prefix forbids.
- **Locally realizable** (`out_realize_captor.txt`): there is a finite lattice, after the Dedekind–MacNeille
  completion, with no witness on all nine cells and exactly these relations.

**Consequence (sharp negative for work package A).**
- F-monotonicity does give compatibility in the weak sense A′. But a good-word argument cannot proceed by extending one
  chain toward the captors of its pivots: the captor can be locally unreachable from the current chain, helpers
  included.
- An infinite Sperner argument therefore has to *revise* earlier words, giving up compatibility in the brief's sense.
  So the König-style construction of A3 does not go through as formulated.
- **The open target that remains for A:** a revision-tolerant invariant, i.e. a quantity attached to the current
  finite instance that improves under capture even when the word is re-chosen. The pivot value itself cannot be one,
  because adaptive capture moves it in side-dependent directions (§34.3z¹⁰⁷, `GoodWordPivot.lean`).

## 34.3z¹⁰⁹ ChatGPT brief "revision-tolerant pivot research": fresh-word pivot monotonicity is refuted, and the reason is structural

**Audit (HEAD 2813f63).**
- The brief's premises are correct: pivot extraction, captors, and the pivot-direction lemmas.
- The reduction "Lemma A ⇒ Cut" is valid (via `fresh_strict_up` / `fresh_strict_down` and ACC/DCC).
- Lemma A is false locally, and the reason is combinatorial.

**The structural fact (Lean).**
- **Pivot cells are balanced.** The pivot of a good word is its |P|-th cell, the prefix of |P| letters. So
  |P| − |A| + |B| = |P|, i.e. |A| = |B|.
- **The only forced comparisons are the cell order.** Monotonicity of F forces z(c) ≤ z(c′) exactly along the cell
  order (A′ ⊆ A, B ⊆ B′). No chain of forced inequalities passes through boundary representatives, since that would
  need some p ≤ u.
- **Distinct balanced cells are cell-incomparable** (`balanced_comparable_eq`).
- **Hence pivot progress is never forced** (`pivot_progress_not_forced`, `pivot_regress_not_forced`): after a captor
  p ≥ z* (resp. u ≤ z*), the new pivot cell is never above (resp. below) the old one.
- So Lemma A can hold only through comparabilities of F-values that the finite data do not force.

**Lean.**

| theorem | file | content |
|---|---|---|
| **`exists_pivot`** | `FinGenAll.lean` | extracted from `captured`, with no duplication. A witness-free response table has a nonempty, **balanced** pivot cell (A, B) with z ≰ pᵢ for all i and uⱼ ≰ z for all j. `captured` is now a three-line corollary. |
| `card_filter_val_le` | `FinGenAll.lean` | positions ≤ t of a word of length n > t are t + 1 letters |
| `balanced_comparable_eq` | `GoodWordRevision.lean` | balanced cells comparable in the cell order are equal |
| `pivot_progress_not_forced`, `pivot_regress_not_forced` | `GoodWordRevision.lean` | a captor-separated new pivot cell is never above (below) the old one |
| `fresh_strict_up`, `fresh_strict_down` | `GoodWordRevision.lean` | if a comparison z* ≤ z′ (z′ ≤ z*) holds after a captor, it is strict: the brief's progress step |

The full build passes (1388 jobs), with no `sorry` and standard axioms only. `captured`, `cut_finite` and
`cut_fingen_dcc` still build.

**Deliverable 1: unrestricted re-selection does not rescue the 2 × 2 captor example**
(`experiments/infcapture/fresh_word.py`, `out_fresh_word*.txt`).
- **Method.** For each instance, z3 builds a finite partial order with exact meets and joins (a lattice after
  Dedekind–MacNeille completion). It contains:
  - a down-set, maximal p's and minimal u's;
  - monotone cell values, with no witness on every cell of the enlarged universe;
  - the old pivot data: a genuine pivot of an old good word, on the stated side of D, captured by the new letter;
  - "no balanced cell other than the old pivot lies on the required side of z*".
- **Check.** In the concrete model, every admissible selector system and every good word of the enlarged instance is
  enumerated.

| instance | old pivot | enlarged good-word pivots | on the required side of z* and of D? |
|---|---|---|---|
| case 2, 2 × 2 captor system (+ u₁) | ({p₀}, {u₀}), outside D | ({p₀}, {u₁}), **in D** | none |
| case 2, ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀, u₁}) + u₂ | ({p₀}, {u₀}), outside D | ({p₁}, {u₁}), ({p₁}, {u₂}), both **in D** | none |
| case 1, ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀}) + p₂ | ({p₁}, {u₀}), in D | ({p₂}, {u₀}) | none |
| case 1, ({p₀, p₁}, {u₀, u₁}) + p₂ | ({p₁}, {u₁}), in D | ({p₂}, {u₀}), ({p₂}, {u₁}) | none |
| case 1, ({p₀, p₁, p₂}, {u₀, u₁}) + p₃ | ({p₁, p₂}, {u₀, u₁}), in D | ({p₃}, {u₀}), ({p₃}, {u₁}) | none |

- **Lemma A fails in every instance**, including the brief's sizes (2, 2), (2, 3), (3, 2) and (4, 2).
- **Side-preservation fails too.** In both case-2 instances the old pivot is outside D and every new pivot is inside
  D.
- These are locally realizable counterexamples, i.e. exhaustive verification within explicit finite models. They do not
  refute Cut.

**Deliverable 3: the pivot spectrum** (`pivot_spectrum.py`, `out_pivot_spectrum.txt`).
- **Random selectors.** Both natural one-letter transport identities fail for random selectors:
  - T1 (spectra agree on the common cells) fails in 614/1200, 423/750, 427/750 and 120/180 enlargements at (2, 2),
    (3, 2), (2, 3), (3, 3);
  - T2 (the parity of pivots in a D-filter, i.e. a down-set of cells, is invariant) fails in 317/1200, 156/750,
    197/750 and 46/180.
- **The known 2 × 2 system.** The spectra are {({p₀}, {u₀})} → {({p₀}, {u₁})}: disjoint supports, values
  incomparable, and the D-filtered parity goes 0 → 1 (1 → 0 for (2, 2) + u₂).
- **Verdict.** As instructed, the invariant is discarded. No order-sensitive invariant of the spectrum can survive,
  because by the structural fact the old and new supports carry no forced comparison at all.

**What this says.**
- Response tables z(A, B) = F(⋀A, ⋁B) compare only along the cell order, and pivots are pairwise cell-incomparable.
  So every progress mechanism built from one response table and its enlargements is blind to the order between
  successive pivots.
- **The contrast with the grid.** The proof of `cut_fingen_dcc` gets its comparisons from **iterating** F: the
  asynchronous meet step F(m_T, W(t)) ≤ W(t + 1_T) compares an F-value with a *column* of the grid, which no cell order
  provides.
- **Recommendation.** A revision-tolerant invariant for the infinite case should come from orbit columns, where
  F-iteration creates comparabilities, not from response tables. That brings the problem back to one-sided grid
  capture (`cut_of_grid_capture`): the open question is again how the uncaptured grid columns of successive row
  families relate under F.

## 34.3z¹¹⁰ ChatGPT brief "first-entry frontiers, orbit transitions and finite-support escape": the cube escape lemma, its sharpness, and why work package B is the conjecture

**Audit (HEAD 9712b94).**
- A1–A3 are correct. The brief's inequalities are `boundary_predecessor_response` and `boundary_pairwise_meet`.
- Work package B rests on a misreading. The brief's first-stage target a(⊥) ∨ ⋁{y : b(y) = ⊥} is exactly `rk G 1`, and
  the repository already proves `cutMaps_of_rk_one`: if `rk G 1 ∈ R G` for every G on the lattice, then Cut holds for
  every F.
  - So "first stage for every monotone F" is **equivalent** to the conjecture (`first_stage_iff`, below), not weaker.
  - §34.3z⁴² already recorded this ("the universal R-based statements, even the bottom critical pair, are equivalent
    to the conjecture").
  - **The cardinality-two case is not a milestone either.** In the normal form (R = R₁, a ≡ ⊥ on R) the first stage
    is ⋁R ∈ R, i.e. R principal, which `cut_principal_down` excludes. So the case of a two-element support is Cut
    for exactly those normalized counterexamples whose ⋁R is a join of two elements of R. A two-element support does
    not make R two-generated.

**Lean (`FirstEntryBoundary.lean`; full build passes, 1389 jobs; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `exists_min_boundary` | below every D-valued grid point there is a first-entry point r: every predecessor W(r − eᵢ), rᵢ ≥ 1, is outside D (well-foundedness of ℕᵏ) |
| `meet_row_below_advanced` | F(m_T, W(s)) ≤ W(π_T s + 1_T), where π_T s + 1_T advances the coordinates in T and resets the rest to 0 (via `asynchronous_meet_step`) |
| **`cube_escape`** | for an outside grid point s and nonempty T, any finite family of elements above W(π_T s + 1_T) — e.g. capturers of every boundary point ≤ π_T s + 1_T — has meet ≰ m_T |
| `grid_constraint` | the covering case (T ⊆ labels of W(π_T s + 1_T)) is impossible. This is exactly constraint (iii) of the grid abstraction (§34.3z¹⁰⁵) |
| **`first_stage_iff`** | (∀ G, rk G 1 ∈ R G) ↔ ∀ G, CutMaps G |

`cube_escape` is the correct finite-dimensional form of `capturer_meet_escapes`, in every dimension and for every
arity. Its hypothesis is a **common outside lower point** s of all the chosen boundary points.

**Sharpness (z3, `experiments/infcapture/boundary_cube.py`, `out_boundary_cube*.txt`).**
- **Model.** A preorder model with exact meets; the quotient is a poset, and the Dedekind–MacNeille completion
  preserves the meets. It contains:
  - rows g₀, g₁ ∈ D (maximal among the named D-elements) and m = g₀ ⊓ g₁;
  - orbits from ⊤ with first entry at step 3 (N = (2, 2)) and all grid columns W(t) as exact meets;
  - a staircase first-entry boundary {(3, 0), (2, 1), (1, 2), (0, 3)};
  - monotone cells F(row, W(t)) with no witness on every cross cell;
  - new capturers P_t ≥ W(t), with W(t) below neither row (the uncaptured case).
- **Base consistency.** The base model is SAT (with the free shape too). A first version wrongly imposed
  antisymmetry between differently named elements while also imposing the orbit equalities; that made every query
  UNSAT and was corrected before any result was used.

| capturer set | common outside lower point? | meet ≤ m |
|---|---|---|
| P₂₁, P₁₂ | yes, (1, 1) | **UNSAT** (control, `cube_escape`) |
| P₃₀, P₂₁ | yes, (2, 0) | **UNSAT** (control) |
| P₃₀, P₁₂ | no | **SAT** |
| P₃₀, P₀₃ (face points) | no | **SAT** |
| P₃₀, P₂₁, P₁₂ (pairwise adjacent only) | no | **SAT** |

- **The lemma is sharp.** Without a common outside lower point, capturer meets are unconstrained, and the smallest
  failure is in dimension 2.
- **Pairwise escape does not give triple escape.** Here P₃₀ ⊓ P₂₁ and P₂₁ ⊓ P₁₂ both escape, yet the triple meet can
  lie below m. This is the brief's warning made concrete.
- These are locally realizable finite models, not refutations of Cut.

**Answer to A4–A5 and to the brief's final question.**
- **What holds.** The first-entry incidence structure has exactly one exchange property: escape for families with a
  common outside lower point, which is the grid constraint.
- **What does not.** Families without such a point (non-nested in the sense of §34.3z⁵⁹) are unconstrained locally, in
  every dimension tested.
- **Why finite support does not close the gap.** Finite support of the stabilized aggregates m_n = ⋀Gₙ selects a
  finite subfamily, but nothing makes that subfamily have a common lower point. Escape for it is the very gap.
- So the transitions between successive orbit-grid boundaries do *not* create collective structure beyond `cube_escape`
  locally. The collective structure that exists in the finitely generated case is global: it is `cut_finite` on the
  grid lattice, a Sperner-parity statement over all boundary points at once.
- **The missing finite-dimensional theorem.** A version of the grid argument (`cut_of_grid_capture`) that tolerates
  uncaptured columns: something proved by parity over the whole grid lattice, not by local exchange.
- **Concrete next deliverable.** Apply `cut_finite` (`FinGen.exists_pivot`) to the grid lattice Λ_G of
  `cut_of_grid_capture`, and record *which* uncaptured column its pivot produces. Then test whether successive grids
  can be chosen so that these pivot columns are comparable: they are grid columns, where F-iteration does create
  comparabilities, unlike the response-table pivots of §34.3z¹⁰⁹.

## 34.3z¹¹¹ Continuation: full-capture refinement, the persistent outside parent, and the localization gap

Follow-up to the "next deliverable" of §34.3z¹¹⁰: track the uncaptured columns of successive orbit grids.

**Full-capture refinement.** Rows G₀ ⊆ G₁ ⊆ …, with orbits from ⊤. Each grid is finite by DCC. Gₙ₊₁ adds, for every
D-valued column of the Gₙ-grid that Gₙ leaves uncaptured, one capturer (a maximal element above it, by ACC). Under a
counterexample every stage has an uncaptured D-valued column (`uncaptured_column_exists`).

**Lean (`GridRefinement.lean`; full build passes, 1390 jobs; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**
- `W_le_proj`: a column of the larger grid lies below its projection to the sub-grid.
- **`uncaptured_parent_outside`**: in a full-capture refinement, every column of the larger grid that is not captured
  projects onto an **outside** column of the sub-grid. Otherwise the projection is captured, by an old row or by a new
  capturer, and so is the column below it. No membership hypothesis is needed.
- **`aggregate_contracts`**: every grid column is contracting for the grid's aggregate row:
  F(⋀G, W(t)) ≤ W(t).

**Consequences.**
- **No descent through uncaptured columns.** The uncaptured columns of Gₙ₊₁ are never below uncaptured columns of
  Gₙ: those are captured at stage n + 1. They are always below outside columns of every earlier grid. This answers the
  comparability question of §34.3z¹¹⁰: successive uncaptured columns are related only through their outside parents.
- **The persistent outside parent** (proof; the König step is not formalized):
  - *The tree.* Its level-j nodes are the outside columns of the Gⱼ-grid, finitely many. Each uncaptured column c of
    the Gₙ₊₁-grid gives a path of length n + 1, made of its projections; they are outside by `uncaptured_parent_outside`
    and up-closure of the complement, and descending by `W_le_proj`.
  - *König.* It gives an infinite branch o₀ ≥ o₁ ≥ ⋯ of outside elements. DCC stabilizes it at some o = o_K.
  - *Conclusion.* For every j ≥ K some uncaptured column c (of a later stage) has projection o to Gⱼ, so c ≤ o. These
    columns are pairwise distinct, because a column uncaptured at stage n is captured from stage n + 1 on.
- **Normal form of a counterexample relative to refinement.** There is a fixed outside column o of a finite orbit grid
  G_K with:
  - F(⋀G_K, o) ≤ o (`aggregate_contracts`);
  - infinitely many distinct uncaptured D-valued grid columns below o.

  Each of them is o met with orbit points of rows added later, and none lies below the capturers added before it.

*Correction (§34.3z¹¹²):* the branch can be the all-⊤ branch, so o = ⊤ is possible and this normal form may be vacuous.

**The localization gap (the precise missing step).** The natural next move is to apply induction or Cut inside ↓o,
which is again chain-finite and has the proper cut D ∩ ↓o. It does not transfer:
- With rows restricted below m = ⋀G_K, the map F(x ⊓ m, y) sends ↓o × ↓o into ↓o, because F(m, o) ≤ o.
- A witness y ≤ F(x ⊓ m, y) of this map is a witness of F.
- But a witness F(x ⊓ m, y) ≤ x is not one: it would need F(x ⊓ m, y) ≤ x ⊓ m. Forcing values below m means row
  contraction F(m, y) ≤ m, which no-witness forbids for y ∉ D.
- So ↓o carries a *one-sided* (column-side) reduction only.

**The missing lemma, stated exactly.** A Cut statement for maps on a chain-finite lattice whose row-side witnesses only
need to lie below the row *after* meeting with a fixed m ∈ D, i.e. a relative Cut on the rectangle ↓m × ↓o. That is
the form in which the persistent parent can be exploited.

**Status.**
- *Proved (Lean):* the one-step lemma and the contraction.
- *Proved (paper, standard König + DCC):* the persistent-parent normal form.
- *Open:* the relative rectangle Cut.

## 34.3z¹¹² ChatGPT brief "relative Cut and persistent refuge": a correction to the persistent parent, the localization is an induction over ideals, and the relative grid pivot

**Correction to §34.3z¹¹¹: the persistent parent can be ⊤.**
- The König branch of outside columns may be the all-⊤ branch. An uncaptured column of the next grid may use only the
  coordinates of the newly added capturer rows. Its projection to every earlier grid is then the index-0 column,
  W(0) = ⊤, which is outside D.
- So "infinitely many uncaptured columns below a persistent outside o" holds trivially with o = ⊤. The normal form
  carries information only if o < ⊤, and nothing in the argument forces that.
- Formalizing the König step, which the brief asks for first, would therefore formalize a possibly vacuous statement.
  It is not done. `uncaptured_parent_outside` and `aggregate_contracts` (Lean) remain correct, and so does the one-step
  statement.

**Work package A: localization.**
- **A2 is formalized** (`LocalizedCut.lean`):
  - `weak_witness_normalize` (DCC): a weak row-side witness F(x₀ ⊓ m, y) ≤ x₀ with x₀ ∈ D descends to a fixed
    response x = F(x ⊓ m, y) with x ≤ x₀ and x ∈ D;
  - `weak_witness_escapes`: under no witness, x ≰ m.
  - This is exactly no witness at the cell (x ⊓ m, y), so it adds no information beyond the fixed-point form.
- **The A3/A4 saturation needs a source of weak witnesses, and the only source is Cut.** The only source offered is
  "ordinary Cut applied to H_m on ↓o", i.e. Cut on the chain-finite lattice ↓o.
  - That is the conjecture itself, on a smaller lattice. So the saturation can only be an induction step over
    principal ideals: prove Cut on ↓o assuming Cut on every ↓o′ with o′ < o (DCC well-founded induction).
  - Such a step is equivalent to the conjecture.
  - With o = ⊤, which the correction above allows, ↓o = L, and there is no inductive hypothesis to use.
- **The endpoint.** In case II, o* = o is the situation where a new application of Cut on ↓o may return the same weak
  witness; no measure decreases. No argument for it was found.
- **The finite tests of A5 are not meaningful.** They (weak witness, q ∉ D, the closure returning to o) presuppose a
  persistent tail, which finite lattices cannot carry: they satisfy Cut (`cut_finite`).
- **Status of A.** No rigorous relative-Cut or saturation theorem. The endpoint is open, and the route is an induction
  over ideals whose step is as strong as the conjecture.

**Work package B: the relative grid pivot.** This is the parity argument without the full-capture hypothesis.
- **`relative_grid_pivot`** (`LocalizedCut.lean`, DCC). Run `cut_finite` on the grid lattice with D_Λ the
  **captured** region C_G instead of the true D-region E_G. Then for every finite family of rows gᵢ ∈ D there is a
  D-valued grid column c = W(t), captured by no gᵢ, and a nonempty T with either:
  1. F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T: a row contraction at a D-valued column; or
  2. uᵢ(tᵢ) ∈ D for all i ∈ T: c lies past the entry points of the T-orbits.

  This locates the uncaptured column, where `uncaptured_column_exists` only asserts one.
- **`interior_row_contraction`.** If every D-valued column lying past some orbit entry is captured, there is an
  **interior** uncaptured D-column c (every coordinate before its orbit's entry) with F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T.
- **z3 check** (`boundary_cube.py pivot`, `out_boundary_cube_pivot.txt`; two rows, N = (1, 2) and (2, 2), free
  D-shape):

| constraint set | result |
|---|---|
| every D-valued column captured | UNSAT (= `cut_of_grid_capture`) |
| alternative 1 forbidden everywhere | SAT; every uncaptured column lies on a far face (alternative 2 is needed) |
| columns past an entry captured, alternative 1 forbidden | **UNSAT** (= `interior_row_contraction`) |
| columns past an entry captured | SAT; exactly one interior uncaptured column c, with F(m, c) ≤ m |

**Answer to B.**
- Yes, the finite Sperner argument retains information about the uncaptured region. Its pivot is an uncaptured
  D-valued column carrying a row contraction F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T, a D × D cell that would be a witness if c were outside
  D, unless the uncaptured region reaches the far faces.
- This is a precise, machine-checked relative parity statement.
- **Whether the contraction survives refinement is open.** After a capturer p ≥ c is added, F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T does not
  obviously transfer to the new grid.
- **Next target.** Test whether one can maintain an invariant "the distinguished interior uncaptured column carries a
  row contraction for a fixed T" across full-capture refinements (faces captured at each stage). If the contracting
  rows m_T stabilize (DCC on the meets) while the columns change, the contractions F(m_T, cₙ) ≤ m_T at infinitely many
  distinct D-columns cₙ are the candidate source of a contradiction.

**Lean.** `LocalizedCut.lean` (`weak_witness_normalize`, `weak_witness_escapes`, `relative_grid_pivot`,
`interior_row_contraction`). The full build passes (1391 jobs), with no `sorry` and standard axioms only.

## 34.3z¹¹³ ChatGPT brief "relative pivot refinement, new-row generation and global stabilization": replacement certificates, forced join compression, and a two-step obstruction model

**Audit (HEAD abb7000).**
- A1–A3 are correct, with two simplifications.
  - The response bound F(m_T, c) ≤ p_T of Alternative II needs no Sperner internals. It is
    F(m_T, c) ≤ ⋀_T uᵢ(tᵢ + 1) ≤ ⋀_T uᵢ(tᵢ) = p_T (asynchronous step, then antitone orbits).
  - Alternative II needs no first-entry index: p_T = ⋀_T uᵢ(tᵢ) with uᵢ(tᵢ) ∈ D.
- The brief's same-family warning (§4) is right; nothing below uses `interior_row_contraction` across families.

**Lean (`PivotReplacement.lean`; full build passes, 1392 jobs; no `sorry`; standard axioms only).**

| theorem | content |
|---|---|
| `far_face_meet_is_uncaptured` | Alternative II: p_T ∈ D; c ≤ p_T; every r not capturing c does not capture p_T; F(m_T, c) ≤ p_T |
| `alt1_replacement` | Alternative I: h = F(m_T, c) ∈ D and h ≤ gᵢ for i ∈ T |
| **`alt1_not_expanding`** | at an Alternative-I pivot, ¬ c ≤ F(μ, c) for every μ ≤ m_T: expansion would capture the pivot |
| `unified_replacement` | in both cases some h ∈ D has F(m_T, c) ≤ h |
| `antitone_stabilizes`, `stable_core` | DCC stabilizes the row aggregates μₙ; then μ ≤ c, μ ≤ h, F(μ, c) ≤ h |
| **`join_compression`** (ACC) | if each new pivot is uncaptured by the running join Jₙ = c₀ ⊔ ⋯ ⊔ cₙ whenever Jₙ ∈ D (i.e. Jₙ is added as a row), then some Jₙ ∉ D |

**Deliverables 1 and 2.** These are the first five rows of the table (Lean).

**Deliverable 4: the join dichotomy is decided by ACC, not by the pivots.**
- Under the join-compression rule (add the running join of the pivots as a row while it is in D), **Case I is
  impossible**. The joins stabilize by ACC, and a stable D-valued join would capture the next pivot.
- So every counterexample produces finitely many uncaptured D-valued pivots c₀, …, cₙ with **c₀ ∨ ⋯ ∨ cₙ ∉ D**
  (Case II).
- **The witness route the brief proposes is blocked.** C ≤ F(μ, C) would follow from expansion cⱼ ≤ F(μ, cⱼ) at the
  support pivots. But `alt1_not_expanding` shows expansion is impossible at every Alternative-I pivot.
- **The additional condition needed.** Expansion at enough support pivots to cover C. It holds at no Alternative-I
  pivot, and at Alternative-II pivots nothing forces it.

**Deliverable 3: two replacement steps reach Case II with no witness** (`experiments/infcapture/join_compression.py`,
`out_join_compression.txt`; preorder model with exact meets and joins, completed by Dedekind–MacNeille).
- **Model.**
  - *Step 1:* one row g, orbit γ entering D at γ(2), pivot c₁ = γ(2) (Alternative II, h₁ = c₁), with c₁ ≰ g.
  - *Step 2:* rows {g, c₁}; the new row's orbit δ enters D at δ(N_δ + 1); the grid columns are γ(a) ∧ δ(b). The pivot
    c₂ is a D-valued grid column captured by neither g nor c₁ (nor J₁ = c₁), under Alternative I or II for some
    T ⊆ {g, c₁}.
  - *Case II:* C = c₁ ∨ c₂ ∉ D.
  - *Cells:* rows {g, c₁, μ} × all columns, monotone, with no witness on every cross cell.

| δ entry | step-2 alternative | result | C ≤ F(μ, C)? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | I | **SAT**, c₂ = δ(1) | no |
| 1 | II | **SAT**, c₂ = δ(1) | no |
| 2 | I | **SAT**, c₂ = δ(2) | no |
| 2 | II | **SAT**, c₂ = δ(2) | no |
| 1 | I, plus C ≤ F(μ, C) imposed | UNSAT (control: a witness at (μ, C)) | |

- **Reading.**
  - A stable core can support pivot replacements that reach the forced Case II with no witness, at least for two steps
    with full orbit provenance.
  - The step-2 pivot is the first-entry point of the *new row's* orbit, a column built from the newly added row alone.
    This is the same mechanism that made the persistent parent vacuous (§34.3z¹¹²).
  - These are locally realizable finite models, not refutations of Cut.

**Answer to the final instruction.** The refinement theorem obtained is:
- the aggregate core stabilizes (DCC);
- the running join of the pivots must leave D (ACC);
- the pivot certificates cannot provide the expansion needed for a witness at (μ, C).

Why the replacements stay structurally new: each new row brings its own orbit, and its first-entry point is
automatically an uncaptured candidate pivot. Nothing in the replacement rule relates that point to the earlier rows.

**Missing step and next target.** A rule that ties the orbit of a newly added row to the old grid, e.g. adding rows
only of the form h ≤ m_T (Alternative I), so that the new orbit is dominated by the old one
(`paired_orbit_order`: h ≤ g gives (F h)ⁿ ⊤ ≤ (F g)ⁿ ⊤). Under such a rule the new first-entry point would be bounded
by an old orbit point. The next test is whether pivots restricted to Alternative-I rows (dominated orbits) still
admit an endless replacement sequence.

## 34.3z¹¹⁴ Follow-up (no brief): dominated-row refinement is inert, capturing rows are free

Target set at the end of §34.3z¹¹³: add only rows tied to the old grid (h ≤ m_T), so that new orbits are dominated
and their first-entry points bounded by old orbit points. Lean file `lean/Mordor/DominatedRows.lean`; model
`experiments/infcapture/dominated_rows.py` (output `out_dominated_rows.txt`).

**Proved (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms).** Rows `h ≤ g` (h dominated), orbits from ⊤, F monotone.

| Lemma | Statement |
|---|---|
| `dominated_row_no_capture` | c ≤ h ≤ g ⇒ c ≤ g: a dominated row captures nothing new |
| `capture_needs_nondominated` | if c escapes every old row and c ≤ h, then h ≤ gᵢ for no i |
| `alt1_row_dominated` | F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T ⇒ F(m_T, c) ≤ gᵢ (i ∈ T): Alternative-I rows are dominated |
| `orbit_le_of_le` | h ≤ g ⇒ (F h)ⁿ ⊤ ≤ (F g)ⁿ ⊤ (lattice-with-top form of `paired_orbit_order`) |
| `dominated_column_below_old` | rows rⱼ ≤ g(σ j), section τ of σ: W_r(s) ≤ W_g(s ∘ τ) |
| `dominated_entry_no_later` | (F g)ⁿ ⊤ ∈ D ⇒ (F h)ⁿ ⊤ ∈ D |
| `uncaptured_parent_of_child` | c ≤ P and c escapes every old row ⇒ P escapes every old row |
| `dominated_parent_lt_top` | no witness, g ∈ D, n ≥ 1 ⇒ (F g)ⁿ ⊤ < ⊤ (parents are never the vacuous ⊤ of §34.3z¹¹²) |
| `orbit_stabilizes` | DCC ⇒ each orbit from ⊤ stabilizes, so the old grid has finitely many columns |

**Consequence (proved by composition of the above).** Under refinement by dominated rows:
- the captured region is constant;
- every uncaptured column of the refined grid lies below an uncaptured column of the original grid;
- that original grid is finite (DCC), and its columns of index ≥ 1 are strictly below ⊤.

So a dominated refinement sequence can be continued forever, but it carries no information beyond the finite original
grid. In particular **Alternative-I replacement is inert**: its rows are dominated by `alt1_row_dominated`. Only
Alternative-II rows (pivots past orbit entries) can capture. An uncaptured pivot used as a row is, by
`capture_needs_nondominated`, dominated by no old row, so its orbit is unconstrained by `orbit_le_of_le`. That is the
freedom the §34.3z¹¹³ models exploited.

**Dichotomy.** For a row added in refinement:
- *dominated*: its orbit is tied to the old grid (bounded, enters no later), but it captures nothing new;
- *capturing*: it captures, but it is non-dominated, and no proved relation bounds its orbit by old orbit points.

The §34.3z¹¹³ target (dominated rows with progress) is therefore empty: domination and capture exclude each other.

**Solver (z3, preorder with exact meets, finite diagnostic models; not proof).** One old row g ∈ D with γ entering at
γ(2), one new row h ≤ g, h ∈ D, with δ entering at δ(Nd+1); columns W(a,b) = γ(a) ∧ δ(b); no witness on all cells.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| base consistency, Nd = 0, 1 | SAT |
| base consistency, Nd = 2 (δ enters after γ) | UNSAT (`dominated_entry_no_later`) |
| control: some δ(b) ≰ γ(b), Nd = 0, 1, 2 | UNSAT (`orbit_le_of_le`) |
| uncaptured D-pivot W(a,b) with old parent P = γ(max(a,b)) ≤ g | UNSAT for every (a,b) (`uncaptured_parent_of_child`) |
| uncaptured D-pivot, P ∉ D | SAT for Nd = 0 at W(0,1), W(1,1) (parent γ(1)) |
| uncaptured D-pivot, P ∈ D | SAT whenever max(a,b) = 2 (parent γ(2), an old uncaptured D-column) |
| W(1,0) = γ(1), and for Nd = 1 W(0,1), W(1,1) | UNSAT (not in D) |

Reading: both parent types occur. A dominated refinement can sit under an old uncaptured D-column or under an old
outside column, and never under a captured one.

**Status.**
- Refuted as a route: progress by dominated rows (inert by proof).
- Open: a relation bounding the orbit of a *capturing* (non-dominated) row by old orbit points. Neither the
  replacement certificates nor domination supply one.
- A candidate is a mixed rule: rows h with h ∧ g dominated for some old g and h itself capturing. Its orbit is only
  tied through F(h ∧ g, ·) ≤ F(h, ·), which bounds δ from *below* by the meet orbit. That is the wrong direction for
  bounding first-entry points. This remains to be tested.

## 34.3z¹¹⁵ Follow-up (no brief): the mixed rule is vacuous, and capturing-row orbits are unconstrained

**Correction to §34.3z¹¹⁴ ("Status", last item).** The proposed mixed rule, "rows h with h ∧ g dominated", is vacuous:
h ∧ g ≤ g always holds (`inf_le_right`), so every row satisfies it. Its only content is δ_{h∧g} ≤ δ_h
(`orbit_le_of_le`), a lower bound on the new orbit, which cannot bound a first-entry point from above.

**The fresh-pivot mechanism is already proved.** For a D-row c whose orbit δ from ⊤ first enters D at time E, the entry
point δ(E) satisfies δ(E) ≰ c (`Crossing.endpoint_not_le`, Lemma A of §34.3z¹⁸). So every capturing row produces, by
itself, a new D-point that escapes it. This is the step-2 pivot of the §34.3z¹¹³ models.

**Sharpness of the §34.3z¹¹⁴ dichotomy (z3, preorder with exact meets; finite diagnostic models with partial F, not
proof).** Script `experiments/infcapture/capturing_freedom.py`, output `out_capturing_freedom.txt`.

Setup:
- old rows g₁, g₂ ∈ D, with orbits γ₁, γ₂ from ⊤, each entering D at time 2;
- grid columns W(a, b) = γ₁(a) ∧ γ₂(b);
- the capturing row c is an uncaptured D-column of this grid (c ≰ g₁, c ≰ g₂);
- c's orbit δ enters D at time E;
- no witness on every cell.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| base (no capturing row) | SAT |
| δ enters at E = 1, 2, 3, 4 | SAT for each E (models collapse c with y₂ = z₂ and the W(a,b)) |
| non-degenerate: y₂ ∥ z₂, c ≢ y₂, z₂; E = 1, 2, 3, 4 | SAT for each E (c among the W(a, b)) |
| control: E = 3, 4 with δ(n) ≤ γ₁(n) imposed (orbit domination), both variants | UNSAT (`dominated_entry_no_later`) |

So a capturing row can enter D strictly later than every old orbit, which no dominated row can do. No upper bound on
its orbit by old orbit points holds in general, at least to depth 4.

**Status of the refinement route (§34.3z¹¹¹–¹¹⁵).**
- *Proved:* each refinement row is either dominated, and then inert (§34.3z¹¹⁴), or capturing, and then it generates a
  fresh uncaptured point by itself (`endpoint_not_le`).
- *Solver:* the capturing row's entry time is unconstrained by the old orbits.
- *Consequence:* no row-addition rule built only from orbit comparisons, domination and replacement certificates can
  force termination. Termination would need a relation between F(c, ·) for a column c used as a row and the old
  F(gᵢ, ·). The monotone two-argument signature supplies no such relation: c = W(t) bounds F(c, ·) only by
  F(uᵢ(tᵢ), ·), i.e. by rows that are again orbit points.
- *Open:* the Cut Lemma on chain-finite lattices in which both Max D and Min Dᶜ are infinite. In bounded height, edge
  height 6 is the first open case (§34.3h).

**Next target.** Use the proved finite-grid theorems as derived clauses in the bounded-height ground refutation:
- **Sound:** `uncaptured_column_exists` and `cut_fingen_dcc` instances are theorems.
- **Why they may help:** they encode a Sperner-parity argument, which resolution-based CDCL cannot find efficiently.
  That may be why the edge-height-6 runs (`experiments/sat/cut_ground2.py`, `run_height.py`) stay SAT past several
  hundred terms.
- **Encoding requirement:** grid meets are not terms of the poset theory. They need the lattice ground theory
  (`lattice_ground.py`) or Dedekind–MacNeille cut variables.

## 34.3z¹¹⁶ Follow-up (no brief): grid-capture clauses in the bounded-height ground search do not help

Target from §34.3z¹¹⁵: use the finite-grid theorem as derived clauses in the edge-height-6 lattice ground refutation,
on the hypothesis that its Sperner-parity content is what CDCL is missing.

**Lean (no sorry, standard axioms; `InfiniteCapture.lean`).**
- `cut_of_grid_capture_upto` is the core of `cut_of_grid_capture`, now **without a chain condition**. Its hypotheses:
  - entry times n with every orbit in D at time nᵢ + 1;
  - capture assumed only for grid columns with t ≤ n + 1.

  The DCC in the old statement was used only to find the entry times (`orbit_end`).
- `cut_of_grid_capture` (DCC) is re-derived from it.
- `uncaptured_column_upto`: under no witness, if uᵢ(Nᵢ) ∈ D for all i, then some grid column with t ≤ N is in D and
  below no row. Its instances are sound ground clauses in any lattice: the rows are terms, the orbits F-terms and the
  columns meet terms.

**Ground search** (`experiments/sat/grid_ground.py`; solver runs, inconclusive SAT answers are not results about Cut).
The search is `lattice_ground.LGround` (frontier F-terms, J = 1 lattice term per round) plus grid instances.
- *k = 2 mode (P instances per round):* each instance comes with fresh variables
  x_ab → D(W(a,b)) ∧ W(a,b) ≰ g₁ ∧ W(a,b) ≰ g₂, and the clause is
  ¬D(g₁) ∨ ¬D(g₂) ∨ ¬D(u₁(N₁)) ∨ ¬D(u₂(N₂)) ∨ ⋁ x_ab.
- *maxima mode (P = −1):* one instance per model, whose rows are all maximal D-classes (k ≤ 3), with N = H.

| H | baseline (P = 0) | P = 1 | P = 2 | maxima mode |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 20 terms | | | UNSAT, 65 terms (29 F + 34 lattice), 7 rounds |
| 5 | UNSAT, 147 terms, 85 rounds, 4.9 s | UNSAT, 321 terms (104 F + 215 lattice), 50 rounds, 24.8 s | UNSAT, 379 terms, 48 rounds, 110 s | no result in 590 s (< 25 rounds) |
| 6 | SAT at 474 terms / 15 min (§34.3z⁷²) | SAT at 434 terms (127 F + 305 lattice) / 540 s | | |

Grid clauses cut the number of rounds, but their meet terms cost more than they save.

**Why: the instances are almost never tight** (`grid_bite.py`).
- In 96 % (H = 5, 91 live instance–model pairs) and 100 % (H = 6, 67 pairs) of cases, the instance is already
