  satisfied by an *axis* column: a single orbit point uᵢ(a) in D below neither row.
- By `endpoint_not_le` this holds whenever one orbit's entry point escapes the other row.
- A pair instance carries Sperner content only when each entry point is captured by the other row. The partial models
  avoid that configuration.

**What the partial models look like** (`grid_maxd.py`, H = 6, 120 rounds, 212 terms).
- They collapse to 3–21 classes with **|Max D| ≤ 2 and |Min Dᶜ| ≤ 3** throughout.
- That is the finitely generated regime, where `cut_fingen_dcc` excludes every *total* model.
- They survive by leaving F-cells and orbit terms undefined. When the maxima-mode instance forces the orbits and an
  uncaptured grid column, a fresh meet term becomes a new D-class above the old maxima.
- So the ground search re-enacts the infinite refinement of §34.3z¹¹¹–¹¹⁵ term by term. The height bound, not the
  Sperner argument, has to end it.

**Reading.**
- The hypothesis of §34.3z¹¹⁵ is refuted for this encoding: the finite-grid theorem does not supply the missing
  refutation power at H = 6.
- The obstruction is the same in both settings: every finitary capture certificate can be met by a new maximal
  element. In the ground search, the new maximal element is a fresh term class.
- A useful derived lemma would have to tie the *height* to the number of fresh maxima, which grid capture does not do.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open on chain-finite lattices with Max D and Min Dᶜ both infinite, and at edge-height 6.

## 34.3z¹¹⁷ ChatGPT brief "axis-capture graphs, bounded support and the height-six obstruction": the decomposition and the support bound are theorems; no height progress is visible in partial models

**Audit.** HEAD 6717a15, as the brief states. Its claims, checked against the source:
- **A2:** correct. Situation II meets the `hfaces` hypothesis of `LocalizedCut.interior_row_contraction`, because every
  D-valued axis point uᵢ(n) has n ≥ Eᵢ and so lies below eᵢ. An interior pivot then rules out Alternative II.
- **A3:** correct.
- **A4:** both facts were already in Lean: `InfCapture.maximal_pairwise_join_outside` and
  `minimal_pairwise_meet_inside`.
- **B1:** the proof is correct. It needs only *subset*-minimality of the support, and it **strengthens** (below).
- **B3 (the Ramsey dichotomy):** a conjecture, as the brief says. It was not attempted; see Result 4 for why the
  finite constraints cannot decide it.

**Result 1 (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms).** `lean/Mordor/BoundedSupportPivot.lean`. Here S is minimal for P:
P(⋀_S v) holds, and no proper subset satisfies it.

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `inf_strict_of_minimal` | A ⊂ B ⊆ S ⇒ ⋀_B v < ⋀_A v |
| `minimal_chain` | ⊥ < ⋀_S v ⇒ for A ⊆ S, `chainTo` (⋀_A v) (\|S \\ A\| + 1) |
| `minimal_support_card` (**B1**) | `NoChain α (H+1)` (edge-height ≤ H) and ¬P ⊥ ⇒ \|S\| + 1 ≤ H |
| `exists_minimal_support` | a minimal S ⊆ S₀ exists whenever P(⋀_{S₀} v) |
| `proper_partial_meet_outside` | for P = `Good` (in D, below no row): every proper partial meet is ∉ D |
| `orbit_support_bound` (**B1 strengthened**) | mixed minimal pivot of orbit points, \|S\| ≥ 2, i ∈ S ⇒ tᵢ + \|S\| ≤ H |

The strengthening works because uᵢ(tᵢ) ∉ D is a proper partial meet. The orbit therefore descends strictly from ⊤ to
uᵢ(tᵢ): outside D, no witness gives uᵢ(n) ≰ F(gᵢ, uᵢ(n)). That descent prepends tᵢ strict steps to the
partial-meet chain.

At H = 6 a mixed minimal pivot thus has 2 ≤ |S| ≤ 5, and every support coordinate has tᵢ ≤ 6 − |S| ≤ 4.

`lean/Mordor/AxisCapture.lean` (Program A, same standards). Rows gᵢ ∈ D, exact first-entry times Eᵢ, entry points eᵢ:

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `entry_pos`, `no_loop` | Eᵢ ≥ 1; eᵢ ≰ gᵢ (the graph i → j ⟺ eᵢ ≤ gⱼ is loopless) |
| `interior_iff` | uᵢ(n) ∈ D ⟺ Eᵢ ≤ n |
| `axis_escape_iff` | some D-valued axis point of row i is uncaptured ⟺ out-degree(i) = 0 |
| `axis_or_mixed` (**A2**, DCC) | Situation I (a vertex of out-degree 0) **or** an interior (tᵢ < Eᵢ ∀i) uncaptured D-column c with a nonempty T and F(m_T, c) ≤ m_T |
| `exists_cycle` | every choice of out-neighbours has a periodic point, so Situation II has a directed cycle, of length ≥ 2 |
| `mixed_minimal_support` (**A2 + B1**) | in Situation II at edge-height ≤ H: some interior uncaptured column has a minimal support S with 2 ≤ \|S\|, \|S\| + 1 ≤ H, and tᵢ + \|S\| ≤ H on S |
| `new_maximum` (**A3**, ACC) | rows maximal in D, c ∈ D uncaptured ⇒ there is a maximal q ≥ c in D, incomparable with every row |

Corollary of `mixed_minimal_support`: Situation II is impossible at edge-height ≤ 2.

**Result 2 (exhaustive finite enumeration).** Loopless digraphs with every out-degree ≥ 1, up to isomorphism
(`experiments/sat/axis_capture.py classify`):
- k = 2: one graph, the two-cycle;
- k = 3: seven graphs. Edge lists:
  - {01, 10, 20}
  - {01, 02, 10, 20}
  - {01, 12, 20}
  - {01, 02, 10, 21}
  - {01, 02, 12, 21}
  - {01, 02, 10, 12, 20}
  - the complete digraph

Every one of them is realizable together with all ladder constraints (Result 3).

**Result 3 (partial SAT models; preorder with exact meets and joins; F only on the listed cells; height imposed by a
rank function on the named elements).**

Ladder (cumulative):

| Stage | Constraint added |
|---|---|
| S0 | orbits with exact entry times, the graph imposed exactly (edges and non-edges), no witness on all cells |
| S1 | rows pairwise incomparable and maximal |
| S2 | interior uncaptured D-pivot |
| S3 | Alternative I (cells F(m_T, c)) |
| S4 | new maximum q ≥ c |
| S5 | q's orbit, and joins q ∨ gᵢ ∉ D |
| S6 | minimal complement witnesses below the joins, with pairwise meets in D |

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| control: a loop e₀ ≤ g₀ (H = 6) | UNSAT (`no_loop`) |
| control: pivot W(2,1), E = (3,3), at H = 3 (t + \|S\| = 4) | UNSAT (`orbit_support_bound`); SAT at H = 4 |
| control: Situation II at H = 2 / H = 3 | UNSAT / SAT |
| S0–S6, k = 2, E ∈ {(2,2), (2,3), (3,3)}, at H = 5 and at H = 6 | SAT at every stage |
| least H with S0–S6 SAT | **max Eᵢ + 1** for every case: 3 for all eight graphs with Eᵢ = 2, and 4 for E = (2,3), (3,3) |

SAT is monotone in H, so the least-H runs settle the H = 5 and H = 6 rows for every graph.

**The smallest model** (k = 2, E = (2,2), H = 3, E_q = 1). It has 21 named elements in 13 classes:
- the two-cycle u₀(2) ≤ g₁, u₁(2) ≤ g₀;
- the pivot c = u₀(1) ∧ u₁(1), equal to the new maximum q;
- Alternative I through F(m₀₁, c) = ⊥;
- q ∨ gᵢ = ⊤;
- two complement atoms w₀, w₁ below ⊤ with w₀ ∧ w₁ = ⊥.

Its height is exactly that of the orbit chain ⊤ > uᵢ(1) > uᵢ(2) > ⊥.

**Result 4 (partial SAT models; the candidate height-progress rule is not supported).** Iterated cycle-to-new-maximum
(`experiments/sat/axis_iterate.py`):
- Start from a k-cycle in Situation II.
- At step s, the current family R must be in Situation II.
- It must have an interior uncaptured pivot c_s, a meet of ≥ 2 interior axis points.
- A new maximal row q_s ≥ c_s must be incomparable with R.
- q_s's orbit must have its entry point captured by R, so that Situation II persists.

| Start | Steps n | Least edge-height |
|---|---|---|
| two-cycle | 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 | 3, 3, 3, 3, 3 |
| three-cycle | 0, 1, 2, 3 | 3, 3, 3, 3 |

Height 3 is the minimum forced by the orbit chain alone.

**Reading.**
- With orbit cells, capture data, maximality and the opposite-boundary facts, repeated mixed-pivot generation is
  realizable at constant height 3, a height at which Cut is a theorem (§34.3f).
- So no height-progress theorem can be derived from these data alone, and Program B3's dichotomy cannot be decided
  from them either. A proof must use F on cells *outside* the orbit and pivot cells, i.e. totality of F.
- The proved part, tᵢ + |S| ≤ H, is the only height-sensitive constraint, and it bounds each pivot *individually*.
  It says nothing about how successive pivots relate.

**Candidate (conjecture, untested).** A height-progress rule would have to compare the cells F(q_s, ·) of a new
maximum with the old rows on the *interior* axis points uᵣ(1) (r ∈ R), i.e. the values F(q_s, uᵣ(tᵣ)). These are
the cells that the partial models leave undefined.

**Not done.**
- Program B4 (`support_ramsey.py`): the iteration model is its simplest homogeneous family, and the label space is
  finite by `mixed_minimal_support`. A full label enumeration would only add more partial models.
- Program C: superseded by Result 4. Cross-capture information does not separate H = 5 from H = 6 in partial models.

## 34.3z¹¹⁸ ChatGPT brief "cross-cell closure and boundary interaction": the envelope lemmas are proved; cross cells and seeding add no height constraint

**Audit.** HEAD 469c396, as the brief states.
- **The cross-envelope lemma (A2) is correct and shallow.** It is the no-witness condition y ≰ F(q, y) at the single
  cell (q, y), plus monotonicity F(c, xᵢ) ≤ F(c, y) ≤ F(q, y) ≥ F(q, xᵢ). It needs only c ≤ q with q ∈ D.
  Maximality of q, first-entry orbits and minimal support play no role.
- **There is a row-side dual.** Because c = ⋀ xᵢ, the condition F(c, z) ≰ c, for every outside z, gives a coordinate
  with F(c, z) ≰ x_{i₁}.
- **The B dichotomy is correct.**
- **Package C, taken to its end (closure under all cross cells), is the lattice ground search** (§34.3h, §34.3z⁷²).
  So the new experiment is to *seed* that search with the mixed-pivot configuration (below).

**First deliverable (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/CrossCell.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `sup_not_mem` | xᵢ ∉ D on a nonempty S ⇒ ⋁ xᵢ ∉ D |
| `cross_rectangle_bounds` | F(c, xᵢ) ≤ F(c, y) ≤ F(q, y), F(q, xᵢ) ≤ F(q, y); no witness at (c, y) and (q, y) |
| `cross_envelope_escape` | ∃ i₀ ∈ S: x_{i₀} ≰ F(q, y), and x_{i₀} ≰ F(c, xᵢ), x_{i₀} ≰ F(q, xᵢ) for all i ∈ S |
| `meet_side_escape` | c = ⋀_S x ∈ D, z ∉ D ⇒ ∃ i₁ ∈ S, F(c, z) ≰ x_{i₁} |
| `cross_output_boundary_extension` | q maximal, x ∉ D: either F(q, x) ∈ D lies below a maximal q′ ∥ q, or F(q, x) ∉ D lies above a minimal w ∉ D with x ≰ w |
| `blind_two_point_form` | minimal-support mixed pivot, \|S\| ≥ 2, q ≥ c in D: the blind i₀ has c′ = ⋀_{S∖{i₀}} x ∉ D, x_{i₀} ⊓ c′ = c, and x_{i₀} ≰ F(q, x_{i₀} ⊔ c′) |

**Answers to A3.**
1. S ∖ {i₀} is not a support. Its meet c′ is an outside point (`proper_partial_meet_outside`), so (q, c′) is one more
   cross cell.
2. The pivot always has the two-point form c = x_{i₀} ⊓ c′ with both factors outside D. The blind factor escapes
   F(q, ·) on the whole interval [c′, x_{i₀} ⊔ c′], since F(q, ·) is monotone and bounded by F(q, x_{i₀} ⊔ c′).
   No proved statement makes i₀ persist across refinements.
3. and 4. Nothing found. See the models below.

**Second deliverable (partial SAT models).** `experiments/sat/cross_cells.py`, output `out_cross_cells.txt`. The base is
the ladder S0–S6 of §34.3z¹¹⁷, with these additions:
- the exact join y of all interior orbit points;
- the full cross rectangle F(c, x), F(q, x) for x ∈ X ∪ {y}, monotone with every other named cell and witness-free;
- the output branches: a named maximal q_z ≥ z, or a named minimal w_z ≤ z with x ≰ w_z;
- optionally, the boundary layer: pairwise joins of the named maxima outside D, and pairwise meets of the named
  minimal complements in D.

| Configuration | Least edge-height |
|---|---|
| k = 2, E = (2,2), rectangle / with boundary layer | 3 / 3 |
| k = 2, E = (2,3), rectangle / with boundary layer | 4 / 4 |
| k = 2, E = (3,3), rectangle | 4 (with the boundary layer: not finished in 590 s) |
| k = 3, E = (2,2,2), all seven graphs, rectangle | 3 |
| two-cycle, all q-responses forced into branch I / branch II | 3 / 3 |
| iterated new maxima with cross-generation cells F(q_s, u_r(1)), r in the current family (`axis_iterate.py … cross`) | 3 for n ≤ 4 (k = 2) and n ≤ 3 (k = 3) |

As before, the least height is the orbit chain max Eᵢ + 1. Cross cells defined explicitly, including cross-generation
cells, add no height constraint.

**Seeded ground search: totality, as far as a solver can take it.** `experiments/sat/seeded_ground.py`, output
`out_seeded_ground.txt`.
- **Base:** `lattice_ground` (every axiom family as ground instances; frontier F-terms and lattice terms per round).
- **Seed constants:** g₀, g₁, q, maximal over all terms present and future, and pairwise incomparable.
- **Seed constraints:**
  - orbits uᵢ(1) ∉ D, uᵢ(2) ∈ D;
  - the two-cycle;
  - the pivot c = u₀(1) ⊓ u₁(1) ∈ D, uncaptured, with c ≤ q;
  - Alternative I over T ⊆ {0, 1}.
- **Branch I / II:** additionally the cells F(q, u₀(1)), F(q, u₁(1)), F(q, y), forced into D / out of D.
- **No WLOG unit:** order duality does not preserve the seed, so the baseline omits it too.
- **Soundness:** every clause holds in any witness-free lattice of edge-height ≤ H that contains the configuration. UNSAT
  therefore excludes such lattices.

| H | no seed | seed | seed, branch I | seed, branch II |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | UNSAT, 13 terms | UNSAT, 17 | UNSAT, 21 | UNSAT, 21 |
| 4 | UNSAT, 38 | UNSAT, 65 | **UNSAT, 39** | UNSAT, 71 |
| 5 | SAT at 333 / 278 s | SAT at 413 / 480 s | SAT at 357 / 576 s | SAT at 383 / 578 s |
| 6 | | | SAT at 369 / 577 s | |

**Third deliverable: which branch carries more.**
- In the ground search, branch I (D-valued q-responses, i.e. new maximal elements) closes H = 4 with 39 terms.
  Branch II needs 71, and the unforced seed 65 (solver evidence at one height only).
- In the partial models neither branch changes the least height.
- So branch I is the stronger one, but only as measured by search effort.

**Reading.**
- *The seed does not help.* It costs terms at H = 3 and 4, and H = 5 does not close within budget with or without it.
  The cut theory alone, with the WLOG unit, closes H = 5 at 147 terms (§34.3z⁷²). In this search the order-duality
  symmetry carries more refutation power than the mixed-pivot configuration.
- *What totality contributes* is exactly what the ground search enumerates: values of F at pairs that no
  configuration names. The known H = 3 certificate (§34.3f) uses F(⊥, ⊤), F(⊥, c₁), F(c₂, ⊤), F(c₂, c₁), which are
  ⊥-row and orbit cells, not pivot-rectangle cells. No unsat core of the seeded runs was extracted, so which cells
  their refutations use was not measured.

**Fourth deliverable: the cross-generation closure theorem, and the missing step.** Conjecture B1 asks that the
incidence structure cannot stay closed under:
- F(q_s, x);
- maximal and minimal extension;
- boundary joins and meets.

A finite set closed under F on cross pairs and under ⊓, ⊔ would be a finite witness-free model, which `cut_finite`
excludes. So closure always fails at a finite stage. Persistence means an infinite closed structure, i.e. an infinite
counterexample of height ≤ H containing the seed. **Conjecture B1 is therefore the bounded-height Cut Lemma restricted
to counterexamples that contain the seed, not a step toward it.**

The missing step is a finite-model property: a witness-free bounded-height structure generated by a finite seed must
have a finite quotient that keeps D, monotonicity and no witness. Every incremental route tried so far, refinement,
capture, cross cells and seeding, is an approximation of that quotient.

**Status.**
- *Proved:* the envelope lemmas above.
- *Partial SAT models:* no height progress.
- *Solver:* seeding does not shorten the ground refutation.
- *Open:* Cut at edge-height 6, and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹¹⁹ ChatGPT brief "finite-model property and faithful quotients": the quotient implication is proved and needs no lattice; the obstruction is cut-separation, not finite index

**Audit.** HEAD f6ef7f7, as the brief states.
- **A3 is correct.** Q3 is exactly "no witness in Q", because [F] ≤ [a] ⟺ [F ⊓ a] = [F]. Monotonicity and the
  down-set property descend, and `cut_finite` finishes.
- **No lattice is needed.** The repository already proves Cut on finite bounded *posets*
  (`RelativeCut.cut_finite`) and finite cross-capture (`RelativeCut.cut_cross`). So a lattice congruence is more than
  the implication needs.
- **A4 does not reduce Cut.** The generated algebra A is bounded with ACC and DCC, hence a chain-finite complete
  lattice. A4 is a *filtration* strategy for Cut, valid in form, with all the content in the construction.

**Deliverable 1 (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/FiniteQuotient.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `faithful_image_impossible` | no witness-free F has a finite bounded poset image (Q, G monotone, D̄ down-set, ⊥ ∈ D̄ ∌ ⊤) whose cross pairs lift to cross pairs of L carrying G-witnesses to F-witnesses |
| `finite_cut_faithful_quotient_impossible` | the brief's A3: π a surjective lattice homomorphism onto a finite bounded lattice, with π ∘ F = F̄ ∘ (π × π), D = π⁻¹ D̄, and Q3 on cross pairs ⇒ False. Monotonicity of F̄, the down-set property and the bounds are derived |
| `separating_family_witness` (**half-filtration**) | a finite meet-closed T ∋ ⊥, ⊤ such that every cross pair x ∈ T ∩ D, y ∈ T ∖ D has some s ∈ T with F(x, y) ≤ s and y ≰ s ⇒ a witness exists |
| `separating_family_witness_dual` | the dual: join-closed T, with s ≤ F(x, y) and s ≰ x |
| `omega_witness_free`, `omega_no_faithful_image` | ω + 1 with D = ℕ and F(x, y) = x + 1 is monotone and witness-free (DCC holds, ACC fails), so it has no finite faithful image |
| `omega_two_class_congruence` | there, "a = ⊤ ⟺ b = ⊤" is a congruence for ⊓, ⊔, F; it is D-saturated with two classes; and Q3 fails on it (F(n, ⊤) ⊓ n ≡ F(n, ⊤)) |

How the half-filtration works:
- Take F̄ = cl_T ∘ F, where cl_T is the closure onto T.
- The half F̄(x, y) ≤ x of a witness transfers to L **for free**, since z ≤ cl_T z.
- The separation hypothesis is exactly what the other half needs.
- Cross-closure F(x, y) ∈ T, as in `cut_cross`, is the special case s = F(x, y).

So any finite filtration by a closure (dually, by an interior) has one half of no-witness for free. The whole finite-model
problem is to *separate* y from F(x, y), dually F(x, y) from x, by finitely many elements.

**Deliverable 2: the partition refinement and its exact stability condition.** The refinement of B2 computes the
**syntactic (Myhill–Nerode) congruence** θ_D of D:
- **Definition:** a θ_D a′ ⟺ for every unary polynomial p over (A, ⊓, ⊔, F) with constants, p(a) ∈ D ⟺ p(a′) ∈ D.
- **Computation:** start from {D, Dᶜ}. Split by the classes of a ⊓ b, a ⊔ b, F(a, b), F(b, a) over all b. The fixed
  point is θ_D, the coarsest D-saturated congruence.
- **Finite index:** θ_D has finite index ⟺ D is recognizable in A.
- **Finite-cut-faithful congruences:** any such θ is finer than θ_D. By `finite_cut_faithful_quotient_impossible`, every
  finite-index D-saturated congruence of a witness-free algebra violates Q3.

**Deliverable 3: the decisive test, and what it shows.**
- On a finite structure every congruence violates Q3. The identity gives L itself, which has a witness by `cut_finite`.
- On the partial models of §34.3z¹¹⁷–¹¹⁸, "including every generated cross cell" is the ground search, whose
  stabilization would be a finite witness-free model.
- So the test cannot stabilize anywhere: it is not a test of the conjecture.
- The informative case is an *infinite* witness-free structure. The only ones available lack a chain condition, and
  ω + 1 gives the answer there: **θ_D has two classes (finite index), and Q3 fails.** The obstruction is
  cut-separation, not the number of observational types.
- Ramsey thinning (B3) makes types finite and so attacks the wrong property.

**Deliverable 4: the height-six classification. Not produced.**
- §34.3z¹¹⁸ shows that the seeded search does not close even H = 5 without the WLOG unit (SAT at 333 to 413 terms).
- Order duality does not preserve a seed, so no seeded H = 6 run can discriminate between support patterns within
  the budget: every pattern would "survive" vacuously.
- The H = 5 certificate was inspected instead, as C1 asks.

**C1: anatomy of the Lean-checked certificates** (`experiments/sat/core_anatomy.py`, output `out_core_anatomy.txt`).
These are drat-trim cores, not minimal cores.

| | H = 4 | H = 5 |
|---|---|---|
| F-terms, max depth | 15, 4 | 84, 7 |
| distinct rows | 4 | 17 |
| ⊥-row cells | 6 | 20 |
| terms on some orbit from ⊤ | 8 | 25 |
| ⊥-orbit length | 4 | 5 |
| no-witness: F ≰ x side / y ≰ F side / both | 8 / 13 / 7 | 42 / 60 / 30 |
| clause families | mono 23, rank 22, now 21, trans 9 | mono 233, rank 195, trans 193, now 102, down 30 |
| WLOG unit F(⊥, ⊤) ∉ D | 1 | 1 |

At H = 5, 59 of the 84 cells are *off-orbit*: F(r, x) with x not on r's own orbit from ⊤. These are the cells that
no orbit, pivot or cross-rectangle partial model contains. The ⊥ row is the most used row, the y ≰ F side of
no-witness occurs more often, and the WLOG normalization enters as a single unit clause.

**Deliverable 5: the minimal obstruction.**
- *Proved:* finite-cut-faithful images exist for no witness-free structure, chain-finite or not
  (`faithful_image_impossible`).
- *Proved:* a witness-free structure without ACC exists, ω + 1. Its coarsest D-saturated congruence is finite and
  violates Q3 (`omega_two_class_congruence`).
- *Hence:* the finite-separation conjecture is false without chain conditions, and the property that fails is Q3
  (cut-separation), not finite index. A proof must use ACC and DCC exactly where Q3 is established.
- *Half-filtration:* the closure retraction gives Q3's first half for free, so the precise remaining statement is:

  > **Separation conjecture.** In a chain-finite lattice with monotone F and a cut D, some finite meet-closed
  > T ∋ ⊥, ⊤ separates every cross pair of T: for all x ∈ T ∩ D and y ∈ T ∖ D, some s ∈ T satisfies F(x, y) ≤ s
  > and y ≰ s. Alternatively, some finite join-closed T dually separates: s ≤ F(x, y) and s ≰ x.

  By `separating_family_witness` and its dual, the conjecture implies Cut. The converse is not claimed:
  - Separation alone fails for F ≡ ⊤. The pair (⊥, ⊤) can never be separated, since its only upper bound ⊤ is ≥ ⊤,
    although Cut holds there via ⊤ ≤ F(⊥, ⊤).
  - Whether Cut implies "separation or dual separation" is open.

  ω + 1 shows how both forms fail without ACC. Every finite T has a largest finite element m, and F(m, ⊤) = m + 1:
  - its only T-upper bound is ⊤, which is ≥ ⊤, so separation fails at (m, ⊤);
  - a dual separator would have to lie below m + 1 and not below m, i.e. equal m + 1 ∉ T.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite. The
finite-model route is now stated as the separation conjecture, a sufficient condition for Cut whose content is the
use of ACC (dually DCC) in separating y from F(x, y), or F(x, y) from x.

## 34.3z¹²⁰ ChatGPT brief "finite separators and boundary uniformization": extremal separators are meet-irreducible, aggregation has an exact criterion, and the stage bound is the testable form

**Audit.** HEAD 98571a4, as the brief states.
- **A1 and A2 are correct.**
- **A3 is correct as a procedure.** It is well-defined in every lattice with ACC: each Tₙ is finite, and maximal
  separators exist. Its termination with no bad pair contradicts no-witness (`separating_family_witness`).
- **A4's warnings are correct.** Joins of separators lose y ≰ s. The smallest case is M₃: the separators b and c of
  the column a join to ⊤ ≥ a.
- **Package C is already settled by §34.3z⁴².** `Stable.normalize` turns every cut into one with D = R(G), the same
  cross values, a ≡ ⊥ on R, and R = R₁. So "R(F) is join-closed" is literally "D is join-closed" for a witness-free
  cut; specializing to R(F) adds no structure the separation problem could use.
- **Package B (mixed filtration) is again equivalent to producing a finite witness-free system**
  (`faithful_image_impossible`). For a finite Q ⊆ L, the value G(u, v) must lie in the window
  W_Q(u, v) = {t ∈ Q : t ≰ u, v ≰ t}, and `cut_finite` excludes every monotone selection from windows on every
  finite Q. So no bracket construction can be monotone on a finite Q under no-witness. On ω + 1 the window of the
  top cross pair (m, ⊤), m the largest finite element of Q, is already empty.

**Task 1 (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/ExtremalSeparators.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `exists_upper_separator` (ACC) | under no witness, some s ≥ F(x, y) has y ≰ s, and every t > s has y ≤ t |
| `exists_lower_separator` (DCC) | some s ≤ F(x, y) has s ≰ x, and every t < s has t ≤ x |
| `upper_separator_iff` | maximal in U⁺(x, y) ⟺ above F(x, y) and maximal in V(y) = {s : y ≰ s}; x enters only through F(x, y) ≤ s |
| `upper_separator_inf_irreducible`, `upper_separator_cover` | a maximal element of V(y) is ⊓-irreducible; every t > s lies above s ⊔ y > s |
| `lower_separator_sup_irreducible`, `lower_separator_cover` | duals |
| `upper_separator_mono_col` | a separator for y₀ separates every y ≥ y₀ |
| `common_separator_iff` (**A4, exact**) | finitely many pairs (zᵢ, yᵢ) share one landmark ⟺ yᵢ ≰ ⋁ⱼ zⱼ for all i; then ⋁ zⱼ is one |
| `common_lower_separator_iff` | dual: ⋀ zⱼ ≰ xᵢ for all i |
| `separated_iff_cl`, `dual_separated_iff_int` (**A2**) | separated by a finite meet-closed T ⟺ y ≰ cl_T(F(x, y)); dually int_T(F(x, y)) ≰ x |

**Consequences.**
- Upper landmarks can always be taken ⊓-irreducible, and a finite meet-closed T is the meet-closure of finitely many
  of them.
- The brief's Case 2 (every recorded y_α ≤ p = ⋁ S₀) is exactly the failure of `common_separator_iff` for the
  recorded pairs. In that case no single landmark serves them. The minimal complement u ≤ p and the meets u ⊓ s
  (each = u or ∈ D) give lower bounds. A separator must lie *above* F(x_α, y_α), so these meets are not candidates.
  The aggregation route therefore gives no new separators.

**Task 2: the process, and exactly why it may continue.**
- **Where ACC is used:** ACC is used in exactly one place, the existence of s⁺. On ω + 1, U⁺(m, ⊤) = {m + 1, m + 2, …}
  has no maximal element. With the non-extremal choice s = F(x, y), the process runs forever:
  Tₙ = {0, …, n, ω}.
- **Why it may continue in a chain-finite counterexample:** each new landmark creates new cross pairs, and its
  irreducibility does not prevent that.
  - An added s ∈ D becomes a row of new pairs (s, y′).
  - An added s ∉ D becomes a column of new pairs (x′, s).
  - Meets of landmarks add more of both.
- Termination is not implied by Cut. The process stops only when no pair is bad or a stuck pair with y ≤ F(x, y)
  appears.

**Experiments** (`experiments/separation/finite_landmarks.py`, output `out_finite_landmarks.txt`; random and hill-climb
search on finite lattices, which always end).

| Search | Result |
|---|---|
| 400 random instances (closure systems on 3–5 points, height 2–5) | max stages per height 1, 2, 3, 4; stages ≤ height − 1 throughout |
| hill-climbing on (F, D), 3 × 560 s, heights 2–6 | max stages per height {2: 1, 3: 3, 4: 5, 5: 5, 6: 5}: **stages ≤ height − 1 is false** (3 stages at height 3, 5 at height 4) |
| height-3 linear spaces on 3–10 points (\|L\| up to 22), hill-climbing | max stages 3, 2, 3, 2, 4, 2, 3, 1: no growth with width visible, with a thinner budget at larger width |

**Which route has the best chance.** The best candidate is a uniform stage bound at fixed height, because it is a
statement that finite search can refute:

> **Stage-bound conjecture SB(H).** There is f(H) such that, in every lattice of edge-height ≤ H, for every monotone F
> and cut D, the extremal-separator process ends, separated or stuck, within f(H) stages.

- **SB(H) ⇒ Cut at edge-height ≤ H.** In an infinite witness-free structure the process never sticks (no y ≤ F), so
  ending means a separating T.
- **The converse is unclear.** An infinite lattice *with* witnesses could make the process run forever.
- **Finite data:** f(H) = H − 1 is refuted. Growth with width at fixed height was not observed at H = 3 (random search
  evidence only). Cut is proved at H ≤ 5, so the test case is H = 6, and searching widths there needs a faster
  implementation.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²¹ ChatGPT brief "uniform termination of extremal-separator refinement": the process in Lean, the causal chain, and f(4) ≥ 4

**Audit.** HEAD 4666311, as the brief states. The brief describes the process (§2) and the implication SB(H) ⇒ Cut
(§3) correctly. The quantification over separator choices matters, see below.

**Deliverables 1–2 (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/SeparatorStages.lean`.**

| Declaration | Content |
|---|---|
| `meetClose`, `subset_meetClose`, `top_mem_meetClose`, `meetClose_closed` | the stage operator: meets of all subsets (a `Finset`, so every stage is finite) |
| `Separated`, `Bad`, `IsMaxSep` | separation by T; a cross pair of T that T does not separate; a maximal upper separator |
| `Step T T′` | T′ = meetClose(T ∪ S): S consists of maximal separators of bad pairs of T, with one for every bad pair (existential in the choice) |
| `Run T` | T₀ = {⊥, ⊤}; each stage is terminal (no bad pair) and repeated, or followed by a `Step` |
| `run_closed`, `run_mono`, `separated_mono` | every stage is meet-closed and contains ⊥, ⊤; separations persist |
| `step_separates`, `step_strict` | bad pairs are separated at the next stage; a stage with a bad pair grows strictly |
| `step_exists` (ACC, no witness) | every finite T has a step |
| `terminal_witness`, `cut_of_terminal_run` | **a run that reaches a terminal stage, or a stuck bad pair (y ≤ F(x, y)), yields a witness** |
| `no_witness_strictMono`, `exists_run` (ACC) | without a witness every run grows strictly at every stage, and a run exists |
| `bad_after_step_new`, `new_element_below_separator` | **causal chain**: a bad pair of stage n + 1 involves an element of Tₙ₊₁ ∖ Tₙ, and every such element lies below a separator chosen at stage n |

So an N-stage run carries a chain of separators s₀, …, s_{N−1}. Each sₙ separates a bad pair (xₙ, yₙ), i.e.
F(xₙ, yₙ) ≤ sₙ and yₙ ≰ sₙ, and xₙ₊₁ ≤ sₙ or yₙ₊₁ ≤ sₙ. The dual process (lower separators, join-closure) is
symmetric. It is not formalized separately.

**Deliverable 3: quantification, and the strongest conjecture the data allow.**
- *Existential* ∃-SB(H): some run (some choice of separators) ends within f(H) stages. This suffices for Cut
  (`cut_of_terminal_run`).
- *Universal* ∀-SB(H): every run ends within f(H). This implies ∃-SB.
- *Deterministic*: a fixed rule, here greedy ascent through the lowest-indexed upper cover.

Measured on small lattices (`compare`, 300 instances, exact branching): **existential minimum ≤ greedy ≤ universal
maximum**. The existential minimum is below greedy in 10 instances and the universal maximum above it in 18, with
gaps of at most 2.

**Deliverable 4: adversarial search** (`experiments/separation/separator_stages.py`; bitset closure-system lattices,
exact branching for the existential minimum; random search on finite lattices, which always end). Output
`out_separator_stages.txt`; replayable maximal instances, with lattice, F table, D and trace, in `stage_records.json`.

| Height | max greedy stages | max existential minimum (hill-climb on the existential count) |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | | 2 |
| 4 | | **4** (\|L\| = 12; universal maximum also 4) |
| 5 | 6 (\|L\| = 27; existential 2 on that instance) | 4 |
| 6 | 6 (\|L\| = 24; existential 1 on that instance) | 5 (\|L\| = 27) |
| 7 | 6 | 6 |

- **f(H) = H − 1 is refuted in every reading.** The height-4 instance has existential minimum = universal maximum = 4.
  Its lattice is the closure system {∅, 1, 2, 3, 4, 23, 14, 24, 34, 124, 234, 1234} on four points; the replay
  rechecks monotonicity and the down-set property.
- **Greedy is a poor proxy.** Its record instances (6 stages) have existential minima 1 to 3.
- **Every observed existential count is ≤ H.** The strongest conjecture consistent with the data is **∃-SB(H) with
  f(H) = H**. This is random-search evidence, at widths |L| ≤ 70.
- **Most record instances end stuck**, i.e. they find a y ≤ F(x, y) witness. Separated endings are rare at the maximum.

**Deliverable 5: what the process consumes.** The optimal run of the height-4 instance, with element ranks in
parentheses:

| Stage | Bad pairs | Separator | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | (⊥, ⊤) | 9 (rank 3) | |
| 1 | (⊥, 9) | 6 (rank 2) | |
| 2 | (⊥, 6) | 10 (rank 3) | 10 is not below 6 |
| 3 | (7, 10) | 8 (∈ D) | 7 = 9 ⊓ 10, a new meet |
| 4 | | | stuck at (8, 6) |

- The causal chain alternates columns, yₙ₊₁ = sₙ along the ⊥ row, with rows that are new meets.
- **Naive descent is refuted.** The column chain along the ⊥ row is not monotone: 9 → 6 → 10.
- A candidate potential, recorded as an untested conjecture: ρₙ(z) = rank cl_{Tₙ}(z). For every z this is
  non-increasing in n, and it strictly drops at the response z = F(xₙ, yₙ) of every processed pair: there
  yₙ ≤ cl_{Tₙ}(z), but yₙ ≰ sₙ, so cl_{Tₙ₊₁}(z) < cl_{Tₙ}(z). The missing step is a bound on the closure rank of the
  *new* responses created by the causal chain, in terms of the ranks consumed. Such a bound would give f(H) ≤ H.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.
∃-SB(6) with f(6) = 6 is the concrete finite conjecture. A proof of any ∃-SB(6) would prove Cut at edge-height 6.

## 34.3z¹²² ChatGPT brief "rank-decreasing causal chains": the rank drop and the fork are proved, and every rank-only potential fails at height 4

**Audit.** HEAD 7c6c51d, as the brief states.
- **A2 is correct.**
- **The RP ⇒ SB arithmetic is correct**, provided stuck stages are exempted. A stuck stage still has bad pairs, but
  it already gives a witness. The brief's statement omits this, and the audit's first pass confirmed that the
  omission matters (30 spurious failures in 400 instances).
- **The B2 dichotomy is correct, but it needs no causal hypothesis.** It is maximality of s alone: any pair lies
  below s, or its join with s is > s and therefore above y.
- **B3 is correct.** At H = 6 it holds unconditionally, through the Lean theorem at edge-height ≤ 5 applied to ↓s.

**Results 1, 2 (Lean, no sorry): `lean/Mordor/SeparatorRank.lean`.**

r is any strictly monotone rank.

| Declaration | Statement | Axioms |
|---|---|---|
| `cl_antitone_family` | T ⊆ T′ ⇒ cl_{T′} z ≤ cl_T z | standard |
| `bad_iff_le_cl` | for meet-closed T ∋ ⊤: bad ⟺ cross pair with y ≤ cl_T(F(x, y)) | standard |
| `response_closure_strict_drop`, `response_closure_rank_strict_drop` | a bad pair of T processed by `Step T T′` has cl_{T′}(F(x, y)) < cl_T(F(x, y)), hence a strict rank drop | standard |
| `rank_parent_stage_bound` | **RP ⇒ ∃-SB**: if every bad pair of a non-stuck T_{n+1} has a bad parent in Tₙ with strictly larger response closure rank, and r ≤ H, then some stage ≤ H is stuck or T_H has no bad pair | standard |
| `next_bad_pair_carrier` | **causal fork**: for a maximal separator s of (x, y) and any (x′, y′) with y′ ∉ D, either x′, y′ ≤ s, s ∉ D and s ≠ ⊤, or v = s ⊔ x′ ⊔ y′ > s is outside D above y, x′ and y′ | standard |
| `contained_branch` | Cut on ↓s, s ∉ D and F(s, s) ≤ s ⇒ a witness | standard |
| `contained_branch_height6` | at edge-height ≤ 6 (`NoChain α 7`), under no witness, a maximal separator s ∉ D has F(s, s) ≰ s, and s ⊔ F(s, s) > s lies above y | standard plus `Lean.ofReduceBool` and `Lean.trustCompiler`, inherited from `cut_height5` (`bv_decide`), as documented in §34.3g |

**Result 3: the rank-parent conjecture is refuted, by exhaustive choice enumeration.** `rank_audit.py` enumerates
every run (all maximal-separator choices). Output `out_rank_audit.txt`.

| Instances | Some run satisfies RP (causal / any parent) | All runs satisfy RP | Carriers nest / do not |
|---|---|---|---|
| 800 random (closure systems on 4–5 points) | 800 / 800 | 793 | 0 / 50 |
| greedy-maximal records, H = 5, 6, 7 | yes, yes, yes | no, no, yes | 7 / 54 |
| existential-maximal records, H = 3, 5, 6, 7 | yes | yes, yes, no, no | 1 / 43 (with H = 4 below) |
| **existential-maximal, H = 4, \|L\| = 12** | **no / no** | no | 1 / 9 |

On the height-4 instance (§34.3z¹²¹) **no run satisfies RP, causal or not**. The obstruction is structural:
- A bad pair needs y ≤ cl_T(F(x, y)), so its response closure rank is ≥ rank(y).
- The optimal run's columns are ⊤ → 9 → 6 → 10, with ranks 4, 3, 2, 3, and its response ranks are 4, 3, 2, 3.
- At stage 3 a *new row* (the meet 7 = 9 ⊓ 10) meets an *older* column of higher rank (10, the stage-2 separator).
- The child's closure rank is at least 3 > 2, which is its parent's rank.

**Package C is refuted as stated, by exhaustive enumeration** (`multiset_audit.py`). All 4 runs of the height-4
instance repeat the multiset of response closure ranks, with {3} at stages 1 and 3. So no function of that multiset
can strictly decrease along any run, whatever its order (multiset extension, histograms, weighted sums, sorted
tuples). Other records also repeat: the H = 6 existential record has the shortest run {6}, {4}, {3}, {2}, {5}.

**Package B4 is refuted empirically.** Carriers of consecutive causal edges almost never nest: 8 nested against 147
non-nested over all audited edges. The contained branch never occurred in the record instances (IH-branch count 0).

**Result 4: the smallest obstruction.** The height-4 instance (|L| = 12, closure system
{∅, 1, 2, 3, 4, 23, 14, 24, 34, 124, 234, 1234}) has four stages, so f(4) ≥ 4. Every potential that is a function of
response closure ranks fails on it: scalar parent ranks (RP) and multisets alike.

The missing ingredient is the *age* of coordinates. The rank increase happens exactly when a newly created meet row
pairs with a column created earlier. A viable potential must therefore track, per column, the separators already
consumed, e.g. the rank of cl_T restricted to the separators chosen *for that column*, rather than closure ranks of
responses. This is conjecture only. ∃-SB(H) with f(H) = H remains consistent with all data, but no proof route
survives.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²³ ChatGPT brief "separator ancestry, coordinate age and column-conditioned coverage": supports and fans are proved, and the gap potential survives every test

**Audit.** HEAD 9cdd1c7, as the brief states.
- **A1 is correct.** Every stage is the meet-closure of {⊥, ⊤} and earlier tokens (`run_tokens`).
- **A2 is correct.** It needs a no-⊥ form of `BoundedSupport.minimal_chain`.
- **A3 holds in a stronger form:** *every* support of a new element contains a fresh token.
- **The B2 and B3 bounds are generic.** Any inclusion-minimal A with P(⋁A) and ¬P(⊥) has |A| ≤ H, with no use of u
  or D; dually for meets.
- **Two remarks on the brief's framing:**
  - Fresh tokens make bad-pair profiles automatically non-repeating, so "profiles never repeat" carries no
    termination information.
  - Aggregate-first (B4) restricts the existential separator choices: one separator above F(⋁X, y) for a whole fan.
    So it can never lower the existential minimum.

**Deliverables 1, 3 (Lean, no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/SeparatorAncestry.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `mem_meetClose_iff`, `meetClose_meetClose_union` | meetClose(meetClose X ∪ S) = meetClose(X ∪ S) |
| `run_tokens` (A1) | every stage of a run is meetClose({⊥, ⊤} ∪ U), with U consisting of maximal separators chosen at earlier stages |
| `fresh_token` (A3) | every support A ⊆ T ∪ S of an element outside T contains a token of S ∖ T |
| `minimal_meet_support_bound` (A2) | a subset-minimal A with ⋀A = t has \|A\| ≤ H (`NoChain α (H+1)`) |
| `minimal_join_support_bound` (B2, B3) | a subset-minimal A with P(⋁A) has \|A\| ≤ H |
| `cone_separates` (B1) | a separator of (x_s, y) separates (x, y) for every x ≤ x_s |
| `row_fan_collapse` (B2 I, ACC) | ⋁X ∈ D, y ∉ D: one maximal separator of (⋁X, y) separates every (x, y), x ∈ X |
| `column_fan_collapse` (B3 I, DCC) | x ∈ D, ⋀Y ∉ D: one minimal lower separator of (x, ⋀Y) dually separates every (x, y), y ∈ Y |
| `potential_parent_stage_bound` | any stage-dependent potential φ ≤ B on bad pairs, strictly decreasing from some bad parent to every bad child of a non-stuck stage, ends the run (stuck or terminal) by stage B + 1 |

**Deliverable 2: history-aware traces** (`experiments/separation/ancestry_audit.py`, output `out_ancestry_audit.txt`).
These replay the existential-optimal and greedy runs of every record.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| minimum supports | at most 3 everywhere, well under H |
| fresh token in every later bad pair | always (it is a theorem) |
| coverage-cone violations | 0 (it is a theorem) |
| fans (existential-hardest runs) | none: one bad pair per stage |
| fans (greedy runs) | many, mostly row fans with join in D |
| aggregate-first greedy vs greedy, 300 random instances | identical stage counts in all 300 |
| cross-generation response matrices F(x_i, y_j), i ≠ j | witnesses in 0–45 % of entries; finite lattices always have some |

**Deliverable 4: the height-four instance, explained.** Tokens are written value^(birth stage).

| Stage | Bad pair | Row support | Column support |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | (⊥, 11) | ⊥ | ∅ |
| 1 | (⊥, 9) | ⊥ | {9⁰} |
| 2 | (⊥, 6) | ⊥ | {6¹} |
| 3 | (7, 10) | {9⁰, 10²} | {10²} |
| 4 | (8, 6), stuck | {8³} | {6¹} |

- The rank increase at stage 3 is a **shared token**. The row 7 = 9 ⊓ 10 contains the column's own token 10², so
  x ≤ y, and the row's rank rises together with the column's.
- The hardest existential runs of every height are the same pattern: chains along the ⊥ row, (⊥, s₀), (⊥, s₁), …,
  with x ≤ y in every non-stuck bad pair.
  - H = 7: column ranks 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2.
  - H = 5: column ranks 5, 4, 3, 2, 1.
- So the response rank is the wrong measure, and the **interval [x, y]** is the natural one.

**Deliverable 5: a surviving termination conjecture** (`experiments/separation/potential_audit.py`, output
`out_potential_audit.txt`). The parent-decrease property was tested for four potentials, exhaustively over all
separator choices, per instance.

| Potential | 600 random | 8 records | ≈ 2.9 · 10⁶ distinct mutations of hard instances (existential minimum 3–6) |
|---|---|---|---|
| resp = rank cl_T F(x, y) | survives | **fails** (H = 4, \|L\| = 12) | not tested |
| col = rank y | survives | **fails** (H = 4, \|L\| = 12) | not tested |
| **gap = rank y − rank x** | survives | survives | **no failure** |
| **respgap = rank cl_T F(x, y) − rank x** | survives | survives | **no failure** |

The first "hunt" from random starts was dominated by trivial instances and is recorded for completeness only. The
mutation hunt (`hunt_from`, 4 × 540 s) tested 6.5 · 10⁶ states, about 2.9 · 10⁶ of them distinct (summed over the four processes, which may overlap), with existential
minima 3: 4.26 · 10⁶, 4: 1.67 · 10⁶, 5: 0.53 · 10⁶, 6: 0.04 · 10⁶.

> **Conjecture (∃-gap-RP).** In every lattice of edge-height ≤ H, for every monotone F and cut D, there is a choice of
> maximal separators such that every bad pair (x′, y′) of a non-stuck stage has a causal parent (x, y), bad at the
> previous stage, whose separator lies above x′ or y′, with rank y′ − rank x′ < rank y − rank x.

- By `potential_parent_stage_bound` with φ = rank y + H − rank x (B = 2H), ∃-gap-RP gives ∃-SB(H) with f(H) ≤ 2H + 1.
- If moreover x < y in every non-stuck bad pair, as in all hard runs, it gives f(H) ≤ H.
- By `cut_of_terminal_run`, it proves Cut at edge-height H, and at H = 6 the first open case.
- Evidence is exhaustive per instance, but on finite instances only.
- The proof would have to show that a parent can be chosen whose interval [x, y] strictly contains the child's.
  When x ≤ y, a natural mechanism is the shared token, as in the height-four case.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.
∃-gap-RP is the current concrete target.

## 34.3z¹²⁴ ChatGPT brief "Stage 2: new proof strategies" (anchor envelopes, crossing-interval refinement, Ω³): the interval order is well-founded, the envelope is not witness-preserving, and the refinement step is not a sub-problem

**Audit.** HEAD aee4ae2, as the brief states. All twelve cited declarations exist under the names given.
- The **finite supremum/infimum support lemma is already in the repository**: `Stable.sSup_finite_support` and
  `sInf_finite_support`, via Mathlib's `CompleteLattice.WellFoundedGT.isSupFiniteCompact`. Nothing new is needed.
- Note on the brief's H_D. Its anchors {⊥} ∪ {h ∈ D : ↑h infinite} differ from those of `TwoSided.two_sided`, which
  takes {⊥} ∪ all hubs.
- On `cut_two_sided_capture`: the remaining obstruction is a crossing infinite *value* interval [w, v] (w ∈ D,
  v ∉ D) of the reset G. The arguments of the cross cells still range over all of L.

**Lean (no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/IntervalRefinement.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `IntervalLT`, `intervalLT_key` | strict containment [s′, t′] ⊊ [s, t] is a subrelation of < on αᵒᵈ × α under the key (s, t) ↦ (toDual s, t) |
| `intervalLT_wellFounded` | well-founded under ACC + DCC (the brief's B, first item) |
| `refine_dm`, `dm_wellFounded` | replacing one interval of a multiset by finitely many strictly smaller ones is a Dershowitz–Manna step on keys, and that order is well-founded (Mathlib `wellFounded_isDershowitzMannaLT`) |
| `envelope`, `envelope_mono`, `envelope_anchor` | E is monotone, and E = F on anchor cells H × C |

**Falsification** (`experiments/intervals/envelope_falsify.py`; z3, every lattice with ≤ 5 elements and every cut;
output `out_envelope_falsify.txt`).

| Proposition | First failure | Example |
|---|---|---|
| (A1) cellwise witness preservation of E, the brief's anchors (only ⊥, ⊤ in a finite lattice) | **C4** (holds on C3, B2) | D = {⊥}: E ≡ F(⊥, ⊤) = 2 has the witness 2 ≤ E(⊥, 2), while F(⊥, 2) = 1 and F(⊥, ⊤) = 2 are witness-free |
| (A2) the same with any finite anchors H ∋ ⊥ in D, C ∋ ⊤ outside D | **C4** | (as above) |
| (B) a crossing interval with witness-free inner cross cells is mapped into itself by F | **B2** (holds on chains to C5) | D = {⊥}, [⊥, a]: F(⊥, a) = b leaves the interval |

**Reading.**
1. **Strategy A.** The global form, "F witness-free ⇒ E witness-free", cannot be tested on any known structure.
   - On finite lattices both sides always have witnesses (`cut_finite`).
   - On ω + 1, the only witness-free structures known lack ACC, and there H_D = ℕ, C_U = {ω}, so E = F.
   - The testable cellwise form fails on C4. An envelope witness is not located at any cell it reads.

   The existing reset `Hubs.reset_core` preserves no-witness only through its *pinning* clause (unchanged rows that
   are pinned), which the closed formula E lacks. With finitely many anchors, E is the reset without pinning, and so
   needs a global transfer "E-witness ⇒ F-witness". That transfer is the Cut Lemma itself.
2. **Strategy B.** Well-foundedness and the Dershowitz–Manna machinery are now in place. The refinement step is not a
   sub-problem, though.
   - Crossing intervals are not F-invariant (B2 above).
   - After `two_sided`, a cross cell's value interval is not determined by intervals containing its arguments.
   - So one cannot recurse on [w, v] with the restricted bifunction.

   **The exact unresolved step.** A confinement theorem: for each crossing infinite interval [w, v] of the reset G,
   there is a witness-free monotone modification G′ that agrees with G outside the cells valued in [w, v], and whose
   values on those cells lie in finitely many intervals strictly contained in [w, v]. Given confinement, `refine_dm`
   and `dm_wellFounded` turn the induction into a proof. Without the hub-finiteness of `two_sided` *inside* [w, v],
   no construction for G′ is known. With it, the existing reset applies relative to [w, v] only for cells whose
   arguments lie in the interval, and those are not the cells in question.
3. **Ω³.** The three-way interaction is already identified in §34.3z⁗–⁸. Ω × Ω × Ω funnels into the plane theorem
   relative to finitely many layers, and the missing information appears at generation depth 2 (conjecture DC2,
   verified exhaustively on M_ω² and M_ω² × C₂). It is not repeated here.

**Recommendation.** Do not invest in envelope formulas without pinning (Strategy A). They reduce to the global
transfer, which is the Cut Lemma. Strategy B is worth a restricted theorem: Cut on chain-finite lattices in which
every crossing interval has finitely many relative hubs and co-hubs. It needs the confinement step above, now with
the termination machinery in place. The concrete finite target with the most evidence remains the gap rank-parent
conjecture of §34.3z¹²³.

The brief's discipline point holds throughout: every mechanism here (envelopes, aggregated joins, interval keys)
works with suprema and infima. A proof must still recover membership. None of the new lemmas does, beyond the
anchor cells.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²⁵ ChatGPT brief "interface preservation and separator ancestry": traces and the conditional edge-height theorem are proved; safe selection and strict containment are refuted

**Audit.** HEAD f8f8193, as the brief states. All cited declarations exist. One finite fact is decisive for
Programme A. In a finite lattice every value of I = [w, v] lies in a proper subinterval ([w, z] or [z, v]), so
"confinement to finitely many proper subintervals" is vacuous there. Its finite content is the **both-endpoints-strict**
range reduction that `two_sided` provides: new crossing values in the open interval (w, v). That is the proxy used
below.

### Report A: interface confinement

**1. Interface traces (Lean, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/InterfaceTrace.lean`.**

G : α × α → α is monotone, S is any set of cells, ℓ(p) = ⨆_{q ∉ S, q ≤ p} G q and u(p) = ⨅_{q ∉ S, p ≤ q} G q.

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `lowTrace_mono`, `highTrace_mono` | ℓ and u are monotone |
| `lowTrace_le_highTrace` | ℓ ≤ u |
| `lowTrace_outside`, `highTrace_outside` | both equal G off S |
| `between_traces` | every monotone G′ agreeing with G off S satisfies ℓ ≤ G′ ≤ u |

**2–3. Safe selection is refuted** (`experiments/intervals/selection_falsify.py`, z3; output
`out_selection_falsify.txt`).

The proposed lemma: all windows W(p) = [ℓ(p), u(p)] ∩ (w, v) ∩ {z ≰ x, y ≰ z} nonempty ⇒ a monotone selection
exists.

| Instances (G witness-free on the crossing cells of S_I) | some window empty | selectable | **blocked** (all windows nonempty, no selection) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2,195 over 5 seeds | 418 | 1,720 | **57** |

The smallest counterexample found has **|L| = 6**: the lattice {∅, {0}, {1}, {2}, {1,2}, {0,1,2}} with
D = {∅, {1}, {2}} and I = [∅, {1, 2}], a B2 square whose two atoms lie in D. Its column is y = {0}, outside D.

| Cell | Window |
|---|---|
| ({1}, y) | {{2}} |
| ({2}, y) | {{1}} |
| (∅, y) | {{1}, {2}} |

Monotonicity forces the value of (∅, y) below {1} ⊓ {2} = ∅ = w, which is excluded. In any witness-free monotone
selection one of the two upper cells must take the endpoint v. The original G puts v on all three. The C4 and B2
regressions, sampled throughout, gave no blocked instance (no blocked instance had fewer than 6 elements; random
search, not exhaustive).

**Proposed pinning certificate.** It has two kinds.
- *External pinning:* the window is empty, i.e. the trace forces an endpoint.
- *Internal pinning:* two rows a₁ ∥ a₂ in D ∩ I share a column y with a cross cell (x, y) below both,
  x ≤ a₁ ⊓ a₂. Their admissible values z₁ ≰ a₁ and z₂ ≰ a₂ in (w, v) satisfy z₁ ⊓ z₂ ≤ x for every choice.

A pinned cell must keep an endpoint value. The certificate is stated, not proved complete; its survival under the
modification is open.

**4. Restricted theorem.** Not obtained. With the pinned cells allowed to keep v (dually w), range reduction is again
trivial on finite lattices. The content moves entirely to the infinite case.

**5. The exact remaining obstacle.** In an infinite crossing interval, the selected values must be *covered by
finitely many proper subintervals*. Pinned cells at v are covered by [w′, v] for any w < w′ ≤ v. The other values
must avoid infinitely many middle antichain elements of I. That is a finite-relative-anchor condition on I, i.e. the
restricted hypothesis itself, and it is not implied by chain-finiteness. The input cells range over all of L
throughout, so `box_reset` on I alone does not apply.

### Report B: separator ancestry

**1. Formal statement (Lean, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/GapPotential.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `height H x` | `Nat.findGreatest` of the chain lengths below x, capped at H |
| `chainTo_height`, `le_height`, `height_le` | basic properties of `height` |
| `height_strictMono` | under `NoChain α (H+1)`, `height` is strictly monotone |
| **`cut_of_gap_parent`** | for a run of the separator process: if every bad pair of a non-stuck stage has *some* bad parent at the previous stage with height y′ + (H − height x′) < height y + (H − height x), then F has a witness |

The proof of `cut_of_gap_parent` combines `potential_parent_stage_bound` (B = 2H) and `cut_of_terminal_run`.

**2. Strict interval containment is refuted** (`experiments/separation/containment_audit.py`, exhaustive over all
separator choices; output `out_containment_audit.txt`).

| Property | 600 random | 8 records |
|---|---|---|
| contain: x ≤ x′ ≤ y′ ≤ y, strict, causal parent | survives | **fails on the H = 4 instance** (|L| = 12): (⊥, 6) → (7, 10) has 10 ≰ 6, and no run avoids it |
| gap (∃-gap-RP), causal parent | survives | survives |

**x ≤ y in bad pairs** (`xy_audit.py`, all runs):
- It holds on every optimal run: 600 / 600 random instances and all records.
- It fails on other runs: in 1 non-stuck bad pair among 935 in 600 random instances (smallest |L| = 12, height 4), and
  on non-optimal runs of the H = 4 and H = 7 records.
- So x ≤ y is at best existential. The tighter bound H, as opposed to 2H + 1, needs a good separator choice.

**3. ∃-gap-RP is not proved.** It survives all tests, here and in §34.3z¹²³ (about 2.9 · 10⁶ mutated hard instances).
The shared-token mechanism of the height-4 case gives x′ ≤ y′ for the child, not a comparison with the parent's
interval. Strict containment is the natural geometric form, and it is false.

**4. Edge-height-6 consequence (Lean).** `cut_of_gap_parent` with H = 6: a run with the gap parent property yields a
witness within 13 stages. So **∃-gap-RP at edge-height 6 ⇒ Cut at edge-height 6** (machine-checked implication;
the hypothesis is conjectural).

**5. Files.**
- `lean/Mordor/InterfaceTrace.lean`, `lean/Mordor/GapPotential.lean`;
- `experiments/intervals/selection_falsify.py`;
- `experiments/separation/containment_audit.py`, `xy_audit.py`.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²⁶ ChatGPT brief "global gap descent and compatible monotone selection": max-gap ⟺ gap-parent, patching and pinning are proved; descent needs global choice, and pins have unbounded arity

**Audit.** HEAD d28f8f5, as the brief states.
- **Part A's equivalence is correct**, and the parent in `cut_of_gap_parent` is indeed not required to be causal.
- **Part B's patching argument is correct.**
- **Weak Veinott (Kukushkin 2009, Theorem 3.5) fails on the six-element square, as it must.** With p = (∅, y) < q = ({1}, y),
  z = {2} ∈ W(q) and t = {1} ∈ W(p): z ∨ t = v ∉ W(q) and z ∧ t = w ∉ W(p).

### Report A: maximum-gap descent

**1. Equivalence (Lean, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/GapMax.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `parent_iff_max_drop` | for nonempty finite B, B′ and any φ: (∀ c ∈ B′, ∃ p ∈ B, φ c < φ p) ⟺ max_{B′} φ < max_B φ |
| `badSet`, `mem_badSet`, `gap` | the stage's bad pairs as a `Finset`, and the gap potential |
| `cut_of_max_gap_drop` | if every non-stuck stage n + 1 with bad pairs has a bad stage n and a strictly smaller maximum gap, F has a witness (through `cut_of_gap_parent`) |

**2–3. Descent, by exhaustive enumeration of all separator choices per instance** (`gapmax_audit.py`, output
`out_gapmax_audit.txt`).

| Instances | Some run descends | All runs descend |
|---|---|---|
| 600 random | 600 | 600 |
| 8 records | 8 | **4** (fails at H = 4: Mₙ = 8, 7, 5, 5; also at the H = 5 and H = 6 greedy records and the H = 6 existential record) |
| ≈ 160,800 distinct mutations of hard instances (existential minimum ≥ 3) | **all** | 14,760 have a non-descending run |

**The one-step lookahead rule fails.** The rule chooses the separators minimizing M_{n+1}. It fails on the H = 4 and
H = 6 existential records and on 15,242 of ≈ 165,500 mutations. Its smallest failure is |L| = 12, height 4, with
M = 8, 7, 5, 5.

So **separator choices need global optimization**:
- the universal form is false;
- a myopic deterministic rule is false;
- the existential form never failed.

**4. Edge-height-6 consequence (Lean).** `cut_of_max_gap_drop` with H = 6: a run with strictly decreasing
maximum gap gives a witness within 13 stages. Existential max-gap descent at edge-height 6 implies Cut at edge-height
6; the hypothesis is conjectural.

**5. Smallest counterexamples.** The height-4 instance (|L| = 12) is the smallest found against both the universal
form and the lookahead rule. No counterexample to the existential form is known.

**The central step (unproved).** The brief's sequence ends with "a child with potential ≥ Mₙ yields a witness or an old
bad pair with potential > Mₙ". It cannot hold for every run, since the universal form is false. Any proof must
therefore construct the run, i.e. choose separators with knowledge of later stages.

### Report B: monotone selection and pinning

**1. Patching (Lean): `InterfaceTrace.patch_mono`, `patch_no_witness`.** A selection g on S that is monotone on S
and lies between the traces, patched into G, is globally monotone. It is witness-free if G is witness-free off S and
g is safe on the cross cells of S.

**2. Weak-Veinott status** (`experiments/intervals/veinott_audit.py`, four seeds, 1,236 instances, output
`out_veinott_audit.txt`).

| Class | Count |
|---|---|
| WV holds | 631, **all selectable**, consistent with Kukushkin's theorem; chain-finiteness supplies chain-completeness |
| WV fails, selectable | 345 (WV is sufficient, not necessary) |
| some window empty (external pin or trace-forced) | 229 |
| blocked (all windows nonempty, no selection) | 28 |

**3. Pinning (Lean).**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `external_pin_top`, `external_pin_bot` | a trace equal to an endpoint forces that endpoint |
| `internal_pin` | rows a₁, a₂ over a shared column with a safe lower cell, any two admissible upper values ≠ v meeting below x ⇒ one upper cell takes v |
| `general_pin`, `general_pin_dual` | any cells p ≤ q₁, q₂ (resp. q₁, q₂ ≤ p) with admissibility predicates: if no admissible lower value lies below the meet of two admissible upper values ≠ v (dually above a join, ≠ w), a monotone G′ puts v (resp. w) on q₁ or q₂ |

Empirically:
- The narrow internal pin of §34.3z¹²⁵ misses obstructions. The smallest it misses has |L| = 10: the meet of the two
  upper values is w, below the lower window, though not below x (`pinfree_blocked_counterexample.json`).
- **The general pin occurs in all 28 blocked instances.**
- Pinning once (adding the forced endpoint where admissible) makes 26 of the 28 selectable. Iterating to a fixed
  point gains nothing: 2 instances (smallest |L| = 11) need the endpoint on cells outside every pin.
- After pinning, WV holds in only 12 of the 28. **The conjecture "after pinning the residual windows satisfy WV" is
  false.**

**4. Infinite-antichain obstruction** (`antichain_pins.py`, output `out_antichain_pins.txt`; Lean
`atom_family_block`).

The crossing interval [⊥, v] of L_n: ⊥ < a₁, …, a_n < v < ⊤, ⊥ < y < ⊤, with D = {⊥, aᵢ}. The cells are (aᵢ, y)
with windows {a_j : j ≠ i}, and (⊥, y).
- The whole family is blocked, while every family missing one row is selectable (n = 2, …, 7, z3). So **the
  minimal pin has arity n + 1**, and minimal-support bounds do not bound pin arity.
- For M_ω (n = ∞), every finite subfamily is selectable, but the whole family is blocked by `atom_family_block`.
  So the obstruction has **no finite certificate**.
- The original values G ≡ v are monotone and witness-free there, since y ≰ v.

**5. Restricted theorem and missing hypothesis.**
- On finite S, Kukushkin's theorem with WV gives a monotone selection. For the Mordor windows, WV is neither
  automatic nor restored by pinning.
- The M_ω example shows that confinement of a crossing interval can fail although every finite sub-problem is
  solvable. So a restricted theorem needs a hypothesis excluding infinite antichains of D-rows that share a column
  within a crossing interval. A finite-relative-anchor hypothesis of exactly this kind is the precise missing
  hypothesis.
- Under it, the problem is finite, and WV or explicit search decides it.
- No restricted confinement theorem is proved here.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²⁷ Follow-up (no brief): shortest runs descend; no local or canonical rule does

Target from §34.3z¹²⁶: a separator choice that realizes existential maximum-gap descent. All tests below are
exhaustive per instance over all maximal-separator choices. The instances are the records plus mutations of hard
instances (shortest length ≥ 3); this is random search evidence. Outputs `experiments/separation/out_shortest_nested.txt`.

**Shortest runs** (`shortest_audit.py`, 3 × 540 s, ≈ 241,000 distinct instances).

| Descending runs among the shortest | A longer run also descends | Instances |
|---|---|---|
| all | no | 193,521 |
| all | yes | 25,808 |
| some, not all | no | 21,351 |
| **none** | | **0** |

Records: all shortest runs descend except at the H = 4 and H = 6 existential records, where some do.

> **Conjecture (shortest-run descent).** Every instance has a run of minimum length whose stage maximum gap strictly
> decreases.

It implies existential max-gap descent, hence, by `cut_of_max_gap_drop`, Cut at every edge-height H.

**Deterministic rules fail** (`nested_rule.py`, ≈ 80,000 distinct mutations each).

| Rule | Failures | Smallest failure |
|---|---|---|
| one-step lookahead, minimizing M_{n+1} (§34.3z¹²⁶) | 9.2 % | \|L\| = 12, H = 4 |
| **nested-first**: prefer a maximal separator s ≤ y | 7.1 % | \|L\| = 21, H = 7; fixes H = 4, fails at the H = 6 record (M = 12, 10, 9, 8, 9) |
| nested-first, least height | 7.1 % | \|L\| = 21, H = 7 |
| **the full process**: every maximal separator of every bad pair (a valid `Step`) | 9.1 % | \|L\| = 12, H = 4 (M = 8, 5, 5) |

**What the non-descending runs do.** On the H = 6 record, every non-descending run ends with the same trap. A newly
created D-row (the separator 10, born at stage 3) meets an *old* high-rank column (25, rank 5, born at stage 0).
Their pair (10, 25) is unseparated, so the gap rises from 8 to 9. In every descending run that pair is already
separated by a separator chosen earlier (21 or 23). Avoiding the trap requires anticipating rows not yet created.
That explains why one-step and canonical rules fail, and why the full process, which adds more rows, fails too.

**Lean (standard axioms): `GapMax.nested_child_gap`.** A separator s ≤ y of (x, y) is strictly below y, so the child
(x, s) has strictly smaller gap at edge-height ≤ H. This is the mechanism of the descending ⊥-row chains. By the
table above it is not a selection rule.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite. The
concrete target is the shortest-run descent conjecture, whose choice of separators is necessarily global.

## 34.3z¹²⁸ Follow-up (no brief): shortest-run descent on wide height-6 lattices; why no potential can be universal

**Why a universal potential is excluded.** Take run 2 of the height-4 record (§34.3z¹²⁷): stage 2 has the single
bad pair ({24}, {234}), and its only maximal separator {34} produces stage 3 with the single bad pair ({34}, {124}).
Both pairs have
- rank x = 2,
- rank y = 3,
- gap 5,
- response rank 2 (F = {34}, resp. {14}).

Stage 3 is not stuck: its pair is processed and the run ends stuck at stage 4. So no function of the multiset of
(rank x, rank y, rank F(x, y)) over the bad pairs strictly decreases on every run. That covers gap, column, response
and lexicographic combinations. A choice-free termination argument must use information beyond these ranks, and
§34.3z¹²⁷ shows that the realizable choices must be global.

**Wide height-6 lattices** (`experiments/separation/wide_h6.py`, 4 × 550 s, output `out_wide_h6.txt`).
- *Lattices:* closure systems on 6 points with edge-height exactly 6, binned by size.
- *Search:* hill-climb toward large existential minimum. On every accepted state with shortest length ≥ 3, test
  whether some shortest run descends.
- *Scale:* 68,327 accepted states, not necessarily distinct, at |L| = 20–69. Of these, 4,437 have shortest
  length ≥ 4.

| \|L\| bin | 20 | 30 | 40 | 50 | 60 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| longest shortest run found | 5 | 5 | 5 | 4 | 4 |
| failures of shortest-run descent | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

**Reading.**
- At edge-height 6 the existential stage count stays ≤ 5 = H − 1 at every width tested. The H = 4 record (4 stages)
  shows f(H) ≥ H is possible, but no height-6 instance needed more than 5 stages.
- Shortest-run descent never failed.
- Both are random-search evidence at |L| ≤ 69, not a proof. They support ∃-SB(6) with f(6) ≤ 6 and shortest-run
  descent. By `cut_of_max_gap_drop` the latter would give Cut at edge-height 6.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹²⁹ Follow-up (no brief): two candidate clause families for edge-height 6, both negative

**1. Interval induction adds nothing to the existing height-induction clauses.**
- *The idea.* Under no witness at edge-height H, take a crossing interval [t, s] (t ∈ D, s ∉ D, t ≤ s,
  (t, s) ≠ (⊥, ⊤)). If t ≤ F(t, t) and F(s, s) ≤ s, then F maps [t, s]² into [t, s]. That interval is a bounded poset
  of edge-height ≤ H − 1, and Cut there (`cut_height5` at H = 6) gives a witness. So
  ¬(t ≤ F(t, t) ∧ F(s, s) ≤ s) is sound for every proper crossing interval.
- *Why it is redundant.*
  - If t ≠ ⊥, already t ≤ F(t, t) makes [t, ⊤] F-stable: that is clause (A) of `cut_ground.add_ih` / `ih_violations`.
  - If t = ⊥, the condition reduces to F(s, s) ≤ s with s ≠ ⊤: that is clause (B).
- So the interval family is implied by the principal one. §34.3z⁵³ recorded that the principal clauses never fire at
  H = 6 in 300 terms.

**2. Extremal-separator Skolem constants are sound but counterproductive** (`experiments/sat/sep_ground.py`,
output `out_sep_ground.txt`).
- *The idea.* For chosen cross pairs (x, y) of terms, add constants s and r with
  - D(x) ∧ ¬D(y) ⇒ F(x, y) ≤ s, y ≰ s, and s ≤ t ⇒ t ≤ s ∨ y ≤ t for every term t (maximality);
  - dually r ≤ F(x, y), r ≰ x, and t ≤ r ⇒ r ≤ t ∨ t ≤ x (minimality).
- *Soundness.* Such separators exist in every witness-free chain-finite structure (`ExtremalSeparators`). The WLOG
  unit remains sound.

| H | without (K = 0) | K = 1 per round | K = 2 per round |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | UNSAT, 7 terms | UNSAT, 31 terms | UNSAT, 37 terms |
| 4 | UNSAT, 20 terms | SAT at 358 terms / 191 s | UNSAT, 309 terms / 64 s |
| 5 | UNSAT, 147 terms / 4.1 s | no result in 190 s | not run |

The constants bring their own cross pairs and an extremality clause per term, and the solver spends its effort on
them. The separator structure that organizes the finite runs (§34.3z¹²⁰–¹²⁸) does not transfer to the ground
refutation as Skolem terms.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹³⁰ ChatGPT brief "shortest-run descent": the finite state graph in Lean; the state-wise conjecture is false, exchanges reach back up to three stages, and extraction fails on absoluteness

**Audit.** HEAD 8becfd3, as the brief states; the brief's `records.json` is `stage_records.json`. One framing point
governs both reports.
- On a finite lattice every run terminates and the completion distance is defined. Cut is already a theorem there.
- In a witness-free structure no goal is reachable, so d = ∞ and "shortest run" has no meaning.
- A *descending* run, on the other hand, is a finite certificate: at most 2H + 1 stages, finitely many separators
  (`cut_of_max_gap_drop`).
- So shortest-run descent is a finite selection principle, and Report B's extraction problem is exactly its
  transfer.

### Report A: finite shortest-run theory

**1–2. State graph and distance (Lean, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/ShortestRun.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `Goal T` | no bad pair, or a stuck bad pair |
| `step_exists_of_not_goal` (ACC) | every non-goal stage has a `Step`; no no-witness hypothesis, since a non-stuck bad pair has F(x, y) itself as a separator |
| `Reach k T`, `reach_mono` | a goal is reachable within k steps |
| `reach_finite` | on a finite lattice, within card α − \|T\| steps |
| `dist T`, `reach_dist`, `dist_le` | the completion distance |
| `optimal_successor` | a non-goal T has a step T′ with dist T′ + 1 = dist T, and dist T ≤ dist T′ + 1 for every step |

**3. The optimal gap-successor conjecture is false** (`experiments/separation/state_graph_audit.py`; full reachable
state graph over all choices; output `out_state_graph.txt`). A successor is *good* if d(T′) = d(T) − 1 and
(Goal(T′) or M(T′) < M(T)).

| Among 242,017 distinct mutations of hard instances (d(T₀) ≥ 3) | Count |
|---|---|
| some reachable non-goal state has no good successor | 22,114 |
| some state **on a shortest path from T₀** has no good successor | 21,477 |
| no descending shortest run from T₀ | **0** |

Among the records, the H = 4 and H = 6 existential records each have one state on a shortest path with no good
successor. The smallest failure is the height-4 record itself (|L| = 12). So only the existential statement from T₀
survives. It cannot be strengthened to every state on every
shortest path.

**4. The exchange.** For each non-descending shortest run, let k be its first non-descending transition. Let j ≤ k be
the latest stage from which a descending optimal continuation exists. The exchange depth is k − j.

| Exchange depth k − j | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| runs (three hunts) | **0** | 25,970 | 16,815 | 16,519 |

The failing stage itself *never* has a good alternative: once a run reaches the trap state, every optimal
continuation fails. The revision must be made one to three stages earlier, which is the anticipation seen on the
H = 6 record (§34.3z¹²⁷). An exchange lemma must therefore pull a future obstruction back across up to three
meet-closures to an earlier *legal* separator choice. No such lemma is proved. The data bound the needed look-back by
3 on the instances tested (heights 4–7).

**5. Machine-checked shortest-run descent.** Not obtained.

### Report B: generalization

**1. Finite obstruction extraction, and why it fails.**
- *The desired lemma:* "if L (chain-finite, edge-height H) has no descending run, some finite structure L′ has none".
  Together with the finite conjecture, it would give ∃-SB(H) and Cut.
- *Downward absoluteness:* maximal-separator status passes **down** to substructures (Lean
  `ShortestRun.maxSep_restrict`): an L-maximal separator in L′ is L′-maximal.
- *Failure of the converse:* L′ can have maximal separators that are not L-maximal. Example: the chain
  0 < 1 < 2 < 3 with y = 3 and response 1 has the maximal separator 2, while in {0, 1, 3} it is 1.
- *Consequence:* an L′-descending run may use spurious separators and need not be an L-run. Closing L′ under
  L-maximal separators restores correspondence but can be infinite (next point). Heights are also not absolute
  (h_{L′} ≤ h_L), so M changes.

**2. Infinite antichains.**
- *Infinite branching:* ⊥ < a < cᵢ (i ∈ ℕ) < ⊤ with F(⊥, ⊤) = a. The pair (⊥, ⊤) has the maximal separators
  {cᵢ : i ∈ ℕ}, an infinite antichain. So the state graph of a chain-finite lattice can be infinitely branching at
  stage 0, and König's lemma is not available.
- *No compactness:* the selection-level warning `InterfaceTrace.atom_family_block` (every finite subfamily solvable,
  the whole family not) shows that compactness cannot be assumed for choice problems over such antichains.

**3. The exact extension obstacle.** A descending run is a finite object, but its *legality* is not finitely
checkable. Maximality of a separator is a universal statement over all of L, it is not preserved upward to
substructures, and the alternatives at a stage may be infinitely many. Any extraction must produce a finite L′ that
is closed under the maximal separators the runs use *and* agrees with L on maximality and heights. Neither is
available in general.

**4. Recommended route.**
- First prove shortest-run descent on finite lattices as pure combinatorics. An exchange lemma with look-back ≤ 3 is
  the concrete target; the data say depth 3 suffices up to height 7.
- For arbitrary chain-finite lattices, replace "maximal separator" by a notion that is absolute for suitable finite
  substructures, e.g. maximal *relative to a fixed finite anchor set*. The process then lives inside a finite
  sublattice from the start. Its soundness rests on `separating_family_witness`, which needs only some separator, not
  a maximal one. Whether descending runs survive this relativization is the next experiment.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹³¹ Follow-up (no brief): the response process and a finite transfer theorem

**The idea.** §34.3z¹³⁰ located the extension obstacle in the non-absoluteness of *maximal* separators. Any separator
s (F(x, y) ≤ s, y ≰ s) keeps the process sound, since `separating_family_witness` needs no maximality. The canonical
choice is the **response** s = F(x, y), a separator whenever the pair is not stuck. The response process is
T_{n+1} = meetClose(T_n ∪ {F(x, y) : (x, y) bad in T_n}). Its run refers only to the stages, the responses of their
pairs, the order and D. Every finite prefix of it can therefore be reproduced inside a finite lattice.

**Lean (no sorry, standard axioms): `lean/Mordor/ResponseRun.lean`.**

| Declaration | Statement |
|---|---|
| `respStep`, `respRun` | the response process, frozen at goal stages |
| `respRun_closed` | every stage is meet-closed and contains ⊥, ⊤ |
| `cut_of_resp_goal` | a goal stage gives a witness |
| `SubOK`, `subLattice` | a finite inf-closed S ∋ ⊥, ⊤ is a finite bounded lattice in the induced order (joins are closures cl_S(a ⊔ b)) |
| `map_meetClose`, `bad_iff`, `goal_iff`, `step_map`, `run_map` | with G′ = cl_S ∘ F on S and D′ = D ∩ S: bad pairs, goal stages, steps and the run correspond to those of F in α, as long as S contains the responses of the pairs of the stages concerned |
| **`resp_transfer`** | if every *finite* lattice of edge-height ≤ H has a response run reaching a goal by stage N, so does every lattice of edge-height ≤ H |
| **`cut_of_resp_bound`** | under the same finite hypothesis, Cut holds on every lattice (finite or not) of edge-height ≤ H |

The finite witness S is the meet-closure of ⊥ with the first N + 1 stages and the responses of all their pairs.

> **Response-bound conjecture RB(H).** There is N(H) such that, in every finite lattice of edge-height ≤ H, the
> response run reaches a goal within N(H) steps.

By `cut_of_resp_bound`, RB(H) implies Cut at edge-height H. RB(H) is a purely finite combinatorial statement: no
maximality, no infinite branching, no extraction step. In finite lattices the response run always terminates, since a
non-stuck bad pair's response is a new element. The content of RB(H) is the **width-independence** of the bound.

**Experiments** (`experiments/separation/general_sep.py`, `response_length.py`, `response_width3.py`; output
`out_response_rule.txt`; random and hill-climb search).

**Descent along the response run.**

| Rule | Records | ≈ 245,000 hard mutations |
|---|---|---|
| response rule, max-gap descent | 8 / 8 descend (H = 4 record: 1 stage instead of 4) | fails on 0.7–0.8 % (smallest \|L\| = 12, H = 4) |
| polar rules (responses kept outside D, maximal separators for responses in D) | fail at H = 4, 5 or 7 | fail on 5–7 % |
| existential descent over general separators | holds | holds on all 243,577 instances tested |

The response-rule descent failures are the low-new-row trap. A response in D of small height r becomes a row, and
its pair (r, ⊤) has gap 2H − h(r). On the smallest failure this happens at ({34}, ⊤), with gap 6 after M = 5.

**Response-run length against width (hill-climbing).**

| H | lattices | longest response run |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | closure systems on 4 points, \|L\| < 20 | 6 |
| 3 | linear spaces on 4–14 points, \|L\| ≤ 36 (≈ 200,000 lattices) | 4–6 at every width |
| 4 | \|L\| < 20 | 7 |
| 5 | \|L\| < 40 | 7 |
| 6 | \|L\| < 70 (≈ 8,000 lattices climbed) | 6 (by size bin: 1, 4, 6, 5, 4, 3, 3) |

No growth with width was observed at any height. This is evidence, not proof, for RB(3–6) with N(H) ≤ 7 on these
families.

**Reading.**
- The transfer theorem moves the whole problem at bounded height to finite lattices. RB(H) for all H would give Cut
  at every finite edge-height, though not on chain-finite lattices of unbounded height.
- Descent is no longer needed: a uniform length bound suffices.
- RB(6) is now the concrete target for edge-height 6, and it is testable, or refutable, on finite lattices alone.

**Status.** Unchanged: Cut is open at edge-height 6 and on chain-finite lattices with both boundaries infinite.

## 34.3z¹³² Follow-up (no brief): RB(3) is false; the response run grows linearly with width at height 3

**Exact maxima** (`experiments/separation/response_sat.py`). SAT, via CaDiCaL, computes the maximum response-run
length of a *fixed* lattice over all monotone F and cuts D:
- the stages, bad pairs, responses, meet-closure and stuck test are encoded exactly;
- k increases until the instance is UNSAT;
- every satisfying model is decoded, checked for monotonicity and the cut property, and re-simulated with the reference
  `general_sep.response_run`.

Output `out_response_sat.txt`.

| Lattice | Height | Exact maximum |
|---|---|---|
| chains with 3–7 elements | 2–6 | H − 1 |
| closure systems on 4 points, \|L\| = 7–12 | 3, 4 | 5–7 at H = 3; up to **10** at H = 4, \|L\| = 12 |
| height-3 linear spaces, \|L\| = 9, 10, 11–13, 15, 16, 18 | 3 | 6, 7, 8, 8–10, 10, 10 |
| **near-pencils on k = 4, 5, 6, 7 points** | 3 | **7, 9, 11, 13 = 2k − 1** |

The hill-climbing of §34.3z¹³¹ (≤ 6 at H = 3) badly underestimated these maxima.

**An explicit family** (`pencil_family.py`).
- *Lattice:* the near-pencil on k points p, q₁, …, q_m (m = k − 1), with lines lᵢ = {p, qᵢ} and the long line
  Q = {q₁, …, q_m}. Its height is 3.
- *Cut:* D = {⊥} ∪ atoms.
- *Bifunction:*
  - F(⊥, ⊤) = p, F(p, ⊤) = l₁, F(p, l₁) = q₁;
  - for j < m, F(qⱼ, ⊤) = l_{j+1} and F(qⱼ, l_{j+1}) = q_{j+1};
  - F(q_m, ⊤) = ⊤;
  - every other cell with an atom row is ⊥; every row outside D is ⊤.
- *Monotone:* every line contains p, so F(⊥, ⊤) ≤ F(a, ⊤) for every atom a; columns hold because q_{j+1} ≤ l_{j+1}.
- *The run:* it zigzags (⊥, ⊤), (p, ⊤), (p, l₁), (q₁, ⊤), (q₁, l₂), …, adding one element per stage. Every other
